| `HIPFIRE_NGRAM_MOD_N_MATCH` / `HIPFIRE_NGRAM_MOD_N_MIN` / `HIPFIRE_NGRAM_MOD_N_MAX` | **5 / 1 / 63** on admitted Qwen4 targets with the copy lane armed (the `auto` default on gfx1151 / gfx1201); **5 / 3 / 3** there with `HIPFIRE_MTP_NGRAM_COPY=off` (the 5/3/3 pool); **24 / 48 / 64** elsewhere | Developer overrides for the ngram-mod triple (match length / min draft / max draft), applied per component over the per-arch default. With the copy lane off, `n_max` on Qwen4 is capped at 63 (the 64-row verify holds the seed plus up to 63 candidates). With the copy lane on, `n_match` is the shortest key of the copy matcher (the longer keys are 8, 16 and 32), `n_max` is capped at 63 (the 64-row spec verify capacity holds the seed plus up to 63 candidates) and is the proposal and observation length, not the take cap: a takeover takes at most `min(n_max, 7)` candidates, 7 being one below the rows a verify forward runs bitwise like the single-row decode (the per-GPU row-exact verify rows, `rdna_compute::prefill_invariant::qwen4_row_exact_verify_rows`, 8 on gfx1151 and gfx1201 today; a wider forward runs prefill kernels). |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED` | **on**; `0` opts out | `speculation.mtp_sampled` (stable; introduced as an experimental opt-in and flipped to default on in 0.4.1): Qwen4 (Flash-Next) native MTP also serves sampled (temperature > 0) requests by speculative rejection sampling — a draft drawn from `q` (the request's truncation applied to the draft head's 8 exactly re-scored candidates) is accepted with probability `min(1, p/q)`, a rejection emits a draw from `(p − q)+`, an all-accepted window emits its bonus from `p`, where `p` is exactly the AR sampler's distribution (`llama::sample_top_k_p`: temperature, top_k with absent = 20 and 0 = 64 candidates, min_p, top_p). Lossless against AR in distribution, not byte-identical to AR for a seed; one seed replays the same text. Greedy requests are unchanged. `0`: sampled requests run AR. Under `state_quant = "q8_ef"` sampled requests run AR whatever this variable says, until the q8_ef sampled-MTP law is verified (load line `qwen4 native MTP sampled: off for GDN q8_ef …`). Repeat/presence/frequency penalties stay on MTP (0.4.1): every target row — the first token, each verify row and the bonus — is penalized exactly as the host AR sampler penalizes it, against the trailing `repeat_window` tokens of the full prompt + emitted tokens + the window's earlier drafts, before truncation; the draft `q` is penalized the same way. Temperature-0 requests with non-neutral penalties run the Qwen4 AR producer (penalize-then-argmax). |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_OWN_PREFILL` | **unset / off**; `1` opts out | Qwen35 MTP prompt fill. Default: the trunk prefills the prompt through AR's own route (same outer chunks, widened chunk, GDN chunk scan, standard dispatch) and hands its hidden rows to the MTP head, so the prompt's KV, DeltaNet state and first-token logits match AR's. `1` restores MTP's previous route: 512-row trunk chunks captured as a speculative verify (sequential GDN recurrence; on Q8 KV, no query16 flash prefill). Resolved once per MTP load (`hipfire_config::mtp_own_prefill`); serve.log prints `qwen35 MTP prompt fill route: …`. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_FAST` | **on**; `0` opts out | Qwen35 MTP prompt fill only (native decode and the MTP auto sidecar/AR prompt route are unchanged). Default: the head's KV-only Append writes K/V with two same-stream `hipMemcpy2DAsync` pitched D2D copies instead of 2n synchronous per-row concat memcpys, omits the unused Q branch (including its residual), runs the identical K-only RoPE kernel with `n_heads_q = 0`, and reuses the same FWHT-rotated activation for eligible wk/wv (MQ4G256, no AWQ). Fill preparation zeroes the head's KV buffers on device (ordered `hipMemsetD32`) instead of host-side zero vectors with H2D uploads; a cold or missing predecessor's zero also uses `hipMemsetD32`, and the valid prefix carry-copy is unchanged. On eligible gfx1201 Q8-KV heads it uses lean fill-only scratch (borrowing inactive fields) and larger head slices (default 512 → 1,688 rows; 384 → 1,264), capped so scratch does not exceed the previous full scratch plus rotation bytes. The separate `HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_PM_GEMM` switch controls the exact head GEMM replacement. `0` restores the prior qwen35 MTP prompt fill. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_PM_GEMM` | **on**; `0` opts out | Qwen35 MTP fast prompt fill only, exact gfx1201 and MQ4G256-v1 eh_proj/wk/wv (including shared-rotation wv). Uses the byte-identical PeaceMaker four-weight-row GEMM, sharing each activation load across rows. Requires `HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_FAST`; other dtypes, architectures and callers retain the incumbent. `0` restores `gemm_hfq4g256` without changing fill slicing or rotation. Resolved once per process through the developer environment reader. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_MTP` / `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_MTP_K` | Retired; no runtime readers | These former Qwen35-specific controls no longer affect loading. Use unified `speculation.mtp` (`auto` by default, which keeps Qwen35 on AR because it declares no measured AR floor; `on` / `--spec mtp` opts in): the loader then uses a bundled MTP head first, then a `.mtp` sidecar. Use `speculation.mtp_k` for the draft cap; see [Speculative decode](CONFIG.md#speculative-decode). |
| `HIPFIRE_DEEPSEEK4_SPEC_DECODE` / `HIPFIRE_DEEPSEEK4_SPEC_K` | DeepSeek MTP legacy | |
| `HIPFIRE_DEEPSEEK4_DSPARK` / `HIPFIRE_DEEPSEEK4_DSPARK_CONF_THRESHOLD` | DSpark | |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN3_DSPARK_CONF_THRESHOLD` / `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_DSPARK_CONF_THRESHOLD` | per-arch conf | |
| `HIPFIRE_DDTREE_BUDGET` / `HIPFIRE_DDTREE_TOPK` | tree draft | Runtime defaults 256/8 if env-only; CLI config defaults 0/4 |
| `HIPFIRE_DDTREE_*` | research/diag family | See inventory; not product defaults |

### Qwen4 / Qwen3.8 Flash-Next (arch 16)

Read only by the Qwen4 carrier and its kernels; no other model reads them.

| Variable | Default / sense | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_MQ4` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Exact-wide MQ4G256V2 x4 GEMV candidate on gfx1151/gfx1201: admitted actual rows 9..16 select `_wide_r16`, 17..32 `_wide_r32`. Unset/`0` and unsupported shapes retain S1's exact <=8-row launch groups. Does not raise `Gpu::few_row_limit()` or admit prefill. Default stays off pending architecture-local byte/state/resource/timing gates. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_MQ6` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Same admission and fallback as `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_MQ4`, for MQ6G256V2 single/x4 exact-wide GEMVs; independent of the prefill MQ6 WMMA/PM switches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_BF16` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Same admission and fallback, for ordinary BF16 x4 and HC k4 wide candidates. Unsupported scalar-domain shapes retain exact row tiling. HC quarter folds/activation and the existing <=8-row entries are unchanged. Tiny HC gates may retain the exact tiled specialization. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_Q8` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Same admission and fallback, for Q8 K2560 single/x2, K320, small-K four-chain and HC k8 wide candidates. Uses the existing decode copies and each shape's decode arithmetic recipe; does not create copies or reinterpret BF16 router/selector weights as Q8. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_TWIN_K1` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Sealed-slab K1 lane-union candidates on gfx1151/gfx1201: admitted lanes 9..16 select `_c16`, 17..32 `_c32`; <=8 keep narrow entries. Otherwise S1 retains exact <=8-lane groups. Descriptor capacity 32 reserves 7688 bytes before capture; the 512-thread planner preserves first-occurrence order. Capacity is not admission: the shared per-architecture `Gpu::few_row_limit()` still controls routing. The twins exist only for the shared MQ4/W3 K1 images: layers with an E8P cold bucket (gfx1201) and gfx1100 keep the S1 exact <=8-lane groups. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_GROUPED_MOE` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Eager 9..=`Gpu::few_row_limit()` verify of the sealed QT44/QT53 routed MoE keeps the grouped, expert-unioned route of the <=8-row call (scatter, grouped gate/up, fused unscatter, per-slot down) instead of the per-(row, slot) indexed singleton route that `wide_exact` selects. Shared-expert and HC fusions keep their wide-exact route. Read once. Not a qualification: the byte oracle is `qwen4_wide_grouped_moe_oracle`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_GROUPED_DOWN` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | With `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_GROUPED_MOE=1`, a 9..=`Gpu::few_row_limit()` verify runs the grouped, expert-unioned routed down (`gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10`) and the grouped combine instead of the per-slot indexed down, so each expert's down weights are read once per call. <=8 rows are unchanged. Read once. Byte oracle: `qwen4_wide_grouped_moe_oracle`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA` | on unless `0` | Prefill F16 WMMA arms (grouped MoE gate/up and down, BF16 dense projections through an F16 shadow, HC read, full-window QSA, chunked GDN) from 512 rows. Not bit-exact; admitted by KLD against the BF16 source. `0` keeps the bit-exact F32 arms. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_ELEM` | on (Route-A twin of `kernels/src/qwen4_g942_glue_elem.hip` sealed); `0` off (developer). Read once | Exact gfx942 (MI300X) Qwen4 decode and lane steps, C1 elementwise launch folds (bitwise their unfused routes). HC read: the BF16 down GEMM of 1..=16 rows runs `qwen4_g942_gemm_bf16_rows16_hc_act` (`_x2` for an F32 `x` of 9..=16 rows), a single-member copy of `gemm_bf16_mfma_gfx942_rows16` whose output store applies the activation chain (`bf16_roundtrip_f32`, `scale_f32`, `bf16_roundtrip_f32`, `silu_f32`, `bf16_roundtrip_f32`) to the F32 accumulator: 6 launches → 1 per HC read, 5 × 97 = 485 fewer launches per C1 decode token (`beat/C/FoldElem.md`). Declined (unfused launches kept) when `HIPFIRE_G942_TRUNK_GEMV=1` at one row, under a projection-split hook, or when the rows16 entry would decline (rows > 16, alignment, `K % 8`). Retirement: the switch is deleted (fold unconditional where the rows16 entry accepts) when the gfx942 decode step moves onto the 0.5.0 typed Step program (#666), or after one release default-on without a regression, whichever is first. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_ELEM_IMAGE` | unset (developer); an absolute path to a device ELF | Twin-proof hook for `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_ELEM`: when set, the module `qwen4_g942_glue_elem` (entries `qwen4_g942_gemm_bf16_rows16_hc_act`, `_x2`) is loaded from that code object through `ensure_embedded_kernel` instead of the hipcc source, so the Tier C cases run the Route-A twin's bytes. Unset: the hipcc source, unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA_GFX1201` | gfx1201: on unless `0` | Exact gfx1201, eager-only: runs the `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA` BF16-projection and HC-read arms on gfx12 WMMA kernels. The M=1 shared selector stays on exact multirow (U1a owns fusion). X rounds F32→F16 RNE; BF16 weights convert to F16 (exact for in-range normals); WMMA F32 16-element substeps and fixed-order split-K reduction change summation order, so it is not bit-exact against the multirow/SIMT arms. Admitted by Flash-Next KLD against the BF16 teacher on an R9700 (WikiText-2 −0.0033 [−0.0082, +0.0006], code +0.0047 [−0.0030, +0.0131] vs `=0`); pp8192 2,169 → 3,598 tok/s with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_FUSE=1` and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TILE`. `0` keeps gfx1201 on the multirow/SIMT arms; this is the kill switch. Recording/capture and other architectures retain their existing routes. Empty split-K work launches nothing. |
| `HIPFIRE_G942_BF16_WIDE` | **off** (developer) until the Route-A twin of `kernels/src/gemm_bf16_mfma_wide.gfx942.hip` is sealed; `1` on. Read once | Exact gfx942 (MI300X) BF16 trunk projection groups of 17.. rows: while `rdna_compute::cdna::gfx942::wide_max_rows` admits any member (weights of at least 2^20 elements at every row count; smaller ones up to 456 rows), one launch per 64 rows of `gemm_bf16_mfma_gfx942_wide64` / `_m32` / `_m64` replaces the rows16 chain / 32-row tile entry: each weight is read from HBM once per 64 rows, the weight tile and the activation are staged through LDS. Bitwise the tile entry's bytes (Tier C `qwen4_gemm_bf16_mfma_gfx942_wide`). Unset: the P1a route (`HIPFIRE_G942_ROWS16_CHAIN`), unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_G942_BF16_WIDE_IMAGE` | unset (developer); an absolute path to a device ELF | Twin-proof hook for `HIPFIRE_G942_BF16_WIDE`: when set, the module `gemm_bf16_mfma_wide_gfx942` (entries `gemm_bf16_mfma_gfx942_wide64`, `_m32`, `_m64`) is loaded from that code object through `ensure_embedded_kernel` instead of the hipcc source, so the Tier C case `qwen4_gemm_bf16_mfma_gfx942_wide` runs the Route-A twin's bytes. Retired with the twin (the sealed image replaces the hipcc source). Unset: the hipcc source, unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_FRONT_FUSE` | exact gfx1201 and exact gfx1151: on unless `0` | Exact gfx1201 or exact gfx1151, eager-only (no recorder or graph capture), sealed grouped MoE prefill on the symmetric IU4 route with the BF16 router + shared-expert F16 WMMA group launch (on gfx1151 also with the fused shared gate/up PeaceMaker launch, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_GU_PM`, whose router + selector groups read the F16 X). The HC `mixed` rows are read once by `mq_rotate_x_i4_f16out`, which emits the routed gate/up `block_i4_128` sidecar (the bytes of `mq_rotate_x_i4`) and the router / shared-expert F16 X (the bytes of `convert_f32_to_f16`), and the F32 `mq_rotate_x` is not run where the chain launches it (gfx1201; no reader of its `x_rot_batch` exists on this route). Byte-identical to the unfused chain, which `0` restores exactly; every other arch and route, the S1 layer-major prepare/slowlink tiles, CPU split and recorder/capture keep the unfused chain. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_X_LDS` | `auto` (developer): exact gfx1151, on where the F16 WMMA MoE route runs; `1` forces, `0` kills | Values `auto`, `1` or `0`. Exact gfx1151 F16 WMMA routed-expert GEMMs: gate/up SiLU takes `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_wmma_k2_silu_bf16out_x4` (M % 64 == 0, K % 256 == 0) and the BF16-output down GEMM `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_wmma_gfx1151_x8_bf16out` (M % 128 == 0): four/eight row-tile waves share each expert run's X through LDS, output bytes equal to the per-wave entries'. `auto` (unset) arms exactly when a layer's MoE runs on the F16 WMMA route, i.e. its symmetric IU4 arm is not selected (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0`, or experts that fail the symmetry check such as the asymmetric `qwen3.8-flash-next.mq4`); the default IU4 route of the GPTQ3 artifact never reaches these GEMMs and is unchanged. `1` forces the entries wherever admissible (the same route and arch); `0` keeps the per-wave entries and launch geometry everywhere. F32 output, decode and other architectures are unchanged. These entries are HIP, not PeaceMaker-emitted. Measured on Strix Halo, F16 route (`qwen3.8-flash-next-gptq3.mq4` with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0`, `hipfire bench --matrix` synthetic stream, 3 fresh processes per arm): pp512 +6.6 %, pp1024 +7.4 %, pp8192 +9.4 % over the per-wave entries, decode unchanged. The gfx1151 byte oracle is `qwen4_moe_f16_lds_oracle --mode check` (build with `--features lab`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_TWIN` | on unless `0` (developer): exact gfx1151 | Exact gfx1151, Qwen4 grouped MoE down GEMM (M = 2560, K = 640, one X row per top-10 slot) on batches of >= 9 tokens below the F16 WMMA window (the >= 512-token WMMA arm is checked first): runs `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_xs4_gfx1151` (module `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_xs`; four waves, 32 rows per block, each group's X chunk staged in LDS once per block) instead of `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_gfx1151`. Bitwise the O8×R16 output: same per-output decode, accumulation order and wave32 reduction; only where X is loaded from changes. `0` restores O8×R16 for every row count. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TWIN` | on unless `0` (developer): exact gfx1151 (>= 16 rows) and exact gfx1201 (<= 8 rows) | Qwen4 HC read tail `hyper_read_up_fused`: runs `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx` (module `hyper_read_up_fused_twin`) instead of `hyper_read_up_fused_f32` on exact gfx1151 for batches of >= 16 rows; on exact gfx1201 for batches of <= 8 rows (decode, verify, fn-lane steps) the twin family is admitted and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_T1` (default on) picks its one-token-per-wave entry `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx_t1` over this one. Same kernel arguments, block and dynamic LDS; bitwise the incumbent's output: only the output stores move (held in registers until the last pass), so the wave-uniform X loads become scalar loads (gfx1201: `crates/rdna-compute/tests/hyper_read_up_twin_hw.rs`, raw f32 bits, F32 and BF16 `normalized`). Other row counts and architectures keep the incumbent. `0` restores `hyper_read_up_fused_f32` for every row count (including the `_t1` entry). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_T1` | on unless `0` (developer): exact gfx1201, <= 8 rows | Qwen4 HC read tail `hyper_read_up_fused` on exact gfx1201 for batches of <= 8 rows (decode, verify, fn-lane steps): runs `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx_t1` (module `hyper_read_up_fused_twin_t1`, `kernels/src/hyper_read_up_fused_twin_t1.gfx1201.hip`), the `_sx` twin with one token per wave (8 waves = 8 tokens per block, grid `[hidden / 8, ceil(rows / 8)]`; the `_sx` twin leaves 6 of 8 waves idle at 8 rows). Same kernel arguments, block and dynamic LDS; per-(token, row) arithmetic textually the twin's, bitwise the incumbent's output (`crates/rdna-compute/tests/hyper_read_up_twin_hw.rs`, raw f32 bits). `0` falls back to the `_sx` twin (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TWIN=0` still forces `hyper_read_up_fused_f32`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_T1_K320` | on; `0` off (developer): exact gfx1201, 2..=8 rows, `low_rank == 320` | Qwen4 HC read tail on the t1 rows: runs `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx_t1_k320` (same module `hyper_read_up_fused_twin_t1`) instead of `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx_t1`. K is a compile-time 320, so the five 16-byte weight loads per thread issue before the first LDS store and the wave's `normalized` element loads early; per-(token, row) arithmetic text unchanged, bitwise the t1 output (`crates/rdna-compute/tests/hyper_read_up_twin_hw.rs`, 0 mismatches in 3,870,720 words). Standalone microbench (6.55 MB up weights, cold, back-to-back): 20.8 -> 17.7 us. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_ROT` | on; `0` off (developer): exact gfx1201, 2..=8 rows, `low_rank == 320`, `hidden % 256 == 0`, not recording/capturing | The HC read tail also writes the next mixer's `mq_rotate_x` of `mixed` in the same launch (`hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx_t1_k320_rot`, module `hyper_read_up_fused_rot_t1`, `kernels/src/hyper_read_up_fused_rot_t1.gfx1201.hip`): the wave of the last of the 32 blocks that write a token's 256-column group (zeroed arrival counter per (token, group), the fused GDN step's `gdn_pair_counters`, reset by that wave) runs the FWHT; the next matching `rotate_x_mq_batched` skips (`ScratchState::prerotated`). Removes one `mq_rotate_x` per HC read at C8 (2 per layer). Bitwise the t1 `mixed` and `mq_rotate_x(mixed)` (`crates/rdna-compute/tests/hyper_read_up_rot_hw.rs`, 0 mismatches over 84 cases, counters back to zero, skip verified). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GATEUP_TWIN` | on unless `0` (developer): exact gfx1151 | Exact gfx1151, Qwen4 grouped MoE gate/up GEMM (M = 1280, K = 2560, ten X rows per token) on batches of more than eight tokens below the F16 WMMA window (the >= 512-token WMMA arm is checked first): runs `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r8_pipe_gfx1151` (module `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r8_pipe`) instead of `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r8_x4`. Bitwise the O4×R8 X4 output: same per-output arithmetic; only the scheduling changes (fully unrolled ten-group walk, waves per block, prefetch depth). Batches of eight tokens or fewer keep the O4×R2 incumbent. `0` restores O4×R8 X4 for every row count. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE32_GU` | on (unset = `1`); `0` off; `2` on without dead-slot zero stores (developer): exact gfx1201 | Flash-Next grouped MoE gate/up (M = 1280, K = 2560, ten slots per token) on 9..=48 source rows, shipped or F1 drop-zero planar layout: `gemm_mq4g256v2[p]_moe_grouped_top10_g32_{r8,r4}` (module `gemm_mq4g256v2[p]_moe_grouped_top10_g32`) replace the O4×R8 X4 kernel: grid `[M / 32, ceil(grouped_rows / 16)]`, block 256 (one 16-slot tile x 32 rows per block, live-slot-only double-buffered LDS X, 3-group register weight ring). `1`: every output word (dead-slot zeros included) is byte-identical to the O4×R8 X4 kernel (`moe_wide_oracle` on the real expert tensors); `2` selects the `_nodz` entries, which leave dead slots unwritten (live grouped rows identical; the unscatter reads live rows only). Read once at device creation; `Gpu::set_moe32_gu` overrides. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_EP_A8` | unset: **on only for expert-parallel forwards below 512 rows outside the ordinary-prefill intent, and for serve lane prefill tiles / waves below 512 rows while `serve.batch_exact` is off** (the lowered-floor tile route, see `HIPFIRE_EP_PREFILL_TILE_MIN_ROWS`); every ordinary prefill forward (single request, session resume, C1 non-lane; any extent: sub-512 tails and the session cache's lineage split included, so EP2 logits do not depend on `prefill.chunk_rows`), every lane tile under `serve.batch_exact`, every >= 512-row forward and the single-card chain keep A4. Lane prefill is distribution-equivalent, not chunk-invariant, by design: a wave runs outside the ordinary-prefill intent (only the A8 chain is proven per-slot independent in a lane wave), and `serve.batch_exact` lanes never wave (their canonical A4 tiles run serially); exact `1` on in every forward the chain serves; exact `0` off (kill switch: the A4 chain) (developer) | Exact gfx1201 Flash-Next expert-parallel prefill: the owner-routed symmetric MoE chain (gate/up + down, every prefill forward it serves: the >= 512-row product route and any lowered floor) quantizes its activations to int8 per 128 (A8, FWHT256/128 producers, signed-IU8 GEMMs, planar and shipped layouts) instead of 4 bits per 32 (A4): byte-checked against an independent CPU integer reference on card (0 mismatching bytes, 14/14 negative controls), relative L2 vs the exact route ~14x closer than A4 (gate/up .004-.007 vs .042-.10). Paired prefill KLD with the sub-512 route lowered to the chain (`HIPFIRE_EP_PREFILL_TILE_MIN_ROWS=9`): A4 +0.0070 WT2 / +0.0058 code24, A8 -0.0020 / -0.0010 (within noise of today's exact route). On >= 512-row prompts A8 measured +4.4% pp4096 prefill time (j7430, 5 ABBA pairs: median 1.801 vs 1.725 s) and no measurable KLD change (j7431: WT2 -0.0020, code24 +0.0020, CIs +-0.01), which is why the default does not apply there. Eager only: under graph capture or a recorded replay the A4 chain runs. The first launch of each stage prints `qwen4 moe EP A8: first launch ...`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DEAD_STORES` | skip unless `1` (developer): exact gfx1201 | Flash-Next grouped routed MoE kernels (down `gemm_mq4g128v2[p]_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16`, shipped and F1 planar, every row count it serves; gate/up `o4_r8_x4` many-row arm): default **skips the +0.0 stores of dead grouped rows** (sentinel tiles `tile_id < 0` / beyond the live tiles, and pad slots `sorted_slot < 0` of live tiles); live rows are bitwise the zero-store kernels' words. The grouped Y consumers address it only through `inverse_perm` / `sorted_slot_index` (combine, unscatter). The skip entries are the same sources compiled with `HF_NODZ` (module `..._nodz`, entry `<name>_nodz`). `1` restores every zero store (byte-identical grouped buffers to the pre-skip kernels). `Gpu::set_moe_dead_stores` overrides. The production launch grid carries `ceil16(slots + 15*min(slots, 512)) / 16` tiles (310 at 31 rows) for ~79 live ones; the zero stores of the dead tiles were ~45 MB per down launch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_A8` | **on** (default on, exact gfx1201, EP lanes >8 rows); `0` kills (developer); `1` forces it on any exact gfx1201 device | Flash-Next routed-expert DOWN as int8 per-128 activations x symmetric qt53 weights (iu8 WMMA grouped kernel `qwen4_moe_down_a8_grouped*_gfx1201`, `qwen4_moe_down_a8.rs`). Unset on an EP rank of exact gfx1201 (set by the EP attach) it serves the **9..=64-row window only** (singleton verify blocks, prefill tails, the all-rows / wide-class lane groups); decode, AR8, C1 and every <= 8-row launch keep the incumbent down by construction (`down_a8_min_rows`). Needs the loader's symmetric verdict for every routed layer (and the MTP head layer); a failed or unchecked verdict keeps the incumbent down (INFO line). `0` kills it everywhere; an explicit `1` keeps the old opt-in meaning (any exact gfx1201 device, every row count 1..=64) with the same verdict gate; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_A8_INDEXED=1` (default off) additionally enables the per-pick dot4 arm for the gathered decode rows. KLD cost accepted for A8 in verify rows (served, j7564): code24 +0.0035 [+0.0003, +0.0073], WT2 +0.0007 [-0.0013, +0.0027]. Refused inside the lane row-exact scope (`Gpu::lane_row_exact_scope`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ROW_EXACT`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_A8` | **on, owner-masked** (unset = on; exact `1` also on); exact `2` on but UNMASKED (developer byte A/B); exact `0` off (kill switch, developer): exact gfx1201 | Flash-Next EP2 decode / verify routed MoE of **9..=64 source rows** (the `g32` gate/up window, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE32_GU`'s: `Gpu::moe_wide_verify_rows`) on the trunk shard's partial-FFN route (the call whose EP row gather is a no-op: `MoeRowGather::gather_skipped`): the int8 per-128 chain of the EP prefill (`qwen4_moe_ep_a8.rs`: `qwen4_moe_rotate256_i8` -> `gemm_qwen4_moe_gate_up_silu_a8_sym` -> `qwen4_moe_rotate128_i8` -> `gemm_qwen4_moe_down_a8_sym` -> `moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in`) replaces the `g32` gate/up, the unscatter + FWHT128, the PM A8 down (and its `qwen4_moe_down_a8_quant`), the down unscatter and the expanded top-10 combine (`ep_a8_wide`, `qt44_qt53_prefill.rs`). Per-call conditions: grouped path 2 above the few-row limit, drop-zero planar experts with the loader's symmetric verdict (`Qt44Qt53GroupedSymmetric`), BF16 recipe, F32 `x_norm_batch`, gate/up K 2560, `mi == down_k == 640`, top-10, not the symmetric IU4 prefill route, no tile exchange / CPU split, eager (no graph capture or replay record; EP is eager-only). Default (owner-masked): the decode tiles are masked after the scatter (`ep_tile_mask`: a copy of `expert_tile_ids` with every non-owned expert's tile `-1`; the rank's static `e % ranks` ownership, an owner table and a masked-id scratch built once at EP attach and offered by `MoeRowGather::decode_owner_mask`), both A8 GEMMs read the copy and skip those tiles (no zero-dummy tile runs), and the combine is the owned BF16-row one (`moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_owned` over the same owner table: a foreign rank adds +0 with its own weight and rounding). `2` runs the same chain over the scatter's unmasked ids on the zero-dummy expert tables (every grouped row written, +0 for dummy tiles) and the plain BF16-row combine; the two are byte-identical. A call that does gather (the MTP head, a non-shard EP, a balanced / partitioned layer) needs F32 slot-ordered rows and keeps the incumbent chain. Only numeric change: the int8-per-128 gate/up input (E0, KLD-neutral); for the same SwiGLU rows the down records, BF16 down rows and combine residuals are byte-identical to the incumbent chain's. `mq_rotate_x` is skipped on this route unless a shared projection is `MQ4G256V2`. The int8 scratch is reserved at EP load (`reserve_qwen4_moe_ep_a8`, also when `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_EP_A8=0`). One `[INFO] qwen4 moe GU A8: first wide-verify launch ...` stderr line (naming `owner-masked` / `unmasked` tiles) proves the route ran; `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1` adds the A8 GEMMs' `[moe-entry]` receipts. `0` restores the incumbent chain (g32 gate/up, PM A8 down, expanded combine). Refused inside the lane row-exact scope (`Gpu::lane_row_exact_scope`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ROW_EXACT`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DEAD_POISON` | unset (lab/developer only; never set in product) | `<u32 seed>`: before every grouped routed gate/up and down launch the whole Y span is filled (`hipMemsetD32Async`, stream ordered) with a quiet-NaN word derived from the seed and a per-`Gpu` invocation counter, so dead grouped words differ per run and per call and any live read of a dead row shows up as a byte difference. Proof arms: `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DEAD_STORES=1` control versus the default skip with seeds 1 and 2. `Gpu::set_moe_dead_poison` overrides. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_PROJ_REGIONS` | **opt-in**; off unless `1` | F16 WMMA projection route only: virtually concatenate router/shared gate/up/selector rows of K=2560 into one LDS launch; gfx11 HC-down (320×10240) can use it alone. Ascending 16-element K steps are unchanged. gfx1201 keeps its exact short selector and split-K=4 HC-down separate; this flag does not admit the F16 route itself. PLE, shared-down and HC-write dispatch are unchanged. Default off pending G0 and timing approval. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_DOWN_EPI` | **opt-in**; off unless `1` | Fuses BF16 shared-down GEMM and row-scaled residual add for BF16 recipes on the existing F16 WMMA route (512+ rows, M >= 1024, eager-only). Rounds the value, row scalar, residual, product and sum independently to BF16, storing BF16-rounded F32; preserves the incumbent WMMA accumulation. gfx1201 also requires its F16 WMMA route (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA_GFX1201`, default on); it does not admit that route itself. Unset/`0`, recording/capture, other dtypes and non-BF16 recipes retain the separate kernels. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_R8_PK` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere (gfx1100 not admitted; needs `HIPFIRE_MQ6_ROWS_SPLIT` on) | Byte-identical. Exact gfx1201, `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows` and `_x4` at 3..=8 rows and total M <= 16384 (the Flash-Next GDN / QSA / out trunk projections of decode, native-MTP verify and DFlash verify; never the LM head): runs `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows[_x4]_pk_r{R}_w{W}_s{S}` (`kernels/src/gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows_pk.hip`, same `(W, S)` as the `HIPFIRE_MQ6_ROWS_SPLIT` table) instead of the `_split_` kernels. Same main loop, per-lane streams and fold tree; the per-output 32-lane `shfl_down` reduction is replaced by a packed butterfly (`permlanex16` + DPP `row_xmask`, two outputs per register per level), about 650 fewer instructions per wave at 8 rows. `0` restores the split kernels exactly. Oracle + cold-HBM timing: `mq6_r8_pk_oracle` (`cargo run --release -p rdna-compute --features lab --example mq6_r8_pk_oracle`; 0 mismatches over 4368 cells on gfx1201). Receipts: byte oracle `mq6_r8_pk_oracle` on gfx1201 (j7717-era card-C run, 4368 cells, 123,913,520 words, 0 mismatches vs the per-row decode singleton); rocprof j7716 (served C8 AR, 324 vs 321 steady 8-row steps): dense MQ6 trunk 3365 -> 2933 us/step, traced step 29.90 -> 28.83 ms, `_pk_r8` kernels 20.6 / 42.3 / 39.7 us vs split 22.1 / 50.7 / 44.2 us; served identity j7716 on vs `=0`: AR 20/20 (C1/C8 greedy and seeded), MTP 6/20 with C1 greedy identical (beta MTP A/A is 11/20); served decode A/B j7719 (3 loads/arm) B/A 0.979-1.048, no load CI below 1.. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_X4_GFX1201` | Qwen4 prefill chunks of >= 512 rows: on; set, on only for `1` | Unset, it applies only inside the Qwen4 (Flash-Next) forward, on prefill chunks of >= 512 rows (decode keeps the GEMV); `1` forces it for every MQ6G256V2 GEMM at every row count, any other value (`0`) disables it. Exact gfx1201, eager-only MQ6G256V2 trunk projections: BT8 four-row-tile X-LDS WMMA overwrite replaces memset + residual; shared rotation produces F16 directly, and chunked GDN qkv may store BF16 bits with RNE. Keeps ascending K tiles and the baseline F16 dequantization. Capture/retained recording and other architectures retain their existing routes. Bitwise-identical to the memset + residual route it replaces; `0` keeps that route. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_X4_TILE` | Qwen4 forward on gfx1151: `auto` | Exact gfx1151 MQ6G256V2 X-LDS overwrite tile: unset, the Qwen4 (Flash-Next) forward uses the measured per-shape table in `docs/quant-formats/mq-v2-family.md`; `BVxRWxprefetch` (`BV` 8/12/16, `RW` 4/8, prefetch 1/2) forces one tile, and `auto` the table, for every MQ6G256V2 GEMM, including the a/b/z region fold and the HC-write epilogue launch (those two have BV8/BV16 twins only; BV12 keeps their incumbent; the table routes them only at their measured N=8192/2048 rows); `0` keeps the incumbent BT8 x4 kernels. Every tile keeps each output's ascending-K WMMA chain and epilogue expression, so the bytes are identical. Other arches ignore it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_X4_REGIONS` | Qwen4 forward on gfx1151: on; else off unless `1` | Exact; unset, on only inside the Qwen4 (Flash-Next) forward on gfx1151, `1` on gfx1151/gfx1201 everywhere: folds the chunked GDN a/b/z MQ6 F32 projections (sharing one prepared F16 rotation) into one row-region launch with independent accumulators; bytes identical to three launches. `0` keeps three launches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK` | `auto` (gfx1151 only; effectively `off` elsewhere) | Flash-Next family-B MQ6 input-projection trunk: `off|auto|on|emulate`, config `fusions.npu_trunk` (alias `npu_trunk`). Auto arms only with an open NPU and matching schema-4 BF16-direct calibration selecting a nonempty family set. The planner admits rebalanced pairs and row-adaptive 256-row-wave chains above the stored crossover; at most four distinct wave counts are built per load, and a pair needing a fifth runs GPU-native. There is no I32 serving contract; QSA and GDN out projections are not shipped on this route. Any auto load refusal logs one reason and stays GPU-only (`WARN` when a qualifying load fails to arm the NPU); an NPU fault at request time turns the `auto` route off for the rest of the process (one `WARN`) and the faulted request reruns GPU-only, while `on` fails the request closed (see [NPU trunk](npu/npu-trunk.md), Faults). The armed trunk pins 1,053,843,456 B of userptr arenas against `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK`; grant a bounded limit of 1,318,060,032 B (`LimitMEMLOCK=1318060032`, `ulimit -l 1287168`), see [host memlock limit](npu/npu-trunk.md#host-memlock-limit). `off` is the kill switch. Off and non-gfx1151 auto do no NPU/index/calibration I/O, allocations or logging. Forced on/emulate refuse other architectures; emulate is GPU-only. See [NPU trunk](npu/npu-trunk.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_CAL` | `<hipfire home>/npu-trunk/calibration.json` | Config `fusions.npu_trunk_calibration` (alias `npu_trunk_calibration`); empty uses the default. Schema 4 requires `epilogue = "bf16"` and has no `glue.fold_ms_per_col_8192` I32 field. A file takes precedence over compiled-in measured Halo overlay/stock calibration; only an absent file falls back. A file requires identical GPU PCI address/NPU firmware/build/uname/model-index/epilogue/trunk-route keys; the built-in record binds on arch/build/model-index/epilogue/trunk-route only; armed logs include `cal_source=file|builtin`. Invalid/unreadable files never fall back. Read only in effective auto/on/emulate. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_FAMILIES` | unset (developer) | Comma list of `qkv`, `zab`, `q`, `kvidx`, or `all` (names are the calibrated family groups: GDN `qkv`; GDN `z`+`a`+`b`; QSA `q`; QSA `k`+`v`+`idx`). Read once at load; forces the NPU trunk to run exactly that subset in every mode (`auto`, `on`, `emulate`), replacing the calibration-selected family set. Unset or empty keeps the mode's own selection (`auto`: calibration-selected; `on`/`emulate`: all families); `all` selects every family. Only effective when the trunk is armed (read like `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_TRACE`, never in effective `off`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_FAULT_CHAIN` | unset (developer) | Developer fault injection: a 1-based relay chain number whose chain watchdog is forced to 1 ns, the real `NPU chain watchdog timed out` path, to exercise fault containment (`auto`: the route latches off, one `WARN npu-trunk: NPU pair fault ...` line, and the faulted request finishes GPU-only; `on`: the request fails closed). Counts every chain the relay runs, in relay order. Unset or empty injects nothing; a value that is not a number >= 1 (including `0`) logs one `npu-trunk: HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_FAULT_CHAIN=... ignored` line and injects nothing. Only read when the NPU runtime opens (never in `off` or `emulate`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_SAMPLE_LOG` | unset (developer) | `1` prints one `npu-trunk: pair-sample ...` line per executed pair under `auto`: the measured NPU chain time, stall-free GPU window, GPU wait on the NPU and pair time, next to what the request was planned with (`planned(genN)`: model NPU, window, wait, pair, GPU-only time and saving), the built-in prior and the scales after the sample. Reads only the always-on pair events; adds no synchronization. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK_TRACE` | unset (developer) | Append per-(layer, bank) JSONL for successful physical pairs. GPU prepare/rotation/pack/wait/mixer events are resolved after the pair against a monotonic anchor; relay submit/done/terminal-signal timestamps use CLOCK_MONOTONIC. No added mid-pipeline completion wait, but timing events and anchor/final GPU synchronization can change scheduling. Keep unset for primary correctness/performance runs; use a separate traced run for diagnostics. Unset or empty writes nothing; ignored when the route is not armed. See [NPU trunk](npu/npu-trunk.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_WMMA_GATHER` | gfx1151, gfx1201: on unless `0` | Runs QSA prefill attention for chunks of >= 512 rows that the full-window dense route does not take (gfx1151: the past-budget chunks of the F32 state and every such chunk of the q8 state, the gfx1151 default; gfx1201: every such chunk) on the gathered F16 WMMA kernels (`indexed_attention_gathered_wmma.gfx1151.hip` on the gfx1151 F32 and q8 states, `indexed_attention_gathered_wmma.gfx1201.hip` on the gfx1201 fp8 state) instead of `indexed_attention_attention_*_batched_hg4`. Each call first writes the layer's cache rows `[0, end)` as F16 into the route's own workspace (2 KiB per context token: 128 MiB at 64K, 512 MiB at 262K). The load reserves its address for the whole `max_seq` before any capture (logged as `qwen4 QSA gather workspace`). With VMM QSA state (`kv_backend` resolved to `vmm`, gfx1151 and gfx1201) each prefill forward maps only the prefix `[0, end)` it reads, and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS=auto` charges the first chunk's bytes; with legacy state the load commits all of it and the reserve charges all of it. Not bit-exact. Acceptance bar: its error against an f64 reference of the same quantized source is no worse than storing Q/K/V/P as BF16. On the worst-error 1536-row snapshot per arch (training-storage calibration) the gathered route measured max |err| 1.93e-3 (gfx1151) / 2.78e-3 (gfx1201) against 1.58e-2 / 1.68e-2 for BF16 storage, p99 9.8e-5 / 1.24e-4 against 1.01e-3 / 1.23e-3. The bar compares aggregate max and p99 on these two snapshots, not each element (93.4–93.5% of elements are within their BF16-storage error) and not a model-global bound. `0` keeps every launch of the incumbent route. Other arches and state formats ignore it; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA=0` also disables it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_F16_ARENA` | **opt-in**; off unless `1` (gfx1151 F32 QSA K/V, head_dim 256 only) | BF-1, exact relative to today's gathered prefill attend: each QSA layer keeps a persistent F16 K and block-transposed V copy, converting only invalid/new rows (at most three valid rows reread for four-token alignment) with the existing producer before gathered WMMA attention. Resolved once at load; requires the gathered route enabled. Adds 2 KiB/token/layer for 2 KV heads, rounded to four-token capacity, charged in admission and state accounting. On unified-memory (UMA) archs the context admission charges the QSA growth including this arena, the resident PLE prewarm/load-fill budget and the existing headroom, so an explicit `max_seq` that no longer fits is refused at load under the default auto OOM guard (`card_cap` reports the largest fitting context with current free memory; the limit moves with free host memory and the PLE prewarm budget); a discrete GPU is unchanged. Both VMM demand mapping and legacy storage are supported. Rollback/truncation clamp the valid prefix; reset, restored/replaced prefixes and failed forwards invalidate it. Decode, MTP verify and the MTP head keep F32 K/V. `0` restores whole-prefix per-chunk conversion in the shared workspace. Hardware oracle: `qwen4_qsa_arena_check` (`reference-parity`), `QSA_ORACLE_COMPARE=1` runs fresh off/on processes; `QSA_ORACLE_KV_BACKEND=legacy` also exercises legacy storage. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_POOLED_BF16` | gfx1151: on unless `0` (exact gfx1151, F32 QSA state, index_dim 128, PM BF16 score module present, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_PM` not `0`; ignored elsewhere) | Developer switch, read once at load; `0` is the kill switch. Each QSA layer keeps a derived BF16 mirror of its pooled index keys (`[pooled block][128]`, the high 16 bits of each F32 key, exact because `qsa_pool_rope` stores BF16-rounded values) so the PM BF16 score kernel replaces the F32 one on mirror-covered select calls; selected indices, attention, state bytes and logits are unchanged. Module `qsa_pooled_bf16_mirror` converts only the blocks past a per-layer block watermark after pooling; the canonical F32 pooled arena stays authoritative and the mirror is never snapshotted. Rollback/truncation clamp the watermark to the retained complete blocks; reset, restored/replaced prefixes and failed forwards invalidate it; a select call whose mirror is not fully valid uses the F32 keys. Adds 256 B per pooled block per QSA layer (2 MiB/layer at 32K tokens, 16 MiB/layer at 256K, compress 4), charged in admission and state accounting. Direct launches only: under recording/capture the sync neither launches nor advances the watermark. Hardware oracle: `qwen4_qsa_arena_check` with the variable set must match the off arm. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_PM` | gfx1151, gfx1201: on unless `0` | Runs the gathered route's (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_WMMA_GATHER`) F16 K/V producer and attention from the embedded certified builder (PeaceMaker) module (`kernels/qsa_gather_pm_gfx{1151,1201}.hxaco`) instead of the JIT-compiled hipcc kernels: same ABI, grid, block, LDS layout and output bytes. The module has no producer for the q8 QSA state (the gfx1151 default): q8 always converts with the hipcc `indexed_attention_kv_f16vb_q8` and attends through the module. A cache whose F16 scratch or token stride would overflow the module's 32-bit buffer offsets (or `v_mad_u32_u24` operands) keeps the hipcc kernels. `0` keeps the hipcc kernels everywhere; with the gathered route off it does nothing. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_X4_PM` | gfx1151, gfx1201: on unless `0` | Runs the Qwen4 MQ6G256V2 overwrite trunk GEMM from the embedded certified builder (PeaceMaker) module instead of the JIT-compiled hipcc kernel, with the same arguments and output bytes and a 256-token batch tile (the module's `n_tile`). gfx1201: the F32 overwrite (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ6_X4_GFX1201` route, non-BF16 output; `kernels/qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1201.hxaco`, entries `qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1201_w4`/`_w8`/`_w16`) replaces `gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_gfx12_bt8_x4`; rows <= 64 use the 64-row 128-thread `_w4` entry; larger M uses the 256-row 512-thread `_w16` entry when `ceil(M/256) * ceil(tokens/256)` is a multiple of 32 (one 16-wave workgroup per WGP, whole rounds over the R9700's 32 WGPs), else the 128-row 256-thread `_w8` entry; the `_w8`/`_w16` epilogues stage the output tile through LDS for coalesced stores; BF16 output, the residual, regions and HC-write twins keep hipcc. gfx1151 is selective (`kernels/qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1151.hxaco`): only the plain F32 overwrite, the BF16-output overwrite and the a/b/z regions fold take the module's 128-row 256-thread entries (`qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1151_w8`, `_w8_bf16out`, `_w8_regions`, replacing the U3 twin or `gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_gfx11_bt8_x4*`), and only for launches of >= 2560 rows (regions: summed rows) and >= 2048 tokens. The 64-row `_w4` entries, the HC-write (`hcw`) launch, smaller launches and the residual route keep U3 / hipcc. Every other architecture keeps hipcc. `0` keeps the hipcc kernels on both (same-binary control). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_PM` | gfx1151: on unless `0` | Runs the live QSA selector pair's rows16 F32 score kernel from the certified builder (PeaceMaker) module `kernels/qsa_select_pm_gfx1151.hxaco` instead of hipcc. Exact gfx1151 only: at least one row (prefill, decode and verify calls alike; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM_DECODE=0` removes the calls below 512 rows), 4 index heads, dimension 128, <= 130816 pooled blocks, budget <= 512 and buffer extents within the module's 32-bit offsets. Selection is byte-identical. On BF16 index arenas (the q8 QSA state, the gfx1151 default) the same calls run the BF16-pooled score kernel of `kernels/qsa_select_bf16_pm_gfx1151.hxaco` (contract `crates/peacemaker-isa/kernels/qsa_select.gfx1151.score_bf16.contract.json`) followed by the select kernel, in place of the fused hipcc `indexed_attention_select_bf16_batched`; `0` keeps the fused kernel for those arenas. Recorder/graph capture, unsupported extents and other architectures keep hipcc; `0` keeps the hipcc score kernel independently of the select flag. Defined in `crates/rdna-compute/src/tensor_ops.rs`; emitter `crates/peacemaker-isa/src/kernels/qsa_score.rip.rs`, aggregator `qsa_select.rs`, contract `crates/peacemaker-isa/kernels/qsa_select.gfx1151.score.contract.json`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM` | gfx1151: on unless `0` | Runs the live QSA selector pair's select-from-scores kernel from the same certified builder module `kernels/qsa_select_pm_gfx1151.hxaco`, with the same eligibility and byte-identical selection as `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_PM`, including decode and verify calls unless `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM_DECODE=0`. On BF16 index arenas it applies only while `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_PM` is on; `0` there selects with the hipcc `indexed_attention_select_from_scores`. Recorder/graph capture and other architectures keep hipcc; `0` keeps the hipcc select kernel independently of the score flag. Defined in `crates/rdna-compute/src/tensor_ops.rs`; emitter `crates/peacemaker-isa/src/kernels/qsa_topk.rip.rs`, aggregator `qsa_select.rs`, contract `crates/peacemaker-isa/kernels/qsa_select.gfx1151.select.contract.json`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM_DECODE` | gfx1151: on unless `0` | Developer switch, read once, for the builder (PeaceMaker) QSA score/select pair of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_PM` and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM` on calls below 512 rows (decode and verify; admission uses the original or invariant-profile row count, whichever is larger). `0` disables the pair for those calls, which then keep the hipcc F32 score/select pair or, on BF16 arenas, the fused hipcc selector; calls of 512 or more rows are unaffected. Unset, the builder pair runs at every admitted row count (>= 1) on exact gfx1151 outside recording and graph capture while the PM score/select flags are on. Defined in `crates/rdna-compute/src/tensor_ops.rs`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_VMM_GFX1100` | exact gfx1100: on unless `0`; every other architecture: not read | Kill switch for Qwen4 (Flash-Next) VMM on exact gfx1100 (7900 XTX). Unset or `1`: automatic `kv_backend` resolves to VMM demand-mapped QSA context arenas (commit pages as requests touch them instead of all of `max_seq` at load; measured ~3.5 GiB lower peak VRAM, byte-identical output, same speed) and the QSA gather / split-KV decode workspace is a stable-VA VMM owner (`Gpu::qsa_gather_vmm_capable`). `0` restores the legacy `hipMalloc` arenas (the whole `max_seq` committed at load, zeroed per request) and the legacy exact-size gather slot, and an explicit `kv_backend=vmm` is then refused naming this switch. The gfx1100 VMM path only ever maps forward: it never unmaps or remaps a live VA (hardware defect: `hipMemUnmap` + `hipMemMap` leaves stale translations; `hipMemSetAccess` rejects 4 KiB sub-ranges); a larger gather reservation retires the old VA. Read once per process. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_EXACT` | **opt-in**; off unless `1` | `1` runs the batched QSA selector (`indexed_attention_select_{f32,bf16}_batched_exact`, `indexed_attention_select_exact.hip`) for launches with <= 2048 complete pooled blocks and <= 512 budget blocks outside a recorder or graph capture: 256-element tile sorts plus a fixed-order top-512 merge replace the all-pairs ranks, with the incumbent's score association, score-descending / index-ascending order, `-1` fill, tails and mirror. Selected bytes are byte-identical to the incumbent. Every other launch (longer contexts, recording, capture, the serial kernel) keeps the incumbent selector; unset or `0` keeps it everywhere. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_BF16_SPLIT` | exact gfx1201 and exact gfx1100: on unless `0`; every other architecture: not used | Exact gfx1201 and exact gfx1100 (the latter unless `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SPLIT_GFX1100=0`), BF16 pooled index keys (one row of four heads, dimension <= 128, budget <= 512 blocks). Replaces the fused `indexed_attention_select_bf16_batched` selector with the exact hipcc BF16 score kernels (`kernels/src/qsa_bf16_scores.hip`, rows8 up to 8 rows else rows16, scores bit-identical to the fused per-block score) followed by the exact sampled filter trio (`qsa_filter_select.hip`; select-from-scores never beat it, so `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT=0` keeps the fused selector instead). Selected indices, causal tails and the final-row mirror are byte-identical to the fused selector. From 2048 pooled blocks for calls of at most 256 rows (decode, verify) and 8192 above (prefill), measured on an R9700 as graph-replayed GPU time per call: at 2048 blocks fused 64.6 / 64.3 / 67.6 us vs 40.1 / 43.6 / 65.8 us for 1 / 8 / 16 rows, at 65536 blocks (256K tokens) 552 / 546 / 545 us vs 313 / 263 / 211 us; below the crossover the fused selector stays. Safe for the retained decode tape: recorded, the crossover reads the pooling capacity (`shape_blocks`) instead of the live count, the score stride, score grid (`ceil(shape_blocks / 256)`) and filter compact grid (`ceil(shape_blocks / 4096)`) come from it, the block count and `position_start` of every launch are declared to the recorder, and the score/filter scratch lives in the dedicated selector scratch slot, which `Qwen4Bundle::attach_forward` reserves (about 80 MiB) before any capture. hipGraph capture, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_EXACT=1`, gfx1151 and every other architecture keep the fused selector. `0` is the kill switch and always keeps the fused selector; only `0` disables, any other value keeps the default. Read once. Defined in `crates/rdna-compute/src/tensor_ops.rs`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SPLIT_GFX1100` | exact gfx1100: on unless `0`; every other architecture: not used | Kill switch for exact gfx1100 (7900 XTX) on the BF16 split QSA score/filter-select route (see `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_BF16_SPLIT`; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_BF16_SPLIT=0` and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT=0` still keep the fused selector everywhere). Unset/1 uses the same crossovers as gfx1201 (2048 pooled blocks for at most 256 rows, 8192 above); measured on gfx1100 for up to 16 rows (2048 blocks: fused 61.8 / 61.8 / 64.6 us vs filter 42.1 / 44.5 / 59.3 us for 1 / 8 / 16 rows). Route, scratch reserve and expert-residency census all follow it. Read once. `0` restores the previous gfx1100 behavior (fused selector) exactly. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_LANES` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201 Flash-Next fn-lanes steps with fp8 K/V (`serve.vmm_batch`): the QSA Core of up to 8 consecutive lanes (`Step::IndexedAttention` Core steps, 1..=8 rows each) runs as four launches (`indexed_attention_decode_prologue_fp8_lanes`, `indexed_attention_pool_rope_bf16_lanes`, `indexed_attention_select_bf16_lanes`, `indexed_attention_attention_fp8_lanes` in module `qsa_lanes`; lane = blockIdx.z, per-lane state pointers, positions and rows in a by-value kernarg table) instead of four per lane. Each lane runs the incumbent device templates, so every output and state byte is meant to equal the per-lane launches'. Admitted only for the fused LDS-resident select (live pooled blocks below the BF16 split crossover, no `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_EXACT`), the per-head attention policy, no F16 arena or pooled BF16 mirror, and never under a Redline recording, hipGraph capture or `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_EXTENT_CHECK`; every other set keeps the per-lane launches. Takes effect only where the lane stage policy keeps the QSA cores adjacent (QSA in/out projections shared across lanes). Default on for exact gfx1201 after the GPU byte oracle (`crates/rdna-compute/examples/qsa_lanes_oracle.rs`: 126/126 PASS) and the EP2 / PP2 A/B (CHANGELOG); `=0` restores the per-lane launches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_LANES_FUSE` | on; `0` off (developer); exact gfx1201 with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_LANES` on | Byte-identical, default on. A QSA lane group (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_LANES`, 2..=8 lanes) in which every lane has exactly one row (AR decode) runs as TWO launches instead of the four lane launches plus the output projection's `mq_rotate_x`: `indexed_attention_core_fused_fp8_lanes` (decode prologue + incremental pool + fused select, grid `[1 + heads + kv_heads + 1, 1, lanes]`; workgroup 0 of a lane does the index path: the index-query norm+RoPE (four heads per pass, one wave per head, the incumbent serial sum), the pool block the row completes (the newest raw key rebuilt from the index row's key part, so no workgroup waits on another) and the incumbent `qsa_select_batched` with the persistent mirror; the other workgroups are the query heads, the K/V heads and the index-key round trip) and `indexed_attention_attention_rot_fp8_lanes` (the incumbent per-head attention, then wave 0 of each head's workgroup applies `mq_rotate_x`'s signs / FWHT256 / scale to the head's 256 outputs into the out-projection's rotation scratch row; `execute_indexed_attention_core_lanes` records `scratch.prerotated`, so the OutProj phase's `rotate_x_mq_batched` is skipped, and the memo is dropped after that step). Both kernels are module `qsa_lanes_fuse` (`kernels/src/qsa_lanes_fuse.gfx1201.hip` appended to the `qsa_lanes` translation unit; the unfused module and kernels are untouched). The rotation is fused only when the next step is this layer's OutProj over an Aligned256-basis F32 input whose bank rows the lanes tile exactly; otherwise the second launch skips it. Falls back to the four-launch route for any lane with more than one row, under replay recording / hipGraph capture, and when the select LDS leaves under 16 KiB of the 64 KiB limit. Oracle: `rdna-compute` example `qsa_lanes_oracle --fuse [--heads N]` (per-lane singleton chain + `rotate_x_mq_batched` vs `launch_fused`, 1..=8 lanes, contexts of a few hundred tokens up to ~4k, 12 consecutive steps, every state / scratch / rotated byte). Read once. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT` | gfx1151: on unless `0`; other gfx11+: off unless `1` | Eager gfx11+ SIMT score/select calls with at most 512 budget blocks replace select-from-scores with deterministic sampled threshold, tiled candidate compaction and exact finish (`qsa_filter_select.hip`) when the call has more pooled blocks than the measured crossover of the select it replaces: 4096 against the hipcc select; against the gfx1151 PM select 24576 for calls of at most 16 rows (decode, verify) and 49152 otherwise. Underfilled or overflowing candidate rows repair exactly in-kernel; ordered selected indices, causal tails, padding and final-row mirror remain byte-identical. Uses 2048 samples, target 1024 and candidate capacity 4096. Recording/capture and uncovered calls retain the incumbent; score arithmetic and PM score admission are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_FEW_ROWS32` | **opt-in** (developer); off unless `1` | Candidate admission only, default off: with `1`, exact-wide verify calls of 17..=32 rows (`Gpu::few_row_limit` above 8, eager verify forward; never prefill, recording or capture) use the gfx1151 PM few-row filter crossover (24576 pooled blocks) instead of the 49152 many-row one, matching a one-row decode's threshold. Selection is exact either way (each row selects over its own block count, same filter trio and in-kernel repair, byte-identical indices, tails, padding and mirror); only the select-versus-filter performance choice changes, so keep it off until the PM-select versus filter crossover at 16/32 rows is measured. Unset or `0` keeps the existing thresholds (4096 hipcc, 24576 for at most 16 rows, 49152 above). Read once; no effect without `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT` admission. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORE_ROWS32` | **opt-in** (developer); off unless `1` | Candidate admission only, default off: with `1`, exact-wide verify calls of 17..=32 rows (`Gpu::few_row_limit` above 8, eager verify forward; never prefill, recording or capture), index width 4 x 128, score with one rows32 tile (`indexed_attention_select_scores_rows32_{f32,bf16}_pm_gfx1151` on the PM arms, `indexed_attention_select_scores_rows32_{f32,bf16}` on the hipcc arms) instead of two rows16 tiles. Same score bits and selection either way; the grid-y divisor always matches the symbol's tile. Extent checks (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_EXTENT_CHECK`) treat the PM tile as reading all pooled blocks of the call and the hipcc tile as reading only up to its last row's visible blocks. Stays off until the rows32 tile is measured to beat two rows16 tiles. Read once. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_DECODE_SPLITK` | exact gfx1151 (F32 split-K and q8 split-KV) and exact gfx1100 (q8 split-KV): on unless `0` (kill switch); ignored on every other architecture | KLD-gated, **not bit-exact**. Covers both F32 K/V (split-K) and q8 K/V (split-KV); `=0` disables both. For F32 K/V runs QSA decode attention as split-K: `qsa_decode_splitk_partial_f32` cuts each (row, KV group) selected list into 32 contiguous slices (one workgroup each, per-slice softmax partials in the QSA per-launch scratch) and `qsa_decode_splitk_combine_f32` merges them in slice order (module `qsa_decode_splitk`, `qsa_decode_splitk.hip`), so decode fills the GPU at a few heads and rows. Scoring is the 32-way KV-group layout: one workgroup per KV head scores its 12 query heads together, reading each K/V row once for all 12 heads. The slice merge and the grouped dot reassociate the softmax and dot sums, so output differs from the per-head kernel in the last bits and the route is accepted by KLD, not byte equality (decode KLD against the BF16 reference, split-K minus per-head route: code24 +0.0011, WT2 +0.0009). For q8 K/V (`kv_cache = "q8"`, the exact-gfx1151 and exact-gfx1100 default) the route is split-KV, part of the same policy: `qsa_decode_splitkv_partial_q8` (one workgroup per KV head, split and row, 32 splits, Q8_0 K/V dequantized in registers into a shared F32 LDS tile) and `qsa_decode_splitkv_merge_f32` (module `qsa_decode_splitkv_q8`, `qsa_decode_splitkv_q8.hip`, the q8 subset of the measured f1771197a kernels), launched on 24 query heads over 2 KV heads at head_dim 256; every row splits its own selected list and the split-KV LDS is fixed, so the selected length never gates it. Exact gfx1151 for F32 K/V (split-K) and exact gfx1151 or exact gfx1100 for q8 K/V (split-KV), head_dim 256, 12 query heads per KV head, 1..=`Gpu::few_row_limit` rows (8; up to 32 inside an eager exact-wide verify forward, where each row partitions its own selected length and the same kernels run with grid z = rows), eager launches only: no recorder, no graph capture; every other shape, format, arch and recorded or captured launch keeps the one-row per-head kernels (q8 K/V only at 24 query heads over 2 KV heads), and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_DECODE_SPLITK=0` keeps them everywhere. This is one policy (`qsa_decode_attention_policy`, tensor_ops.rs) for every admitted call: architecture, flag, K/V format, head_dim, head grouping, the LDS fit of the context-derived longest selected list (F32 only), and eager versus recorded/captured launch decide split-K versus per-head; the only row-count term is the `few_row_limit` admission, so an admitted call never takes the batch-size-only hg4, hg12 or WMMA attention routes (n rows reproduce n one-row decodes of the same mode). Calls past the limit (prefill, or 9..=32 rows outside an eager verify) keep the row-routed choice. AR decode in a recorded replay or graph (per-head) and an eager verify (split-K or q8 split-KV) are different arithmetic: `qsa_decode_attention_eager_differs_from_recorded` reports it. The F32 workspace is reserved once at 32 rows x 48 splits and the q8 workspace (records of 257 F32) at 32 rows x 24 heads x 32 splits, both in the one QSA per-launch scratch, and only the launch's actual `rows x heads x splits` records are mapped. Split count 32 and scoring layout 2 are the constants `QSA_DECODE_SPLITK_SPLITS` and `QSA_DECODE_SPLITK_SCORING_LAYOUT` in `crates/rdna-compute/src/tensor_ops.rs`, selected from an R9700 (gfx1201) sweep (lowest summed time over the 1131- and 8188-token cases; S16 is fastest at 1131 tokens, S32 at 8188), pending Strix Halo KLD and performance. The kernels accept up to 48 splits and the direct test entry also reaches the per-head layouts 0 and 1. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_NORM_EPI` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch); ignored on every other architecture | Byte-identical. In the QSA prefill's batched-launch branch the q (interleaved q-gate rows, M = 24 x 512) and k (M = 2 x 256) projections that take the MQ6G256V2 int8 PM route write their output already RMS-normed, weighted and RoPE'd: the GEMM runs the `gemm_mq6g256v2_mmq_i8_pm_set_nr_gfx1201_t256x128` entry with a per-call RoPE table (`indexed_attention_rope_table_f32`, one launch), and the standalone q / k `indexed_attention_norm_rope_f32_batched` launches are skipped. Admitted only for eager runs, F32 output, head_dim 256, rotary_dim 64, `M % 256 == 0`, no NPU arena source and no installed QSA projection hook; the index-q norm stays a separate launch. `=0` restores the unfused chain exactly. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_EXTENT_CHECK` | off (developer); exactly `1` or `2` enables | Parsed once into the GPU flags. `1` checks QSA kernel byte extents against VMM mapped prefixes or current allocation sizes and aborts with arena, logical shape, byte limit, required end, depth and launch site on overflow. `2` also calls `hipDeviceSynchronize` immediately after each checked launch and aborts with that site's diagnostics on error. Every other value is off; the disabled path only tests the flag. This is fault-localization instrumentation, not a serving setting; mode 2 serializes launches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_CODE_CHECK` | off (developer); exactly `1` enables | Parsed once into the GPU flags. Snapshots non-writable loaded-code ranges through verified CPU aliases after module load, then compares once after the first host-side HIP error 700; no GPU copies or synchronization. Unsupported aliases are unavailable, and aborts before a host-side 700 produce no capture. A mismatch shows persistent CPU-visible corruption at capture time, not its writer; equality does not establish fault-time bytes or exclude instruction-cache, PTE/translation, transient corruption or a bad PC. Every other value is off: no enumeration, HSA lookup or hook, only the flag test. The `qsa_code_check_selftest` example checks startup alias/baseline eligibility through the same CPU snapshot path without consuming the first-700 slot; build/run instructions are in its source. See docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_HG4_Q8_LITE` | exact gfx1100: on unless `0` (kill switch); ignored on every other architecture | Byte-identical. q8 K/V grouped QSA prefill attention (head_dim 256, four query heads per workgroup, selected-row counts up to 4,088; the shipped kernel stops at 3,071) runs `indexed_attention_attention_q8_batched_hg4_lite` instead of `indexed_attention_attention_q8_batched_hg4`: the same arithmetic in the same order, with no per-selected-row token array in shared memory (dynamic LDS `selected * 16 + 128` bytes instead of `selected * 20 + 4112`: 32,944 instead of 45,132 at 2,051 selected rows, so three workgroups fit a WGP instead of two; the per-thread max array and its serial 256-step reduction are replaced by wave-shuffle maxes), value loads through a wave-uniform buffer descriptor with an SGPR row offset and one unsigned validity compare, in two 16-token ping-pong batches, the score loop requesting the next key pair, the next tile's first pair and the token indices two tiles ahead before the current pair is decoded, and the per-head softmax denominators summed once by wave 0 instead of redundantly by all eight waves. Measured on a 7900 XTX, hip_loop ABBA at the N=768 S1 tile (synthetic inputs, 4-token blocks, 2,048-token budget): 0.467 of the shipped kernel time at mid position and 0.438 at late, output bit-identical (card round r2 `R2Qsa-05-hg4-pipe-kpf`; `.text` sha256 b6fec136). `=0` runs the shipped kernel and its LDS size. The `_lite` symbol is also compiled (never launched) in the gfx1151 and gfx1201 `tensor_ops` objects. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SCORES_RESERVE` | on; `0` off (developer): exact gfx1201 | Exact gfx1201, QSA select (`indexed_attention_select_rows8_core`, the select-kernel route): before taking its exact `group * block_count` score view of the shared F16 scratch (`Gpu::qwen4_f16_x_scratch`), the call reserves that scratch at its shape bound (`shape_blocks`, the pooled capacity, with the same group rule and 64 MiB budget). The block count grows by one every `indexer_compress_ratio` tokens, and each exact-size growth ran `hipDeviceSynchronize` + `hipFree` + `hipMalloc` and dropped captured state: 0.6 times per rank per served EP2 MTP decode step (call-site ledger, SyncCut; first found by BetB2). Kernels, views and values are unchanged; `0` restores exact-size growth. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4` | gfx1100, gfx1151 and gfx1201: on unless `0` / `false` (developer) | gfx1100, gfx1151 and gfx1201: runs Qwen4 prefill chunks of 512+ rows on the grouped symmetric IU4 MoE route (stable grouping, A4 gate/down activations, IU4 gate/up and down) for layers whose every routed-expert QT44/QT53 header is verified symmetric (`zp == -8*sc`) on the device at load. Only a symmetric requant artifact (GPTQ3) takes it: shipped asymmetric artifacts fail the check and stay on the F16 WMMA route automatically, and the load log prints `N/48 layers verified symmetric`. Trade on gfx1151 (Strix Halo, GPTQ3 artifact): pp8192 ~1,775 tok/s vs ~1,459 on the F16 route, BF16-reference KLD 0.1027 vs 0.0661. Trade on gfx1201 (R9700, `auto` placement, GPTQ3 artifact): pp8192 2,171.5 vs 1,449.1 tok/s (median of 3 each), BF16-teacher KLD WikiText-2 0.0877 vs 0.0572 (+0.0306) and code 0.1018 vs 0.0733 (+0.0285), the same deltas as the Halo's. Trade on gfx1100 (7900 XTX, Flash-Next `mq4-xts`): **TODO-evidence: pp8192 / pp32768 tok/s IU4 vs F16, BF16-teacher KLD WikiText-2 / code deltas and the S1 context-mean KL, from the gfx1100 default-on job record**. Needs the default two-candidate A4 producers (gfx1151: `HIPFIRE_GFX11_A4_CANDIDATES` unset or `2`; gfx1201: `HIPFIRE_G12_A4C2` unset or `1`). The GEMMs are certified builder (PeaceMaker) expert-run tiles: four 16-slot tiles per expert weight stream, eight on gfx1201 for layers whose experts are host-mapped (spilled past the VRAM budget); every width is byte-identical to the 16-slot tile. Not bit-exact against the F16 WMMA route. A typed boolean (`developer.qwen4_moe_sym_iu4`): `true` / `1` requests the route and `false` / `0` keeps the whole F16 route (scatter, producers, GEMMs) on symmetric artifacts too; any other spelling is rejected with `expected a boolean: true, false, 1 or 0` rather than ignored. Decode, MTP and smaller prefill always keep it. Every other arch ignores it. An explicit `0` prints one load-time warning (the F16/SIMT fallback is a developer reference route, ~2.9x slower prefill on a single R9700, ~13x slower on EP2) and is refused outright on a `topology.ep >= 2` load. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_SYM_IU4` | on by capability (developer, typed boolean); `0`/`false` is the kill switch | Symmetric IU4 grouped routed-expert prefill route (A4 gate/down activations, PeaceMaker IU4 gate/up and down, BF16 slot boundaries with the F32 ordered top-k combine `moe_slot_ops_pm_gfx1201`, no atomics) for qwen35 MoE artifacts whose routed experts are symmetric qt44 gate/up + qt53 down (A3B-class geometry: top-k ≤ 8, hidden % 256 == 0, intermediate % 128 == 0). Default on wherever the capability holds: every routed header loader-verified symmetric, gfx1201 with the PeaceMaker objects (`qwen4_moe_iu4_sym_pm_gfx1201` NT4 resident / NT8 host-mapped, `moe_slot_ops_pm_gfx1201`) and the two-candidate A4 producers (`-DIU4_A4_CANDIDATES=2`, `kernel.g12_a4c2`). Every grouped (Path 2) prefill chunk of any size takes it, under HIP graph capture too (MTP verify / batched-slot graphs). Such an artifact never runs its slow qt53 F32 fallback (~3.7× slower prefill) silently: an incapable arch (gfx1100, gfx1151 and every non-gfx1201 arch today), unverified headers or a failed scratch reservation refuse the load naming the remedy; `0` (or an explicit `HIPFIRE_IU4_PREFILL=0`, which stays authoritative) loads with one WARNING and runs the fallback for every prefill. Inside a bound load, a Redline recording or any other per-call refusal at prefill size logs a WARNING (first, then every 1,000). Decode and narrow host-mapped verify use the qt53 indexed GEMV. Independent of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4`. Not bit-exact to the F32 fallback (paired Qwen KLD +0.0205 nats, accepted). Read once at process start. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_COMBINE_V2` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_combine_v2`; `0`/`false` restores the incumbent grouped BF16-input combine. Uses the admitted `moe_slot_ops_v2_pm_gfx1201` route without changing ordered top-k accumulation. |
