The lane-batched kernels behind it have their own switches (`HIPFIRE_A3B_LANE_PROJ`, `_LANE_WO`, `_LANE_HEAD`, `_MOE_DEDUP`, `_MOE_DEDUP_DOWN`). |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_LANE_WO` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | A3B-class (qwen3.5/3.6 MoE, hidden 2048, MQ4G256V2, no AWQ / Paro) exact VMM continuous-batching route: the `wo` residual projection of every DeltaNet-MoE and FullAttention-MoE layer (m 2048, K 4096, 1..=8 decode lanes) runs `Gpu::gemv_mq4g256v2_residual_lanes` (`kernels/src/gemv_mq4g256v2_residual_lanes.gfx1201.hip`) instead of the PM x-batch residual (`pm_xbatch::residual`). Each weight row is streamed once and decoded once per group while the lane columns run the singleton `gemv_mq4g256v2_residual`'s exact per-lane FMA chains, accumulator order, combine, reduction tree and `y + acc` residual add, so every lane's bytes equal the singleton's (and the PM x-batch's); the lab gate is `crates/rdna-compute/tests/a3b_lane_wo.rs`. `0` restores the PM x-batch launch. Dense models' `wo` / `w_down` always stay on the PM x-batch residual. |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_LANE_PROJ` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | The A3B exact VMM continuous-batch route (`qwen3.5/3.6` MoE `.mq4rs`, hidden 2048, MQ4G256V2 projections) runs each projection group of up to 8 decode lanes as ONE lane-batched multi-weight launch (`gemv_mq4g256v2_lane_proj_k2048_gfx1201`, `rdna-compute/src/lane_proj.rs`): the DeltaNet QKVZA group (wqkv + wz + w_beta + w_alpha), the FullAttention QKV group (wq + wk + wv) and the MoE router + selector + shared gate/up quartet. Every weight row is streamed once for all lanes and each lane's output is byte-identical to the singleton `fused_qkvza_mq4g256v2` / `fused_qkv_mq4g256v2` launch on that lane alone (proved by `crates/rdna-compute/tests/lane_proj.rs` and the `cb_vmm_state_oracle` byte gate). `0` runs one `pm_xbatch` plain GEMV per weight again (the previous route). Groups the launch does not admit (non-gfx1201, AWQ / ParoQuant / non-MQ4G256V2 weights, K != 2048) always take the `pm_xbatch` loop; dense 27B layers are untouched. |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_NORM_FOLD` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | A3B-class (qwen3.5/3.6 MoE, hidden 2048, MQ4G256V2 projections) single-row decode on exact gfx1201 runs the single-workgroup `fused_rmsnorm_mq_rotate` that precedes each MQ4G256V2 projection GEMV inside that GEMV's launch (`fused_rmsnorm_proj4_mq4g256v2_fold_gfx1201`): the mixer QKV (`fused_qkv_mq4g256v2`) and QKVZA (`fused_qkvza_mq4g256v2`) windows, and the MoE block's router + selector + shared gate/up quartet (1281 rows). Every workgroup recomputes the row's RMSNorm + FWHT with the incumbent norm's exact code into LDS and then streams its share of the output rows with the consumer's exact per-row arithmetic, so the rotated vector and every projection output are byte-identical; the vector is still stored to `x_rot` for the later readers. 80 launches and their dependent boundaries per A3B token disappear. `0` restores the separate norm launch. Refused (separate launches kept) for any other model, arch, hidden width, AWQ or non-MQ4G256V2 weights, batched/verify rows, calibration capture and hidden dumps. |
| `HIPFIRE_BATCHED_HEAD` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | Multi-slot decode (`experimental_multi_slot`, the A3B serve route) with an MQ4G256V2 `lm_head` on exact gfx1201: the final per-slot logits run as one multi-column `gemv_mq4g256v2_multirow_r2_xbatch_pm` launch per run of up to 8 consecutive head slots (weights read once per run) instead of one single-vector `gemv_mq4g256v2_multirow_r2` per slot (8 x 478 us per C8 A3B step). Per slot the input is unchanged (the same `rmsnorm_f32` and lm_head input rotate on that slot's last row); the multi-column twin is column-identical to the singleton head, so every logits row, hence every token and sampled-lane selector input, is byte-identical (lab: `rdna-compute` example `head_batch_oracle`, A3B head shape 248,320 x 2,048, B = 1..8 at row offsets, repeat-deterministic, negative control). Needs at least two head rows in the step; one row, other dtypes, other arches and CPU-offloaded weights keep the per-slot head (`MQ4G256` heads keep their existing `gemv_hfq4g256_xbatch` route). `0` restores the per-slot head. |
| `HIPFIRE_BATCHED_HEAD_VERIFY` | off (developer lab check, read once per process) | `1` makes every batched multi-slot head (`HIPFIRE_BATCHED_HEAD`) also run the per-slot head into a side buffer and byte-compare every head row of the step (downloads, synchronises; eager steps only, so not with `HIPFIRE_SLOTS_DECODE_GRAPH=1`). Any differing f32 word logs `[batched-head-verify] MISMATCH` and fails the step. |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_LANE_HEAD` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | A3B exact VMM continuous-batch route (`exact::decode`), 2+ decode rows, MQ4G256V2 lm_head with K = 2048, no AWQ/Paro: the final norm runs as ONE `rmsnorm_f32_rowsplit` launch over the rows (the singleton norm kernel with `grid.y = rows`) plus ONE batched `mq_rotate_x`, and the lm_head as `gemv_mq4g256v2_head_lanes_n<N>` (`kernels/src/gemv_mq4g256v2_head_lanes.gfx1201.hip`), which reads each weight row once for up to 8 rows and writes each row's logits to its slot. Every row's bytes equal the singleton `gemv_mq4g256v2_multirow_r2` launch (lab gate `crates/rdna-compute/tests/head_lanes.rs`). `0` restores the per-row norm + rotate and the PM multi-column lm_head per run of consecutive slots. |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_MOE_DEDUP` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch; developer, read once per process) | A3B exact VMM continuous-batch route (`exact::decode` -> `Gpu::moe_decode_lanes_mq4rs`), 2+ decode lanes: the routed gate/up and the routed down + combine read each distinct expert's weights once per layer-step across the lanes (on a C8 batch the 64 (lane, slot) picks hit only ~46 distinct experts) instead of once per pick: `gemv_mq4g256v2_moe_gate_up_k8_dedup_rows`, `gemv_mq4g128v2_moe_down_dedup_rows` + `gemv_mq4g128v2_moe_down_combine_rows` (`kernels/src/moe_dedup_mq4rs.gfx1201.hip`). Per-lane bytes equal the singleton `gemv_mq4g256v2_moe_gate_up_k8_indexed` / `gemv_mq4g128v2_moe_ninepath_d4` launches (lab gate `crates/rdna-compute/tests/moe_dedup.rs`). `0` restores the lane twins (`gemv_..._rows`). One lane always uses the twins. |
| `HIPFIRE_A3B_MOE_DEDUP_DOWN` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (developer, read once per process; only meaningful while `HIPFIRE_A3B_MOE_DEDUP` is not `0`) | Same route: `0` keeps the lane twin for the routed down + combine while the gate/up stays deduplicated (the down kernels are compute/latency-bound, so the dedup there lands within a percent of the twin; this isolates it). |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_IU4_WIDE_TILES` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_iu4_wide_tiles`; `0`/`false` keeps incumbent tiles. Selects the existing NT8 gate/up pair only for admitted VRAM-resident expert runs with sufficient checked slot occupancy. |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_IU4_PRODUCER_FUSED` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.prefill_iu4_producer_fused`; `0`/`false` keeps standalone preparation. Fuses existing RMSNorm/FWHT or SwiGLU/FWHT preparation with the same eight-candidate IU4 quantization contract. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROUTER_FUSED_PREFILL` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_router_fused_prefill`; `0`/`false` keeps separate softmax/top-k/renormalization. Prefill-only exact fused top-8 router, subject to existing geometry and execution admission. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROUTER_TOPK_MULTIROW` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_router_topk_multirow`; `0`/`false` disables the wave-per-row fused router (one wave32 per row, 8 rows per workgroup, any row count). Byte-identical to the one-row fused router; requires fused-router admission. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_SHARED_PREPARED_IU4` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_shared_prepared_iu4`; `0`/`false` keeps per-consumer preparation. Reuses one admitted shared-expert IU4 activation for its selector and gate/up consumers. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROTATE_I4_PM` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_rotate_i4_pm`; the admitted byte-identical PeaceMaker down-projection rotate/quantize twin. `0`/`false` restores the HIP incumbent. |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_IU4_C8_PM` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.prefill_iu4_c8_pm`; byte-identical PeaceMaker C8 rotate/RMSNorm-rotate twins nested under the resolved producer-fusion route. `0`/`false` restores the HIP producers. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_CHUNK_PREP_PM` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.gdn_chunk_prep_pm`; byte-identical PeaceMaker chunk-prep twin nested under the admitted MoE GDN chunk scan. `0`/`false` restores the incumbent. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_QK_INDEXED` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.gdn_qk_indexed`; sequential Q8-state MoE prefill indexes raw key-head Q/K by value-head ratio instead of repeating Q/K. `0`/`false` restores repeat/copy. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_MOE_PRE_FUSED` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.gdn_moe_pre_fused`; admitted MoE prefill uses the existing fused GDN preamble instead of separate sigmoid/conv/norm/repeat launches. `0`/`false` restores the incumbent. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_MOE_CHUNK_SCAN` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.gdn_moe_chunk_scan`; admitted MoE prefill uses the existing GDN chunk scan with Q8 state and its EF residual. `0`/`false` restores the sequential route. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_SHARED_DOWN_SIGMOID_EPI` | on where qualified, exact gfx1201 | Typed boolean `developer.moe_shared_down_sigmoid_epi`; sigmoid-gate shared-down epilogue in the existing HIP `full_add_v3`, not a PeaceMaker kernel. `0`/`false` restores the separate gated residual add. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA` | gfx1151: on unless `0` (developer) | gfx1151 only. Flash-Next prefill tiles of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA_MIN_ROWS`..511 rows take the >= 512-row kernel family instead of the F32 SIMT routes. These are agent-turn appends, tool results and short prompt tails. The tile gets the canonical routing of `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PM_INVARIANT`, applied to that tile only: F16 WMMA dense and MQ6, WMMA QSA, BF16 HC streams and WY GDN. Its MoE takes the F16 WMMA grouped arm, with symmetric IU4 suppressed for the tile. This changes the logits of those tiles. Strix Halo, 32K depth: 145 / 273 / 401-row appends take 428 / 665 / 900 ms -> 282 / 359 / 434 ms. KLD against the BF16 teacher, with every scored row from appends of at most 145 / 273 / 401 rows: WT2 -0.0011 / -0.0000 / -0.0006, code24 +0.0032 / +0.0013 / +0.0008. `0` keeps those tiles on the F32 routes. Decode, MTP verify, tiles of 512+ rows and the `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PM_INVARIANT` profile are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA_MIN_ROWS` | `48` (developer) | Smallest prefill tile row count the append route (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA`) takes. Values below 2 are ignored. On Strix Halo the route loses at 33 rows (211 -> 217 ms) and wins at 55 (279 -> 238) and 81 (337 -> 252). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_CAPTURE_RING_COMPACT` | on; exact `0` disables (developer) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) GDN verify capture ring: allocate `rows + 1` recurrent-state slots per GDN layer instead of `2 * rows`. Only one slot per half is ever written (the capture and rollback kernels leave the last row's state in view slot `rows - 1`), so the other slots were dead bytes. Byte-identical output (layout only; logical slot numbering and digests unchanged); `0` is the kill switch back to the double ring. Frees `(rows - 1)` GDN state slots per layer per MTP lane (K=2, 3 lanes: about 175 MB at Q8; K=5, 8 lanes: about 1.2 GiB per rank), reflected in every lane/native-MTP census because they all size the ring through `Qwen4State::row_capture_allocation_sizes_scoped`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_RESERVE_TRIM` | on; exact `0` disables (developer; registered as `hipfire_config::qwen4_lane_reserve_trim`) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) GDN verify capture ring: every state's ring (an MTP lane's, the singleton's) is ONE shared pool allocation of `2 * (gdn_layers - 1) + rows + 1` recurrent-state slots whose per-layer windows overlap only in ring slots no kernel touches, instead of `rows + 1` slots per GDN layer. The capture and rollback kernels read only the live / source slot and write only view slot `rows - 1` (the two write targets of a layer, `rows - 1` and `rows` of its window, are distinct from every other layer's), so the kernels, views, pointer table, logical slot numbering and bytes written are unchanged (CPU model test `shared_capture_pool_matches_per_layer_rings`); only dead allocation is removed. Needs the compact ring (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_CAPTURE_RING_COMPACT` on). `0` restores the per-layer allocation and the previous lane reservation byte for byte. symtrunk (36 GDN + 12 QSA layers, Q8 GDN, K5 = 6-row ring): ring 222,299,712 B -> 80,373,312 B per lane (-141,926,400 B = 175 slots of 811,008 B), so one MTP lane's per-rank increment over an AR lane goes 264.85 -> 129.50 MiB (QSA fp8, 16384+ tokens, 2 MiB VMM granules) and an 8-lane EP2 store frees 1.057 GiB per rank. Every lane / native-MTP census sizes the ring through `Qwen4State::row_capture_allocation_sizes_scoped`, so the load reservation and the stage check follow. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_APPEND_SHARE` | on; exact `0` disables (developer; registered as `hipfire_config::qwen4_lane_append_share`) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) native MTP with a staged MTP lane store: the singleton drafter's prompt-fill Append scratch (`MtpAppendScratch`, 1024 rows of the head's row-batched operator buffers) is a non-owning view of the lane store's own scratch (`MtpAppendScratch::shared_view`) instead of a second allocation of the same shape, and an EP load that reserves MTP lanes charges the scratch once (the lane store's) in its native-MTP census. Never in use at the same time (one driver thread, one stream, and the lane store and the singleton already share the bundle's one forward scratch); no scratch tensor carries state between two passes (`append_rows` writes every `rows`-row view it reads; the prompt-id buffer is uploaded right before the sub-chunk passes that read it); the view's `free_gpu` frees nothing. A prompt chunk longer than the lane store's id capacity (1024 rows) takes the existing per-sub-chunk id upload, same bytes. `0` restores the per-drafter allocation and the previous reservation byte for byte. symtrunk EP2 serve (8 lanes, K5, max_seq 16384, 4096-row chunk): native-MTP census 773,874,712 B -> 514,069,528 B per rank (-259,805,184 B = 247.78 MiB, about 102 resident expert units of 2,534,400 B per rank). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PAGE_BUDGET` | on; exact `0` disables (developer; registered as `hipfire_config::qwen4_lane_page_budget`) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) VMM lanes, single device and EP: admission page budget. A lane store charges each lane its fixed state plus the first `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_COMMIT_TOKENS` context rows at load (2048 by default); everything a lane maps past that (trunk and MTP-head QSA context arenas, the shared gathered-attention workspace) is demand-mapped from whatever device memory is free, so admission by slots and length alone could pass a burst of long requests that then failed a `hipMemCreate` mid-step (`provision_step: map ...`) and failed every lane of the step. With the budget a request reserves its worst-case rows at admission (`prompt + max_tokens + 1 + the widest verify window's draft rows`, capped at `max_seq`; the decode map-ahead's own bound) and is admitted only when, on EVERY rank, the growth already promised to live lanes plus its own fits `free - (load-time lane charge not yet consumed) - margin`; free memory is re-measured at every admission, the byte figures are the lane census' own at the driver granule. A request that does not fit stays queued and is retried at the next step boundary (the daemon prints one `qwen4 lane page budget: queued ...` line when a request starts waiting and one `... admitted ... queued` line when it is released); one that cannot fit with no lane active is refused as a retryable overload (HTTP 503, `lane page budget:` prefix). On EP the root evaluates one verdict over every rank's numbers, so all ranks agree. A request that stays inside the load-time charge is always admitted. `0` restores admission by slots and length exactly (nothing is measured, reserved or queued). The same change classes the EP mesh window's preflight and head-arena mapping refusals as capacity refusals (`provision_step: map ...` marker): a retryable overload with no mesh poison. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PAGE_BUDGET_MARGIN_MIB` | `256` (developer; read through `hipfire_config::qwen4_lane_page_budget_margin_bytes`) | Free device memory in MiB per rank that the lane page budget (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PAGE_BUDGET`) never hands out to lane context growth: slack for unmodelled transient allocations and driver rounding. Lower values admit more long requests concurrently and leave less room for error; the worst-case EP2 measurement (8 concurrent ~16.3k-token prompts at max_seq 16384) peaked at 21 MiB of free memory with the 2048-row charge. Ignored under `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PAGE_BUDGET=0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_COMPACT_SCRATCH` | on for W3/MQ4 expert-slab artifacts and for symmetric-IU4 packed artifacts (with the IU4 route requested) on exact gfx1201 single-device and `topology.pp` band loads; `0` keeps the full plan | Exact gfx1201, storage-only: selects the compact Flash-Next forward-scratch plan (`Qwen4ForwardScratchMode::FnSymGfx1201`) instead of the full one. Unset (the default) takes it for an expert-slab artifact (manifest recipe `FlashNextSlab`, e.g. the shipped `mq3-xts`: every routed trunk layer is a slab arena) on exact gfx1201 with the grouped MoE GEMM on and BF16 PLE key/value weights (no `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_REQUANT` rule on them); `0` keeps the full plan. A `topology.pp` load chooses the plan once from the whole artifact and every band attaches it. A symmetric-IU4 (non-slab, `FlashNextPacked`, e.g. the canonical `mq4-xts` split base) artifact takes it by default too (it was opt-in before 0.4.3; unset and `1` select it, `0` opts out): besides the exact-gfx1201, grouped-MoE and BF16-PLE terms it needs the symmetric IU4 route requested (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4` not `0`). The default prefill chunk is unchanged (4096 on gfx1201). The load does not scan the routed headers on the CPU: the plan is chosen optimistically (artifact assumed symmetric) and the GPU symmetry check at attach (`qwen4 symmetric IU4 MoE: V/N layers verified symmetric`, header `zp == -8*sc`, finite) is the authority; when it verifies fewer than all routed layers the compact attach is refused after releasing its scratch and `attach_forward` re-plans the Full scratch at the largest chunk rung that fits (`qwen4 forward scratch: compact plan refused by the GPU symmetry check (V/N layers verified); re-planned Full at R rows`; see `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_COMPACT_FORCE_UNVERIFIED`). An expert-parallel load, any other arch or flag (grouped MoE off, IU4 off) keeps the full plan. The plan sizes the grouped-MoE and shared-down buffers for the BF16 grouped route (with a 512-row F32 fallback) and aliases phase-disjoint PLE, HC and GDN buffers. A slab layer fits it: rows within the few-row limit (decode, verify, at most 32 rows) run the K1 lane-union GEMVs into the F32 gate/up and down rows the 512-row fallback covers, longer rows run the K2 grouped IU4 GEMMs that write the BF16 grouped rows the plan sizes. On Flash-Next the forward bytes at the default 4096-row chunk are 4388 MiB full vs 2267 MiB compact (2121 MiB saved), and at 8192 rows 8603 MiB vs 4363 MiB (4240 MiB saved), so `auto` expert placement (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS`) is charged the compact plan and keeps more routed layers resident. No kernel, route, chunk partition or arithmetic changes: outputs are byte-identical. A compact scratch refuses a >512-row MoE invocation that would need the full buffers before its first launch, and a load whose MoE layers are not all on one route (all slab arenas or all symmetric IU4). The load log prints the plan and the reason when it is compact. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_COMPACT_FORCE_UNVERIFIED` | off (developer) | Test knob for the compact-scratch Full fallback. `1` makes the compact-plan acceptance check in `Qwen4GpuForward::new_inner` fail as if one routed layer were unverified (`Qwen4GpuForwardError::CompactUnverified`), so `attach_forward` re-plans Full. Per-layer symmetric verification and the IU4 route choice are unchanged: the fallback run computes exactly what a Full-mode run computes. No effect on a Full plan or an all-slab load. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE` | on for exact gfx1201, and (for the borrowed-donor stage, packed and slab-arena layouts) for any discrete device whose measured host-to-device link is narrow (< 16 GB/s); off for unified memory and for an unmeasured link; `1`/`0` force | Qwen4 long prefill with host-mapped experts (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS` below the layer count): each forward of at least `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` rows DMA-copies every host layer's unchanged QT44/QT53 expert bytes into VRAM ahead of its MoE, through static pointer tables and compute/copy events, instead of reading them over PCIe. Placement is unchanged: the two stages borrow the expert VRAM of the last two resident layers once their own MoE has run (no dedicated stage VRAM), and those layers are copied back from pinned host copies (2 x 1,275 MiB, taken at load; host RAM *on top of* the placement's pinned experts, so identical placement does not mean identical total pinned bytes) before the forward returns, so decode, short prompts and MTP verify run on today's residency and mapped reads. A slab-arena artifact (`mq2-xts`, `mq3-xts`) borrows the same way: per layer parity, the latest resident slab layer whose arena allocation holds the largest cold arena plus its 4 KiB tail pad (the last two resident layers when the arenas are equal-sized, as in an all-E8P `mq2-xts`) lends its own arena; each cold layer's pointer tables are rebased onto the donor arena, its codec tags stay its own, and after the last host layer of the parity the overwritten prefix of the donor arena is copied back from a pinned host copy taken at load (2 x 637.5 MiB for E8P arenas, host RAM on top of the placement's pinned experts), before the forward returns. A mixed-codec artifact's arenas differ per layer (XTX `mq2-xts`: 782-840 MiB; the largest even cold layer is 840.45 MiB, the largest even resident layer 806.84 MiB): a parity whose resident layers are all smaller than its largest cold arena, but within a sixteenth of it, lends its largest resident layer instead (its whole allocation is the window and the restore copy), and each cold layer that does not fit stages its leading experts whole (the cut is an expert boundary: an expert's gate/up and down entries are both staged or both keep their host-arena address) while the trailing experts keep their host-arena address in the pointer tables and read in place as an unstaged forward does; the load logs each borrowed host layer's staged and host-tail bytes once (`qwen4 slab stage layer N`) (the XTX `mq2-xts` load: 7 resident layers, donors 2 and 1, seven even cold layers reading at most 33.6 MiB of their arena in place, 1621 MiB of restore copies; the byte results are unchanged and no VRAM or resident layer is added). The restore overlaps the last host layer and the LM head; what is left is charged to that chunk. Byte-identical: no arithmetic changes. Off (one `qwen4 long-prefill expert staging: off` line) when the host RAM or GTT checks refuse the restore copies, fewer than two layers are resident (a slab artifact: no resident slab layer of a parity comes within a sixteenth of the cold arena), or the artifact is not the Flash-Next QT44/QT53 geometry; a stream capture keeps the mapped path. **Link-adaptive default:** with the variable unset, the load measures the device's host-to-device link once (cached per device for the process; one `qwen4 host link:` log line): a 64 MiB pinned `hipMemcpyAsync` host-to-device copy on a private stream, one untimed warm-up and the best of 5 event-timed copies, in GB/s (10^9 bytes/s). Below 16 GB/s the link is *narrow* and the borrowed stage defaults on at construction. On gfx1100 the same narrow predicate also selects the 8192-row default prefill chunk that sizes the expert placement (see `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS`), so that probe runs before placement; the stage charge the placement applies reads only the probe-free switch (explicit `1`/`0`, else gfx1201), not the link. Slab-arena layouts keep their dedicated, placement-charged stage buffers under the probe-free switch (explicit `1`, or gfx1201); where only the measured narrow link enables the stage (gfx1100) they borrow resident slab layers instead, charged nothing and displacing no resident layer. Per-expert tier layouts (the expert hotset) have only the dedicated stage, so they stage only under the probe-free switch and otherwise keep the zero-copy mapped reads. A probe failure is *unmeasured* and never narrow (fail closed: default stays off). Unified-memory devices (gfx1151 and other APUs; `hipDeviceAttributeIntegrated`, answered "unified" if the query fails) are never copy-probed and never narrow. gfx1201 (an R9700 in a Gen5 x16 slot included) never consults the measurement: its default stays on exactly as before. An explicit `1`/`0` is never overridden by the measurement. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` | `min(2048, effective prefill chunk rows)`; on a measured narrow discrete link off gfx1201 a fixed `768` (not lowered to the chunk); exact gfx1201 keeps `2048` | Smallest forward (prefill chunk) that stages under `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`; values below 2 keep the default, an explicit value is authoritative. The default is capped at the forward's prefill chunk (`HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` or the arch default, as admitted at load), so a full chunk always qualifies where the chunk is below 2048 (gfx1100 on a fast link: 1536 rows) instead of the stage never engaging. A layer copy takes 23.5 ms on gfx1201, which a 2048-row layer already hides (pp2048 1057 -> 1377 tok/s on card B, 18 ms of restore left exposed per chunk), so gfx1201 stays at 2048 whatever its link. Off gfx1201 on a narrow discrete link (see `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`) the mapped read of a host layer costs more the narrower the link, so forwards of 768 rows and up stage; shorter forwards, and any chunk admitted below 768 rows, keep the zero-copy mapped reads. The load log line reports the effective value and its source (`env` or `default`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_DEFAULT` | unset = measured selector; `1` forces; `0` never | Typed boolean developer key `developer.qwen4_d2_default` (`true`/`false`/`1`/`0`; any other spelling is rejected when the layer is built, never ignored), read once per Qwen4 load. Controls the per-load **D2 measured default** of an unset `memory.offload_exec` (see `HIPFIRE_OFFLOAD_EXEC` and [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md#partial-gpu-offload-memorygpu_layer_budget--memorymoe_expert_budget--memoryoffload_exec)): unset runs the measured decode-cost comparison (the production CPU arm timed on the model's real host experts plus the current copy-transport handoff, against PCIe from live zero-copy link samples; D2 only when the slowest arm sample still beats the fastest link sample by at least 15%, whatever the spread); `1` turns the D2 split on for every host-placed Flash-Next layer it can serve (hot-set tier layers, un-tiered host-mapped aggregate QT44/QT53 layers and host-mapped expert-slab layers whose present experts all have a CPU decoder) **without** the cost comparison, for lab and receipt runs only; `0` never arms the default. `1` does not bypass the other gates: an explicit `memory.offload_exec` (every value wins both ways), `HIPFIRE_MOE_CPU_EXPERTS=0`, no host-placed layer, unified memory, EP/AWQ/FP8, or a forced `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` of at most 4 rows still leave a layer on PCIe. It changes who multiplies the host-mapped experts of decode and `<=4`-row verify only: prefill (every window above 4 routing rows), expert placement and host allocations are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_HEAD_D2` | unset = follows trunk D2; `1` arms (same); `0` never | Typed boolean developer key `developer.qwen4_mtp_head_d2` (`true`/`false`/`1`/`0`; any other spelling is rejected, never ignored), read once per load at native MTP head attach. Unset and `1` run the MTP head's routed experts on a rows=1 D2 CPU split executor when the trunk's D2 split is armed on the same load (otherwise the head keeps the GPU route and the load line says why); the head executor resolves its return/join transport exactly as the trunk does (same `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN` / `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN` / arch default, so mapped + cpwait on gfx1100, with the same refusal-to-copy/host routing, logged per layer); `0` (the kill) keeps the GPU head unchanged. See [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md). Load line: `qwen4 MTP head D2: armed (rows=1, ...)` / `off (<reason>)`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_HEAD_RESIDENT` | unset = off; `1` keeps the native MTP head's routed experts in VRAM; `0` is the default | Typed boolean developer key `developer.qwen4_mtp_head_resident` (`true`/`false`/`1`/`0`; any other spelling is rejected, never ignored), default **off**, read once per single-card Qwen4 load. Every single-card placement host-maps the MTP layer's 512 routed experts (stacked `mtp.layers.0.mlp.experts.gate_up_proj` + `down_proj`, MQ4G256V2 / MQ4G128V2, 2.61 MB per expert, 1,275 MiB in all; the same bytes and the same ordinary resident tensor upload path as a resident trunk layer, no new format); with `1` they stay VRAM-resident instead, charged against the expert hot set's budget exactly like non-expert weights (the hot set shrinks by the same bytes: 512 trunk experts of the same size), so the draft steps read them at GPU speed instead of on the head's D2 CPU split (10 cold experts per draft step, `K` steps per window) or over PCIe. Applies only where a ranked hot set is applied (the sidecar placement: gfx1201 by default, any arch with an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=/abs/path`) and the native MTP head attaches; otherwise, and when the head's bytes leave the hot set no budget, the load refuses the knob with one `qwen4 MTP head resident: refused (...)` line and keeps the host-mapped head (a pinned `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOT_BUDGET_MIB` that no longer fits after the charge fails the hot set like any other pin). A resident head never runs the rows=1 D2 CPU split (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_HEAD_D2` is then moot; the load line says `qwen4 MTP head D2: off (HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_HEAD_RESIDENT ...)`), so its experts run the GPU kernel instead of the CPU arm. The contract against the head-on-D2 path is the existing D2 KLD contract (the CPU arm's accumulation order is not the GPU kernel's): compare target ids and tau (accepted drafts per window) against the head-D2 arm with the sibling counters, not by byte identity. Expert-parallel and pipeline-parallel loads ignore it. See [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN` | unset = `mapped` on exact gfx1100, `copy` on every other arch; an explicit `copy` / `mapped` always wins; `copy` is the kill value | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_return` (exact lower-case `mapped` or `copy`; any other spelling, including empty, refuses the Qwen4 load with an error naming both values), resolved once per load. Selects how the D2 CPU split returns its cold-expert results to the GPU. `copy` H2D-copies the cold slots into `down_expanded` behind the GPU routed tail. `mapped` has the CPU write the same raw F32 slot rows into one owned `hipHostMalloc(Mapped\|Coherent)` allocation per layer (`max_rows * top_k * hidden * 4` bytes) that the unchanged canonical combine then reads directly over PCIe: no cold-slot H2D is issued, and host arithmetic, masks, reducer ABI, original ids/weights and combine order are unchanged (the combine sees the same bytes). **Default on exact gfx1100 (7900 XTX): unset requests `mapped`** (with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN` unset requesting `cpwait`), byte-exact; 7900 XTX Flash-Next, 32K, 256-token greedy decode, ABBAAB n=3/arm: 33.3 -> 34.6 tok/s (+3.9%, 29.94 -> 28.84 ms/token, every `mapped`+`cpwait` cell faster than every `copy`+`host` cell, id sequence identical across all six cells). `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN=copy HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN=host` restores the previous transport exactly (kill switch). Every other arch keeps `copy` unless `mapped` is set. Honoured only on all-cold layers (the default whole-layer host layers); a mixed hot/cold layer, a failed allocation or a missing/null device alias keeps `copy` and logs the refusal at load (`qwen4 D2 return: …`) — the alias is never replaced by the host pointer; the load summary names the effective per-layer modes (`cold return: N mapped, M copy`) and `transport request: default (gfx1100)` when the arch default applied. The previous generation's consumer is proven retired (event query, then wait) before the buffer is written again, and teardown synchronizes before the allocation is freed. Eager only: capture and retained replay are refused exactly as for the D2 split. Counters `output_copy_calls`, `mapped_publications` and `mapped_retire_waits` and the per-layer return mode are reported by `qwen4_cpu_split_parity`. The gfx1100 default is confirmed at 32K only; 131K and 250K confirmation is pending. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_READY` | unset: `event` for a hot-set TIER split on exact gfx1100 (the gfx1100 tier package, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_GFX1100`), `sync` for every other split and arch; an explicit value always wins; `sync` is the kill value | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_ready` (exact lower-case `sync` or `event`; any other spelling, including empty, refuses the Qwen4 load with an error naming both values), resolved once per load. Selects how the D2 CPU split's `prepare` learns that the three input downloads (rotated activation, original ids, original weights) reached the host. `sync` (the default everywhere except a gfx1100 tier split, see the first column) is the original boundary: a stream synchronization after the downloads and the two older pinned-transfer retirements. `event` records one event on the stream after the downloads and spins on `hipEventQuery` (`std::hint::spin_loop`) for a bounded 2 ms (`cpwait::READY_SPIN_BUDGET`), then falls back to the blocking synchronization; the mask and output transfers are retired by the completed proof only when their last enqueue was on the same stream with a smaller process-wide sequence number, otherwise by their own synchronization. Scheduling only, byte-exact: host arithmetic, masks, slot layout and combine order are unchanged, and a failed query or record leaves the buffer pending and fails the transaction (fail closed). Counters `ready_polls` and `ready_fallback_syncs` are reported by `qwen4_cpu_split_parity` (`d2_readiness_counters`); the load summary prints `input ready: N event, M sync`. The configured mode applies identically under `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN=host` and `cpwait`: the CP-wait join does not replace it. Not promoted: abandon if H1 shows it adds API work or loses more than 2% of the CPU-arm latency. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN` | unset = `cpwait` on exact gfx1100 when the effective return is `mapped`, `host` otherwise and on every other arch; an explicit `host` / `cpwait` always wins; `host` is the kill value | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_join` (exact lower-case `host` or `cpwait`; any other spelling, including empty, refuses the Qwen4 load with an error naming both values), resolved once per load. Selects how the D2 CPU split joins the CPU result with the GPU tail. `host` enqueues the canonical combine after the CPU arithmetic finished. `cpwait` (CP-side wait, scheduling only, byte-exact) needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN=mapped` on an all-cold layer and a device whose `hipDeviceAttributeCanUseStreamWaitValue` is non-zero (`hipDeviceGetAttribute` 10013; symbol presence alone is refused), plus one 8-byte `hipMallocSignalMemory` join word per layer and one process-wide watchdog thread; any other layer, a failed allocation or a refused capability keeps `host` and logs `qwen4 D2 join: layer N: cpwait refused, host kept: …` at load. **Default on exact gfx1100 (7900 XTX): unset requests `cpwait`** together with the `mapped` return default (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN=copy` alone also keeps the join `host`); 7900 XTX Flash-Next, 32K, ABBAAB n=3/arm: 33.3 -> 34.6 tok/s (+3.9%), ids identical, 131K and 250K confirmation pending. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN=copy HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN=host` restores the previous transport exactly (kill switch). Per layer: `prepare` learns input readiness through the configured `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_READY` mechanism (`sync` or `event`, unchanged, default `sync`) exactly as under the host join; `cpwait` does not force a GPU-written readiness word. After the GPU tail is enqueued and BEFORE the CPU arithmetic the program enqueues `hipStreamWaitValue32(join_word >= gen)` and then the unchanged canonical combine reading the mapped view; the join step then runs the CPU arithmetic into the mapped buffer, publishes, issues a release fence and volatile-stores `gen` into the join word. Generations are 31-bit, monotonic, wrap is refused; the reserved `u32::MAX` abort value is `>=` every generation. Every error between the wait enqueue and the flag store stores the abort value BEFORE any device synchronize (a synchronize with a pending wait never returns), poisons the executor, and the request must be discarded (the combine consumed garbage); `free_gpu` and drop do the same. A watchdog (10 s per armed wait, `cpwait::CPWAIT_WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT`) stores the abort value and poisons on expiry. Eager only: capture, retained replay and the lab parity modes are refused under it (a gfx1100 lab parity run sets `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN=host`). Counters `cpwait_joins`, `cpwait_aborts` and `watchdog_fires` are reported by `qwen4_cpu_split_parity`; the load summary prints `join: N cpwait, M host`. The bridge ready-word API (`arm_ready_word` / `try_complete_ready_word`) is not used by the executor; only the `d2_mapped_return_pilot` `ready-word` arm exercises it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_MIXED_CPWAIT` | unset = ON on exact gfx1100 (the gfx1100 tier package, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_GFX1100`), off on every other arch; `0` is the kill (a mixed hot/cold tier layer keeps the copy return + host join); `1` opts in anywhere | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_mixed_cpwait` (bool; default ON on gfx1100, off elsewhere), read once per load. A mixed hot/cold D2 tier layer (some experts device-resident, some host-mapped) is refused the mapped return and the CP-wait join today because the mapped view REPLACES the combine's expanded input (`mixed hot/cold placement ... copy kept` / `cpwait refused, host kept` in the load log). With `1`, and only when the load requests `mapped` + `cpwait` (the exact-gfx1100 default, or explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_RETURN=mapped HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_JOIN=cpwait`), such a layer takes a mixed CP-wait join: the executor's mapped buffer stages ONLY the CPU-computed cold slot rows; `cpwait_arm` queues `hipStreamWaitValue32`, then one `hipMemcpyAsync` H2D per run of adjacent cold slots from the mapped buffer into the same offsets of the device `down_expanded` rows (queued behind the wait, so after the masked GPU tail's zero stores, and read-before-write ordered by the wait), then the canonical combine over the device rows; `join` computes the cold slots into the mapped buffer (no zero fill of the hot rows) and releases the wait with the generation store. Same CPU arm bytes and same combine bytes, so numerics, `host_exec_identity` and the greedy ids are unchanged (`qwen4_kld s11ref --decode` rows with the knob on must equal the off rows byte for byte); only the cold-row install and the combine's enqueue leave the host thread. The selection is atomic per layer: any refusal (no `CanUseStreamWaitValue`, a lab parity configuration, signal/watchdog setup) drops the mapped staging again and logs both reasons, leaving the copy + host executor. Every error between `cpwait_arm` and `join` stores the abort value first, exactly as the all-cold CP-wait form. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_READY=event` composes with it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_PCIE_SHARE` | unset = `off` (every cold expert of an armed hot-set tier layer is CPU-owned, today's D2); `off` / `0` are the same mode; `auto` sizes the share per layer at load; a decimal fraction in `[0, 1]` pins it | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_pcie_share` (exact `off`, `auto` or a plain decimal `0`..`1`; any other spelling, including empty, a sign, an exponent or an out-of-range number, refuses the Qwen4 load with an error naming the accepted values), resolved once per load. Only reads on a **hot-set tier layer whose D2 CPU split is armed** (the measured default or `memory.offload_exec=cpu`); every other load or layer ignores it and runs no probe. The share `p` is the fraction of a layer's host-mapped (cold) experts, **by sidecar routing mass**, that the GPU arm reads over PCIe (zero-copy, the exact PCIe-route kernel) instead of the CPU arm computing them. The cold experts are partitioned deterministically per layer (`cpu_splice::pcie_partition`): visited in cold rank order (routing count descending, expert id ascending, each weighted count+1), an expert goes to the GPU arm exactly when that keeps the running GPU-owned mass nearer `p` times the mass visited so far (error diffusion, exact `u128` arithmetic), so GPU-owned experts are spread evenly across the cold rank order (`0.5` alternates CPU, GPU, ...) and the GPU-owned mass stays within half the heaviest cold expert of `p` x the layer's cold mass. `p = 0` leaves the executor's ownership mask byte for byte today's; a layer whose share leaves the CPU arm no cold expert (`p = 1`) is not armed and stays on plain PCIe. `auto` prices each layer's two arms per call as an expectation of the per-call maximum (`d2_default::ShareModel`): `T = handoff + max(i*c, H + [j>=1]*max(l1, j*b*L))`, with `i` / `j` the CPU- / PCIe-owned cold experts routed (Poisson-binomial over the layer's sidecar counts, top_k = 10), `c` the selector's measured median CPU arm per expert, `L` its measured zero-copy link ns per byte, `l1` a measured single-expert zero-copy read (fresh host-mapped buffer per sample, net of launch), `H` the device-resident experts priced from one device copy sample, `b` the bytes per expert. The GPU tail is latency-bound, so one PCIe-owned expert costs a whole read regardless of share (a linear byte-balance, which an earlier `auto` used, mispredicts it: XTX K arm 59.3 -> 40-42 tok/s). It evaluates the no-share time and shares 5%..95% in 5% steps and takes the best only when it beats the no-share time by at least **10%** (`SHARE_MIN_GAIN_PERCENT`, stated in the load log); otherwise the layer stays all-CPU. On a 7 GB/s link (XTX) every layer resolves to off. It needs the same load-time probe the D2 default runs (cached per process when the selector already ran it) and keeps `off`, with the reason logged, when a sample is unusable. Numerics: PCIe-owned experts run the GPU kernel, CPU-owned experts the D2 CPU arithmetic, so a share is bounded by the all-CPU D2 contract (paired decode KLD D2-minus-PCIe, `docs/CONFIG.md` D2 lab gate); the partition enters `host_exec_identity` (`/pcie-share-v1=<digest>` on the tier part, absent when no layer owns a PCIe expert), so different partitions never share a cache. The load logs one `qwen4 D2 pcie share:` line (mode, measured CPU-arm and link GB/s, per-layer share min/mean/max, CPU-owned and PCIe-owned cold expert counts); `Qwen4Bundle::split_route_counters` reports the routed slots per owner (device-resident, PCIe cold, CPU cold). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_PCIE_CONTENTION` | unset = `1` (no contention) | Lab variable `developer.qwen4_d2_pcie_contention` (a plain decimal in `[1, 8]`, no sign or exponent; any other spelling refuses the Qwen4 load), resolved once per load, read only by `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_PCIE_SHARE=auto`. The factor by which the `auto` sizing slows both cold arms (`C` and `L`) while they overlap: the CPU-arm and link samples are taken one arm at a time, but on a host whose CPU GEMV and PCIe DMA read share one DRAM (a unified-memory APU host such as Strix Halo) running them together slows each. It multiplies the CPU and PCIe terms of a call in which both arms are busy, slowing both arms only while both are busy in the same call. The load log names it when above 1. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_WINDOW_ROWS` | unset = the load default: `8` on exact gfx1100 (aggregate / tier / whole-layer owners; not a slab recipe, not EP), `4` (`CPU_SPLIT_MAX_ROWS`) on every other arch and for slab recipes (mq3-xts / mq2-xts); an integer `4`..`8` wins; `4` is the kill on gfx1100 | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_d2_window_rows` (a plain integer `4` to `8`; any other spelling, including empty, `3` or `9`, refuses the Qwen4 load with an error naming the range), default 8 on exact gfx1100 without slab owners (resolved once per load by the Qwen4 loader before any executor is sized), 4 elsewhere; the env snapshot is read once per process. Raises the widest whole-window verify the D2 CPU-cold/GPU-hot split qualifies from 4 rows to `N` without the lab-wide `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_VERIFY_ROWS` override: the executors are built for `N` rows, decode and every whole non-prefill window of up to `N` rows run the cold experts on the CPU arm (never the PCIe read), the native-MTP `auto` K cap follows (`K = min(GPU row-exact rows, N) - 1`, so `N=8` is K7; an explicit `HIPFIRE_MTP_K` keeps its precedence up to the same cap) and the bundle's qualified verify rows (`cpu_qualified_verify_rows`) read it. Prefill tiles, tails of a long prefill, lanes and expert parallelism are unchanged, and a D1 CPU splice (`memory.offload_exec=cpu` whole-layer layers) keeps its 4. The CPU arm's multi-row grouped arithmetic is byte-identical to `N` single-row CPU calls per slot (oracles `gemv_shared_window_rows_match_one_row_at_every_position`, `d2_window_rows_are_byte_identical_to_one_row_ar_for_every_position`, `canonical_join_multirow_windows_5_to_8_match_one_row_ar_for_sampled_masks`, widths up to 8); the GPU masked tail at 5..8 rows is the few-row indexed route and is qualified only by the card sweep in [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md#d2-status-and-lab-gate). Host cost: the executors' mapped return is `N * 10 * 2560 * 4` bytes per armed layer (0.8 MB per layer at `N=8`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_CAPTURE` | on unless `0` | Allows Qwen4 long prefill with wide-hidden output capture (native-MTP prompt fill) to use `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`, subject to its existing placement and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` admission. This is output capture, not HIP graph capture: hidden rows are copied after the trunk program on the compute stream. `0` restores the old exclusion, keeping wide-hidden forwards on mapped expert reads. Decode, below-threshold verify/short chunks, the QSA parity observer and HIP stream capture keep their existing exclusions. No measured identity or performance result is claimed for this extension. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE` | unset = `on` (`DECODE_SHARE_DEFAULT`) | Developer-only, read once per Qwen4 load (`developer.qwen4_decode_share`). Values `on` (also unset and `auto`), `off` / `0` (and every unrecognised spelling), `bind`, `bank`, `fixed`. **Default ON on admitted loads**: exact gfx1201, single device, the ranked hot-set tier, the `FnSymGfx1201` scratch plan, a prefill chunk above 512 rows, and none of expert parallelism, a `topology.pp` band, the NPU trunk, an S1 slow-link profile, a DFlash speculator, a CPU expert split (`memory.offload_exec` `cpu` / `passback`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_DEFAULT=1`) or continuous batching (lanes are staged after the load: `continuous batching stages lanes (decode-share is single-stream)`). Every other load behaves exactly as without decode-share; a refusal is logged (`qwen4 decode-share: refused: <reason>`) only when the variable was set explicitly, so an unset default stays silent on loads it does not apply to. An explicit hot budget (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOT_BUDGET_MIB`) does not grow, so `on` is `off` for that load (`qwen4 decode-share: off (explicit hot budget: no residency to reclaim)`); `fixed` and `bind` still apply. **Placement.** The loader plans a donor tail for `bind`, `fixed` and `on`: the coldest resident experts of layers that also hold host-mapped experts, packed contiguously at the end of the device owner behind a zero guard, sized to the borrowed prefill scratch span, mirrored byte-for-byte in a window of the pinned host owner. Donors lie in physical (layer, expert) order and a prefill capacity class (1024, 2048, ... up to the prompt chunk) overwrites only the byte prefix of the tail its scratch spans, so the plan carries one immutable BorrowPrefill view of the expert tables per class beside the canonical Resident view: class `c` points the first `n_c` donors (those whose extent starts below the class span; a partly overlapped donor counts) at the mirror and leaves every other expert, later donors included, at its VRAM address (views are nested; the largest class is the all-donors view). Each class view owns device pointer tables (24,576 x 2 x 8 B, all of them charged in the placement fixed point). The long-prefill stage is built from the largest class view (donors count as host experts; a staged donor is a copy of its mirror, correct in every class). `on` alone charges only the dedicated forward (resident 512-row bank, persistent token ids, speculative buffers: `qwen4_decode_share_dedicated_bytes`) as the forward term of BOTH the census and the historical floor (the reserve log reads `forward N MiB (decode-share dedicated; M MiB prefill scratch borrowed from the donor tail)`), so the hot budget grows by the difference (about 4.4 GiB at a 16384-row chunk) and the planner keeps that many more experts resident; `bind` / `fixed` keep the plain budget and are the controls. If the donor tail cannot be supplied, or anything after the reserve was lowered refuses sharing (host RAM, GTT cap, executability, the free-VRAM re-query), the placement is recomputed from the plain reserve and equals the `off` plan exactly. **Forward.** `bind` reads the BorrowPrefill runtimes of the tile's own class (and builds the stage from the largest class view) for every prompt tile of at least 512 rows, with the ordinary dedicated scratch; decode, verify, lanes and smaller tiles keep the Resident runtimes. `bank` runs a 512-row resident scratch bank plus one prefill bank per capacity class over an owned test buffer (a correctness mode, not charged by the placement) with the Resident runtimes. `fixed` and `on` run the same forward: the prefill banks are views over the real donor tail (nothing allocated or written at attach), prompt tiles of 512+ rows read the BorrowPrefill runtimes of the epoch's dirty class (the largest class any tile of the current borrow epoch has run on, this tile included, so a small tile after a large one keeps the large view), and a loan controller keeps the tail whole for everything else: before a tile the span of its class is zero-filled (only the growth within one epoch) and the tail marked dirty; a prompt that ends `Ok` leaves the tail dirty and enqueues nothing (the first token already came from the prefill head; a following prompt, e.g. back-to-back prefills, continues the same borrow with no restore between them); the next decode/verify/lane/sub-512-row call outside a prompt enqueues the copy-back of the whole dirtied prefix from the pinned mirror on the compute stream and host-waits for it, so the second-token latency after a 16K prompt includes about 88 ms (R9700: 4.8 GB at ~57 GB/s) and the restore is not part of TTFT; any tile error takes a blocking drain-and-copy recovery, and a recovery that fails (or a restore needed under a HIP stream capture) poisons the loan so every later forward fails naming the reason. On a `fixed` / `on` load the prefill chunk the placement was sized for is final (no rung downshift), a compact-plan symmetry refusal at attach fails the load naming `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE=off` (a Full plan cannot fit the donor tail), and the measured D2 default stays off (`qwen4 D2 default: off (decode-share load)`; `bind` keeps failing the load instead, being a numerics control). The forward needs the load to carry a donor tail whose class spans equal its banks' (a second forward on the same lease refuses); without one it keeps the ordinary single bank (logged for an explicit knob only). **Logs.** `qwen4 decode-share: mode <mode>, K donors (B MiB) from L layers, device tail [off, end), mirror host +B MiB, guard G B, borrow digest <16 hex of the largest class>, resident digest <16 hex>` and `qwen4 decode-share: classes [1024:184, 2048:255, ...] donors dirty` (rows of each class: donors it reads from the mirror); for a grown `on` plan `qwen4 decode-share: on, hot budget +X MiB (+N experts: A -> B), K donors (D MiB) from L layers, resident bank R rows (M MiB dedicated), prefill classes [...], restore <= D MiB`; the forward logs `qwen4 decode-share: BorrowPrefill runtimes for N tier layers (donors in M layers), C class views` and, at free, one `donor loan` summary (epochs, prompt tiles, restores, restored MiB, recoveries, zeroed MiB, host wait ms). A donor tail that cannot be supplied (`Insufficient`) logs `qwen4 decode-share: ineligible: ...` and keeps the plain plan. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_VERIFY` | off | Developer-only, read once per Qwen4 load (`developer.qwen4_decode_share_verify`). Exactly `1` makes a decode-share load synchronize the device right after materialization, copy the whole donor tail back in bounded chunks, compare it byte-for-byte with the pinned mirror and log `qwen4 decode-share: donor tail verified sha256 <hex>`; a mismatch fails the load naming the first differing tail byte. Under `fixed` / `on` the same check also runs over the restored prefix after every donor restore (a mismatch poisons the loan). A debug oracle with no effect unless `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE` planned donors. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_SYNC` | off | Developer-only, read once per Qwen4 load (`developer.qwen4_decode_share_sync`). Exactly `1` makes a `fixed` / `on` load enqueue and host-wait for the donor restore at the end of a prompt, instead of at the next decode/verify/lane/sub-512-row call outside a prompt (an A/B measurement switch: it puts the restore inside TTFT and prevents back-to-back prefills from sharing one borrow). No effect without `fixed` / `on`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_LOG` | off | Developer-only, read once per Qwen4 load (`developer.qwen4_decode_share_log`). Exactly `1` logs one line per donor borrow epoch of a `fixed` / `on` load when its restore completes: `decode-share epoch E: tiles T, class span X MiB, zeroed Z MiB, restore R MiB, wait W ms` (`wait` is the time actually blocked in the host wait). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_SHADOWS` | `1` (on) | Developer-only, read once per Qwen4 load (`developer.qwen4_decode_share_shadows`). Exactly `0` keeps the BF16 -> F16 weight shadows and the shared-expert INT8 shadows resident (as without decode-share). Otherwise an admitted `on` / `fixed` load on exact gfx1201 keeps them in the start of the donor tail instead: they are read only by prefill tiles of at least 512 rows, which on a decode-share load are always borrowed prompt tiles, so they are rebuilt in place (F16 `convert_bf16_to_f16`, INT8 rotate + A8 quantize) at the first acquire of every borrow epoch, after the scratch zero fill, and vanish with the next restore. The placement reserve then charges neither the `F16 weight shadows` nor the `INT8 shared-expert shadows` census term (about 1,477 MiB on Flash-Next, which become resident experts under `on`), every class span grows by the region, and the load line reads `shadows S MiB in donor tail (F16 F MiB, INT8 I MiB)`. Neither shadow cache can ever allocate elsewhere on such a load: a lookup of a weight outside the planned region, or outside a borrow epoch, is an error. Under `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_VERIFY=1` the first rebuild of a load is checked byte-for-byte against a reference built the ordinary way into a temporary allocation (a mismatch poisons the loan naming the first differing member and offset); `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_SHARE_LOG=1` adds the rebuild time to each epoch line. No effect without `fixed` / `on`. |

Both produce the same bytes; this is a same-binary control. gfx1201 has only the builder bundle and ignores it. |

Off (the default), a mesh lane routes its draft depth from its own agreement, from the gfx1151 window-cost table, with the measured depth cost removing AR windows where a drafting window pays (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_MEASURED_DEPTH`; depth 0 is a one-token AR window, never sticky). Off since CpFix (cp8/cp9 code C8 VOID): the lane floor priced each drafting window an even share of its step's row-group spill and AR none, so one lane's retirement looked like a saving while the other lanes kept drafting and the step did not shrink, and a lane whose first window rejected early held the shallow cap it was moved to until it retired. On EP2 R9700 code C8 one or two of eight lanes retired in every load and then emitted 1 token per ~72 ms step while the rest emitted ~6, so the last stream ran alone for ~1.8 s (all-8-decoding 0.39-0.48 of the span, 269-292 tok/s); off: 434-443 tok/s and a pass (j8164); floor off / on, 2 loads each (j8168): story C4 1.22, story C8 1.05, code C8 1.34. Singleton (one-request) MTP keeps its floor. | `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/src/mtp_lanes.rs` (`ep_mesh_lane_floor_enabled`) |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_MEASURED_DEPTH` | on; `0` off (developer): with the gfx1201 lane cost profile (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COST`), EP mesh lanes with the floor off (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_LANE_AR_FLOOR` unset) | Default on: the root asks the measured step cost only whether a drafting window beats an AR window for an EP lane; every nonzero depth stays on the gfx1151 `MTP_WINDOW_COST` table. The price is a store-pooled, decayed (0.99) ridge fit (`mtp_lanes::LaneDepthCost`) of the priced step wall `W = F + a*C + r*e + q*(e/C)` (`C` decoding lanes, `e` verified drafts, `e/C` the mean draft depth = the step's sequential draft rounds) from the prior `[10.92, 1.99, 0.20, 1.35]` ms (fitted to the j8730 storyaccept l3kill2 priced steps, story C1 / C4 / C8, weighted rms 0.80 ms); a window of `d` drafts costs `1 + (r*d + q*d/C) / ((F + a*C)/C)` AR rows and `batched_depth` over those prices decides AR versus drafting, so a draft row costs ~1.55 ms at C1 but ~0.37 ms at C8. The static table priced a one-draft window at 1.7 AR rows, so story windows went AR 8-10 % of the time where a drafting window pays; when the table picks AR and the measured price says drafting pays, the window drafts at the table's best drafting depth, and a nonzero table depth is never changed (j8730 clean pair: forced depth 2 beats the default story tok/ms x1.024 / x1.043 / x1.062 at C1 / C4 / C8; forced depth 3 against depth 2 x0.994 / x1.017 / x0.993). `0` restores the static table for AR too. The legacy profile (other arches, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COST=0`), the floor-on path, the lab route modes and the singleton MTP chooser are unchanged. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COST_TRACE` shows the fit (`depth_fit_us`) and the prices (`depth_costs`). | `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/src/mtp_lanes.rs` (`lane_measured_depth_enabled`, `LaneDepthCost`) |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COST_TRACE` | off; `1` enables (developer) | One `QWEN4_LANE_COST_TRACE` JSON line on stderr per lane route decision (`"event":"route"`: lane request tag / owner generation / window and step sequence, lanes in the step, profile (`legacy` / `gfx1201`) and fit scope (`request` / `pooled`), the fit's step count and `fit_us {F,a,r,G}`, the store's measured depth fit `depth_fit_us {F,a,r,q}` and the per-depth window prices the AR-vs-drafting decision used (`depth_costs`, empty when the static table did, see `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_MEASURED_DEPTH`), the window's draft budget, per depth the predicted native window price in AR rows at this step's lane count with no spill, its expected tokens at the lane's measured agreement and their ratio against the AR unit of 1 per token, the floor's own `QWEN4_FLOOR_TRACE` state (`floor`) and the chosen route `ar_synced` / `ar_retired` / `batched:K`) and one per step the fits learn from (`"event":"step"`: lanes, spill row groups, verified drafts, executed rows, row groups, controller wall, priced). Stderr only, no behaviour change. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_LAB_ROUTES` | `floor` (developer); `batched`, `cycle` | The EP/batch lane store's window router at load (`LabRoutes`): `floor` is the production per-lane depth chooser; `batched` drafts every lane window at its whole budget (depth `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_K`, no AR windows); `cycle` walks the lab route cycle (`Batched{K}`, `Batched{1}`, `ArSynced`, `Batched{2}` three times, then `ArRetired`). An unknown value keeps `floor` and prints one stderr line. Every route is an exact window. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ROW_CAP` | off; `1` on (developer): with the gfx1201 lane cost profile (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COST`) | `1`: the gfx1201 root plans each EP lane step inside one 8-row once-over-all-rows group: the group's 8 rows are split evenly over the step's lanes in request order (odd rows to the first lanes) and a lane drafts at most its share minus its seed row (`mesh_lane_protocol::plan_window`), from when a second group cost `G` = 31 ms (cp5: 4 lanes with 3 native windows 77 ms, 2 native 41 ms, AR 24.5 ms). A lane squeezed to a zero budget routes AR-synced without observing, so the cap never retires it. Default off since the all-rows lane step (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ALLROWS` / `_MOE`) runs a >8-row step as one pass: every lane drafts up to its own budget and the floors price the spill (served decode-ab j7655: code C8 ×1.49, code C4 ×1.33 per-stream decode). Lab route modes ignore it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_LONE_MTP_LANE` | on; `0` off (developer): Flash-Next EP serve with a staged lane store and a loaded speculator (native MTP) | A lone request arriving at an idle EP server enters the lane driver as a cohort of one (`batch.rs` `qwen4_lone_lane`), so later arrivals join at the next step. Off, it takes the MTP singleton, which cannot be promoted: a burst then serves its first arrival alone for its whole generation and prefills the rest only after it (served EOS-on checkpoint on 5fab83b69a: every story C4 / C8 cell serialized, first-arrival TTFT 0.11 s, the rest 3.6-4.8 s). PP with a speculator and single-card loads are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_COPY` | on; `0` off (developer): Flash-Next EP lanes where the singleton's n-gram copy policy is armed (`speculation.mtp_ngram_copy`, gfx1151/gfx1201) | The n-gram copy lane in EP lanes: a lane may verify up to 7 candidates copied from its own history (the first candidate is confirmed by the head's row-0 argmax; a miss falls back to the native window, whose draft 0 is that proposal, and a sampled lane carries the head's candidate read for it). Only the first `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COPY_LANES` lane slots hold the widened 8-row capture ring and may copy (later slots keep the `k + 1`-row ring: resident experts are not given up at 8 lanes) and a step with more lanes offers no copy window. Measured (symtrunk, decode-ab, tg256, per-stream decode, copy on / off, CI over loads): copy-heavy edit C1 1.30x (tokens/step 5.7 to 7.6), C4 1.00x, C8 0.99x (CI spans 1); story/code C1..C8 within noise of 1 (0.99-1.00). | `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/src/ep.rs` (`ep_default_copy_config`) |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_COPY_LANES` | `2` (developer): with the copy lane | Lane slots that hold the copy lane's widened capture ring and may take copy windows; a step with more lanes than this offers none. `0` turns the copy lane off (as `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_COPY=0`); larger values buy more copy lanes with 66 MiB of ring per lane per rank. The store's step buffers hold the larger of the native `k + 1` rows per lane and the widest copy step (`min(lanes, this)` lanes of 8 rows), so a small store (`serve.continuous_batch_size` 2, or up to 5 at `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_K=2`) still takes its copy windows; at 8 lanes the buffers are the native ones. | `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/src/ep.rs` (`copy_wide_lanes`) |

Measured on the lab stack (stepshep/scale2): 32-row step 2.43x -> 1.90x AR8 (j7554 -> j7617). Kill switch for today's step: `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ALLROWS_MOE=0`. | developer |

A scope already in force no longer refuses the all-rows stages. The scoped MoE call takes the grouped route only with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_WIDE_GROUPED_MOE=1` (and `_DOWN`). Default off keeps the unscoped variant. Inside this scope the lane WMMA and the MoE int8 gate/up / down arms also refuse (`Gpu::lane_row_exact_scope`), for every lane class; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ROW_EXACT` turns the same scope on per step for the row-exact lane class. | developer |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ROW_EXACT` | compatibility override of the user config key `serve.batch_exact` (CLI `--batch-exact`; default `off`): `0` / `off` / `false` / unset (any unrecognised value warns once and means off); `greedy`; `seeded` (also `1`, `on`, `true`) (opt-in): exact gfx1201, needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ALLROWS` | **By default concurrent native-MTP output may depend on batch composition**: Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP / single-card lane AR and verify steps of 9..=64 rows (>= 3 MTP lanes) take the all-rows segment, whose routes (trunk A8, lane WMMA, MoE int8 gate/up / down) are not bitwise the <= 8-row route, so a greedy or seeded lane's text can change with the other requests sharing its steps. C1 and AR (steps of <= 8 rows) stay exact. Opt-in: a step carrying any lane of the selected class runs every all-rows stage inside the wide few-row verify scope of `n` rows (the row-exact singleton-derived tiles, as `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ALLROWS_EXACT`) and refuses lane WMMA, the trunk A8 projections and the MoE int8 gate/up / down there (`Gpu::lane_row_exact_scope`), so each row is bitwise the <= 8-row route's and the output is batch-invariant. Any opt-in also keeps prompts of 9..=64 tokens on their serial singleton prefill tile (no small-lane EP prefill wave, `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_SMALL_LANES`); `seeded` also draws sampled lanes on the host row-read sampler (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_DEVICE_SAMPLE` off). `greedy`: greedy lanes; `seeded`: greedy lanes and sampled lanes with an explicit request seed. Cost of an exact step (cp12 method, seeded T=1 load, `seeded` vs `0`, CHANGELOG): code C4 / C8 +41% / +82% step time, story C4 / C8 +33% / +60%; greedy requests pay it under either opt-in. The environment value takes precedence over the config file, as for every compatibility variable; `--batch-exact` takes precedence over both. |

The batch embeds from the lane forward's own device token ids (`scratch.token_ids` rows of the step's ring upload, used only while the host mirror of that upload equals the kept tokens and the buffer is the same), so it uploads nothing; otherwise `append_rows` uploads its own copy. One kept draft keeps the per-row Append. Draft request state, positions and the pending hidden are byte-equal to the per-row loop, but on exact gfx1201 with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_WMMA` on (default) the head rows are NOT byte-equal to the per-row Append: `append_rows`' two FC projections go through `gemm_bf16_xf32_multirow`, which takes the lane WMMA arm at 16..=64 rows (fc_hidden at `4 * rows`, so a lane keeping >= 4 rows; fc_embedding at >= 16 rows); DraftRebuild S3 oracle: 61 of 64 lane-cases with >= 4 kept rows differ, 0 of 210 with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_WMMA=0`. Native-MTP mesh lanes keep their sampled drafts (drawn by the root from the head's q) and are verified with Leviathan acceptance against each draft's q (`min(1, p/q)`, residual `(p-q)+` on the first rejection, bonus from p; a copy proposal is the point mass); AR lanes (`--spec off`), lane-executor verify windows (argmax drafts as point masses) and the prompt-completing first draw go through the same call.
