New entry symbols: 10 `lwmma_tile_bf16_*` entries (PeaceMaker Route-A twin proof-only, dispatch stays HIP). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LWMMA_MULTI_PART` | **on** (default on, exact gfx1201, with the lane-WMMA route `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_WMMA`); `0` off (developer): one `lane_wmma_gemm` launch per part as before | Flash-Next (Qwen4) dense BF16 projections of ONE activation at 16..=64 rows that take the lane-WMMA arm: the router, the shared selector, the shared gate and the shared up of the wide MoE block run as ONE `lwmma_gemm_bf16_t2_m<1..4>_n1_multi` launch (up to four parts, each its own auto split-K and fold, part-private partial slices in a second fixed scratch) instead of one `lane_wmma_gemm` launch per part: `Gpu::gemv_bf16_xf32_x4_rows` (lane-WMMA arm) and `project_weights` (BF16 members of one K declining the F16 WMMA route), and on the unscoped wide route the router joins the shared gate/up stage's group (`router_with_shared_multi`), so the 4 front launches become 1. Every output word is bitwise the per-part launch's (`qwen4_lwmma_multi_part` oracle). On by default; `=0` is the kill switch. The PM Route-A twins of the four `_multi` entries are proof-only (dispatch stays HIP). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC8` | **on** (default on, exact gfx1201, when the `<model>.hc8` sidecar is present); `0` off (developer): the sidecar is ignored (not even read), the BF16 base HC mixer weights load and run exactly as before (bit-for-bit, no extra log line); ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next (Qwen4) HC mixer weights (`input_mix_weight_down` `[320, 10240]` / `input_mix_weight_up` `[10240, 320]`: 48 layers x {attn, mlp}, the final mixer and the native-MTP head's) replaced at load by the offline 8-bit sidecar built with `hipfire-quantize hc8 build` (OCP E4M3 per row with a power-of-two f32 scale; qt 57 `HC8E4M3Row`, `DType::HC8E4M3Row`; the weight `q * s` is exactly a BF16). The sidecar binds to the base like the trunk overlay (arch id, file length, index sha256, per-tensor shape / size / payload sha256); one that fails to bind or match logs `qwen4 hc8: REFUSED ...` and the BF16 base weights run. An applied load logs `qwen4 hc8: applied N tensors (before X B, after Y B)`. Decode / verify HC reads (`1..=64` routed rows) read the E4M3 bytes on the PeaceMaker `hc8_wmma_pm_gfx1201` kernel (`Gpu::hc8_gemm_tensor`: BF16 WMMA on the exactly decoded weights, f32 accumulate, scale in the epilogue, `hc_act` epilogue on the down; every row is bitwise the one-row result). Prefill-class rows (> 64) keep the existing BF16 kernels fed by an exact per-GPU BF16 dequant scratch (`Gpu::hc8_bf16_scratch`, PM entries `hc8_dequant_bf16` / `hc8_dequant_f16`), with the F16 weight shadow built from the E4M3 buffer itself (keyed on it, never on the scratch). The DET MTP append (exact gfx1151) refuses HC8 weights by name. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_R4` | gfx1151 and gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 and gfx1201 Flash-Next MQ4G128V2 routed down at K = 640 (single-token decode, MTP verify and fn-lanes rows), eager (no recorder or graph capture): `gemv_mq4g128v2_moe_down_top10_indexed_batched_expanded_k640_r4` computes four weight rows per wave from one load of the pick's activation slice, each row with the one-row kernel's expression, group order and reduction, so every expanded row is byte-identical (gfx1201: `crates/rdna-compute/tests/moe_down_r4_hw.rs`, raw f32 bits vs the one-row kernel). `0` keeps the one-row kernel. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_DOWN_FUSE` | gfx1151: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 Flash-Next single-token decode, BF16-recipe Q8_0 shared-expert down at K = 640, eager: `gemv_q8_0_wide_k640_staged_bf16_scaled_add` runs the staged GEMV and the source BF16 shared fold (`bf16_scaled_add_f32`) in one launch; output and residual are byte-identical to the two launches. `0` keeps the two launches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_PIPE` | gfx1201 and gfx942: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201 (F32 and Q8 GDN state) and exact gfx942 (F32 GDN state only), on the batched GDN recurrence without a state capture (prefill; not verify or rollback): runs `gated_delta_step_pipe128_{f32,q8}` (`tensor_ops.hip`, 128-thread blocks, one value column per thread with its 128 state values in registers, no cross-thread handoff per row) instead of `gated_delta_step_halves_state128_persistent256_{f32,q8}`. The output and the final F32 / Q8 state (codes and scales) are bytewise the persistent256 kernel's; on gfx942 the kernel uses persistent256's pinned `__fmul_rn` / `__fmaf_rn` / `__fsub_rn` F32 expressions. The Q8 kernel exists on gfx1201 only (gfx942 refuses a Q8 GDN state). `0` keeps the persistent256 kernel; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_Q8_INLINE` | **opt-in**; off unless `1` | Exact gfx1151 only, on the chunked GDN prefill route with a Q8 GDN state: `1` runs the recurrence as `gated_delta_chunk_gate_q8_wmma` (module `gated_delta_chunk_q8_wmma`), which decodes the Q8 slot to F32 on load and requantizes it (seeded by the last row, `position + rows - 1`) on store, instead of the `gdn_state_q8_to_f32` / `gdn_state_f32_to_q8` launches and F32 scratch around `gated_delta_chunk_gate_wmma`. The gated output and the Q8 slot are bytewise the conversion arm's. An F32 state, a slot not 16-byte aligned, and every other arch keep the incumbent arm. With `state_quant = "q8_ef"` / `HIPFIRE_STATE_QUANT=q8_ef` the load is refused on every arch (`q8_ef` has no inline kernel yet: none has run on a GPU); unset one of them. Unset or `0` keeps every launch of the incumbent route; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_FN_LANE_PM_ROWS` | gfx1151: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 only, inside the Flash-Next (Qwen4) lane step, a Qwen4 speculative verify forward (native MTP and DFlash2, 2..=8 rows) or the DFlash2 draft head (`Gpu::lane_rows_scope`, set by their drivers), outside a recorder or graph capture: routes the four incumbent rows launchers at `rows` 2..=8 to the embedded certified builder module `kernels/qwen4_lane_rows_pm_gfx1151.hxaco` (module `qwen4_lane_rows_pm`): `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows` (K % 256 == 0) to `gemv_mq6g256v2_xbatch_pm`, `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows_x4` to `gemv_mq6g256v2_x4_xbatch_pm`, `gemv_q8_0_k8_rows` (K % 2048 == 0) to `gemv_q8_0_k8_xbatch_pm`, and `gemv_q8_0_staged_rows` at K = 320 to `gemv_q8_0_k320_xbatch_pm`. Each column keeps the arithmetic DAG of the 1-row incumbent (byte-identical to it and to the incumbent rows kernel) while sharing the activation loads across weight rows; operands whose byte offsets exceed 32 bits keep the incumbent. `0` keeps every incumbent launch; this is the kill switch. Byte oracle: `cargo run -p rdna-compute --features lab --example lane_rows_pm_oracle -- [--bench N]`. |
| `HIPFIRE_MQ6_ROWS_SPLIT` | gfx1201 and gfx1100: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201 / gfx1100, `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows` and `_x4` launchers at 3..=8 rows (also each 3..8-row group of a wider verify): routes to the stream-split kernels `gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows[_x4]_split_r{R}_w{W}_s{S}`, byte-identical to the incumbent rows kernels and the one-row decode GEMV. `(W, S)` is chosen by `mq6_rows_split_config(arch, rows, total M)`. `0` restores the incumbent kernels; rows == 2 and gfx1151 (lane-rows PM route) never take it. |
| `HIPFIRE_MQ6_RESID_PORT_GFX1100` | exact gfx1100: on unless `0` (kill switch, typed `FeatureFlags::mq6_resid_port_gfx1100`); ignored on every other architecture | Byte-identical. Exact-gfx1100 MQ6G256V2 residual prefill GEMM (Qwen4 QKV / Z / QG / out projections) at the shapes where hipcc picks `gemm_mq6g256v2_residual_wmma_gfx11_mw8_lds` (N >= 464, `K % 256 == 0`) runs the hipcc "pk twin" ports of the PM twin design from `kernels/src/gemm_mq6g256v2_residual_gfx1100_port.hip` (JIT module `gemm_mq6g256v2_residual_gfx1100_port`, same `A,X,Y,M,K,N` kernargs and WMMA chain, bit-identical to the hipcc kernel on the card): `M >= 6144` -> `gemm_mq6g256v2_residual_gfx1100_port_w8n256` (tile 128x256, block 256, 40 KiB LDS, 16 accumulator chains per wave), `2560 <= M < 6144` -> `gemm_mq6g256v2_residual_gfx1100_port_w4n128` (tile 64x128, block 128, 20 KiB LDS); `M < 2560` (the 48-row `ba` projection) and shapes whose fp16 activation or weight extent reaches 2 GiB stay on the previous route. Card time ratio port/hipcc (hip_loop, N = 768): w8n256 QKV 0.508, QG 0.479, Z 0.507; w4n128 out (M2560 K6144) 0.607, QKV 0.524. Precedence: port -> embedded PM twin (`HIPFIRE_MQ6_RESID_PM_GFX1100`, default on, `=0` skips it) -> hipcc MW8; `=0` here falls back to the PM twin, and `=0` on both restores hipcc. |
| `HIPFIRE_GATED_DELTA_VECTOR_PM` | exact gfx1100 and exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch, developer); ignored on every other architecture | Bit-identical. The generic gated-delta launchers `Gpu::gated_delta_net_vector_serial_f32` (head_dim 128) and `Gpu::causal_conv_depthwise_silu_rows_f32` (ksize 2..=8) run the route-A sealed hipcc twins of `kernels/gated_delta_vector_pm_gfx1100.hxaco` (gfx1100, every shape the incumbent accepts) or `kernels/gated_delta_vector_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (gfx1201, the same two bodies compiled for gfx1201 and card-judged on the R9700, symbols `*_pm_gfx1201`; RDA at `head_dim 128`; conv with the history in place only, since on a separate `hist_out` it measured no faster than hipcc, so that launch keeps hipcc) (`gated_delta_net_vector_serial_f32_pm_gfx1100`: DPP lockstep row reductions with raised priority on the pred butterfly, grid `[n_heads, 16, 1]` x 32 lanes; `causal_conv_depthwise_silu_rows_f32_pm_gfx1100`: 32 rows per workgroup, grid `[ceil(C / 128), ceil(N / 32), 1]` x 128 lanes, row slice 0 alone reads `hist_in` / writes `hist_out`, so in place or not), same kernargs as the hipcc kernels. Selection is inside the generic launchers (no arch-crate change). `0` restores the hipcc `gated_delta_net_vector_serial.hip` / `causal_conv_depthwise_rows.hip` kernels. |
| `HIPFIRE_NPU_TRUNK_PACK_A_PM` | exact gfx1151: on unless `0` (kill switch, developer); ignored on every other architecture | Byte-identical. `Gpu::npu_trunk_pack_a_bf16` / `npu_trunk_pack_a_bf16_ptr` (the Flash-Next K = 2560 and qwen3.8-27b K = 5120 G80 A-stream producers behind the NPU trunk, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK`) launch the route-A sealed PeaceMaker twins of `kernels/npu_trunk_pack_pm_gfx1151.hxaco` (`npu_trunk_pack_a_bf16_pm`, `npu_trunk_pack_a_bf16_k5120_pm`, sealed by `scripts/pm-hip-twin.sh`: lift identity through `v_ceil_f32` and the other gfx11 rows, hazards, kernarg ABI equal to the incumbent, `.text` of each symbol byte-identical to the incumbent hipcc symbol) instead of the JIT-compiled hipcc kernels of `kernels/src/npu_trunk.hip`. Same kernargs, grid and block. `0` restores the hipcc kernels; the generic-K scalar producer is never replaced. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_CHUNK_PM` | exact gfx1151 and exact gfx1100: on unless `0` (kill switch, developer); ignored on every other architecture | Bit-identical (same machine code). The chunked-GDN prefill recurrence + gated RMSNorm (`gated_delta_chunk_gate_wmma`, the one launch of `gated_delta_step_gate_wmma`, reached at >= 512 rows on the Qwen4 / Flash-Next pipeline) runs the route-A sealed hipcc twin of the shipped kernel: `kernels/gated_delta_chunk_pm_gfx1151.hxaco` (gfx1151) or `kernels/gated_delta_chunk_pm_gfx1100.hxaco` (gfx1100). On gfx1151 the NPU-arena entry (`gated_delta_chunk_arena_pm_gfx1151`) and the opt-in inline-Q8 pair (`gated_delta_chunk_q8_pm_gfx1151`, `gated_delta_chunk_q8_arena_pm_gfx1151`) are twinned too. Each twin is the shared source `#include`d under a symbol rename (`kernels/gated_delta_chunk_*_pm_gfx11*.hip`); `scripts/pm-hip-twin.sh` aborts unless its `.text` sha256 equals the incumbent hipcc `.text` compiled with the identical argv, so kernargs, grid `[value_heads]`, block 256 and LDS are unchanged. The route excludes recorders and graph capture, so no captured launch carries a twin symbol. `=0` restores the JIT hipcc kernels. |
| `HIPFIRE_GDN_PERSISTENT_STAGED` | gfx12: on unless `0` (kill switch, developer); ignored on every other architecture | Bit-identical. The GDN verify recurrence `gated_delta_step_persistent256_body` (`gated_delta_step_halves_state128_persistent256_capture_q8`, its `_lanes` variant and `gated_delta_rollback_layers_q8`, plus the F32 / Q8-EF instantiations) computes a few-row block's (<= 8 rows) per-row q/k norm parameters with the whole block: all 256 threads stage the BF16-rounded element squares in LDS (branchless BF16 rounding), and one thread per row then sums them in the same kd order. Previously one wave ran each row's 128-element chain while the block waited. Each verify row's intermediate Q8 boundary also keeps the code as its exact F32 value instead of converting float -> int8 -> float. `=0` compiles the `tensor_ops` and `gdn_lanes` modules with `-DHIPFIRE_GDN_PERSISTENT_SERIAL=1`, which restores the previous body (own JIT cache key). |
| `HIPFIRE_GATED_DELTA_VECTOR_CHUNKED` | exact gfx1201: on (developer); `=0` kills; ignored on every other architecture | Not bit-identical to the serial kernel (reassociated). `execute_gated_delta` offers an ordinary prefill launch (`n_tokens >= 64`, `head_dim` 128, host slot and token count, so never a graph-captured launch) to `Gpu::gated_delta_net_vector_chunked_f32` when the op carries a `GatedDeltaVectorChunkScratch` (Raon's `RdaScratch.chunk`, allocated only with the flag on): C64 chunks, per-key-channel log decay in log space with every exponent relative and <= 0 (per-token log decay clamped to `[-10, 0]`), chunk-parallel prepare plus a per-(head, 16 value rows) FP32-state scan, no atomics, deterministic bytes. Decode, verify and rollback replay rows stay on the PM serial kernel. |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_GROUPED_K2_PM` | exact gfx1100: on unless `0` (kill switch, developer); ignored on every other architecture | Bit-identical. The F32 entry of the grouped MQ4G256V2 MoE GEMM `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_wmma_k2` (`M % 64 == 0`, `K % 256 == 0`; the BF16-output entries never take it) runs the route-A sealed hipcc twin `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_wmma_k2_pm_gfx1100` of `kernels/gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_k2_pm_gfx1100.hxaco`: four waves (64 rows) per 128-lane block share the expert run's X tile through 9 KiB of static LDS and a workgroup also computes the expert's next slot tile (`tile_run` 2); grid `[ceil(M / 64), ceil(m_total / 16), 1]`, same kernargs. `0` restores the hipcc `_k2` kernel. |
| `HIPFIRE_FN_LANE_POLICY` | developer; unset keeps the default lane stage policy | Read once when each Flash-Next (Qwen4) lane store is constructed (`hipfire_config::developer_var`). The value is a `LaneStagePolicy::parse` spec: `production` or `per-lane`, optionally followed by `:<stage>=once\|class\|lane,...` overrides (the stateful stages `ple_conv`, `gdn_core`, `qsa_core` stay `lane`). The same policy is applied to every band (PP) and rank (EP) store, and the selected policy description is logged. A spec that does not parse, or a policy that fails validation, **refuses the load with that error**; there is no silent fallback to the default. Developer opt-in only: the default stage policy is unchanged, and no exactness or performance claim is made for a non-default policy without its own G0 evidence. |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PROGRESS` | on unless `0` (kill switch) | Read once through `hipfire_config::developer_var`. Daemon `prefill_progress` JSONL events (`{"type":"prefill_progress","id","attempt_id","done","total"}`): one line after each prompt-prefill chunk while `done < total` (no event for a single-chunk prompt or at `done == total`), ordered after `gen_start` and before the first `token`/`reasoning`. `done`/`total` count prompt tokens this request prefills (a cache-hit prefix is in neither). `0` suppresses every event; clients ignore the type when absent. See `crates/hipfire-runtime/src/prefill_progress.rs`. |
| `HIPFIRE_PP_LANE_GROUPS` | developer; unset: the stage policy chooses the group count | Flash-Next (Qwen4) PP lane decode (`topology.pp` 2..=8): forces the number of decode micro-batches `G`. A positive integer is **clamped** to `min(active lanes, 2 * stages)`: a value above the active lane count (or above `2 * stages`) runs that maximum rather than refusing. `0` or a non-integer **refuses** with a message naming the variable. The partition is always contiguous and balanced and never splits a lane's rows. The override replaces the policy-driven choice, including the maximal-overlap choice of the no-sharing policy. Unset: under a shared stage policy `G` is adaptive over `1..=min(lanes, 2 * stages)` (bounded exploration of each `G`, then the cheapest observed complete-step wall; statistics are kept per exact row shape, stage count and policy), and under a no-sharing (`per-lane`) policy `G` is the maximal overlap `min(lanes, 2 * stages)`. The default (`per-lane`) policy is unchanged, so the adaptive path is production opt-in only. Exactness requirement: every `G` must produce byte-identical lane outputs to every other `G`; no throughput claim is made here. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_FUSE` | gfx1151: `3`; gfx1201: `1` | Exact gfx1151 and gfx1201, on the F16 WMMA HC read (>= `QWEN4_F16_WMMA_MIN_TOKENS` rows, eager, no recorder or graph capture; gfx1201 also needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_F16_WMMA_GFX1201`, default on, which enables that read). Fusion level of the HC read/write pair around each Qwen4 mixer; every level leaves the HC streams bytewise what the unfused sequence stores. `1`: the HC read's norm launch also projects its paired HC write's gates (`hyper_norm_gate_outputs`), so the write skips its own norm + gate launch. `2`: also the MQ6G256V2 attention output projection (GDN `output`, QSA `o_proj`) applies the HC write in its GEMM epilogue (`gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_gfx11_bt8_x4_hcw`; gfx1201 `gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_gfx12_bt8_hcw`, >= 64 rows); the attention output tensor is not materialized. `3`: also, on gfx1151, a BF16 shared-expert down on the F16 WMMA route folds the BF16 scaled add and the HC write into its epilogue (`gemm_wmma_lds_128_256_32_64_k64_hcsd`); the routed `moe_output` rows are then NOT rewritten with the shared-down sum (they are dead after the write). Levels 2 and 3 measured slower than 1 on gfx1201, which defaults to 1. A level that a given layer's shapes or routes do not admit runs the next lower one. An unparsable value keeps the arch default. `0` keeps every launch of the incumbent route; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_I8_FUSE` | gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next dense-GDN prefill (the `HIPFIRE_FN_GDN_DENSE_SCAN` route), eager, one whole-mixer call (no split core / out-projection phases or row window) whose out projection is the exact-INT8 MQ6G256V2 route (`HIPFIRE_MQ6_I8`, default on at >= 512 rows) and whose HC write does not ride the projection epilogue (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_FUSE` < 2, the gfx1201 default): the gated RMSNorm launch `fn_gdn_dense_gate_i8` (module `fn_gdn_dense`) also FWHT-rotates its 256-wide head-pair groups and emits the out projection's `block_i8_128` blocks, so the F32 gate output (rows x 6144 x 4 B written and read back) and the separate `mq_rotate_x_i8_gfx12` launch disappear. The blocks are bytewise what `fn_gdn_dense_gate_bf16in` followed by `mq_rotate_x_i8_gfx12` leave, and the GEMM output therefore unchanged. `0` keeps that two-launch chain (`fn_gdn_dense_gate_rotate_bf16in` + `mq_rotate_x_i8_gfx12`); this is the kill switch. Every other shape keeps it too. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_CHAIN_FUSE` | gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next dense-GDN prefill (the `HIPFIRE_FN_GDN_DENSE_SCAN` route): the producer runs the load-batched `fn_gdn_dense_producer_v2_{f32,bf16}` entries (module `fn_gdn_dense`). All 19 convolution inputs of a thread are loaded before any is consumed (the v1 entry `fn_gdn_dense_producer` waited on one dependent load per row and reached 415 GB/s of ~600), the redundant BF16 re-roundings of already-BF16 values are dropped, and SiLU + BF16 rounding takes a hardware exp2/rcp path accepted only when it provably rounds to the same BF16 as the exact chain (|x| in [2^-20, 16] and not within 64 ulps of a BF16 tie; verified over all 2^32 F32 inputs). Every changed intermediate and the scan output are byte-identical to the v1 chain (`crates/rdna-compute/tests/fn_gdn_chain_oracle.rs`). `0` restores the v1 producer and today's chain exactly. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_QKV_BF16` | off; `1` opts in (needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_CHAIN_FUSE`, exact gfx1201) | Dense-GDN prefill whose qkv projection is the exact-INT8 MQ6G256V2 route: the qkv GEMM stores BF16 bits (its BF16 entry, bitwise `RNE(F32 accumulator)`) instead of F32 because the producer's convolution reads every qkv value only through that rounding: 20 KB instead of 40 KB per row written by the GEMM and again read by the producer, byte-identical chain. Opt-in because the PeaceMaker INT8 GEMM's BF16 entry is currently slower than its F32 entry at this shape (16384 rows: 5.53 vs 4.96 ms) while the producer gains 0.39 ms; the decision is one pure function of the bank (`gdn_dense_qkv_bf16`, also reported by `gdn_qkv_projection_bf16`) so split phases and the NPU twin agree. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TILE` | gfx1151 / gfx1201: on unless `0` | Exact gfx1151 and gfx1201, on the F16 WMMA HC read: runs the up projection + branch mix on the retiled operand-swapped entries (`hyper_read_up_wmma_bf16_swap` on gfx1151; `hyper_read_up_wmma_bf16_gfx1201_t128` on gfx1201 when `low_rank % 64 == 0`), bytewise the baseline entries' output. `0` keeps the baseline entries; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_V2` | exact gfx1201 and gfx1151: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1201, on the retiled HC read (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TILE`, F16 WMMA route): runs the up projection + branch mix on the persistent W-stationary `hyper_read_up_wmma_bf16_gfx1201_v2` (same module `hyper_read_up_wmma_gfx1201`; grid `[hidden/16, G]` with `G = ceil(rows/8192)` clamped to 1..4, block 512, dynamic LDS 58368 B: the block's 64 weight rows staged once, X fragments pipelined, the gate sigmoid as a 16 KiB LDS table) instead of `_t128`. Taken only for `low_rank == 320`, `hidden % 16 == 0`, rows >= `HC_UP_V2_MIN_ROWS` (128), outside Redline recording and graph capture; bytewise `_t128`'s output. `=0` restores the `_t128` chain; this is the kill switch. On exact gfx1151 the same switch takes `hyper_read_up_wmma_bf16_gfx1151_v2` (module `hyper_read_up_wmma_gfx1151_v2`; grid `[hidden/16, G]`, `G = ceil(rows/4096)` clamped to 1..4, block 256, same dynamic LDS) instead of the retiled `_swap` entry, bytewise `_swap`'s output; `=0` restores `_swap`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_ZINIT` | gfx1151 / gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 and gfx1201, sealed Qwen4 MoE grouped prefill whose combine takes the BF16-row path 2 arm with the shared down after the combine: starts the combine from +0.0 (`moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_zinit`) and drops the `moe_output` zero fill that precedes the call. Bytewise the zero fill + the unchanged combine. The fill stays whenever any condition is unmet (other route, recorder / retained tape / graph capture, target not exactly the cleared tensor); an absorbed fill the combine did not consume fails the call instead of leaving the target uncleared. Also taken on a G2-staged prefill (host-mapped experts), which runs the whole step program in one interpreter call with the stage waits and refills as MoE hooks. `0` keeps the separate fill; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_FFN_CLEAR_ABSORB` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP2 wide-verify int8 MoE chain (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_A8`: the 9..=64-row verify MoE of the trunk layers, no row gather, no tile exchange): the BF16-row combine (`moe_combine_order_top10_owned` + `moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in`) takes its existing `_zinit` entry (`moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_zinit`, the arm `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_ZINIT` uses on the symmetric IU4 / F16 WMMA routes) and the per-layer FFN `zero_f32` of `ffn_output` before the call is not launched (1 launch per layer, 48 per step at C8). Bytewise the zero fill + the unchanged combine: the kernel visits every `(token, column)` of the target whether or not the owner mask marks a rank's rows foreign (a foreign rank adds a +0 BF16 row), so every element the fill zeroed is stored; the shared down and `bf16_scaled_add_batched_f32` still accumulate into it afterwards. Needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_ZINIT` on (the absorb path). The HC row folds keep their own predicate. The few-row (1..=8 rows) `moe_shared_down_combine_rows_r<R>` epilogue route is not covered: its kernel loads the target as the accumulator seed and has no store arm, so its fill stays. `0` keeps the separate fill; this is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_INLINE_ORDER` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (Route-A PeaceMaker twins for both entries); ignored elsewhere; needs the wide-verify int8 chain (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_A8`) | Wide-verify / short-prefill trunk MoE of 9..=64 rows on the owner-masked int8 chain (`ep_a8_wide`, `combine_stage` BF16-row arm; C4/C8 Flash-Next EP2): the owner-masked rank order kernel `moe_combine_order_top10_owned` and the BF16-row combine `moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in[_zinit]` run as ONE launch, `Gpu::moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_ordered` (`moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_ordered[_zinit]`, module `moe_combine_ordered`; 1 launch per MoE layer removed). Each wave computes the ten route ranks' ascending-expert-id order inline (lane r takes slot = number of ranks with a smaller (expert, rank) key; a foreign expert keeps its place and BF16 weight but adds a +0 row, its grouped row is never read) and then runs the identical per-column multiply / add / BF16-round chain; the `order` scratch is no longer written (no reader on this route: the shared down that follows overwrites it). Bytewise the two-launch pair, zinit and accumulate. Needs the owner mask (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRUNK_SHARD_PMASK`); without it the two-launch pair runs. Route-A portable gfx1200/gfx1201 PeaceMaker twins for both entries are committed (full ELF, 68-byte ABI, staleness proof); dispatch still uses the registry HIP source. The strict ledger remains OPEN: verifier state cap, not proven, at `.LBB28` (>512 distinct in-flight states) for both entries including hosted pools; no full strict proof is claimed. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DRAFT_GLUE_FOLD` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch); ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next MTP draft head launch-glue fold, every fold an existing entry and bytewise the launches it replaces (oracle `qwen4_draft_glue`: `cargo run --release -p rdna-compute --features lab --example qwen4_draft_glue -- oracle`): (1) the head step's FC broadcast add (`BroadcastAddOp`, `execute_broadcast_add`) is ONE `broadcast_add_rows_f32` launch instead of four per-branch `add_f32`, and a batched round of `n` lanes runs it once over all rows (`BroadcastAddOp::lanes`) instead of `4n`; (2) `head_prologue` no longer copies `wide_hidden` to `backbone_hidden` (`head_block` reads `wide_hidden` in place); (3) the head ticket snapshot / restore (`MtpGpuState`, three copies) and (4) the batched round's lane staging and epilogue copies (`forward_rows_deferred`, `n` each) are one `copy_regions_u32` launch per group (`copy_tensors`); (5) the draft ranking's per-row `rotate_x_mq` (`DraftHead::draft_rows_enqueue`) is one `rotate_x_mq_batched` per run of consecutive rows. Default off until the lane identity and step profile gates land. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DRAFT_TOPK8_ROWS` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch); ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next MTP draft ranking with an MQ6G256V2 head: a batched ranking class runs one `topk8_partial_f32_rows` + `topk8_rescore_mq6g256v2_k2560_rows` launch pair per run of consecutive rows (at most 8) instead of one `topk8_partial_f32` + `topk8_rescore_mq6g256v2_k2560` pair per row; each row's partial slot and 72-byte output slot are the one-row pair's bytes (oracle `qwen4_topk8_rows`: `cargo run --release -p rdna-compute --features lab --example qwen4_topk8_rows -- oracle`). One-row classes, the vocab-sharded path and Q8_0 heads keep the per-row launches. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PREPARE_ASYNC` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0` (kill switch: restores the prepare barrier); ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP mesh lanes, native-MTP windows: the prepare phase of a window (`RankLane::prepare_commit`: GDN ring rollback, trunk restore copies, the head's history commit-fill `append_rows`, the pending-hidden copy) is enqueued and the rank returns WITHOUT its end-of-phase `hipDeviceSynchronize` + `take_device_wait_error`, so the host turn (collect, publish, token emission, next plan, `begin_window`, the next draft round's launches) runs while the phase's ~1 ms device tail finishes. The next draft round's launches are stream-ordered behind it on the same per-rank stream; same kernels in the same order, so outputs are byte-identical. **Semantic change: a device fault in the window prepare is detected one host turn late.** It is found at the rank's next device wait (the first readback of the next draft round or verify) and reported as `prepare (deferred): ...`; a settle runs the same wait before the work that would otherwise carry the fault under another label or drop it (a VMM arena growth in `begin_window`, admission, a prefill tile's `provision_step` / `forward_step`, `map_ahead_decode`, the elastic reserve's cache release / lend / reclaim, mesh close). Step N's tokens are correct (they come from the verify, not from the prepare) and are already streamed when the fault is found; the requests of step N+1 (the step whose wait found it) fail; the mesh is quarantined (`EP mesh lane step aborted (mesh quarantined, reload to recover)`; reload to recover). With `=0` the prepare waits for the device as before: a prepare fault aborts the step before it publishes and step N's tokens are never emitted. `prepare_us` of the step evidence measures the host ENQUEUE time when async and its `PhaseTiming` is `synchronized: false` (stats only: no chooser, floor or pricing reads it; the step interval `wall_us` they read simply loses the overlapped tail). Waits that remain in the window cap the gain: the first draft or verify readback, an arena growth, admission into a reused slot, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_TOKEN_RING=0`. |

Route-A PeaceMaker twins of the folded kernels exist only as byte-identical proof artifacts; dispatch stays on the HIP (hipcc) kernels, not PM. Eager only (declined under replay recording / graph capture, like the chain it folds). `0` keeps the unfused chain. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_MOE` | on (C1 fold wave; Route-A twin sealed); `0` off (developer): exact gfx942, stock-FP8 (qt=55) sealed grouped MoE | gfx942 Flash-Next MoE glue folds (every fold is bitwise the unfused launch sequence; `kernels/src/qwen4_g942_glue_moe.hip`, module `qwen4_g942_glue_moe`, 7 glue launches per layer become 2 plus a combine that carries the shared add): fold 1 `qwen4_g942_moe_group_scatter_desc` (one 1024-thread workgroup, up to 1024 flat slots / 102 rows, else the unfused chain) is `qwen4_moe_group_ranks` + `_prefix` + `_scatter` + `gemm_fp8b128_moe_tile_desc_pad16`; fold 2 `qwen4_g942_moe_unscatter_act` is `fp8b128_moe_unscatter_silu_gfx942` + `fp8b128_moe_shared_activation_gfx942` (the `SharedActivation` stage defers its launch to the `Unscatter` stage; nothing reads the shared activation before the shared down); fold 3 `qwen4_g942_moe_combine_shared_add` is `moe_down_combine_grouped_top10` + `bf16_scaled_add_batched_f32` (the `Combine` stage defers to the `SharedDown` stage, which runs the shared down GEMM then the one launch). Taken only for the plain grouped tail (no CPU split / deferral, no EP exchange, shared down after the combine, no HC write carried by the shared down, no absorbed target fill) of a state that runs its whole lowered program. Tier C: `test_kernelsQA --filter qwen4_g942_fold_moe` compares each fold with the unfused launches byte for byte (rows 1..=128). Retirement: the switch is deleted (folds unconditional on the plain grouped tail) when the MoE program moves onto the 0.5.0 typed Step program (#666), or after one release default-on without a regression, whichever is first. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_MOE_IMAGE` | unset (developer): absolute path of a device code object | Route-A twin proof hook for `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_G942_FOLD_MOE`: when set, the three `qwen4_g942_glue_moe` entries (`qwen4_g942_moe_group_scatter_desc`, `qwen4_g942_moe_unscatter_act`, `qwen4_g942_moe_combine_shared_add`) load from that explicitly supplied image through `ensure_embedded_kernel` instead of the hipcc-compiled source, so Tier C (`test_kernelsQA --filter qwen4_g942_fold_moe`) can run the twin ELF against the unfused launches. | `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_ROW_FOLD` | gfx1151 / gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_FUSE=3` and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_ZINIT=1` (both gfx1151 defaults) on the sealed Qwen4 MoE grouped prefill whose combine takes the BF16-row path 2 arm, eager, BF16 HC streams, when the MoE block's HC write is directly followed by the next layer's HC read on the same streams and the F16 WMMA read route: runs the shared-down GEMM as a plain BF16 store (`gemm_wmma_lds_128_256_32_64_k64_bf16st`) and replaces the combine, the shared-down fold + HC write epilogue and the next read's `hyper_norm_gate_outputs` with one per-token row kernel (`hc_row_fold_norm_gate`) that combines the ten expert rows in rank order, folds the shared row, writes the four HC streams and emits the next read's F16 normalized row and its paired write's gate logits; the next read then skips its norm-and-gate launch. Bytewise the unfused chain (streams, F16 row, gate logits). Idea from Gufo upstream (gufo-org/gufo @1071b361, MIT). **Exact gfx1201** (default `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_FUSE=1`; the GEMM epilogue levels 2/3 stay off there and are not required): the same two folds on the gfx1201 default chain. MoE tail (`hc_row_fold_moe_gfx1201`, with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_COMBINE_ZINIT=1`): the generic shared-down GEMM runs unchanged but stores its F32 rows into the routed target (not `down_expanded`, which then keeps the rank order), and one per-token row kernel replaces `moe_down_combine_grouped_top10_bf16in_zinit`, `bf16_scaled_add_batched_f32`, `hyper_write_bf16x8_gfx1201` and the next read's `hyper_norm_gate_outputs`; the combine output never reaches DRAM. Mixer tail (`hc_row_write_norm_gate_gfx1201`): a pregated HC write immediately followed by the next HC read of the same streams (F16 WMMA read route) runs the write of the out projection's F32 `mixed` and that read's norm / gate projection as one kernel. Both need eager (no recorder or graph capture) BF16 HC streams, hidden 2560, at least 16 rows and 16-byte aligned streams / `mixed` (exactly the launches `hyper_write` gives the x8 kernel), the MoE tail also 16-byte aligned grouped expert rows; the qt=55 FP8 route and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_DOWN_EPI=1` are excluded, and any mismatch runs the unfused launches. Bytewise the unfused gfx1201 chain: streams, F16 row and gate logits, NaN payloads included (`hc_row_fold_moe_gfx1201_is_bytewise_the_default_chain`, `hc_row_write_norm_gate_gfx1201_is_bytewise_the_default_chain`). `0` keeps the existing launches on both arches; this is the kill switch. |

| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SLOWLINK_PREFILL` | unset = AUTO default selector: automatic candidate selection from 1,537 rows (first genuinely multi-chunk request, one row past the 1,536-row tile bound); `0` kill switch; `1` forces the shipping-path candidate (developer) | S1 slow-link layer-major prefill (`developer.qwen4_slowlink_prefill`). Typed boolean: unset runs the AUTO default selector on a load and request that satisfy every check below, `1`/`true` forces the shipping-path candidate (a capacity shortfall fails the load with its exact byte deficit instead of falling back to the incumbent), `0`/`false` is the kill switch, any other spelling (empty included) is rejected at config load. Read once per process through the config snapshot (the selector is resolved at load, never per request); the automatic selector never probes the link on a killed or otherwise declined load. Considered only on exact gfx1100, a discrete device with a measured pinned H2D link below 16 GB/s, the qualified MQ4 packed Flash-Next recipe, a single-device auto-placed whole-model load, a long enough context and a request of at least 1,537 rows (automatic and forced share that floor); a short context, a fast link, unified memory or an unmeasured link never selects it under either selector, and EP/TP/PP, an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS`, hot set, CPU splice, NPU trunk, externally captured prefill and an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE=0` all stay on the incumbent before any reserve or mutation. When selected: the purpose's mixer tile (768 rows for the product profile that every state, headroom, greedy and perf cell runs on; 512 rows for the primitive self-test profile that proves kernel correctness only; 1,536 is only the generic validator bound and the approved named incumbent bypass for requests of at most 1,536 rows), the largest trunk-layer resident count that fits the actual post-initialization free device memory (ten preferred, never a floor; zero is valid when the base budget fits and a host layer remains; only a base budget that does not fit fails the load, with its exact-byte deficit), one expert sweep across the link per window with next-layer prefetch. Gate and receipt contract: `docs/VALIDATION.md`. Measured base on the XTX at pp16384 is 1,738.9-1,750.2 tok/s (accepted release, ~1.87x beta); 2,000 tok/s is not a release block. The required followup stack (ordered placement + IU4 + QSA) is not promotion: QSA currently fails 4K and no waiver exists. No f32 GDN carry and no inert Q8 WMMA trunk lever ship in base. D2 keeps its truth: decode and <=4-row verify only, armed by the per-load measured default or an explicit `memory.offload_exec`, unchanged by S1. Serve-kit note: thinking-off requests derive the off sentinel cap 1 (None), never an explicit 0 that the daemon reads as uncapped on effort-carrying requests. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SLOWLINK_RESIDENT_LAYERS` | unset or `front` = leading layers `0..N` (incumbent); `spread`; or a comma list of exactly `N` layer indices (developer, **default `front`**) | Which trunk layers an admitted S1 slow-link load keeps resident; the COUNT `N` is still the admission's decision. `front` (ASCII case-insensitive, trimmed) is the incumbent placement. `spread` puts the `k`-th of `N` at `floor(k*T/N) + floor(T/(2N)) + 1`, clamped to `T-1` (`T`=48, `N`=9: 3, 8, 13, 19, 24, 29, 35, 40, 45); `N == T` is every layer. A list must have exactly `N` distinct entries, each below `T`, and is sorted ascending. Any other value, including an empty or blank one, a wrong count, a duplicate or an out-of-range index, fails the S1 load with an error naming this variable; the resolved set is appended to the S1 stderr line only for a non-`front` value. MTP-layer experts are always host-mapped. Free-form developer value read once through the config snapshot (`developer.qwen4_slowlink_resident_layers`) and consulted only by a load that S1 admitted. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP` | unset = **on for exact gfx1100** (off elsewhere); `0` is the kill switch (developer) | Typed boolean developer key `developer.qwen4_sp` (`true`/`false`/`1`/`0`; any other spelling is rejected when the layer is built, never ignored), read once per Qwen4 load. Hybrid SP, **the gfx1100 default** (KLD-gated: V4 Flash-Next WT2 dKLD -0.0020, CI [-0.0037, -0.0004]; code24 dKLD -0.0023, CI [-0.0067, +0.0007]; TTFT at 1K rows 8.05 s -> about 2.1 s on the 7900 XTX). Unset runs it on exact gfx1100 and not elsewhere; `0`/`false` is the kill switch (touched-only SP, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_TOUCHED`, then runs alone); an explicit `1`/`true` asks for it on any arch (every other arch declines `sp: arch not calibrated`). When a load-time rule declines the hybrid mode (a small `max_seq` window's region fit, no CPU quant, the pinned budget, `serve.prefix_cache` on, ...) the touched-only mode (default on, byte-identical to S1) runs instead and the load prints `qwen4 SP: hybrid mode declined, falling back to touched_only: <reason>`; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_TOUCHED=0` removes the fallback. The mode is the short-prompt per-window mode of the S1 slow-link prefill: after each host layer's count barrier the top-k\* experts by routed-token count stream to the GPU, the rest run on the CPU (D2 E1 engine, BF16-canonical rows) and one canonical combine applies every slot in expert-id order. With SP off (`0`, and touched-only off too) every path is byte-identical to a build without SP; hybrid SP changes the session-cache identity (`sp-e1v1` scope token), so snapshots are not shared with a lineage that ran without it. Load-time admission (all must hold, else a sticky named `sp: <reason>` decline and S1 unchanged): an admitted S1 profile, exact gfx1100 (`arch not calibrated` otherwise), the `Aggregate` or `AggregateSymIu4` window route (on the gfx1100 default, the symmetric IU4 profile, SP-qualified windows run the F16 `Aggregate` route through a per-window override of the tile scope's MoE arm while every other window keeps the IU4 route; the dry run of the F16 arm on the admitted tile and the F16 SP layout fitting the IU4 route region must hold, else `sp: f16 route`; the load logs `qwen4 SP: hybrid mode armed (SP windows use Aggregate F16; others SymIu4)`, the region fit on that profile is 4,507 rows, and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0` loads the F16 profile instead), 512 routed experts, CPU quants for the host layers' gate_up and down dtypes, a splice pool, the SP window layout fitting the S1 route region for at least 2,048 rows (the region is the admitted S1 window's, `min(16,896, max_seq)` rounded to tiles: a 4,096-row window fits only 769 rows on the symmetric-IU4 profile and declines `sp: region fit`; a load needs a window of about 9K rows, e.g. `max_seq` 16,896 or more, 262,144 in the product) (`sp_max_rows = min(5,376, fit)`; zero new VRAM, resident layers and the S1 census never move), the SP pinned host owners (about 158 MiB at 5,376 rows) fitting the pinned budget, and `serve.prefix_cache` off. Per window (pre-mutation, else a named `sp_bypass`): 5..=`sp_max_rows` rows, no interior session cut, no one-row tile, no K1 rows after the F32-tail tiles are K2-ized, capture `None` or `Bounded`. A request of 5 rows up to one tile is admitted to S1 only when its single window is SP-qualified, otherwise the incumbent runs. SP output is numerically different from S1 in CPU-arm slots (KLD-gated). Source: `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/src/slowlink_sp.rs`, `slowlink_admission.rs`. | With both knobs on the hybrid mode wins and touched-only is its fallback (see `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_TOUCHED`); the receipt's `sp.mode` names the armed mode.
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_FORCE_K` | unset = `auto` (lab builds only) | Exact lower-case `all` (k = U: every touched expert streams, no CPU expert), `cpu` (k = 0: every expert on the CPU) or `auto` (the planner); any other spelling is an error naming the variable. Compiled only under the `lab` feature of `hipfire-arch-qwen4`; a product build never reads it. Only meaningful with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP=1`. `all` selects the touched-only mode (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_TOUCHED`: S1's exact per-panel pipeline over the touched experts, tail tiles kept on S1's F32 path), which equals stream-all S1 byte for byte on every window including those with F32-tail tiles (the V2a exact-mechanics gate); the hybrid K2-ized-tail k = U placement it used to run is no longer reachable. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_TOUCHED` | unset = **on for exact gfx1100** (off elsewhere); `0` is the kill switch (developer) | Typed boolean developer key `developer.qwen4_sp_touched` (`true`/`false`/`1`/`0`; any other spelling is rejected when the layer is built, never ignored), read once per Qwen4 load. Touched-only SP, **the gfx1100 default (exact, byte-identical to the stream-all sweep; card-proven on the 7900 XTX: greedy ids equal to S1 at N=1024/1536/2304)**. Unset runs it on exact gfx1100 and not elsewhere; `0`/`false` is the kill switch; an explicit `1`/`true` asks for it on any arch (every other arch declines `sp: arch not calibrated`). The mode: on the same short windows as `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP` (single window of 5..=`sp_max_rows` rows, no interior session cut, no one-row tile, capture none/bounded; below S1's 769-row floor all-or-incumbent) the S1 slow-link prefill runs its own per-panel pipeline (K2 GEMMs, ordered per-panel combine, K1 / F32-tail cohort tiles masked to the panel's binding) over a **selective weight schedule**: after each host layer's count barrier only the experts the layer routes to (a K2 grouped row or a cohort slot, read from the one barrier's counts) stream, packed ascending into groups of at most 32 (one ring page each, all enqueued at once, never prefetched), instead of the full 16-group page-order sweep. There is no CPU expert, no pinned host owner, no K2-ized tail tile (tail tiles keep S1's exact F32 path) and no new VRAM; the result is byte-identical to the stream-all S1 sweep by construction (the per-expert arithmetic and the ascending-id ordered combine do not depend on how the experts are partitioned into bindings; the doc comment of `Run::touched_host_layer` and the CPU test `slowlink_combine_touched_only_panels_equal_stream_all_panels` argue it), so it does not change the session-cache identity and needs no CPU quants, splice pool or `serve.prefix_cache` off. Load-time admission: an admitted S1 profile, exact gfx1100, the `Aggregate` or `AggregateSymIu4` window route (the gfx1100 default is the symmetric IU4 route, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0` selects the F16 one), an S1 window of at least 2,048 rows and a ring of at least one page per 32 experts (`sp: pages`); a failed rule is a sticky named `sp: <reason>` decline and S1 unchanged; the load prints ONE `qwen4 SP:` line to stderr (like the S1 load line, so the examples without a tracing subscriber show it too) naming the armed mode or the decline, and each request logs per window at DEBUG whether SP engaged (`qwen4 SP window`). With the hybrid mode armed (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP`, the gfx1100 default) it wins and this mode is its fallback when it declines; with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP=0` this mode runs alone. Hybrid wins, except that the lab placement `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_FORCE_K=all` (every touched expert streams, no CPU expert) selects this mode. The receipt's `sp` object carries `mode` (`touched_only` or `hybrid`). A mid-window failure drains the ring, restores the transaction and disables the mode for the process (`sp_unavailable`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_SLAB` | unset = **on** (slab loads only); `0` is the kill switch (developer, read once per load through `developer_bool`) | Exact gfx1100 with the mq3-xts expert-slab artifact (`WindowRoute::SlabIu4`, the MQ4 hot + W3 cold tier): run the HYBRID short-prompt SP on the slab route, **the default there** (the aggregate routes were already covered by `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP`; this knob decides only the slab route and is inert for aggregate/mq4 loads, pinned by `admit_sp_slab_hybrid_follows_the_slab_fact_and_keeps_the_slab_route`). Receipts (7900 XTX, `bin-mq3s6`): paired KLD SP on minus off code24 -0.0041 [-0.0099, +0.0009], WT2 -0.0026 [-0.0053, -0.0001]; same-route determinism byte-identical; n=5 alternating fresh processes `hipfire bench --matrix --pp N --ctx 1 --tg 1 --max-seq 32768`: pp256 481.2 tok/s vs 43.4 S1-only vs 419.5 mq4, pp1105 970.7 vs 186.5 vs 846.7. A window's top-k* experts stream through the S1 ring and run the cold-codec and MQ4 K2 per codec bucket; the remaining experts run on the CPU with each expert's own quant (MQ4 or W3, hipfire-cpu), one canonical combine. The slab window also carries the rotated F16 activation plane the CPU arm reads (hidden x 2 B a row, charged inside the SP layout of the S1 route region), and the planner charges each streamed expert its own payload bytes over the MQ4 expert's. Not row-exact against the GPU K2 (D2-style CPU arithmetic contract): greedy ids equal the S1 sweep's on 4 of 8 prompts, the others diverge at tokens 16-39 (CPU E1 rows), the paired KLD above is the gate. Touched-only SP is not wired for the slab route. Windows cap at 4.5-5.4k rows and larger prompts keep the S1 sweep (S1-slab pp16384 2,200 vs mq4 1,893 tok/s, n=3); decode is unchanged by construction but was not A/B'd with SP on. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP_SLAB=0` restores the pre-flip S1-only route for slab loads; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SP=0` and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SLOWLINK_PREFILL=0` also still kill it. |

| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SYM_VERDICT_CACHE` | on unless `0` (developer) | Read once per scan call. Caches the Qwen4 routed-expert symmetry scan verdict (`artifact::routed_headers_symmetric`, about 13 s on Flash-Next) per model file in `<HIPFIRE_HOME>/cache/qwen4-sym-verdicts.json` (`HIPFIRE_HOME`, else `$HOME/.hipfire`) and in an in-process memo. A record is keyed by the canonical path and holds the file's stat key (device, inode, size, mtime), the scan version, a SHA-256 over the routed tensor names, quant types, shapes and payload byte ranges, and the verdict; a hit needs all of them equal, any difference rescans. Only completed scans are stored; writes are atomic and best effort and never fail a load; a missing or corrupt file is a miss. An artifact with a REAP/head overlay always scans. `0` disables the file and the memo: every call scans and nothing is read or written. |

With BF16 key/value weights on the F16 WMMA route the PLE rows are also gathered straight to F16 (`grouped_gather_convert_bf16_f16`), dropping the F32 rows and both F32 -> F16 conversions; the 4-tap dilation-3 PLE convolution runs a register-window kernel (`ple_conv_add_bf16s_k4d3`). The gate scalars and norm inverses live in the `ple_normed` scratch prefix; the F32 widened query, gated, normed and conv-output tensors are not written. Never taken under a recorder, retained tape or graph capture, with F32 streams, more than 4 branches or `hidden % 32 != 0`; those keep the incumbent chain. `0` keeps the incumbent chain; this is the kill switch. Every output element keeps the incumbent's ascending-K WMMA chain from +0 (no split-K), so the result is bit-identical. Measured on a 7900 XTX, hip_loop ABBA at M = 320, K = 10240, N = 768 (synthetic inputs): 0.904 of the 64 x 64 tile's time (confirm run, 12/12 rounds, p = 2.4e-4; card round r2 `R2Hc-03-fill80x32-pin`; `.text` sha256 6f567c58). Other M (including the M = 1 shared-expert selector) and K keep the 64 x 64 tile; `=0` restores it. The kernel is compiled only into the gfx1100 object of the `qwen4_gemm_wmma_lds256` module. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_DOWN_FUSED` | exact gfx1201 / gfx1151: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | One-launch HC down projection + activation (`Gpu::gemm_bf16_xf16_hc_down_fused`) in place of the chain `gemm_bf16_xf16_f16_wmma` + `hc_activation_fused_f32`. Exact gfx1201: `qwen4_hc_down_fused_b64_k32` (`kernels/src/qwen4_hc_down_fused.hip`) replaces the 160×80 split-K=4 tile + `qwen4_wmma_lds_reduce` + activation at route rows >= `HC_DOWN_FUSED_MIN_ROWS` (1536). Exact gfx1151: `qwen4_hc_down_fused_gfx1151` (`kernels/src/qwen4_hc_down_fused.gfx1151.hip`; five 64-row waves own the whole M = 320 and 32 tokens per workgroup, K staged 64, so each X row is read once instead of once per 160-row M tile) replaces the single-ascending-K-chain 160×64 / 64×64 tile + activation at route rows >= `HC_DOWN_FUSED_GFX1151_MIN_ROWS` (1024). Taken only for M = 320, K = 10240, `rows*K*2 < 2^32`, outside Redline recording and graph capture; it writes only the packed BF16 activation the WMMA read consumes, byte-identical to the chain of the same arch. `=0` restores the chain. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_INT8` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Flash-Next MoE shared expert prefill on INT8 (`crates/rdna-compute/src/shared_i8.rs`, `kernels/src/qwen4_shared_i8_gemm.gfx12.hip`, `kernels/src/qwen4_shared_i8_quant.gfx12.hip`) instead of the F16 WMMA route (BF16 weights through F16 shadows: gate/up GEMMs, `shared_expert_activation_bf16_f32`, the hidden's F32 -> F16 conversion, the down GEMM). Taken at `route_rows >= 512` on exact gfx1201, eager, for an all-BF16 shared gate/up/down with the router's K, `hidden % 256 == 0` and `intermediate % 128 == 0`, on the BF16 recipe and outside path 2's fused unscatter activation. The router and selector keep their F16 WMMA group. Weights: a model-lifetime INT8 shadow built on first use (FWHT-rotated like the activations, A8 `block_i8_128` per 128 K, gate/up rows interleaved so the SwiGLU is lane-local), 1.0x the BF16 bytes / 2 + 3% scales, freed with the other weight caches. Activations: the fused MoE front's third output (`mq_rotate_x_i4_f16out_i8`) or `mq6_i8_prepare_x`; gate/up GEMM (`qwen4_shared_i8_gate_up_silu_bf16`, signed int8 x int8 `v_wmma_i32_16x16x16_iu8`, per-128 `fma(RN(d_w*d_x), C, acc)` fold, BF16-recipe SwiGLU in the epilogue); `qwen4_shared_rot128_i8` (FWHT-128 + A8 + the selector sigmoid); down GEMM (`qwen4_shared_i8_down_set_f32`) into the F32 rows the BF16 scaled add / HC row fold already consume. **Numeric, KLD-gated** (int8 operands, not byte-identical to the F16 route; see the CHANGELOG entry for the gate record). `=0` restores the F16 route. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL` | **unset**: per-window choice | Native MTP verify route. Unset picks, per window, a batched `(K+1)`-row verify or the interleaved route (one target row per draft, stop at the first rejection); the batched depth comes from the AR floor's chooser (measured depths plus fitted neighbours, at the depth that minimizes cost per emitted token). `0` forces batched at the full `mtp_k`; `1` forces interleaved. Both emit AR's greedy tokens. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_AR_FLOOR` | on (unset) | Qwen4 native MTP same-request AR floor (a measured target-only token prices AR; a request whose speculative options never beat it retires to head-free AR). A retired request copies each AR token's target hidden row to the spec hidden capture and owes the head that row; the head appends the owed rows teacher-forced, as an n-gram takeover appends its kept rows (one batched decode fill where it runs, else one Append per row), every 1024 tokens and before the terminal pending-seed flush, so a retired turn still ends with head and target together and the conversation's next turn continues live. A retired request re-arms once per newly emitted tokenizer think-close (`</think>`) token after retirement, within the same request: the floor resets to the request-start thresholds and config (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_MIN_WINDOWS`, `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_AR_PROBE`), the head catches up on its owed rows first, then AR is re-calibrated and MTP probed again, so a thinking phase that retired the request does not pin the answer after it to AR. Re-arm mutates neither the sampler nor the target. `0` disables the floor and restores the pre-floor per-window chooser exactly: no calibration token, no probe windows, no retirement, no re-arm, no floor timing syncs; each window routes by the batched-depth chooser (interleaved when no depth pays). Any other value keeps the floor on. Read once per request. Diagnostic / A-B use; distinct from `HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL`, whose forced routes win when both are set. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_AR_PROBE` | `6` | Fresh ordinary AR tokens a singleton Qwen4 native MTP request's floor times at a would-be retirement, to re-price AR where the request is now instead of by its single first decode token (under the Flash-Next expert hot set that token's cost is a property of its routed experts: 7–26% off the request's mean AR token on R9700), at most once per `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_MIN_WINDOWS` windows. With the probes on, a retirement must also lose to the slow-memory (~25 windows) realized per-token cost of all the request's windows, so a streak of rejected drafts no longer retires a request that wins. The probe tokens are real, exact AR tokens (the calibration step: head kept synced). An integer ≥ 0; `0` is the kill switch and restores the single-calibration-token floor; anything unparsable keeps the default. Continuous-batching lanes are unchanged. Route only: greedy output is byte-identical at every value and a sampled request stays distribution-exact. Read once per request. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_MIN_WINDOWS` | `16` | Speculative windows a singleton Qwen4 native MTP request observes before its AR floor may retire it; measured routing still starts after the 3-window probe budget, and inside the grace a losing request keeps drafting on its cheapest measured depth. A think-close re-arm after retirement starts the same grace again from this request-start value. An integer ≥ 1, anything else keeps the default; `3` restores retirement at the probe budget. Route only: greedy output is byte-identical at every value. Read once per request. Shallow re-probe before retirement (singleton and lane floors, no separate environment switch): a request whose draft budget is above 3 and that has run a deep (cap > 3) window does not retire on deep evidence alone. Unless it already has 3 priced shallow (K ≤ 3: batched cap ≤ 3 or interleaved) windows since that deep window, the floor routes it at cap ≤ 3 for a bounded re-probe that ends at 3 priced windows or 6 windows in all (a first-use unpriced window and external windows spend the bound). AR is re-priced first where it can be (the singleton's AR re-price probes); a lane with a 0 AR probe budget still gets the bounded shallow control, starting at its first would-be retirement. A shallow cost below AR holds the request on its best measured shallow depth (route capped at 3) until a deeper candidate has priced below both the shallow depth and AR for 3 consecutive windows; a shallow cost at or above AR, or no priced shallow window, retires it. A budget of 3 or less never re-probes. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_TRACE` | off; `1` enables | One `QWEN4_FLOOR_TRACE` JSON line per calibration / AR-probe / AR token and per native or external window on stderr (a native window when the next window is planned): wall us (start to next plan), emitted tokens, drafted depth, the AR price, every chooser candidate's us per emitted token (`"route":"capN"` for routing at depth N, with its mean window `win_us`, realized `emit`, mean `drafted` depth, and `"modeled":true` when it is priced only from a neighbouring cap), the fitted per-row cost `row_us`, the 8-window and slow realized cost, probes left, retirement. Before `options` each line also carries the shallow re-probe fields: `budget_k` (draft budget of the last routed or observed window), `deep_seen` (a window routed at cap > 3 since the last shallow win), `shallow_state` (`idle`, `probing`, `holding`, `lost` or `unpriced`), `shallow_windows` (windows spent in the current re-probe, priced or not), `shallow_priced` (those with a valid timing), `shallow_budget` (the hard bound, 6), `shallow_since_deep` (priced shallow windows since the last deep window; 3 or more is fresh evidence and skips the re-probe), `shallow_us_per_tok` (the probe's realized shallow us per emitted token, decayed while holding) and `climb_streak` (consecutive windows a deeper candidate priced below both the shallow depth and AR; 3 resumes the climb past cap 3). A drafter's first batched window at a drafted depth traces as event `native_batched_first_use`. Diagnostic. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_PENALTY_PREPASS` | on (unset) | Qwen4 / Flash-Next repeat, presence and frequency penalties on the GPU. A penalized greedy AR request penalizes each decode logit row with a GPU prepass (`logit_penalty_table_rows`) and keeps the device argmax; a penalized sampled native MTP request penalizes its target verify rows with the same prepass (all rows of a batched verify in one launch) before downloading them. The host computes every per-token factor and subtrahend with the CPU policy's arithmetic, so each row is bit-identical to `sampler::apply_logit_policy_cpu`; draft `q`s keep the host policy over the same histories. `0` restores the host path (download the row, apply the policy on the CPU). Read once per request. Diagnostic / A-B use. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED_MODE` | `leviathan` | Sampled (temperature > 0) Qwen4 native MTP verifier, with `speculation.mtp_sampled` on. `leviathan`: speculative rejection sampling (drafts drawn from the head's 8 re-scored candidates; output equals AR in distribution). `naive`: SpecInfer naive sampling (argmax drafts, one AR-sampler draw per verify row, accept iff equal); a seeded request emits AR's exact tokens. Any other value fails sampled requests. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DEVICE_ACCEPT` | off; `1` enables | Flash-Next sampled Leviathan MTP only: resident parallel target distributions plus ordered device acceptance, with reusable host draft-q staging and an 8-byte result readback. Requires neutral penalties (no history window/prepass), finite positive temperature, exact gfx1151 or gfx1201 with device sampler support, at least one draft, pool `1..=64` and cap `1..=pool`; unsupported, penalized, naive, greedy and target-only AR-floor paths remain host routes. Covers native batched/incremental and n-gram point-mass takeover. Unset (or any value but `1`) keeps the unchanged host path. Enabled target law uses lower-token-id ties and fixed-tree sums, not host byte identity; accept law and consumed `SpecRng` chronology stay unchanged. gfx1201 end-to-end A/B ties the host path (+1.83 %, CS95 −2.76 … +6.63 %); default stays off pending the per-seed stability decision. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_SAMPLED_PICKS` | off; `1` enables | Flash-Next sampled MTP, only on the `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DEVICE_ACCEPT=1` Leviathan route: skips the unused greedy verify-pick D2H through private resident verify/capture entrypoints, keeping the same heads, argmax arithmetic, logits and target/head transaction state. Covers native and n-gram batched/incremental windows; the public `SpecTarget::verify_block` contract still returns real picks. Unset keeps pick downloads. Trace or phase timing keeps real picks; greedy, naive, penalty, unsupported and target-only AR-floor paths stay unchanged. No separate speed claim. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_DRAFT_HEAD` | `mq2r` | Draft ranking head: an `mq2`..`mq6` copy of the LM head (`r` suffix = re-score its top 8 exactly against the head's own Q8_0 or MQ6G256V2 rows). |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_TRACE` / `HIPFIRE_MTP_PHASE_TIMING` | off; `1` enables | Per-window MTP trace / per-phase `hipEvent` timing to stderr (diagnostic). Qwen4 `QWEN4_MTP_PHASE` lines carry `source` (`mtp`\|`ngram`), `verify_route`, `k` (drafts verified), `accepted`, `emitted` and `window_us`, wall time after a device synchronize (timing mode only); a takeover confirmed by the head's first draft adds `probe_us`. Batched and takeover windows split the verify forward (`verify`) from the target-row work (`accept`: penalty prepass, row downloads, host distributions). Qwen3.x single-GPU native MTP prints `QWEN35_MTP_PHASE` lines with `source`, `k`, `accepted`, `emitted`, synced `window_us`, and the host time and launch/upload counts of its penalty prepass (`penalty_host_us`, `penalty_launches`, `penalty_uploads`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_APPEND_DET` | exact gfx1151: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Default on (exact gfx1151), **prompt fill only** (`mtp_prefill` and lane prompt tiles): routes all seven projections of the head's batched KV-only Append through fixed-row native kernels instead of the incumbent multirow / K4 / staged-Q8 kernels: `fc_embedding`, `fc_hidden` (`rows * hc_count` rows), HC down, HC up (BF16 weights, `gemm_bf16_xf32_mtp_dense_det_pm`) and the Q8_0 index, K and V projections (`mtp_q8_det_pm_launch`, after one shared F32 to F16 conversion of the mixed row). Each is the PM F16 WMMA with F32 accumulate and one ascending-K chain per output (no split-K), so a row's bytes do not depend on its batch, sub-chunk size or position in the pass. HC down is the raw WMMA output followed by the existing `hc_activation_fused_f32` (scale `1/hc_count`, in place) instead of the K4 GEMV epilogue. **Numerics are explicitly not the incumbent's (G1) and are not byte-equal to it or to the per-row Append:** BF16 weights go through a model-lifetime F16 shadow (BF16 widened to F32, rounded to F16 RNE, once) and X is rounded F32 to F16 RNE on every call, so F16's narrower exponent range can underflow or overflow values BF16/F32 hold, and the gfx11 WMMA's internal dot order is its own. The head's QSA rows, draft state and pending hidden therefore differ from the incumbent's bytes (deterministic run to run); a session snapshot carries whichever arithmetic built it. Target outputs are unchanged (speculative verification): on Strix Halo greedy output is byte-identical to `0`, TTFT is −1.67% at 16K and −2.79% at 9.3K tokens, decode is not slower, and only the draft acceptance (τ) moves slightly per prompt. Never taken by the decode takeover step's head fill (explicitly `DecodeFill`, always the incumbent kernels) or by any other arch, nor on gfx1201. A lone prompt row (a one-token tail) takes the same rows kernels instead of the per-row `append_token` (with `0` a lone row stays per-row). The batched-fill scratch is allocated (and charged in admission) even with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL=0`; there DET runs one row per pass, byte-equal to the batched passes by row invariance. Under expert parallelism the drafter is replicated and `mtp_prefill` fans out to every rank, each rank running this same prompt fill on its own GPU (the head's Append step runs no MoE and no EP exchange), so a gfx1151 mesh would take DET identically on every rank; EP itself is gfx1201-only today, where DET is never taken. `0` is the kill switch (the incumbent kernels); read at every `mtp_prefill`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL` | gfx1151 or gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | Exact gfx1151 or exact gfx1201 only (gfx1200 and every other arch keep the per-row loop), Qwen4 native MTP prompt fill (`mtp_prefill`): runs the head's KV-only Append step over each prefill chunk as one row-batched pass (sub-chunks of up to 1024 rows, about 17 launches each) instead of one ~20-launch single-row `forward_token` per prompt row. Every operator is the single-row kernel with a rows grid, or a multi-row kernel whose per-row reduction order is the single-row kernel's, and never the F16 WMMA route, so the head's QSA state (`full_keys`, `full_values`, `raw_index_keys`, `pooled_keys`, lengths), the draft request state and the pending hidden row are built to match the per-row fill byte for byte; both fills pair token p with target hidden p - 1 (the prompt's first row keeps its own hidden). Read at each `mtp_prefill`. On gfx1201 the per-row references are the singleton `gemv_bf16_xf32` (embedding, HC up), `gemv_bf16_xf32_x4_rows_r4` (hidden projection), the singleton K4 down projection and the unstaged `gemv_q8_0`; gfx1151 keeps its own references. Allocates about 247.77 MiB (259,805,184 bytes) of row-batched scratch per drafter at the first fill (1024 rows, 4096 token IDs), counted in the per-request bytes of `native_mtp_device_bytes` (the MTP reserve is max(build scratch, request bytes)). The same gate feeds expert placement, so on gfx1151 and gfx1201 the auto VRAM reserve can rise by up to that amount and, because resident expert layers are floored to whole layers, can leave one routed-expert layer fewer in VRAM depending on the remaining slack. `0` keeps the per-row loop, allocates nothing and charges no batched-fill reserve, so reserve and placement return to the per-row values; other arches always take the per-row loop. The byte-identity statements above describe the incumbent kernels only: on exact gfx1151 the prompt fill uses the DET projections by default (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_APPEND_DET`; `0` restores these kernels; DET is not byte-equal to the per-row fill), and the scratch is allocated and charged even with `0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_REUSE_PREFILL_IDS` | off unless `1` | Qwen4 native MTP prompt fill on the batched path (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL`, exact gfx1151 or gfx1201): the head's batched Append embeds each sub-chunk from the token ids the same chunk's trunk forward already uploaded to its device `token_ids` buffer (rows `off..off + n`, read in place) instead of uploading them again. Taken only when that forward uploaded the ids whole from the host outside graph capture and its host mirror equals the sub-chunk's tokens; a HIP single-row body, a device-argmax id or any mismatch uploads the head's own copy. The kernel sequence, syncs and output bytes are unchanged, and the MTP decode-window append always uploads. Read at every `mtp_prefill`. Default off (the warm path was not output-identical on the RC4c gate); unset or `0` keeps the head's own upload. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_SKIP_INTERMEDIATE_PICK` | on unless `0` | Qwen4 native MTP prompt fill (`mtp_prefill`): a prefill chunk that does not end the prompt (a capture-split or natural chunk) skips the final hyper, LM head, argmax and host readback, because only the prompt's last chunk's pick is read (the seed, and the last row's logits for a sampled draw). Such a chunk runs the trunk with `Qwen4OutputPolicy::None` but keeps the full wide hidden capture, so target and head state, the hidden rows the head consumes, the prefix-checkpoint capture boundaries (end of prompt is final and keeps its pick) and the seed are unchanged; the final chunk keeps full logits and readback. Same tiles and trunk route, no new kernel or threshold. It only helps prompts filled in more than one chunk (a capture split or a prompt longer than `prefill_rows`); a one-chunk fill (for example a short cache-hit suffix) has no non-final chunk and is unchanged. Read at each `mtp_prefill`. `0` keeps the head and argmax readback on every chunk. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_G942_DEPTH` | unset; an integer `0..=7` (gfx942 only; ignored elsewhere; a malformed value fails the window) | Qwen4 native MTP on gfx942 (MI300X): pins every native window to `min(k, budget)` drafts on the batched route (`0` = the interleaved route) with no AR floor, calibration or cost table; `HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=1` still wins and `HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=0` takes this depth. Without it the gfx942 planner serves only depths that `MTP_WINDOW_COST_MS_GFX942` (`mtp_spec.rs`, measured with `qwen4_mtp_g942_cost`) publishes: a request with no priced depth runs AR (one `QWEN4_MTP gfx942: native MTP refused` line), and a depth with no measurement is never priced from the gfx1151 table. For the identity / distribution oracles and the window-cost tool, which need a fixed depth before any gfx942 price exists. Under `HIPFIRE_G942_TRUNK_GEMV=1` (whose one-row decode is not bitwise the multi-row verify chunk) the planner refuses native MTP altogether, forced depth included. Route only: greedy output is AR's at every value. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_PLE_ASYNC_UPLOAD` | on unless `0` | Qwen4 prefill of more than one token whose PLE rows are staged after layer 0 (no device token, no graph capture): the host-staged rows upload with an asynchronous copy instead of a blocking `hipMemcpy`, so the host no longer waits for the layers already enqueued. The single reusable host staging buffer is fenced by a hip event recorded behind the copy; the next write to it, the teardown and the drop all retire the fence first (an enqueue or fence failure falls back to a device drain, and a drop with a copy pending leaks the bytes instead of freeing them). Bytes, order, kernels and the decode device-token branch are unchanged. Read when the forward is built. Default on (=0 disables the fenced asynchronous deferred-PLE upload and restores the blocking one; =1 forces on). Graph capture and the device-token path stay blocking. Developer knob, not a stable config key. Default flipped in 0.4.1.1 after identity gate (warm/CQ outputs byte-identical); no performance claim. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_PLE_WIDE_READERS` | on unless `0` | Qwen4 SSD-resident PLE rows: a row request with more than 1,024 scattered page reads (a prefill chunk's ~110K) splits them over up to 256 concurrent reader threads (one per 64 reads) instead of 16; smaller requests (decode, MTP verify, short prompts) keep 16. Bytes, order and the row-store cache are unchanged; only the read queue depth differs. Read when the model loads. `0` keeps 16 readers for every request. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS` | on for Qwen4 expert-parallel loads (`topology.ep` >= 2); `1` forces it on any load, `0` kills it | Qwen4 native MTP, MoE head (Flash-Next): allocates the batched head round's rows scratch at load (8 rows of every head operator buffer plus the few-row grouped MoE's grouping buffers, a few tens of MiB) and enables `Qwen4MtpGpu::forward_rows_deferred`, one head step for 2..=8 drafting lanes at once: embedding, both pre-FC norms and FC projections, the HC reads and writes, the MoE (one EP row gather, the head's gather parity advancing once per call on every rank) and the final HC read run once over the `n` rows; the indexed attention runs once per lane on that lane's row of its own head arena; the draft head ranks each row exactly as a one-row draft (each lane's own full-vocabulary hold). Per lane the result is the one-token step's (draft token, margin bits, 72-byte `draft_top1` slot, wide hidden, head KV state), proved per round by the lab probe (`HIPFIRE_LAB_MTP_ROUNDS_PROBE_DIR`, `lab` builds only: writes `<dir>/rank<r>.jsonl` comparing the batched round against `n` serial steps on scratch twins of the lanes' head states; `HIPFIRE_LAB_MTP_ROUNDS_PROBE_REPS` adds alternating timing repetitions, `HIPFIRE_LAB_MTP_ROUNDS_PROBE_MAX` caps the probed rounds). The EP lane draft loop (`ep_mesh_lanes.rs` `draft_round_device`) runs every round of 2..=8 drafting lanes as ONE head call (`mtp_lanes::enqueue_batched_draft`); a round that reads a distribution from a non-re-scoring head, and one lane, stay serial. Measured on symtrunk (oracle ABBA, forced batched K3, slowest rank's Draft phase per window): 2 lanes 5.65 -> 4.31 ms, 4 lanes 11.56 -> 7.75, 8 lanes 23.44 -> 13.88; step wall -6% / -10% at 4 / 8 lanes; lane == singleton PASS and 1363 probed lane-rounds per rank bit-identical to serial steps. Off (`=0`, or a load without expert parallelism unless `=1`) the scratch is not allocated and every lane runs its serial head steps. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_DRAFT_SPLIT` | on unless `0` (developer); the split also needs every rank's head to be lane-local ready (below), else off | Qwen4 native MTP lanes, MoE head, Flash-Next EP2 (`topology.ep` >= 2): each EP rank runs the head work of the lanes it owns instead of every lane. The root promotes a window's head lanes to owners only when the window carries two or more of them (`LanePlan.head_rank`: each lane takes the rank with the fewest owned lanes, ties to the lower rank, in plan order, so 8 lanes split 4/4; one-way, an owner never moves and ends with the lane); a window with one head lane keeps it replicated and runs today's path unchanged (C1 pays nothing). The owner does all of the lane's head work: the draft steps, the copy-confirmation probe, the round-0 owed-row catch-up and the prepare-time head appends and owed rows. Its head MoE reads every expert locally through the head's EP full table over the peer's compact expert tensors (no row gather, no cross-rank crossing, the head's gather parity untouched); the peer keeps only the lane's target side (no head ticket, append or owed row). Each rank returns exactly the proposals of the ops it answers in its per-round draft result (the owner for its lanes, the sampler owner for replicated ones) and the root merges them in op order; sampled draws stay on the root's staged RNG. No new kernels and no new allocation (a pointer table over existing expert tensors); the arithmetic and combine order are the same, only the device that runs a lane's head changes. Read once at the first mesh step, which logs `qwen4 EP draft split: on|off (<reason>)`; off when any rank's head has no lane-local MoE runtime (built at EP attach). `0` replicates every lane on every rank exactly as before (same ledger, same calls, same proposals). `lab` builds: `HIPFIRE_LAB_MTP_DRAFT_TRACE=<dir>` appends one JSON line per answered draft op to `<dir>/rank<r>.jsonl` (merge the ranks' files to compare a split run against `=0` op for op). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DRAFT_SHARDED_MOE` | on unless `0` (developer); acts only with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_DRAFT_SPLIT` active and the `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS` rows scratch, EP2 only | Qwen4 native MTP lanes, MoE head, Flash-Next EP2 (`topology.ep` = 2), on top of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_DRAFT_SPLIT`: a draft round of two to eight ops, every op a lane one of the two ranks owns, that is one batched head call (`draft_round_batchable` over the whole round) runs the head's routed MoE ONCE over all the round's rows on both ranks, instead of each rank running it for its own rows alone over the full expert table (the owned-lane head reads every expert, half of them over P2P from the peer's compact tensors). Each rank still runs the head's front, attention and HC read/write on its own rows only; it then copies its rows of the MLP HC read output (the exact tensor the MoE step reads) into the EP row-gather staging buffer at their global slots, both ranks all-gather those rows into the root's op order through the forward's existing bit-exact row-gather hook (publish, host barrier, copy, with the transport's tags, timeouts and poisoning; the staging buffer is the first of the two gather buffers that differs from the rank's last gather buffer, so the transport's no-same-buffer-twice rule holds), and the existing replicated EP head MoE call (compact tables, real row-gather hook, the head's gather parity advancing once per pass on every rank) runs over the global rows: router, grouped GEMMs and the slot-order top-10 combine are the replicated arm's at that row count. Each rank then keeps its own rows' FFN output for the MLP HC write, the final HC read and the draft head. A rank that owns no row of a round still crosses twice (activation, MoE) in lockstep and writes no head state. Rounds with a single op (C1, and late depths where one lane is left) and rounds with a replicated lane keep their unchanged X2 / replicated path; the decision is made on every rank from the root's round and plan alone (`ep_mesh_lanes::s4_round_shape`). No new kernel, entry or mode (existing launchers and copies; the pre- and post-MoE HC steps run as separate step lists, so the interpreter's cross-step fusions around the head MoE are not applied in these passes); the only allocation is one 8 KiB device owner-mask table per rank (`256 masks x 8 x i32`), made at head install. Bitwise the X2 / replicated head's output (lane-oracle draft ops: token, margin bits and top-1 slot hash equal against `=0` and against `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_DRAFT_SPLIT=0`). Read at head install and per round. `0` restores the X2 path byte for byte (no table, no S4 branch). `lab` builds: the `HIPFIRE_LAB_MTP_DRAFT_TRACE` line gets an `s4` field. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_TIER` | Q8F16 declaration | `mq6` declares the rank-2 trunk attention/GDN matrices at MQ6G256V2 for the quantizer; the loader admits both tiers from the file. |
