| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS_XBATCH` | on unless `0` | Only with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS=1`: the batched head round's draft head ranks the rows that share a layout (front-ordered, or the full vocabulary under a lane's hold) with one x-batched MQ2G256V2 GEMV over the ranking copy (`gemv_mq2g256v2_xbatch`, chunks of eight rows on the gfx1201 PeaceMaker path, four on hipcc; each weight row read once per chunk and each finite random row byte-identical to the one-row GEMV, proved by `mq2_xbatch_oracle`). NaN/Inf edges retain the incumbent x-batched path's exact bytes; preexisting singleton NaN-payload differences are reported separately. `0` ranks every row with the one-row launches (a bisection switch; finite arithmetic is unchanged). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_FEW_PM` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | At 7 and 8 source rows (`MOE_GU_FEW_PM_ROWS`: native-MTP verify of a C8 lane step; 2..=6 rows measured no faster and stay on hipcc) the few-row Qwen4 MoE gate/up on the drop-zero planar expert layout (`gemm_mq4g256v2p_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r2_x4`, M 1280 x K 2560, sealed top-10) runs the embedded PeaceMaker module `kernels/qwen4_moe_gu_few_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (`..._o4_r2_x4_pm_r4_pad16_b128_altch`, WideNet F07: four slots per workgroup, 16-byte LDS pad, b128 activation loads, the hipcc kernel's per-output reduction order) instead of the hipcc kernel: same ABI and output bytes, dead slots included (`moe_gu_few_pm_oracle`). Taken only at those row counts and when the image matches its pinned SHA-256; any other shape, layout or arch keeps the hipcc kernel. Tested byte-exact, not yet proven anchor-relative. `0` keeps the hipcc kernel. Read once. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_C8_PM` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | The `_nodz` gfx1201 `g32` Qwen4 MoE gate/up (9 and more source rows; M 1280 x K 2560, drop-zero planar experts, sealed top-10, at most 1024 16-slot tiles; the default `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DEAD_STORES` not `1`, or `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE32_GU=2`) runs the embedded persistent PeaceMaker module `kernels/qwen4_moe_gu_c8_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (`gemm_mq4g256v2p_moe_grouped_top10_g32_r8_nodz_pm_persist` above 16 rows, `..._r4_nodz_pm_persist` for 9..=16) instead of the hipcc `g32_{r8,r4}_nodz` entry: a grid of `n_blocks` workgroups that walk only the live 16-slot tiles (the hipcc grid covers the worst-case `rows x 10` tiles), and a wave whose rows have only zero scales (the EP zero dummy expert) stores +0 without reading weights. Live slots bit-identical for finite X, dead slots unwritten (`moe_gu_c8_pm_oracle`). Taken only when the image matches its pinned SHA-256; `0` restores the hipcc entry. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_C8_PM_BLOCKS` | unset: `128` above 16 rows, `160` at 9..=16 (the resident capacity of each entry); clamped to 1..=4096 | Persistent workgroup count (grid `[n_blocks, 1, 1]`) of both `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GU_C8_PM` entries. Read once; a tuning knob, the output bytes do not depend on it. More workgroups than fit resident run as a mostly idle second wave. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_C8_PM` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | The grouped A8 routed down at 33+ token rows (`qwen4_moe_down_a8_grouped`, M 2560 x K 640, drop-zero planar experts, nodz path i.e. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DEAD_STORES` not `1`, at most 1024 16-slot tiles) runs the embedded persistent PeaceMaker module `kernels/qwen4_moe_down_c8_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (`qwen4_moe_down_a8_grouped_planar_nodz_pm_persist_gfx1201`) instead of the hipcc `qwen4_moe_down_a8_grouped_planar_nodz_gfx1201` entry, after the same A8 sidecar producer: live tiles only, zero-scale waves skip their dot products. Live slots bit-identical, dead slots unwritten (`moe_down_c8_pm_oracle`). 9..=32 rows stay on hipcc. Taken only when the image matches its pinned SHA-256; `0` restores the hipcc entry. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_C8_PM_BLOCKS` | `256`, clamped to 1..=4096 | Persistent workgroup count (grid `[n_blocks, 1, 1]`, block 128) of the `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_C8_PM` grouped down module. Read once; a tuning knob, the output bytes do not depend on it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_HEAD_PM` | exact gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere | The MTP draft head's MQ2G256V2 ranking GEMV, both the x-batched one of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS_XBATCH` (every chunk of 1..8 rows) and the one-row ranking of a lone drafting row (C1), runs the embedded PeaceMaker module `kernels/gemv_mq2_xbatch_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (`gemv_mq2g256v2_xbatch_pm`, each column retains the hipcc x-batched kernel's ordered reduction) instead of the hipcc `gemv_mq2g256v2_xbatch`: same ABI and incumbent output bytes, including NaN/Inf edges (`mq2_xbatch_oracle`; finite random inputs also match per-column singleton GEMV, while preexisting singleton NaN-payload differences are tallied separately). Taken only where the module covers the shape (exact gfx1201, `K % 256 == 0`, weight and eight-column buffer bytes within 32-bit offsets) and the image matches its pinned SHA-256; anything else keeps the hipcc kernel in chunks of four. Tested byte-exact, not yet proven anchor-relative. `0` keeps the hipcc kernel. Read once. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_PROJ_BATCH` | on unless `0` (exact gfx1201 only) | Only with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS` on, exact gfx1201, every head QSA projection Q8_0: a batched head round of `n` lanes runs the indexed attention's input projections (indexer, q+gate, K, V) ONCE over the `n` rows and its output projection ONCE over the `n` rows (`gemv_q8_0_rows_exact`, each row bitwise the rows == 1 `gemv_q8_0`), around each lane's own attention core (its own position, K/V cache, pool and selection, one row), instead of one whole indexed-attention op (five singleton GEMVs) per lane. `0` restores the per-lane whole ops exactly (byte-identical either way). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_FC_ONE_LAUNCH` | on unless `0` (exact gfx1201 only) | Only with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DRAFT_ROUNDS` on and a BF16 `fc_hidden`: a batched head round of `n` lanes projects all `n * hc_count` branch rows of the hidden FC in ONE launch (`ProjectLadder::TrunkRowExact` → `Gpu::gemv_bf16_xf32_x4_rows_exact`, the all-rows arm at 9..=64 rows, never lane WMMA) instead of one launch per 8-row chunk (4 launches at 8 lanes). Byte-identical: every projected row is the one-row step's (`bf16_allrows_oracle --reread`). `0` restores the 8-row chunks. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_TOKEN_RING` | on; `0` off (developer, read when the head scratch is built) | The MTP head step's token-id upload (`head_prologue`, and the batched round's `forward_rows_deferred`) stages through a pinned, event-fenced 16-slot ring (`LaneTokenRing`) and is enqueued on the active stream without a host wait, instead of `Gpu::upload_stream_ordered`'s H2D + `hipStreamSynchronize` (4.5 syncs per rank per served EP2 code C1 step, each draining the compute queue mid-draft; 410 us mean at C8). Same bytes reach `token_ids`. Kept on the old path while a graph is captured or the replay recorder records, and when `HIPFIRE_STREAM_ORDERED_COPIES=0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_TIER` | recipe tier | `source` keeps the rank-2 MTP matrices at BF16 (quantizer and loader comparison knob). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_REQUANT` | unset | Load-time precision experiment: `pat=fmt;...` requantizes resident rank-2 weights whose name contains `pat` to `mq2`..`mq6` or `q8`, or (`qt44:DIR`) replaces each with the pre-encoded QT44 bytes of `DIR/<tensor name>.bin` where that file exists (an offline GPTQ solve; the file must hold exactly the matrix). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS` | unset: every routed expert in VRAM where it fits, else `auto` | Discrete-GPU capacity knob. Unset on a discrete GPU keeps every routed expert in VRAM only when all of them, the non-expert weights and the `auto` reserve below fit in free VRAM, and resolves to `auto` otherwise (a 32 GB card); unified memory (Strix Halo) stays fully resident. An explicit value always wins. The load logs the choice on one `qwen4 expert placement` line. Set or unset, a daemon started for a Qwen4 model on discrete GPUs gets the two host-memory switches below (`HSA_USERPTR_FOR_PAGED_MEM`, `GPU_PINNED_MIN_XFER_SIZE`), which are decided before the HIP runtime loads; a daemon started for another model (serve switching models) gets them only with the variable set, and its Qwen4 load warns without them. `N` keeps the routed experts of trunk layers `0..N` in VRAM; later layers' and the MTP layer's routed experts are fulfilled into pinned, device-mapped host RAM and read over PCIe (zero-copy) by the unchanged sealed MoE kernels. `auto` picks the largest `N` that leaves a computed reserve of VRAM beyond the non-expert weights. The reserve is the larger of two formulas. The historical floor is 6.5 GiB (measured without MTP at `max_seq` 32768 with legacy QSA state, a full-context prefill included; N=16 on a 32 GB R9700), plus the trunk QSA arenas' committed bytes past those 32768 legacy tokens, plus, when native MTP stays attached (the default on exact gfx1201, `--spec mtp` elsewhere), the MTP head's committed device bytes (its QSA state and the MQ2 draft copy of the language head) and the larger of the draft copy's F32 requant scratch (2048 rows × hidden × 4 B, 20 MiB on Flash-Next, returned to the device at attach) and what the first speculative request allocates (the verify hidden rows and the `K+1`-row GDN capture rings in the state's GDN format). On exact gfx1201 single-device discrete loads the reserve is the census alone, which then carries a measured `HIP runtime` term (1,408 MiB: device memory the HIP runtime holds beyond every counted allocation, measured on an R9700 at 32K-token requests up to 8192-row chunks), and the F16 shadows are charged only at prefill chunks of at least 512 rows (smaller chunks never create them); everywhere else the larger of floor and census applies as before. The census is bottom-up for the first full-context request and is logged term by term on the `qwen4 auto expert placement` line: the trunk QSA context at the admitted `max_seq` as the ACTUAL backend maps it (a VMM arena maps ahead to `max_seq`, rounded to the driver granularity; legacy commits all of `max_seq`), the rest of the request state (GDN recurrent and conv with the snapshot mirror, QSA fixed arenas, PLE conv, hyper feedback), the chunk forward at the largest requested prefill chunk, the lazy F16 weight shadows of the BF16 hyper-connection down mixes, router, shared-expert gate/up/down and PLE key/value projections the long-prefill F16 WMMA arms read (exact gfx1201, queried from the manifest, so a quantized trunk charges nothing), the kernels' pool workspaces (itemised by the engine), the QSA split selector's score/filter scratch, native MTP and the QSA gather workspace. Whole-layer placement (`auto`, and the unset check that decides fully resident, on a discrete card) then keeps a 512 MiB safety margin free beyond whichever formula wins and, on a slab-arena load whose long-prefill expert staging is on (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`, exact gfx1201 by default; not expert-parallel), the two dedicated stage buffers allocated at load, `2 × (largest layer payload + 4096 B)` (it scales with the artifact: twice 1,060 MiB plus the pad, 2120 MiB, for a 1,060 MiB-per-layer artifact), because they are allocated even when every prompt is shorter than the staging threshold. The expert hot set keeps its own margin and tier-plan stage accounting instead. QSA context arenas of the historical floor are charged as committed, never by their virtual extent: legacy state commits all of `max_seq`. A discrete card's floor charges legacy storage of `max_seq` whichever `kv_backend` is selected (the census charges the actual backend's full map); unified memory's floor with VMM state (`kv_backend` `vmm`) charges only the pages the first prefill chunk maps. On the R9700 at `max_seq` 16384 the attached head costs one expert layer (layers `0..15` in VRAM without it, `0..14` with it). The load logs the reserve on a `qwen4 auto expert placement` line, and refuses before allocating when the free VRAM cannot hold the non-expert weights plus the reserve, margin and stage buffers even at N=0 (for example beside another process's model). `0` puts every routed expert (~65 GB) in host RAM. The load refuses before allocating when `MemAvailable` minus the CMA region (the larger of `CmaFree` and the CMA pageblocks: `CmaTotal` or `/proc/zoneinfo` `cma`; only movable allocations can use them, never the pinned host-mapped bytes), plus (with GTT-backed host memory, `HSA_USERPTR_FOR_PAGED_MEM=0`) an estimate of the freed GTT pages in TTM's page pool, is below the pinned bytes plus 4 GiB. The pool is readable only by root, so the estimate is the host memory outside every `/proc/meminfo` counter, minus the GTT amdgpu devices hold and 4 GiB for other drivers, capped at TTM's `page_pool_size`; the load log prints both numbers. `echo 2 \| sudo tee /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` empties the pool. Refused on UMA. With any expert host-mapped, native MTP stays on under `auto` on exact gfx1201 (greedy MTP text equals AR; `--spec off` / `speculation.mtp = "off"` keeps AR) and is opt-in elsewhere (`--spec mtp` / `speculation.mtp = "on"`; `auto` keeps AR). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` | unset: on for exact gfx1201 when `<model>.expert-hotset` exists, else off | Developer switch, read once per load: the Lever1 expert hotset, a decode-ranked per-expert VRAM placement of a Qwen4 aggregate-MQ4 base that replaces whole-layer expert placement on a discrete gfx1201 card. **Unset:** the loader looks for `<model file name>.expert-hotset` beside the model (`hipfire_config::expert_hotset_sidecar_path`) and applies it when it exists and applies; no sidecar means today's whole-layer placement, silently. **`0`:** off (kill switch): the sidecar is not looked at and whole-layer placement runs. **An absolute path:** that file is the sidecar, instead of the sibling one. **A relative or empty value:** a policy error, the only refusal this variable makes. The sidecar is JSON (`kind` `hipfire.qwen4.expert_hotset`, `version` 1). It ranks every routed trunk expert by decode routing count (`rank`: each global id `layer * experts_per_layer + expert` exactly once, `counts` descending, ties by global id ascending; `counts` indexed by global id) and carries `layers`, `experts_per_layer`, `top_k`, `capture_tokens`, `counts_sha256`, `format_map` and `format_map_sha256` (full lowercase 64-hex digests). It is bound to its base by `base_size` (`u64`, the base file's length in bytes) and `base_index_sha256` (lowercase hex sha256 of the base's HFQ header and index), both required; there is no whole-artifact hash. The parser (`expert_hotset::HotsetSidecar`) validates kind, version, dimensions, hashes, counts length and rank ordering and refuses unknown keys. **Anything that does not apply keeps whole-layer placement and never fails the load:** an absent, unreadable or malformed sidecar, a sidecar whose `base_size` or `base_index_sha256` does not match the loaded base or whose dimensions do not match the model, another architecture than gfx1201, unified memory, expert parallelism, or an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS`; the load logs why on one line. The byte budget is derived at load, never read from the file: free VRAM less the non-expert weights, the `auto` reserve, the frozen expert tables and a 512 MiB margin. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_POLICY` | unset: `ranked` | Developer-only, default-off, read once per load beside `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET`: the placement policy of the Qwen4 expert hot set on exact gfx1201. **Unset or `ranked`:** the current policy, unchanged: the ranked per-expert hot set of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` places the highest-ranked experts in VRAM. **`whole-first`:** conservative and decode-safe; the load runs the same whole-layer placement, materialization and route as `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=0` (the same baseline whole layers, no partial or tiered layers, no mixed experts, hot experts = 0). While it is active the sidecar is not consulted at all, even when `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` names an explicit path, and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=0` changes nothing. It keeps the existing long-prefill expert stages of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`: the aggregate stage still borrows resident donor layers and restores them from pinned host copies, and the slab stage still uses the baseline dedicated staging, charged to the same VRAM budget (so dedicated staging displaces baseline whole layers, as in the baseline). The load logs one `qwen4 expert hot set: policy=whole-first, whole layers 0..N (N of M MoE layers), hot experts 0, …` line with the stage and borrowed MiB. **Any other value** is a load error naming the variable. Where a hot set could not apply anyway (pipeline-parallel band, expert parallelism, an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS`, unified memory) `whole-first` is ignored on gfx1201 with one log line `qwen4 expert hot set: policy=whole-first not applied (<reason>); placement unchanged`, and there is no new pipeline-parallel refusal; gfx1100 ignores it silently and keeps its existing placement. It is not a speed feature: it can lose the ranked hot set's long-prefill gain, and decode and prefill may not both fit in the available memory; no new performance claim is made. See `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` above and `docs/CONFIG.md` (Qwen4 expert hotset sidecar). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOT_BUDGET_MIB` | unset: existing automatic hotset budget (developer) | Read once per Qwen4 load from the process configuration snapshot. Positive unsigned decimal MiB (`1 MiB = 1048576 bytes`), checked for `u64` byte overflow; empty, zero, malformed and overflowing values fail the load. Pins the **ranked device-expert byte ceiling**, exactly like lab `qwen4_cpu_split_parity --hot-budget-mib N`, independent of `mtp_mode`; native MTP and other reserves still determine whether that same pin fits. Long-prefill staging is charged alongside the pin, never subtracted from it. The requested ceiling must fit the automatic available hot-expert budget after all reserves, both at planning and at the final free-VRAM re-query; otherwise load fails, never clamps or falls back. An explicit pin also requires an eligible, valid, base-bound ranked sidecar (use `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=/absolute/model.expert-hotset` to pin its identity); any refusal that would normally keep whole-layer placement instead fails the load, including an active `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_POLICY=whole-first`, which consults no sidecar. The load logs the fixed budget and the existing sidecar SHA-256 / tier-plan digest. For AR/MTP identity gates, set the same `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOT_BUDGET_MIB=N` and absolute sidecar path in both arms, leave `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS` unset, and require identical sidecar SHA-256 and tier-plan digest before comparing outputs. Unset leaves all existing defaults and fallback behavior unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_GFX1100` | unset = ON on exact gfx1100; `0` turns the whole gfx1100 tier package off | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_expert_hotset_gfx1100`, read once per load from the process snapshot (`0` off, `1` or unset on). The ONE switch of the gfx1100 (7900 XTX) Flash-Next single-card tier package, active only on exact `gfx1100` with a `<model>.expert-hotset` sidecar beside an aggregate MQ4-XTS base; it changes nothing on gfx1201, gfx1151 or any other arch. On: (1) the default sibling sidecar applies (before, only gfx1201); (2) S1 slow-link prefill runs over the tier plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_S1_TIER`); (3) a hot set whose load does not take S1 is sized from the census reserve (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_CENSUS_RESERVE`); (4) the D2 tier splits use event input readiness (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_READY`); (5) mixed hot/cold tier layers take the mapped staging + CP-wait join (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_D2_MIXED_CPWAIT`). `0` returns every member to its previous default (no default sidecar on gfx1100, S1 declines a hot set, the incumbent reserve, sync readiness, the copy transport); an explicit absolute `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` path still applies on any arch. Each member keeps its own variable, which wins over the package for that member in either direction (`=1` forces it on, `=0` is its own kill). Measured on hipx (7900 XTX, fresh processes, n=4) against the whole-layer placement: native MTP code decode 59.3 vs 53.7 tok/s (+10.4%), AR prose 37.6 vs 36.4, pp16384 2,107 vs 1,875 tok/s, pp8192 1,083 vs 1,024, pp1105 847 vs 837, TTFT 2.15 vs 2.34 s. The paired decode KLD gate of the tier D2 split against the PCIe route is pending. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_S1_TIER` | unset = ON on exact gfx1100 (the gfx1100 tier package, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_GFX1100`), off elsewhere; `0` is the kill | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_s1_tier`, read once per load from the process snapshot (`1` forces on, `0` off): S1 slow-link prefill over a load whose ranked expert hot set (tier plan) applies. Off (the default on every arch but gfx1100, or `=0`), a hot set declines S1 exactly as before (`unsupported mode: ranked expert hot set`). On: every trunk layer is an S1 host layer. The panel ring fills each 32-expert group's page with the layer's COLD experts over the link (as for a whole host layer) and its HOT experts by a device-to-device copy from the tier's device owner (VRAM bandwidth; no host shadow, no extra link bytes), so the kernels, pointer tables, ordered combine and census terms are a whole host layer's; no whole layer stays resident (`resident_layers = 0`). The hot-set budget then follows the S1 reserve instead of the incumbent floor: `free - 1 GiB hold (a grown window) - non-expert - (base census at the 768-row tile + S1 census + granule slack) - tier tables - 512 MiB margin`. The window grows to the covering window of a context within 64 tiles only while it keeps 3/4 of the floor window's hot budget. The SP modes stay off over tiers. If no hot set applies after all, the load keeps the whole layers the same quote fits. Needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET` (or `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_GFX1100=1` with the sibling sidecar) and a narrow link; the S1 selector (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SLOWLINK_PREFILL`, default auto) still decides per request. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET_CENSUS_RESERVE` | unset = ON on exact gfx1100 (the gfx1100 tier package), inert elsewhere; `0` is the kill | Developer variable `developer.qwen4_expert_hotset_census_reserve`, read once per load from the process snapshot (`0` off, `1` on): on exact gfx1100 only, the hot-expert payload is sized from the bottom-up reserve census plus the 1 GiB HIP runtime budget the gfx1100 S1 admission already charges (`S1_RUNTIME_OVERHEAD_BYTES`), instead of `max(historical floor, census)`. The historical floor is an R9700 measurement that dominates the census on a 24 GB XTX (a 262,144-token load: 15,806 MiB floor, 14,452 MiB census). It can only lower the reserve (`min` with the default), never raise it; the 512 MiB safety margin, the tier tables and the long-prefill stage buffers are charged exactly as before; inert on every other arch and for every other reserve consumer. The load log's budget line names the replaced reserve. Prove it with the measured VRAM peak (sysfs `mem_info_vram_used`) before relying on it. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TIER_COUNTERS` | unset (off) | Developer-only diagnostic, default off, read once per D2 split executor construction (`1` enables). Counts the DISTINCT routed experts of every D2 split layer call by tier (device-resident `hot`, host-resident read by the CPU arm `cold_cpu`, host-resident read over PCIe `cold_pcie`) in `CpuSplitExecutor::prepare`, where the route ids are already on the host, so it adds no transfer, synchronization or kernel and, off, one `Option` check. Exists only while D2 is armed: a tier layer on the PCIe route has no host copy of its ids and is not counted. Aggregated per request: printed to stderr at the start of the next cold prefill (`Qwen4Bundle::reset`) and for the last request when the first split executor is freed (unload): `[tier-counters] steps=N rows=N hot=N cold=N hit=P% cold_cpu=N cold_pcie=N link_bytes_per_step=B cpu_bytes_per_step=B`, then a `per_step` line and, when MTP head calls ran, a `head` line. A step starts where a trunk layer label does not rise above the previous trunk call's; head-layer calls (label past the trunk) are counted apart. `link_bytes_per_step` / `cpu_bytes_per_step` are cold PCIe / CPU experts times the uniform per-expert bytes (0 for a codec-mixed slab arena). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP` | unset | Developer kill switch for Qwen4 expert parallelism: `0` forces `topology.ep = 1` (single device) whatever the config says; any other value leaves `topology.ep` in charge. `topology.ep` (`HIPFIRE_TOPOLOGY_EP`, default 1, allowed `1..=8`) shards the routed experts `e % N` (uneven per-rank counts when N does not divide 512, e.g. 86/86/85/85/85/85 for N = 6) across the first N visible cards, which must be one exact arch, gfx1100 or gfx1201 (a mixed set is refused), with peer access between every pair; every card runs the unchanged single-device program. Under the developer-only `HIPFIRE_EMULATE_GPUS` the ranks alias physical devices (rank `r` on device `r % count`, see the logical GPU emulation section below). EP > 1 admits native MTP only when requested explicitly (`--spec mtp` / `speculation.mtp = "on"`; `auto` stays AR): the MTP drafter is replicated on every card, rank 0 owns the window chooser, and `HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED_MODE=naive` is refused (its draws come from the process-wide AR sampler). EP > 1 still refuses DFlash/DSpark/n-gram/DDTree, adaptive KV, drafters, CASK, PFlash, lane batching and the retained Redline default. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET` | unset: the shipped default (on: `hipfire_config::memory::EP_EXPERT_HOTSET_DEFAULT_ON` is `true`, an EP2 gfx1201 Flash-Next load discovers `<model>.ep.expert-hotset` next to the model, which `hipfire pull` fetches; no sidecar: today's whole-layer placement); empty / `0`: off (kill switch) | Developer-only, read once per load. An explicit path to an `.expert-hotset` v1 sidecar always applies, whatever the gate. The shipped sidecar is `<base>.ep.expert-hotset` (distinct from the single-card `<base>.expert-hotset`, which EP never applies), sha-pinned for the split Flash-Next base in the client (`EP_EXPERT_HOTSET_PINS`; `hipfire pull` fetches it only with the gate on). A discovered sidecar that cannot be used (another base, `topology.ep` other than 2, another GPU architecture, a conflicting lever) is skipped with one `qwen4 EP expert hot set: ... present, not applied (...)` line and the load keeps whole-layer placement, never a failure; `0` is the explicit off switch. An explicit path: per-expert VRAM residency under Flash-Next expert parallelism (`topology.ep` >= 2) instead of whole layers. The sidecar is bound to the artifact (a refusal is a load error, never a fallback); each rank walks the ranking from the hottest unit, skipping the other ranks' experts (`e % ranks`), keeping the ranked prefix that fits its budget (free VRAM - trunk - the ordinary reserve, plus the lane store when reserved, minus the long-prefill stage buffers below), charging two 256-byte arena pads per touched layer. A layer with some owned experts resident and some host is MIXED: its two aggregates are replaced by one device arena and one pinned host arena per rank; fully resident and fully host layers keep today's whole-layer path. Owners: a layer that is host on some rank but not on all (a mixed layer, or a host layer beside a mixed or resident peer) runs the balanced PINNED owner assignment (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_PINNED_OWNERS`, default on, next row): resident experts stay with their holder `e % ranks`, host experts are dealt freely across the ranks; with that switch `=0`, or where balanced ownership is not admitted (`HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS=0`, not gfx1201, more than 4 ranks), those layers run static `e % ranks` owners; layers held wholly in host RAM on every rank stay balanced (unpinned) exactly as today. Only the trunk layers are planned; the MTP head's experts follow `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_EXPERTS_RESIDENT` (default on: each rank's owned slice is VRAM-resident and its bytes are charged to every rank's budget before the ranking walk, so the trunk's static core shrinks by exactly them; `=0`: host-mapped and pinned on every rank as before). A load that already fits fully resident ignores the plan with one log line. Prints one `qwen4 EP expert tier rank r/n` receipt per rank, ending in `stage C MiB x 2 charged` (or `stage none`). The EP long-prefill expert stage (G2, `HIPFIRE_EP_EXPERT_STAGE`, gfx1201, owner-routed prefill) needs no `=0` any more: a plan that is not a whole-layer split stages through two dedicated VRAM stage buffers (one per layer parity, nothing borrowed, nothing restored, no pinned mirrors) instead of donor layers; a layer with host experts copies its whole pinned host region (a mixed layer's host arena, a host layer's two aggregates) into its parity's buffer and its compact tables rebase only the entries inside that region, so resident experts keep reading their VRAM arena. The buffers are fresh VRAM, so every rank's budget is reduced by `2 * (C + 4 KiB)` plus the stage pointer tables before the ranking walk, `C` the largest host region of the final plan (a fixed point, at most 8 placements, else the largest whole-layer region); stage off (`HIPFIRE_EP_EXPERT_STAGE=0`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE=0`, `HIPFIRE_EP_OWNER_ROUTED_PREFILL=0`, any arch but gfx1201) charges nothing and plans exactly as without it. Refused (clear load error): a single-device or pipeline-parallel load, an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET`, `HIPFIRE_EP_CHUNK_AUTOFIT=1` and `HIPFIRE_EP_LAB_RESIDENT_LAYERS`; `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_DECOUPLE` is treated as `0` with a note unless `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE=1` plans the units under the decoupled reserve. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_GATHER_DEVICE_WAIT` | on; `0` off (developer): exact gfx1201, read once per EP attach | Flash-Next EP row gather (`EpRowGather`, the MTP head's routed-expert row exchange): a rank's gather waits each peer's producer event on its own stream (`hipStreamWaitEvent`) instead of the host (`hipEventSynchronize`), so the host enqueues the gather copy and the following launches without a round trip per exchange. The publish / host barrier / tag check and the copies are unchanged, in the same stream order; bytes identical. `0` restores the host waits exactly. | developer |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_BUDGET_DELTA_MIB` | unset / `0`: no change (developer, lab) | Signed integer MiB added to (negative: taken from) every rank's per-expert unit budget before the ranking walk of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET`, to sweep the resident-unit count on one binary (about 2.49 MiB per unit). Lab only: a positive delta can plan more experts than the free VRAM leaves (the rank fit check uses the same delta); a non-integer value is a load error. The load prints `qwen4 EP expert tier (lab): every rank's unit budget +N MiB`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_LANE_GROWTH` | unset / not `1`: off (opt-in, developer) | Expert-parallel (EP) per-expert unit plans (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET`, default on with the sidecar) with a lane reservation (`serve` with continuous batching, VMM KV): the lane store is charged only its first prefill chunk of QSA context, and a unit plan fills every byte the budget leaves, so 8 lanes whose contexts grow on demand to `max_seq` (8 x ~16k-token prompts at `max_seq` 16384) can run a rank out of VRAM (`hipMemCreate` OOM in the QSA context map). By default such a burst is refused cleanly: every affected request answers HTTP 503 `overload` (retryable, `rolled_back=true`) and the rank pool stays usable. `1` charges the unit budget the store's device bytes with the context committed through `max_seq` minus the first-chunk figure already charged (about 1.5 GiB per rank at 8 lanes x 16384: 6405 -> 5766 resident units per rank on 2xR9700; `qwen4 EP expert tier: lane context growth charged to the unit budget: N MiB/rank` in the load log), so the burst is served. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE` | unset / not `1`: off (opt-in; the shipped default plans the units under the ordinary reserve) (developer) | With a per-expert unit plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET` or the discovered `<base>.ep.expert-hotset`), the decoupled EP reserve stays instead of being forced off: the unit budget is `free - trunk - (decoupled reserve + lane store)` and the reserve's whole-layer scratch donors are the hottest ranked layers, forced wholly resident on every rank (the reserve plan is re-made over exactly those donor layers, `plan_ep_reserve_with_donors`). The unit stage reads the donors' pinned mirrors during a borrowed prompt epoch. The F16 and shared-expert INT8 weight shadows stay dedicated VRAM in the census (the reserve's historical floor dominates it, so hosting them frees no unit; see `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE_SHADOWS`). A load the donor plan cannot serve (no decoupled reserve plan, unit stage off, donors or shadows do not pack, host budget) falls back to the ordinary-reserve unit plan with a note, or fails when `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_DECOUPLE=1`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE_SHADOWS` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab) | Under `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE=1`, `1` hosts the F16 and shared-expert INT8 weight shadows (about 1.55 GiB per rank) in the donor segments beside the scratch roots instead of keeping them dedicated VRAM: the census drops its `f16_shadows` and `shared_i8_shadows` terms, more donor layers (9 instead of 7 on Flash-Next EP2) are forced wholly resident, the shadows are rebuilt at every borrowed epoch and the resident bank serves calls of at most 511 rows (`plan_ep_reserve_with_donors_and_shadows`). Measured on 2xR9700 EP2 mq4-xts: the reserve's historical floor (4,597 MiB) dominates the census, so no extra unit is freed (8,740 per rank either way) while pp16384 prefill takes about 7% longer; default off. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_STAGE_BORROW` | unset / not `1`: off (developer) | Under `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE=1`, `1` makes the unit stage (the long-prefill copy of each host-bearing layer's pinned host region into VRAM) borrow its two parity buffers from two extra whole-resident layers, the first two trunk layers (0 and 1), exactly as the default expert stage lends two resident layers: each lent layer's gate/up and down tensors are overwritten by staged copies after that layer's own MoE call and restored from pinned host copies at epoch end (about 1.25 GiB pinned per rank). The dedicated stage buffers (2 x 637 MiB per rank on Flash-Next) are no longer charged, so the unit budget grows by about 476 units per rank, and a prompt tile stages the same host bytes as the release default (prefill parity). The scratch donor layers never include the lent layers. A plan or arch that cannot lend them (stage off, a layer that is not whole-resident, host budget) falls back to the dedicated stage with a note. Decode is unaffected while no prompt epoch is open. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_DONORS` | unset / not `tail`: the hottest ranked layers (developer, lab) | Under `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_RESERVE=1`, `tail` makes the decoupled reserve's whole-layer scratch donors the last whole layers of the model (a contiguous late suffix, like the release default's donors) instead of the hottest ranked ones. Lab A/B knob for the prefill position hypothesis; costs decode hits (the donors are cold layers forced whole). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_HC8_PLAN_CHARGE` | on; exact `0` disables (developer) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism (`topology.ep` >= 2) with the native MTP head attached and an applied `<model>.hc8` sidecar (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC8`, exact gfx1201): the plan and every rank's fit census charge the replicated HC mixer tensors at their E4M3 size instead of the manifest's BF16 size. The census subtracts `base - payload` per sidecar tensor (`hipfire_arch_qwen4::expert_residency::ep_hc8_plan_saving`, in the one `qwen4_placement_inputs` every EP budget reads, so the unit ranking walk, the whole-layer split and each rank's fit check agree byte for byte; the plan resolves the sidecar silently with `peek_hc8_sidecar`, each rank with its own resolve). On the fixture each rank applies 200 tensors, 1,310,720,000 -> 659,584,000 B: 621 MiB per rank that used to be charged but never allocated, which the placement now spends on resident experts. Only tensors the runtime would actually replace and the census actually charged count (one device-resident, non-tied BF16 `[m, k]` manifest entry of that name whose census bytes equal the sidecar's bound BF16 bytes, cut by nothing in the trunk shard's census); anything else saves 0, so the sum never exceeds what the apply frees and is NOT the upper bound `hc8_sidecar_saved_bytes` (which also counts tensors a head-less load or a pipeline band leaves out). Zero for a single device, a pipeline band, a load whose native MTP head does not attach, no sidecar or a refused one (today's BF16 charge). After the forward attaches, each rank compares the bytes the sidecar actually replaced with the planned saving and prints one `qwen4 EP rank r: WARNING: the plan charged -X B ... but only -Y B ... was applied` line when fewer were applied (a refused or partly skipped sidecar); the load continues, the placement's 512 MiB margin covers a few tensors. The plan prints `qwen4 EP plan: the hc8 sidecar's HC mixers are charged at their E4M3 size: -N MiB per rank` and the per-rank fit line's `trunk` term names `-N MiB hc8 sidecar applied`. `0` restores the BF16 charge exactly. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_EXPERTS_RESIDENT` | on; exact `0` disables (developer) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism (`topology.ep` >= 2) with the native MTP head attached (`--spec mtp`): each rank keeps ITS OWNED slice of the MTP head's routed experts (experts `e % ranks == rank`, 256 of 512 on EP2; the head's gate/up and down stacks are expert-sharded like every trunk layer's, so the ordinary resident upload carries only the owned experts, drop-zero planar like the trunk: 256 x 2,534,400 B = 618.75 MiB per rank) in VRAM instead of the pinned host copy the decode step read over PCIe (rocprof: 0.4 / 0.5 / 2.1 / 4.4 ms per decode step at about 54 GB/s for story C1 / code C1 / C4 / C8). The bytes are charged to every rank's non-expert budget (`hipfire_arch_qwen4::expert_residency::ep_mtp_resident_decision`, added in the one census `qwen4_placement_inputs` every budget reads) BEFORE the per-expert unit ranking walk and the whole-layer split, so the trunk's static core shrinks by exactly those bytes and nothing over-commits VRAM. The MTP head's MoE already runs the EP owner exchange, so each rank computes only its owned experts; kernels, pointer tables and the planar layout are unchanged. The load prints `qwen4 EP expert tier: the MTP head's experts: N owned units VRAM-resident on every rank (M MiB, charged before the ranking walk)` (or `... stay host-mapped (today's placement, pinned on every rank; <reason>)`), a per-rank `MTP head experts: N owned units` line and the fit line's `trunk ... (incl. M MiB MTP head experts ...)`. `0` restores today's placement exactly: the head's experts host-mapped and pinned on every rank, nothing charged. Not applied (today's placement) when no native MTP head attaches on the load (an AR load), on a single device, or when the manifest declares no head experts (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_SHARD` drops the head). A load that already fits fully resident keeps the head resident as before. The single-card counterpart is `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_HEAD_RESIDENT`. An EP head never runs the head's D2 CPU split (`HeadD2Off::Ep`), resident or not. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_CENSUS` | on; exact `0` disables (developer; read once per load) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism (`topology.ep` >= 2) reserve census (`hipfire_arch_qwen4::expert_residency::placement_inputs`, the one reserve every EP budget reads: the unit ranking walk, the whole-layer split and each rank's fit check). On: (1) the EP2 per-rank HIP runtime term replaces the historical single-device floor on an exact gfx1201 discrete two-rank load: `QWEN4_EP2_RUNTIME_OVERHEAD_GFX1201_BYTES` = 1,088 MiB, measured per rank on 2xR9700 EP2 (symtrunk + hc8, `serve --spec mtp --max-seq 16384`, 8 lanes) as sysfs `mem_info_vram_used` minus every live allocation `rocprofv3 --memory-allocation-trace` reports: 1,080 / 1,084 MiB after one 10,649-token prompt (fabric j8320, receipt `release-0.4.5/expertplace/runs/j3`) and 1,029 / 1,033 MiB after four parallel 10.6-12.4k-token prompts (fabric j8353, `runs/t2`), the larger rounded up to 64 MiB and INCLUDING the 57 MiB idle baseline because an EP plan budgets against the device TOTAL (`qwen4_ep_free_vram`), so the census alone is the reserve (`historical` is `None`, the 5,490 MiB floor retired); three or more ranks and other arches were not measured and keep the floor; (2) on a trunk-shard rank (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRUNK_SHARD`) the census sizes what the rank allocates, not the global config: the GDN state (`state` 64 -> 32 MiB, 24 of 48 value heads), the native MTP GDN capture ring (-41.9 MiB) and the shared-expert INT8 and F16 shadows at the busiest rank's slice (384 of 640 columns: INT8 239.06 -> 143.44 MiB, F16 450 -> 270 MiB; rank 1's 256 columns are smaller); (3) after the shard, `apply_trunk_shard` returns the pooled pre-shard shared-expert copies (the full 3.125 MiB copies `replace_owned` freed into the device pool, which hands bytes back to HIP only on a drain; 147 per rank, about 309 MiB idle) with `Gpu::drain_pool` (free-list buffers only, never a live tensor), logged as `returned N MiB of pooled pre-shard copies to HIP`. The shared-expert F16 shadows stay charged (at the local width) with the INT8 route on: that route declines per call, so a declined call builds them. The load log's reserve line reads `EP census [...] (EP: historical single-device floor retired, ... HIP runtime overhead, 1088 MiB ...)`. Exact `0` restores today's EP behaviour byte for byte: floor kept (`max(5,490 MiB floor, 4,209 MiB census)`), global-config census terms, no pool drain, today's log text. Single-device placement, `qwen4_placement_receipt`, `hipfire fit` and the slowlink admission never read it. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_UNITS` | unset: discover `<model>.ep.hot-units` next to the model (absent: no loan, silent); `0` / blank: off (developer kill switch); an absolute path: that sidecar (read once per load) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP2 hot-unit loan, the sidecar half. The `<model file name>.ep.hot-units` sidecar (JSON, closed schema `hipfire.qwen4.ep_hot_units` v1, `hipfire_config::memory::ep_hot_units_sidecar_path`) is a calibration of the static placement: `counts[layer * experts_per_layer + expert]` is the number of calibration trunk forwards (complete 48-layer forwards) on the owner rank (`expert % ranks`) in which that unit was read from pinned host (skew-log classes `T` or `H`). It is bound to the model (`model_sha256`), the base artifact (`base_size` + `base_index_sha256`, the same binding as the EP hotset sidecar), the plan geometry (`unit_bytes`, layers, experts, ranks) and the EP hotset sidecar the calibration ran under (`base_ranking_sha256` = sha256 of the `.ep.expert-hotset` bytes = the plan's ranking sha). Default on for an exact gfx1201 two-rank Flash-Next load with a per-expert unit plan, `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_CENSUS` on and the D2 tail on, when the sidecar is present; with no sidecar nothing changes (placement is byte-identical to a load without the feature). The ordinary placement (core walk, stage fixed point, D2 tail delta) is unchanged; afterwards each rank additionally keeps resident the hottest calibrated host-demand units it owns (not resident already, `counts > 0`, ordered by count descending then id ascending) up to the loan of `HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_LOAN_MIB`: a unit charges its bytes, plus the two arena tail pads when the rank had no resident owned unit in that layer at that moment; a unit that does not fit is skipped and the walk continues. The extras are core units for the D2 tail (never slotted); the plan's per-rank budget grows by the lent bytes so the fit check admits them. The loan is a named census term funded from the reserve (`qwen4 EP hot-unit loan: rank r +N units +X MiB lent from the reserve census (reserve R MiB -> R-X MiB; cap C MiB; <path>; ...)`) and is refused with one line if it exceeds what the census reserves. A DISCOVERED sidecar that does not validate (another model or base, other geometry, another ranking, a malformed file) is skipped with one `qwen4 EP hot-unit loan: <path> present, not applied (<why>)` line, never a load failure; an absolute path is explicit and a refusal is a load error. A present or explicit sidecar on a load that does not admit the loan (not exact gfx1201, `topology.ep` other than 2, `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_CENSUS=0`, no D2 tail) logs one `qwen4 EP hot-unit loan: <path> present, declined (<why>)` line. Any other value is a load error. Write the sidecar with `scripts/ep_hot_units.py write --ranking <model>.ep.expert-hotset --skew-dir DIR [--skew-dir DIR ...] --corpus STR --out <model>.ep.hot-units` from `HIPFIRE_EP_SKEW_LOG` directories of a calibration run on held-out mixed/diverse prompts, never the bench prompts (`validate F --ranking R` checks schema and binding). Distribution of the sidecar through the registry is a follow-up (no pin yet). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_LOAN_MIB` | unset: the measured safe loan (`QWEN4_EP2_HOT_LOAN_GFX1201_BYTES`, 768 MiB per rank); `0` / blank: off (developer kill switch); a positive integer: that many MiB per rank, clamped to the measured safe loan (read once per load) | Size of the Flash-Next EP2 hot-unit loan per rank (see `HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_UNITS` for the mechanism and the sidecar). The measured cap is the largest per-rank loan that held on 2xR9700 EP2 (symtrunk + hc8, `serve --spec mtp --max-seq 16384`, 8 lanes) in a 512 / 768 / 1,024 / 1,536 MiB sweep against two 16.2k-token prefills, an 8-prompt burst and W8 (8 concurrent ~16.3k-token prompts) with the warm lane-page headroom (`free - earmark` after a long prefill) kept at or above the census margins (512 MiB placement + 256 MiB page): the loan spends that headroom MiB for MiB (about 1.6 GiB per rank without it, about 0.9 GiB at 768 MiB; 1,024 MiB falls under the margins; at 1,536 MiB W8 is refused with `overload`). It also lowers the number of 16k-token lanes that run at once under W8 (5 without the loan, 3 at 768 MiB), with every W8 request answered. Receipt `release-0.4.5/dynres/statichot/STRESS.md`. A larger request is clamped to the cap with one `qwen4 EP hot-unit loan: HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_LOAN_MIB=N clamped to the measured safe loan (C MiB)` line. The bytes charged per unit include the arena tail pads of a layer the rank had no resident unit in, so the units added are a little under `loan / unit_bytes`. A non-integer value is a load error. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_ELASTIC_MIB` | unset: **default on** with a cap of 2,048 MiB per rank (the lend is min(cap, lendable); lendable is what stays free after the page earmark, outstanding lane growth and the 256+512+128 MiB margins, about 1,620 MiB on the 2xR9700 cp15 fixture) (exact gfx1201 EP2 load with the D2 tail); `0` / blank: off (developer kill switch: every elastic path is a no-op and the page budget's warm rule is today's sticky one); a positive integer: that many MiB per rank as the cap; anything else is a load error (read once per load; config key `developer.ep_elastic_mib`) | Flash-Next EP2 elastic reserve. While no prefill tile of 512 or more GEMM route rows is running and no lane prefills, each rank lends up to the cap of idle VRAM to resident routed experts: an elastic bank (one allocation laid out like the D2 pool) of the next-hottest owned units after the hot-unit loan (`HIPFIRE_EP_HOT_LOAN_MIB`), published through the D2 tail's pointer tables. A unit's canonical pointer stays its pinned host copy, so decode outputs are byte-identical to the unlent run (same bytes, same kernels, only table entries differ). The bank is lent only at an idle step boundary (64 steps without a prefill tile of 512 or more rows or a reclaim, nothing queued) when the smallest rank can lend at least 256 MiB: `free - earmark (without the cold reserve) - outstanding lane growth - 256 MiB page margin - 512 MiB placement margin - 128 MiB emergency`. It is reclaimed within one step (rank-local, a target of 5 ms) by whichever comes first: the admission gate (the request's first prefill tile has 512 or more route rows, or the page verdict is not `Fit` while a bank is lent, then it decides again), the per-rank backstop before any step that runs a 512-or-more-row prefill forward or maps lane pages past the charge (every singleton, promoted and lane tile path), and the 8192-row tail borrow. After the same idle window the lazily built F16 weight shadows are given back (`Gpu::release_long_prefill_caches`), so with the reserve on the page budget's `warm` flag means "the long-prefill caches are resident" and the cold reserve (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_PAGE_BUDGET_COLD_MIB`) is held again after a release; the bank is then re-lent after another idle window. Needs the D2 tail (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL`); without it the reserve is inert. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_ELASTIC_LOG` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab; config key `developer.ep_elastic_log`) | `1`: print one `[elastic]` stderr line per rank for every elastic lend, publish, reclaim (with the reason `admit` / `pages` / `prefill` and the wait / tables / free split in ms) and long-prefill cache release (MiB freed), for the reclaim-latency receipts of `HIPFIRE_EP_ELASTIC_MIB`; one `[elastic] tick` line per idle-window probe (the idle step count, the cap, and per rank `free`, `earmark_noncold`, `outstanding`, `lent`, `caches` and the resulting `lendable` in MiB, i.e. the terms the lend decision reads); and a `lend: nothing lent` line when a lend the probe asked for is refused. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` | unset: no effect, a lane prefill tile compares its rows with the stage's own `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE_MIN_ROWS` (2048 on gfx1201) exactly as before; integer >= 2 (developer, lab) | Expert-parallel (EP) only. Row threshold of the long-prefill expert stage for a lane prefill TILE: every lane's prompt runs as ONE singleton chunk forward on the lane's own state (`Qwen4Bundle::lane_prefill_tile`), one lane after another inside a serve step, so the threshold compares with that lane's own rows, never with the rows of the whole step (`[fn-lane-step] pf_rows` is their sum). The value is clamped up to the stage's floor: a forward stages only on the owner-routed compact route (at least 512 routing rows) and, on a decoupled-reserve load, strictly above the resident bank's call limit (513; `ep_reserve_stage_refusal`'s borrowed-only rule), so a lower value is inert, never unsafe. A prompt tile of 60-150 rows (the C8 story / code cells) is below every floor: it keeps its sub-512 MoE route (under the trunk shard the partial route with the partial tile mask, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRUNK_SHARD_PMASK`; off the shard the owner-routed tile route at the lowered floor) and this knob does not reach it. Singleton prefill, decode and verify keep the stage's threshold. Bytes are identical (the stage copies the same expert bytes). One `qwen4 EP lane prefill expert staging` line at the first lane tile when set. `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1` additionally prints one `[fn-lane-tile] dev= tile= rows= ms= mtp= [trunk_ms= head_ms=] route= stage_min_rows=` line per lane prefill tile (device-synchronized wall; an MTP lane's tile splits its trunk forward from its head fill) in the serve log and in `fn_mesh_lane_oracle`, which also prints `[oracle-ref-prefill] rows= ms=` for the singleton reference prefill of the same rows and adds `prefill` {steps, rows, wall_s, ms_per_row} to each K run of `report.json`. `route=` is the MoE route the tile bound: `full-table` (EP full table), `tile` (owner-routed tile exchange), `partial` (trunk-shard partial chunk on the zero-dummy compact table), `partial-mask` (the partial route with the non-owned tiles masked out: only the rank's owned experts run), each with `+stage` when the tile staged. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_PREFILL_TILE_MIN_ROWS` | unset: **9 while the A8 chain is the forward's active owner-routed chain** (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_EP_A8` not `0`, exact gfx1201), else 512; integer 9..512 wins over the default (512 = kill switch of the floor alone); below 9 or not an integer is ignored with one log line, above 512 is 512 (developer) | Expert-parallel (EP) only, needs the owner-routed tile exchange (`HIPFIRE_EP_OWNER_ROUTED_PREFILL` not `0`). The routing-row floor at which an EP PREFILL forward takes the owner-routed symmetric IU4 tile route (each rank computes only its owned experts and the peers' BF16 grouped down rows are pulled over the tile exchange; no full-table / P2P / host-PCIe read of non-owned experts), instead of `QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4_MIN_ROWS` (512). The default is 9 only on the A8 chain: the sub-512 floor is quality-neutral there (paired prefill KLD j7418, floor 9 + A8: WT2 d=-0.0020 [-0.0064,+0.0012], code24 d=-0.0010 [-0.0056,+0.0027]) and fails on the A4 chain (+0.007 / +0.0058), so `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_EP_A8=0` keeps 512. Applies to every forward with the prefill scope (lane prefill tile, singleton prefill, MTP prompt-fill tile): a lane tile and the singleton prefill of the same rows read the same floor and take the same route, so they stay byte-equal; decode, MTP verify (at most 8 rows, the gathered row-twin MoE; 9 is the lowest accepted value) and the MTP head keep 512, and every route at or above 512 rows is untouched. Gates that stay at 512 on purpose (they do not have to follow: the IU4 chain reads the F32 activation and writes BF16 grouped rows whatever dense, GDN or HC-stream route produced the activation): dense projections / GDN chunked recurrence / QSA gathered WMMA / BF16 HC streams (`QWEN4_F16_WMMA_MIN_TOKENS`), the resident-bank call limit (a call of at most 512 rows already runs on the resident bank with the tile exchange), and the expert stage (its own 2048 default and the 512 / 513 floor of `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_STAGE_MIN_ROWS`: sub-512 tiles read their owned host experts mapped). A lane tile at or above the floor takes the tile route, not the EP full table; the exact joint prefill wave (`HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_EXACT`) reproduces whichever route ALL the burst's lanes take (the tile route behind `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_TILE`) and refuses a burst split across both (a lab floor inside the burst's rows). Under the trunk shard (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRUNK_SHARD`, default on EP2) the shard's partial chunk route takes precedence over the tile exchange: a chunk or wave at or above the floor runs the partial route with the partial tile mask (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRUNK_SHARD_PMASK`: owned experts only, no exchange), below it the partial route on the zero-dummy compact table; `[fn-lane-tile]` / `[fn-lane-wave]` print `route=partial-mask` / `route=partial`. One `qwen4 EP prefill tile route: floor N rows (A8 chain)` line at the first lowered forward. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_PINNED_OWNERS` | on with a unit plan under balanced ownership; exact `0` disables | Developer kill switch, read once per EP load, only meaningful with a per-expert unit plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET`) and balanced ownership admitted (`HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS` not `0`, exact gfx1201, at most 4 ranks). Only the exact value `0` is off; unset or any other value is on. On: a trunk layer that is host on some rank but not on every rank (decided from the plan alone, so every rank agrees; a rank whose own layer is wholly resident runs it too) is a balanced layer under the pinned owner-assign kernel (`qwen4_ep_owner_assign_pinned_pm_gfx1201`): per call a routed expert that is VRAM-resident on its holder `e % ranks` is owned by that holder (it exists only in the holder's VRAM arena, so no rank ever reads a peer's VRAM expert), and the routed host experts (device-visible from every GPU through the pinned host arena) are dealt across the ranks alternately in ascending expert-id order. A 512-byte pin plane per such layer (owner rank or `0xFF` free, checked at upload) is uploaded once at attach. This removes the per-layer host-read imbalance of the static owners (about +19% host units, ~1.7 ms per C8 AR step in the K0 estimate). Layers held wholly in host RAM on every rank keep the unpinned kernel exactly as today; layers fully resident on every rank stay on the static route. Outputs are byte-identical on and off (same bytes, a different rank computes a host expert; the combine order is the router's). The attach line reads `qwen4 EP rank r/n: balanced ownership on N host-mapped MoE layers, P of them pinned unit layers`. `=0` is D1 behaviour: static `e % ranks` owners on those layers (never balanced). Loads without a unit plan never read it. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL` | unset: `1536,16` (default on, EP2 load with a per-expert unit plan); `0` / `off`: no tail (developer kill switch) | `T,F[,b=N]`, needs the per-expert unit plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET`; an explicit value on a plan-less load is a load error, the unset default silently stays off there). D2: of every rank's unit budget `B` the static core keeps `B - T` units and a pool of `T` evictable VRAM slots holds byte copies of non-core units (their canonical home stays the pinned host arena, so the host arena grows by `T` units per rank, about 2.6 MB each). After every decode window (one trunk forward over every lane's rows: an AR step or a verify window; the singleton path retires the previous forward at the next one) the rank reads the window's routed ids (one D2D per gather call, one D2H per window), judges them against the slots live at the window start, publishes the fills that completed, then admits at most `F` of the window's missed units (reads-first, least-recently-used eviction, units used by the retiring window are never victims) by rewriting the rank's decode pointer tables and copying on a separate stream; a victim is repointed to canonical before any copy can overwrite its slot. `b=N` (N > 1): admit only every Nth window by decayed heat with 10% hysteresis. Exact by construction (a table entry is canonical or a completed slot of identical bytes). A configured tail runs STATIC `e % ranks` owners: `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_PINNED_OWNERS` is ignored (off) while it is set. Each rank prints `qwen4 EP rank r: D2 tail on: ...` at load. Any device error in the tail freezes it at its last published table (logged once) and never fails a decode. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_FREEZE` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab) | `1`: build the tail (pool, warm seed, usage log) but never admit or evict: the A/B arm that prices the tail's bookkeeping without any fill traffic. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_LOG` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab) | `1`: print a `[unit-tail]` counter line per rank every 64 retired windows and at teardown (core / tail / host read shares, slot and distinct hit, host distinct units and MB per window, fills, publishes, evictions, skipped, stale, late fills, per-window microseconds of the log, retire, table upload and fill phases). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_FILL_OVERLAP` | on; `0` off (developer) | Under the D2 tail (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL`), a retirement's slot fills (pinned host arena -> pool slot H2D copies on the tail's copy stream) are enqueued by the next forward's first row-gather call instead of at the retirement: the EP lane window's `prepare_commit` device-wide sync, which runs right after the retirement, no longer waits for them, and they run under the next forward. The next retirement waits for the last fill batch's event before it polls, so the policy sees every fill complete exactly as the device-wide sync made it (same publications, same tables; values unchanged). The `[unit-tail]` log line (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_LOG=1`) reports `fill_wait_us`, the part of the copies the forward did not hide. `0` issues the fills at the retirement and polls them (the previous behaviour). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_TAIL_LOG_DIRECT` | on; exact `0` kills it (developer) | Under the D2 tail (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL`), the few-row grouped (verify) MoE call of every trunk layer reserves its usage-log slot BEFORE its route launches: the tail takes its whole per-call logging decision there once (window routed count, the deferred-fill issue point, the frozen / released / non-trunk / empty / overflow skips, exactly as the row gather did) and the route kernels (`moe_route_scatter_top10_wide_f32`, `moe_route_scatter_top10_f32`, `moe_router_softmax_top10[_masked]_f32`, the top-10 / top-8 renorm routes) write the routed ids straight into that log slice, which every reader of the call's ids (owner assignment, scatter, gate/up, down, combine, row gather) reads. The row gather then only records `(layer, slots, offset)`: the per-layer D2D copy of the routed ids into the log (48 `hipMemcpyAsync` launches per step) is gone. The logged bytes, the recorded offsets, the window counts and every routing result are byte-identical. A call that is not reserved (switch off, a one-row decode, a borrow epoch, a chunk forward, an adopted or root-produced route, a graph capture or replay record) copies at the gather as before; so do runs with the chunk dump (`HIPFIRE_CHUNK_DUMP_DIR`) or the routing trace (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_ROUTE_TRACE`) on, which read the top-k scratch after the layer. `0` restores the per-layer copy. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PREFILL` | on; exact `0` kills it (developer) | With the D2 tail (default on), the rank's FULL (chunk / prefill) pointer tables follow the tail's slot publications for the owned units whose canonical entries they share with the decode tables (an owned entry that differs stays canonical; peer-owned entries are never written), uploaded in the same retirement as the decode tables and before any fill into a vacated slot. A full-table prefill forward (a prompt of 9 to 511 rows, or any forward the owner-routed tile route does not take) then reads a live slot from VRAM instead of the pinned host arena, which targets the prefill (TTFT) cost of the smaller static core `B - T` (card A/B pending). Byte copies of the same unit, so outputs are unchanged; a forward staged through the unit stage (>= 2048 rows) and the owner-routed route keep their own tables. Needs `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL`; the install line reports the shared entries. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PEER` | on; exact `0` kills it (developer), needs `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PREFILL` | With the D2 tail and its prefill lever, every rank's FULL (chunk / prefill) pointer tables also follow the PEER rank's slot publications for the peer-owned units whose full-table entries equal the owner's canonical ones, so a full-table prefill forward reads a unit the owner holds in a tail slot from the owner's VRAM over P2P instead of from its pinned host arena. A process-shared board carries the publications; an evicted slot is quarantined until every peer acked its table upload plus one lockstep window. Byte copies of the same unit: outputs are unchanged. A note is printed only when the variable is set and ignored. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PEER_RESERVE` | on; exact `0` kills it (developer), needs `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PEER` | With the D2 tail's peer quarantine in per-window mode, a retirement whose candidates fill its whole budget keeps a reserve of free + quarantined slots (2 x the budget, a constant): each candidate fills a free slot and quarantines one least-recently-used victim for a later retirement, so the tail sustains `F` (16) fills per window instead of alternating eviction and refill (~8 at code). Below a full budget the candidate that has to evict waits and is admitted only if it misses again. Quarantine, peer acks and reclaim are unchanged; outputs are unchanged (byte copies of the same units). `0` restores the candidate-waits rule in every retirement, decision for decision. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PINS` | on; exact `0` kills it (developer), needs `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PREFILL` | With the D2 tail and its prefill lever, the tail keeps pinned owners (D1.9) instead of turning them off: the pin plane follows the tail, a unit published in its holder's slot is pinned to its holder at a retirement-lagged, rank-identical point (deterministic across ranks). Effective only where pinned owners are admitted without a tail (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_PINNED_OWNERS` not `0`, balanced ownership admitted); otherwise the tail runs on static owners. `0` prices the pins away (dec2: the tail without pins loses 4-6% against no tail at C8). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_STAGE_GAP` | unset: `8` (developer, lab) | `G` whole units: with the prefill lever the slot-aware unit stage merges two H2D copy runs across a slot-live gap shorter than `G` units (the gap units are copied too and simply not referenced). `0` = exactly the complement of the slot-live units. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_RETIRE_IDS` | unset: `4096` (developer, lab) | `N` routed ids: the tail retires its window log only once the windows since the last retirement carry at least `N` routed ids (rows x top-k x MoE calls) or 8 windows have accumulated; a window that does not complete a group returns without a stream sync or D2H. `0` = retire every window. Rank-independent, so the pin plane stays identical across ranks. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TAIL_BORROW` | unset: on with 8192 borrowed rows (the shipped default, `EP_TAIL_BORROW_DEFAULT_ON`); `0` off (kill switch); `1` on with 8192 borrowed rows; an integer N above 4096, a multiple of 256: N rows (developer) | Flash-Next EP2 compact-scratch prefill on exact gfx1201 with the D2 expert tail and its prefill lever over a per-expert unit plan: the load's prompt chunk becomes N rows (placement, lane commit rows, MTP fill, PLE staging, tile-exchange capacity) while the forward's resident compact scratch bank stays today's 4096 rows beside persistent token ids (and, off the trunk shard, a grouped-down exchange root) sized for N. A prefill tile of more than 4096 rows runs on a second bank laid over the tail's slot pool (no new VRAM, no new pinned memory) inside a borrow epoch: the tail's tables are made canonical, its pin planes (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL_PINS`) set to their static planes and its policy suspended on every rank, an all-rank host barrier crosses, the span is zeroed, the tile runs; the epoch closes once per long prompt (a lane prompt keeps it open across its steps) by re-copying every occupied slot from its canonical pinned host bytes, an all-rank barrier and the tail's resume (live tables and pin planes). Under the trunk shard the grouped-down rows borrow with the arena and the mask-only tile exchange is registered at the resident bank's tiles (it never pulls peer rows; a pull beyond them errors). A failed close poisons the load (every later forward errors, reload). The placement is charged the resident bank plus the growth of the persistent roots to N rows instead of the N-row scratch. Ignored with a note when `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` is set, with `HIPFIRE_EP_CHUNK_AUTOFIT=1` or `HIPFIRE_EP_RESERVE_DECOUPLE=1`, without the tail's prefill lever, without the owner-routed tile exchange, or off gfx1201 or the compact plan. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TAIL_BORROW_VERIFY` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab) | `1`: every borrow epoch's close byte-compares every occupied tail slot with its canonical host bytes after the restore (`UnitTailRank::borrow_verify`) and keeps the FNV-1a digest. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TAIL_BORROW_LOG` | unset / not `1`: off (developer, lab) | `1`: one stderr line per closed borrow epoch per rank (epoch, borrowed tiles, occupied slots, restore bytes and ms, barrier wait ms, digest when verified) and a summary at teardown. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_LAB_UNIT_HITS` | unset / off; `1` on (developer, lab) | With a per-expert unit plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_EXPERT_HOTSET`), each EP rank's row-gather hook downloads every trunk MoE call's routed ids (one sync per layer: lab only) and tallies, per (rank, layer, slots per call), the routed slots and distinct experts the rank holds (`e % ranks`) split into VRAM-resident and host-read by the plan. `fn_mesh_lane_oracle` prints the report (`[unit-hits]` lines) and writes `unit_hits.txt` in its output directory. Never changes an output. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_TRANSPORT` | unset (`auto`) | Harness only, read by `ep_transport_oracle --devices` (feature `lab`); no product path reads it. Selects the oracle's transport when `--transport` is absent: `peer` (P2P reads of peer VRAM after `hipDeviceEnablePeerAccess`), `host` (shared mapped-pinned host banks: each rank publishes its owned rows/tiles into the bank, every rank reads them back; peer access is never enabled and every kernel pointer is checked against the rank's own allocations and its bank alias), or `auto` (`host` when any directed device pair lacks peer access, else `peer`). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DIRECT_LOAD` | on unless `0` (developer); discrete GPUs only | Developer kill switch, read once per Qwen4 load (`hipfire_config::memory::qwen4_direct_load`). Discrete GPU: the weight sweep (VRAM tensors, host-mapped experts and the expert hot set) reads the model file with parallel lanes of small reads (16 lanes of 2 MiB; `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_LANES` / `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_CHUNK_MIB` below) straight into the pinned owner or the pinned device-upload staging, `O_DIRECT` for ranges the page cache does not hold and buffered `pread` for resident ranges, instead of one thread copying out of the mmap. Bytes, allocation order, placement and admission are unchanged. The load logs `qwen4 direct load: on (16 lanes x 2 MiB, O_DIRECT)` (or `..., buffered-only)` when the filesystem refuses `O_DIRECT`) or `qwen4 direct load: off`, and the load-phase report adds `weights.direct_read` / `weights.buffered_read` timers. Exactly `0` restores the unchanged mmap + single-thread copy path. Unified-memory devices (Strix Halo) never take it and print `off`. |
| `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_LANES` | unset (**16**); integer `1`..`64` (developer) | I/O depth of the direct weight reader (`hfq_parallel`, `hipfire_config::memory::hfq_reader_lanes`, read once per process): how many synchronous reads are in flight, each into its own pooled 4 KiB-aligned bounce buffer. Unset is 16 lanes; `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_LANES=4 HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_CHUNK_MIB=16` is the pre-0.4.5 shape (4 x 16 MiB, tuned on `hipx` at 1.8-1.9 GB/s) and the kill switch for this default. Bytes read are identical for every setting (`bench_hfq_reader --verify` compares SHA-256 against a buffered `pread`); only the number and size of concurrent reads change. Host staging is `2 x lanes x chunk` pinned bytes for device uploads (64 MiB by default). An invalid value prints one `hfq reader:` line and keeps the default; it never fails a load. The ordered job reader used by the Qwen3.5/LFM2/DS4 loaders keeps its own 4 lanes. |
| `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_CHUNK_MIB` | unset (**2**); integer `1`..`16` (developer) | Size in MiB of each read of the direct weight reader (`hipfire_config::memory::hfq_reader_chunk_mib`, read once per process). Smaller reads keep an ordinary 6-24 MiB tensor (the typical VRAM tensor of a Flash-Next load) many reads deep instead of one or two, and keep each bounce buffer cache-resident; on the `hiptrx` 9100 PRO NVMe 16 MiB reads measured 7.6 GB/s per 18 MiB call against 9.8-10.4 GB/s at 1-2 MiB (`bench_hfq_reader`). See `HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_LANES`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_GATHER_DIRECT` | on unless `0` (developer) | Developer kill switch, read per load (`hipfire_config::memory::ep_gather_direct`). Flash-Next expert-parallel loads with a per-expert unit plan (`qwen4 EP expert tier ...` lines, drop-zero planar experts): each mixed layer's VRAM and pinned-host unit arenas were gathered by one thread walking the layer's two shipped expert stacks through the mmap (serial page faults, ~0.27-0.33 s per arena, ~30 s of every rank's load: the whole `weights` phase of an EP2 load, 207k major faults) and repacking each expert on that thread. Default on: the owned experts' shipped gate/up and down spans are read in ONE parallel `O_DIRECT` call of the HFQ reader (`HIPFIRE_HFQ_READER_LANES` / `_CHUNK_MIB`) into a scratch and repacked planar on every core; the arena bytes are identical (`bench_ep_gather` compares SHA-256 on real layers, ~8-10x faster per arena on the `hiptrx` NVMe). The load-phase report adds `weights.ep_gather_read`. Exactly `0` restores the serial mmap walk. Single-card and non-planar loads are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_LOAD_WEIGHT_DIGEST` | unset (developer) | Path. When set and non-blank, after a successful Qwen4 manifest fulfillment every rank and band appends one JSONL record per stored weight, `{"name","layer","residency","dtype","logical_bytes","sha256"}`, where `sha256` covers the logical bytes (host-owner hash, or a download of the device tensor). With the expert hot set, the materialization appends two compatible tier rows, `qwen4.expert_tiers.device` and `qwen4.expert_tiers.host`, in the old and the direct path alike: each hashes the actual destination logical payload bytes in deterministic tier copy order, excluding inter-span gaps, tail padding and pointer tables (device owners are read back through a bounded 8 MiB D2H scratch). Only a single-device load truncates the file first; an expert-parallel or pipeline-band run appends from every rank, so the caller supplies a fresh path. A digest write failure fails the load and names the file. Byte-identity oracle for comparing the direct and old load paths (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DIRECT_LOAD=0`); it adds a full read of every weight, so it is for validation only. Unset or blank writes nothing. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS` | on under EP on gfx1201 at 2..=4 ranks; `0` disables | Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism only (`topology.ep` 2..=8), exact gfx1201 at 2..=4 ranks only (the per-call owner-assign kernel ships for that arch and covers at most 4 ranks; on gfx1100 a rank logs `qwen4 EP rank r/n: balanced ownership not admitted (needs gfx1201); static split`, and at 5..=8 ranks `... (needs at most 4 ranks); static split`, and runs the exact static split): balanced per-call expert ownership on the host-mapped trunk MoE layers (the layers whose experts are read zero-copy from pinned host memory). Instead of the static `e % ranks` split, each call deals the distinct routed experts to the ranks alternately in ascending expert-id order, so every rank reads (nearly) the same number of host experts and none waits for a rank that drew more. One small device kernel per host layer rewrites the rank's live expert pointer tables from the routed ids; every routed expert is still computed by the unchanged kernel from its own bytes on exactly one rank and copied bit-for-bit to the others, so output is byte-identical to the static split and to a single card. Applies to one-row decode and few-row forwards (verify, replay, takeover, up to 8 rows); resident layers, chunk forwards and the MTP head keep the static split. With `0`, nothing is built: no tables, no buffer, no extra stage and no extra kernel (EP and single-card bytes unchanged). Logs one `qwen4 EP rank r/n: balanced ownership on N host-mapped MoE layers` line per rank at attach. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_TRUNK_BALANCED_OWNERS` | off; exact `1` admits | Developer opt-in, read once per EP load, Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP on the trunk-shard (partial FFN) route only, with every `HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS` admission condition (exact gfx1201, 2..=4 ranks). Unset or any other value keeps the static `e % ranks` split under the trunk shard; off the trunk shard it changes nothing. `1`: the trunk's host-mapped MoE layers run balanced (per-call) COMPUTE ownership, and every such layer gathers each slot's expanded row from the rank that computed it and folds the fixed REDUCTION partition `e % ranks` (the partitioned top-10 combine `moe_down_combine_top10_batched_partitioned`: a foreign expert's term folds as +0 in its place), so the all-reduced partials are the static route's whatever rank computed an expert, and a row's result does not depend on the other rows of its call (without it, an added MTP draft row changed a verify row's logits and greedy replies differed across loads). Any other combine under that partition refuses before launch; the fused shared-down epilogue and HC row fold decline. No performance claim (the gather is extra work). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_SKEW_LOG` | unset / off; a directory path turns it on (developer, lab) | Flash-Next EP with the D2 tail (`HIPFIRE_EP_UNIT_TAIL`): at every tail retirement each rank appends one line per logged trunk MoE call to `<dir>/skew-rank<r>.log`: `rank retirement call host_monotonic_ns layer slots`, then one token per slot, `<expert><class>`. The classes are `F` (another rank holds it), `C` (static VRAM core), `L` (live VRAM tail slot), `T` (tail unit read from pinned host) and `H` (host-only). It reads only the ids the retirement already downloaded (no added sync or device work) and never changes an output. The skew census joins it with a kernel trace: per fused all-reduce, how many owned / resident / host experts each rank ran. |

Sizes each EP rank's row-gather state for more than 8 rows: the four expanded-row gather buffers (`rows x top_k x hidden` F32), the gather transport's slot capacity (80 -> 480 at 48 rows) and the balanced-owner slot array (80 -> 480 words; the owner-assign kernel's contract is 512 slots). **Unset on an exact gfx1201 EP rank the capacity is 48 rows (`QWEN4_EP_ALLROWS_GATHER_ROWS`)**, the one-call window of `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_ALLROWS_MOE` (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_WIDE_VERIFY_ROWS` sizes it instead when set, 32..=64 lowered to the 51-row stage limit); every other arch keeps 8. `=0` restores the 8-row capacity (the all-rows MoE then refuses every >8-row step and keeps its groups); an explicit `=1` is the old meaning (32 rows unless `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_WIDE_VERIFY_ROWS` says otherwise, also the sampled wide-verify group cap). Capacity only: no route, lane segmentation (`LANE_MAX_ROWS` stays 8) or kernel selection of a <= 8-row call changes with it. The tile-exchange capacity is the owner-routed-prefill chunk capacity and does not depend on it (lane MoE never binds the tile exchange). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_WIDE_VERIFY_ROWS` | 32 (developer); 32..=64 | Rows one wide-class verify group (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_SAMPLED_WIDE_VERIFY` lanes) carries when a wide verify flag is on, and the EP gather capacity when `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP_WIDE_VERIFY` is unset on exact gfx1201 (default 48 there; set explicitly it sizes the all-rows MoE window); clamped to 32..=64, then lowered to the tightest stage limit (owner-assign 512 slots = 51 rows at top-10). Every rank of a mesh must run the same value. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_OWNER_ROUTED_PREFILL` | on under EP (2..=8 ranks); exact `0` disables | Flash-Next (Qwen4), `topology.ep` 2..=8, exact gfx1201 (symmetric IU4 route) or exact gfx1100 (F16 WMMA grouped route).
