A separate copy of tile IDs masks non-owned tiles to `-1`; the scatter output is unchanged. The gfx1201 route needs every MoE layer verified symmetric on every rank; the gfx1100 route runs the BF16-out WMMA gate/up (SwiGLU epilogue) and down on QT44/QT53 experts, needs no symmetric headers (the per-layer verdict is the expert formats, which every rank must agree on) and takes the stable grouping the symmetric route uses (the fused atomic scatter orders rows within an expert non-deterministically, so ranks could not share a grouped layout). Certified PeaceMaker mask/pull kernels copy non-owned BF16 grouped down rows from their owners before the unchanged combine (approximately 105 MB/layer/rank on EP2 or 157 MB on EP4 before grouping padding). Ready event, host barrier, all-peer ready waits, pull, done event, host barrier; every peer's done is waited before the next grouped-row writer. EP2 retains its 48-byte pull specialization behind the same API; EP3..EP8 take the eight-source pull launch. Needs the arch's route and non-compact scratch; collective verdict mismatch or unsupported rank/device/route refuses the lever for the entire load. Tail chunks under 512 rows, few-row forwards, decode and the MTP head keep today's routes. Without an active stream event waits are host synchronizations. Off allocates nothing, lowers no stage and loads no kernel. |
| `HIPFIRE_CHUNK_DUMP_DIR` | unset | Diagnostic (developer): digest the prefill chunk boundaries of selected layers under `$DIR/rank-<device>/chunk-<start>-<rows>/layer-<L>/digest.json` (SHA-256 of the routed ids, the layer's FFN output, the hyper-connection streams and every live tile of `y_down_grouped`). The chunk forward runs in segments ending after each selected layer and synchronizes the device there; `fn_ep_oracle --chunk-dump` sets it per arm and `fn-ep/coal/s1/compare_chunk.py` compares arms. `HIPFIRE_CHUNK_DUMP_LAYERS` (default `0,2,3,31,47`) selects layers; `HIPFIRE_CHUNK_DUMP_RAW=1` also writes the raw bytes (large; failure diagnosis only). |

Only the exact value `0` is off; unset or any other value is on, like `HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS`. Single card is unchanged. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE` remains the pipeline kill switch. Stage only when every trunk MoE binds the compact owner-routed route; full-table EP forwards never stage because they read peer donor VRAM. Each rank copies its owned compact host experts through its last two resident layers (637.5 MiB/layer/rank at EP2, 318.75 MiB at EP4); owned expert `e` uses donor slot `e / ranks`. Two pinned donor mirrors/rank, each charged at the largest rank's share, are charged for all ranks against one host's RAM/GTT. Every rank drains restored donors and crosses the transport's host barrier before any full-table chunk begins. All-resident loads need no host-layer stage; decode row exchange keeps host event waits independently of the staging stream. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_TRUNK_SPLIT` | unset (off) | Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism only (`topology.ep` 2..=8): output-axis split of the replicated trunk projections (#862). `prefill` splits chunk forwards (more rows than the few-row limit), `decode` splits decode and verify forwards of at most eight rows, `on` (or `1`) both; unset, `0` or `off` keep the trunk replicated (nothing allocated). Rank `r` of `N` projects only complete output rows `[r*m/N, (r+1)*m/N)` of the QSA q/k/v/indexer/output, GDN qkv/z/output, the Q8 decode copies of the HC input-mix down and the LM head (the MTP head's QSA included), and a copy-only gather (one pitched copy per rank) rebuilds the full output before any consumer reads it; the LM head is gathered whole so the unchanged full-vector argmax runs on it. BF16 weights (the prefill HC input-mix down), the row-exact wide verify (9..=64 rows), the IU4 trunk and a call too large for the exchange buffers (an all-rows prefill LM head) project whole. Every rank keeps the whole trunk resident (no memory is saved yet) plus two exchange buffers sized for the widest layer at the chunk rows. Attach refuses ranks whose banded-weight census or chunk rows differ, and every exchange checks a geometry tag across ranks. Eager only. Launches below 16 rows (decode, few-row verify) keep the tagged wire: the re-arm costs ~1 us of fixed latency that a few-KB launch does not earn back. Byte-identical: the same bf16 partials, fold order and HC write (only a NaN partial pair can differ in its NaN payload bits). The all-reduce is bandwidth-bound at prefill sizes (raw posted-store ceiling ~40 GB/s per direction on the R9700 pair, independent of store width): an isolated 256-row launch drops 108.5 -> 74.1 us (bf16 streams) and 158.6 -> 79.5 us (f32 streams); launches below 16 rows are unchanged. The mesh attach allocates the exchange buffers (a candidate table per rank, rank 1's packed logits and ids, up to 64 verify rows) and one event per rank before peer access is enabled; rank 1 records its event, both ranks meet one host barrier (released by the mesh's abort, 30 s timeout), and rank 0's stream waits for the event on the device. With the shard each rank ranks its HALF: the split falls on the partial-block boundary (`64 * ceil(ranked / 128)`: row 50,176 of the 100,276-row front layout, 124,160 of the 248,320-row full hold), so each rank runs the (x-batched) MQ2 GEMV over its rows of the copy (a byte sub-view) into packed logits and `topk8_partial_f32_blocks` over its 64 groups (the kernel is unchanged: `topk8_partial_blocks` rebases the pointer by the half's first layout row). Each rank then copies its half of every row's 1,024 (value, token) pairs into the peer's identical partial slot (2 peer copies of 2 KB per row on the active stream), records its event, meets the peer at one host barrier (released by the mesh's abort) and waits for the peer's event on the device, and BOTH ranks run the unchanged exact `topk8_rescore` over the same 1,024 pairs: both hold today's 72-byte slot (token, margin, 8 candidates and exact scores), so the head state, request state and evidence are today's. One exchange per draft call (a batched class of lanes is one call); the partial scratch holds one slot per draft row of the round (8). The ROOT decides per round (`DraftRound.vocab_shard`): the exchange is attached, the round has at most 8 ops and every op is replicated (every head owner runs it; a one-rank-owned op never shards); a rank that cannot run it refuses the round. Only with the MQ2 ranking copy and the re-score path; otherwise the draft runs today's launches. `<receipts pending>` |

Default on; `0` is the kill switch: EP serves AR lanes, as before the mesh lane protocol (the stage log reports `spec=off (HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANES=0)`). Measured trade against the AR lanes (EP2 mq4-xts, 2x R9700, chunk 512, greedy serve, `hipfire bench`, bin `1733c9e902`, n=3 per arm): C1 +-0, C3 +15.2%, C4 +5.1%, C8 -2.7% (MTP slots reserve 1.912 vs 1.020 GiB/rank, one fewer resident expert layer). The stage log line reports `spec=mtp K=.. executor=ep-mesh copy=..` or `spec=off (reason)`; an MTP lane store that does not fit is a labeled refusal followed by AR lanes, never a silently narrower store. Sampled requests (`serve.batch_sampled`, neutral penalties) use the mesh lanes too: rank 0 reads the head's candidates and its target logits rows and the root alone draws (Leviathan acceptance). A lone request keeps the singleton MTP route either way; the lanes serve concurrent requests. Any other value or unset is on. |

| `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_EXACT` | unset / not `0`: on (default); exact `0` off (master switch and kill switch of BOTH routes: every lane prefill tile is its own singleton chunk forward, one lane after another) | Expert-parallel (EP) only. Each lane's rows are per-slot independent (a lane's logits do not depend on the other lanes of the wave): the wave forward runs outside the ordinary-prefill intent under a routing-row cap of 511 (`Gpu::qwen4_route_rows_cap`), so the router, shared-expert and grouped-expert kernels are the sub-512 ones, under the routing scopes of the widest lane (not of the total), while launches carry the real rows. | `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_SMALL_LANES` | unset / not `0`: on (default); exact `0` off (kill switch: lanes of 9..=64 rows run serial singleton tiles, the `MIN_ROWS=65` behaviour); off under any `serve.batch_exact` opt-in | Expert-parallel (EP) only, with `HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_EXACT=1`: lanes of 9..=64 rows join the prefill wave. Under `serve.batch_exact` (`greedy` or `seeded`) those lanes keep their serial tiles and are byte-identical (greedy C8 replies equal to C1: 5 of 8 with the wave, 8 of 8 without). Root cause of the earlier non-exactness: the BF16 multirow projections of the MoE (router, shared selector / gate / up / down) take the 16..=64-row lane WMMA arm (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_LANE_WMMA`, `Gpu::lane_wmma_applies`, keyed on the call's REAL rows, not bitwise the SIMT arm) in a 60-row singleton but the SIMT arm inside a joint call over the wave's rows, so the router logits differed at layer 0 (expert ids equal there, logits and FFN output not). The wave now hands its lane-row table to the device (`Gpu::qwen4_wave_lane_rows`, set only when a lane of at most 64 rows is in the wave, cleared on every return path) and a BF16 multirow call over exactly those rows runs once per lane in the lane WMMA window (and per few-row lane), consecutive wider lanes staying one call; the routed A8 gate/up / down / combine stay joint over all rows. A wave with a lane of at most 64 rows also runs its other row-local stages per lane (`HIPFIRE_EP_LANE_PREFILL_WAVE_JOINT` applies only when every lane is wider than 64 rows: the joint HC / GDN / QSA launches select kernels on the call's real rows). | `HIPFIRE_LANE_ADMIT_COALESCE_MS` | unset: 2; integer ms, clamped to 20; `0` off (kill switch); unparsable is the default | Flash-Next (Qwen4) exact lanes (`fn_batch`), developer. A gap with no arrival ends the wait at once, so a lone request waits this many ms at most (default 2). A single device (and PP with a speculator), which hands a lone request back to the singleton route, never waits for it. |

The same tokens are emitted whatever the wait; only which wave a request prefills in moves. |

The decode advance then skips the seed (`skip_first_forward`, as for a promoted lane). The same tokens are emitted in the same order; only the time of the first one moves earlier by one step. Abort, stop, EOS and length finishes of that first token are handled where it is emitted. |

The executor half's prefill ticks (a lane's committed first token with its abort, stop, EOS and length finishes, `prefill_progress`, the prefill timers) are emitted right after the executor commits and before `ep_mesh_decode_step` (the 14-21 ms commit window measured at 7 lanes), instead of after it. The same tokens are emitted per lane in the same order; every decode and mesh tick keeps its place after the window. The mesh window carries only lanes that were Decoding when the step was planned, never a lane the step prefilled, so a lane finished by its first token is finished before the window runs. `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES` `[fn-lane-step]` reports the time spent emitting ahead of the window under `emit`, not `commit`. |

Admitting a request into an existing lane zeroes the lane's trunk state (GDN recurrent and conv per layer, the QSA K/V, index, pooled, partial and selection arenas, the PLE conv history and HC feedback), its MTP head state and its pending hidden. Each of those buffers used to be a `memset_async` on the steady copy stream followed by its own `hipStreamSynchronize` (about 167 pairs per lane per rank on the served EP2 shape, 28-30 ms of host time in an admission burst with the GPU idle). All the fills of one lane reset are now enqueued back to back and the stream is waited on once. | `HIPFIRE_MOE_ACT_CAPTURE` | unset | Diagnostic: append MoE producer activation rows of every multi-row prefill layer to this file, capped at 64 MiB (appending stops at the cap with one warning). Record: `u32 LE magic 0x41435431` (`ACT1`), `u32 site_len`, `site` bytes (UTF-8), then `u32 layer, rows, k, position` and `rows * k` little-endian f32. Sites: `moe.gate_up.x` (`[rows, hidden]`, the rotated and AWQ-divided input basis the gate/up producer reads: `x_rot` of the generic grouped path, `x_rot_batch` of the sealed legacy route) and `moe.down.x` (`[rows * topk, down_k]`, the rotated SwiGLU rows the down producer reads: `rot` / `rot_batch`, K physical). Sealed routes whose producer input is not an F32 basis buffer (fused front, expert IU4, qt44/qt53 sealed-grouped, FP8, slab) are not captured and print one warning. Synchronizes the device per capture; same capture/recording refusals as `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROUTE_DUMP`. Off by default; bytes and launches are unchanged when unset. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_CUT_LAYERS` | unset | Developer only: comma-separated absolute zero-based trunk layer indices (1 to 7, strictly increasing, each `>= 1`, below the layer count, and after the PLE layer when the profile has one). Executes the Qwen4 single-card trunk step program as separate interpreter runs split before each listed layer: the cut-safe Single schedule of the pipeline-parallel parity gate (no HC fusion or gate state crosses a cut; the PLE split is kept). Eager only: disables retained replay. Refuses the chunk dump, the QSA tap, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_ROUTE_TRACE`, `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROUTE_DUMP`, `HIPFIRE_MOE_ACT_CAPTURE`, expert-parallel forwards and the NPU trunk pair route with an error naming this variable; an invalid list fails runtime construction. Bytes and launches are unchanged when unset. |
| `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES` | `8589934592` (8 GiB); `0` disables | Compat env for `memory.session_cache_bytes` (int 0–1 TiB). Byte ceiling, not a reservation, of one loaded Qwen4, Gemma 4 or Raon-1-mini-preview model's engine-owned session cache; `0` attaches no cache and every turn prefills cold. Gemma 4 snapshots Q8 K/V every 1024 prompt tokens and at the prompt rounded down to the prefill width (never decoded rows), byte-identical to cold prefill. Raon-1-mini-preview snapshots the RDA recurrent + conv state whole and the MLA latent rows as parent-linked deltas, every stride (2048 tokens rounded up to the prefill chunk width) and at the prompt rounded down to the chunk width (never decoded rows); its AR route resumes a follow-up turn or a request sharing a prefix byte-identical to cold prefill (the speculative DFlash2 route prefills cold). Qwen4 snapshots fixed GDN/PLE/hyper/selections and MTP head/draft metadata whole, and append-only QSA/index/head rows as parent-linked deltas; committed snapshots are shared across sessions, evicted LRU over leaves, and restored from the deepest matching prefix. AR and MTP routes never cross. Qwen4 snapshots and live continuations are **session-exact, not a cold-exact guarantee**: boundaries after a tail/resident restore and later live turns remain eligible for pool capture; cold-exact consumers must not treat those snapshots as cold-prefill references. Snapshots live in device memory (VRAM or UMA) and optionally the disk tier. Every allocation keeps the context's growth reserve plus headroom: UMA `clamp(MemTotal / 16, 2 GiB, 8 GiB)`, discrete GPU 256 MiB; captures skip or evict when this guard fails. Live continuation skips the committed consumed history when the canonical prompt strictly extends it on the same route, prefilling only the suffix, with ties preferring live over snapshots. **Short side requests:** one longer conversation can be parked when divergence is within the first 1024 tokens and prompt + maximum output + write lookahead is at most 8192 rows. Parking copies fixed state plus the overwritten row prefixes (cold reset rewrites from row zero), retaining the rest in place, under the same budget/memory guard. The next strict extension of the parked main history restores it byte-exactly when its match is longer than the side history's; another divergence, context pressure or unload frees the parked copy. Long requests, missing bounds or insufficient memory use ordinary reset/snapshot fallback. For Flash-Next native MTP, the fixed state is about 33 MiB; row cost depends on QSA format (q8 versus F32) and includes the F32 MTP head. Serve reports reused tokens as `cached_tokens`. |
| `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_DISK_BYTES` | `0` (off) | Compat env for `memory.session_cache_disk_bytes` (int 0–64 TiB). Byte budget of the session cache's disk tier in `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_DIR`, shared by every model and build whose files are there. A snapshot evicted from memory is written to one file instead of freed, and model unload writes every published snapshot; a restore that hits a disk snapshot loads it back into memory (or a temporary buffer when the memory budget is full) and deletes the file. Disk eviction is LRU over leaves and survives restarts through file mtimes. Files are bound to the daemon executable's SHA-256: another build's files are never restored and are evicted first. Snapshots still in memory at daemon kill or crash are lost. |
| `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_DIR` | empty = `$HIPFIRE_HOME/session-cache` | Compat env for `memory.session_cache_dir`: directory of the session cache's disk tier (`~` expanded). Locked by one process at a time; a second process logs `session cache disk tier off` and keeps only the memory tier. |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_HOT_STORE_BYTES` | `0` (off); `auto` opts in | Compat env for `memory.ple_hot_store_bytes` (`auto` or int 0–1 TiB). Prewarm budget of the Qwen4 PLE hot-row store. The store needs the frequency-rank sidecar `<model>.plehot` (`hipfire-quantize ple-hot-rank`) and the hot-row pack `<model>.plehotrows` cut from it (`hipfire-quantize ple-hot-pack`): the rows of the hottest buckets, copied verbatim from the model file and grouped by rank so the hottest rows share OS pages. Rows the pack holds are read from the pack, everything else from the model file, and both decode through the same row decoder, so output is byte-identical. Before serving, a worker thread checks the sidecar hash and compares 4,096 pack rows with the model. After load it reads (prewarms) the first N bytes of the pack into the page cache, hottest tier first and then tier 0 once more so its pages are promoted to the active list, while no request is in flight (paused for each whole request, resumed 5 s after the last): `auto` = `MemAvailable` minus the CMA region minus 4 GiB at ready, an explicit N is that many bytes, both capped at the pack. The pack's pages are ordinary clean page cache, counted in `MemAvailable`, dropped (never swapped) under pressure and re-read from the pack when needed. Unified-memory GPUs only. |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_FORMAT` | `auto`; `q4` or `q8` pins | Compat env for `memory.ple_format` (`auto` \| `q4` \| `q8`). Which PLE row table a Flash-Next (Qwen4) load reads. `<model>.ple-q8` holds the exact Q8 rows; `<model>.ple-q4` holds 4-bit Lloyd-Max rows derived from them, decode equal to the tested q4 table. `auto` = q4 on gfx1151 (unified-memory Strix Halo), q8 elsewhere; `auto` never derives or downloads and falls back to the other available format with a stderr note. `q4` reads `<model>.ple-q4`; a load never derives it, and a missing file is refused naming both searched locations (the model path as given, then its canonical symlink target), `hipfire pull <tag> --ple q4` and `hipfire ple derive --format q4 <model>` (about 1 minute from the local Q8 rows, progress on stderr). `q8` on a split base (`hipfire ple split <model>`: `<model-stem>-base.<ext>` + `.ple-q8`) needs `<model>.ple-q8` (`hipfire pull <tag> --ple q8`; `serve` downloads it on demand for registry models); the monolithic `qwen3.8:flash-next-mq4-xts` keeps its embedded Q8 rows. A sidecar is bound to its base file (base index sha256 + size) and refused on mismatch. See [`CONFIG.md`](CONFIG.md#qwen4-ple-row-tables). |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_PREWARM_BYTES` | `auto`; `0` disables | Compat env for `memory.ple_prewarm_bytes` (`auto` or int 0–1 TiB). Page-cache prewarm of the q4 PLE table, paused for the whole of every request (resumes 5 s after the last one) `<model>.ple-q4` after the model is ready (rank-ordered: hottest tier first, then tier 0 again; row-ordered: sequential). `auto` is derived from `MemAvailable` minus the CMA region minus 8 GiB, sampled once after the pinned experts and model are loaded, capped at the table's data bytes: on a unified-memory GPU and on a discrete GPU with every expert resident in VRAM it is also capped at 15 GiB (the smaller of the two), and only a discrete GPU with host-placed experts takes the uncapped figure; an unreadable `MemAvailable` gives 0; `0` = off; an explicit N is that many bytes, is never capped at 15 GiB, and overrides the derivation (the table still caps it). The in-load fill (`HIPFIRE_PLE_LOAD_FILL_BYTES`) is unchanged. The pages are ordinary clean page cache, dropped (never swapped) under memory pressure and not charged in admission. See [`CONFIG.md`](CONFIG.md#qwen4-ple-row-tables). |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_LOAD_FILL_BYTES` | `auto`; `0` disables | Compat env for `memory.ple_load_fill_bytes` (`auto` or int 0–1 TiB). Bytes of a rank-ordered q4 PLE table `<model>.ple-q4` read into the page cache while the model loads, hottest rows first, at most 0.2 GB/s, re-reading pages the weight upload's cache traffic evicted (hottest first) until ready; ready never waits for it, and the prewarm after ready continues at the end of the resident prefix. `auto` = 14 GiB; any value is capped at the table, at an explicit `memory.ple_prewarm_bytes`, and at the host RAM available at load start (`MemAvailable` minus the CMA region, plus TTM's page pool) minus the weights the load puts in host RAM minus 8 GiB; `0` = off. See [`CONFIG.md`](CONFIG.md#qwen4-ple-row-tables). |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_HOT_PACK_FILE` | `<model>.plehotrows` | Path of the PLE hot-row pack, overriding the file next to the model. Empty or unset uses `<model file>.plehotrows`. |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_HOT_RANK_FILE` | `<model>.plehot` | Path of the PLE hot-rank sidecar, overriding the file next to the model. Empty or unset uses `<model file>.plehot`. |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_HOT_DEBUG` | off; `1` enables | Developer diagnostic: one stderr line per PLE fetch of at least 4,096 rows (a prefill chunk). It gives the pages and rows served from the hot-row pack, the cache hits and misses, the source read groups, the inline-read and reader counts and the timing of each phase. |
| `HIPFIRE_PLE_INLINE_FETCH` | on unless `0` | Developer kill switch for small PLE fetches (at most 64 source-page groups plus hot-pack runs): try page-cache-only `preadv2(RWF_NOWAIT)` reads inline before dispatching misses to blocking readers. Completed reads use the same row decoder and source-page cache publication. `0` restores the previous blocking/threaded fetch path. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TAIL_SNAPSHOT` | on unless `0` | Kill switch, read once per Qwen4 bundle at assembly (needs the session cache and tokenizer `<|im_end|>` id). A cold or chunk-snapshot-restored prefill of a long prompt also splits before its last `<|im_end|>` and keeps fixed state there as a lineage-only resident (no pool rows). A request extending that final message or repeating the prompt restores it and prefills only its new rows and template. Session-exact, not cold-exact: the extra tile changes the forward schedule. Descendant regular boundaries now enter the session-exact pool, so losing the resident no longer loses the whole lineage. Turns restored from a tail do not split at their own tail. `0` restores the chunk-only schedule. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TAIL_SNAPSHOT_MIN_GAP` | `1024` (rows) | Read once with the switch above. The tail split is taken only when the tail index lies at least this many rows above the later of the prefill start and the preceding chunk boundary (below it the replay it would save is cheaper than the extra tile). |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TAIL_SNAPSHOT_MIN_PROMPT` | `16384` (tokens) | Read once with the switch above. Prompts shorter than this never take the tail split, so short cold prompts pay no extra tile. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TOKEN_READBACK_EARLY` | on unless `0` | Developer kill switch for Qwen4's next-token PLE readback: enqueue a pinned-host 4-byte token copy on the readback stream after it waits for argmax, then synchronize that stream once when staging the next token. `0` restores event synchronization followed by copy and stream synchronization. Token selection and vocabulary validation are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TURN_SNAPSHOTS` | off; `1` enables | Developer switch, read once per Qwen4 bundle (needs the session cache and a tokenizer `<|im_end|>` id). Also splits every prefill (cold, snapshot-restored and live-continued turns) right after each `<|im_end|>` (every message end) and captures a session-cache snapshot there, published at client commit and reusable across sessions like the chunk snapshots. These snapshots are session-exact, not cold-exact (a live turn's state descends from decode, and the extra split changes the cold forward schedule); their scope carries `/turns`, so they never share a domain with flag-off snapshots. Off: only prefill-chunk multiples are snapshotted and a live turn captures nothing. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TOOL_ARG_REPLAY` | on unless `0` | Opt-out for the shared Qwen4 AR/native-MTP and dense Qwen AR/spec repair (the flag name is retained): restore original producer parameter order on the last echoed assistant tool turn of the active committed conversation, preserving typed values. Byte-identical echoes are unchanged. Accept only an exact committed-prefix extension with a nonempty suffix; parse/render doubt falls back to the original safe miss. No lexical sorting, cross-session guarantee, or retired-MTP guard change. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN_PROMPT_CACHE` | on unless `0` | Conversation-reuse kill switch of the Qwen3.5/3.6 and dense generation paths (LCP prompt cache, DFlash/MTP conversation plan). Also gates continuing a Qwen3.5-VL image conversation in place (an image turn whose rendered history extends the resident conversation reuses its KV/DeltaNet rows and images; `0` starts every image turn cold). **No longer affects Qwen4**: Qwen4 reuse is the session cache above (`HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES`). The RC3 Qwen4 prefix cache (end-of-prompt checkpoint, multi-entry radix cache, shared-turn anchors, periodic captures) is removed; only the live continuation described above remains. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN_RADIX_CACHE` | (removed) | Read by nothing. It selected the removed RC3 Qwen4 multi-entry radix cache; setting it has no effect. Use `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES=0` to turn Qwen4 prompt reuse (snapshots and live continuation) off. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN_CACHE_TRACE` | unset | Set `1` to trace Qwen3.5/3.6 and dense/AR serve-path cache decisions to stderr. Qwen4 prints one `[qwen4-session] route=… prompt=… prior=… lcp=… reused=… new=… source=live\|snapshot\|cold` line per request: `prior` is the previous turn's committed history, `lcp` its common prefix with the new canonical prompt and `reused` the tokens the session cache skips; when the prompt diverges from that history a `[qwen4-session ids]` line shows the token ids around the first difference. Tail capture prints `session cache: tail resident at P`; a plan using it prints `session cache: using tail resident at P`. The RC3 `[qwen4-radix]` / `[qwen4-prefix]` lines are gone; a skipped snapshot prints `session cache: skipped N-token snapshot (reason)` regardless of this switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_CACHE_QUALITY_OUT` | unset | Oracle/diagnostic only. Absolute path of a JSONL file that `hipfire_arch_qwen4::cache_quality::cache_quality_event` appends to (one compact JSON line per event, flushed per line; the variable is read once; unset or empty disables capture, a relative path warns and disables it). It is the opt-in production ID capture sink consumed by `quality_driver.py`; no daemon or generate code path calls it in this tree. |
| `HIPFIRE_CQ_SESSION_BUDGET_BYTES` | unset: unbounded | Oracle only: `u64` byte budget of the `SessionCache` the `qwen4_parity --mode cache-quality` hit-vs-cold logits oracle builds (reference parity of the session cache; also honours `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS`). Not read by the daemon. |

S1 pressure host snapshots add no environment variable. With the S1 slow-link profile attached (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SLOWLINK_PREFILL=1` on a load that admits it), the session cache's host budget is the existing `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES` and snapshots captured under device pressure are placed in bounded pinned host memory instead of being skipped; without an S1 profile the cache and its allocations are unchanged. The device side of the policy is the admitted profile's `device_snapshot_budget_bytes` (discretionary capacity above the full S1 peak and the 512 MiB safety margin, not extra reserve). Details and the host envelope estimate (S1 census + 2 GiB pool + pending ceiling + driver allowance, a historical estimate with no GPU pass and no final base CI/serve PASS claimed): [`CONFIG.md`](CONFIG.md).

S1 bounded arenas add no environment variable. On a load that admits the S1 profile, the resident non-expert weights and the F16 shadows of the BF16 weights that get one are held in exact-size bounded device roots planned once per load (bytewise name order, 256 B alignment, next-fit, each root bounded by the largest eligible allocation that load would otherwise have made), so allocation-granule padding is charged per root instead of per weight and the host snapshot tier sees less device pressure; a non-S1 load, a fast, unified or unmeasured link and the incumbent route build no plan and are unchanged, and host session semantics are unchanged. The mixer tile (768/512), the adaptive resident count (ten preferred), the 1 GiB runtime reserve, the 512 MiB safety margin and the whole-product external + HIP 512 MiB minimum-free gate do not change, and there is no architecture, card or format gate. The recovery figures (a real-model source projection of 1,010,670,848 B with 48,509,588 B of slack at N = 10 / tile 768) are not a measurement, and no GPU recovery is claimed. Receipt fields and the fixture projection: [`CONFIG.md`](CONFIG.md).

Assistant-turn token splicing also stores complete call-less `length` bodies,
including open `<think>` spans, keyed by the unchanged content/tool fingerprint
and producer reasoning text. Echoing `content` and `reasoning_content` replays
the emitted IDs without a synthetic `</think>`; the history template still
appends its per-turn `<|im_end|>`. Truncated/malformed tool output, suppressed
hidden calls, stop-sequence, abort and fail-closed terminals remain unstored.
See [Prompt / assistant-turn cache](CONFIG.md#prompt--assistant-turn-cache).

For Flash-Next native MTP with Q8 GDN, short-request parking's fixed parts
occupy 34,180,608 bytes (32.60 MiB). Each token adds 21,632 bytes with Q8 QSA
or 61,568 bytes with F32 QSA, including the F32 head and amortized pooled
index rows. At the 8192-row bound this is 169 MiB or 481 MiB of rows,
201.60 MiB or 513.60 MiB total (plus retained resident buffers and host
metadata). Shorter or non-four-row-aligned requests pay per-part 256-byte
packing and pooled-row rounding; only rows valid in the parked main state
are copied. This is a copy/restore, not a numerical conversion.
Explicit F32 GDN raises the fixed copy to 118,230,528 bytes (112.75 MiB);
with F32 QSA the full 8192-row park is then 593.75 MiB.
Lineage-derived pool snapshots use a separate `/session-exact` domain, so
they do not alias cold-exact reference snapshots. Cache tracing labels a
parked-main restore `source=live`; `prior` and `lcp` still describe the
immediately preceding request, which may be the side conversation.

For Flash-Next/Qwen4 pipeline parallelism (`topology.pp` 2..=8; see
[multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#flash-next-pipeline-parallelism-host-staged)),
`memory.session_cache_bytes` is an independent opportunistic device-cache budget
**per band**, not a mesh-wide reservation. Captures retain the same
free-memory/context-growth guards as Single. Every band captures its own state at
the root's common prefill boundaries; only client-committed, all-band snapshots
are reusable. Aggregate records hold pins on every local piece, are scoped to
placement and mesh/load epoch, and a missing piece makes the whole record a cold
miss. The bounded aggregate index retains at most 64 records. Resident-only
lineage/tail snapshots do not enter that index because their row streams depend
on mutable live state. A load with native MTP (`--spec mtp`) attaches no session
cache on the bands (MTP sessions are not distributed); AR loads keep theirs.
`memory.session_cache_disk_bytes` above 0 and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TURN_SNAPSHOTS=1` are
refused at load under PP; Single and EP session behavior is unchanged.

### Embedding (arch 17)

| Variable | Default / sense | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_VISION_HOST_MAPPED` | **off** (developer); `1` opts in | Under Qwen3.5/3.8-VL `vision.mode=auto` or `on`, keep the large vision matrices in GPU-addressable host RAM and read them directly. Small tensors and patch projection remain resident. The same F16 conversion and FC2 padding as resident are used; explicit `resident` wins and `off` still skips the tower. Unset/`0` retains per-block streaming. Experimental capacity/latency comparison; no new vision mode or arithmetic. |
| `HIPFIRE_EMBED_VISION_OFFLOAD` | `1`/`on`/`true` forces the qwen3.5 embedding model's SigLIP tower into host RAM (GPU-addressable, read over PCIe); `0`/`off`/`false` forces it device-resident; unset auto-decides | The tower is ~250 MB of the arch's ~740 MB and is only touched by an image request, so it is the component to spill when the card is busy. **Auto (the default)** keeps it resident only when it fits in the VRAM free at load plus a 64 MB margin, and spills otherwise — a machine whose desktop already holds most of the card then loads the model and serves text instead of OOMing, at the cost of PCIe reads on image requests. Same tradeoff `HIPFIRE_OFFLOAD_EXEC=pcie` makes for spilled text layers; applied per-model here because the embedding arch can offload the tower whole. Read via `developer_var` in `hipfire-arch-qwen35-embed`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EMBED_GEMM` | `f32` (or `mb8`) forces the f32-activation `gemm_f16_wmma_mb8` on every projection | A/B and bit-fidelity switch for the LDS-tiled macro-tile GEMM (`gemm_f16_x_f16_wmma_lds_auto`) the encoder uses for large shapes. Measured on gfx1101 at n=4096: mb8 runs the trunk's GEMMs at ~1.6 TFLOP/s (2% of the f16 peak) because it is a register-blocked 32-thread block with no LDS staging — right for the small batches it was written for (the vision tower), wrong here; the tiled kernel reaches ~25 TFLOP/s and took the GEMM from 1227 ms to ~80 ms. Its X operand is f16, so activations narrow: ~1e-3 relative, two orders below what 4-bit weights already cost, and the embedding error is unchanged to four digits on the validation fixture. Small shapes (`out < 256`, the GQA `a`/`b` projections) and any `k % 64 != 0` keep mb8 either way. |
| `HIPFIRE_EMBED_ATTN` | `naive` = masked reference per-(query, head) kernel; `no-gemm` (or `tiled`) = the Q-tiled SIMT kernel everywhere | Attention has three paths, selected by shape and this knob. Default: the **eager decomposition** (`S = Q·Kᵀ` as a WMMA GEMM, a row softmax, then `P·V` as a second GEMM) on a wave32-WMMA arch when `n % 64 == 0` and `n >= 256` — the tiled GEMM asserts `k % 64 == 0` and the second GEMM contracts over `n` — and `head_dim % 64 == 0`. Otherwise the Q-tiled SIMT kernel, which handles any `n` and is cheap at small `n` (its cost is O(n²)). To put inputs of any length on the eager path, sequences of 256+ tokens are **padded up to a multiple of 64** (`padded_rows`); padding goes at the end, which the architecture tolerates because GDN is causal (a padded row cannot influence a real row's state), the eager full-attention layers mask the padded keys in a masked f16 row-softmax, and the Q-tiled kernel bounds its score loop by `n_valid` so the padded keys never enter the softmax — and `naive` masks them with an f32 key vector (an unmasked path would let the zeroed pad rows take a softmax share and dilute every output row). Inputs below 256 tokens are not padded and stay on the tiled kernel. Measured: `embed_medium` (2209 tokens) 390.8 -> **165.3 ms** once padded onto the eager path. `naive` reaches the masked reference kernel for A/B. Measured on gfx1101 at 4096 tokens: tiled 1056 ms -> eager 342 ms (**3.09x**), because the tiled kernel's scalar f32 FMA runs at ~2.05 TFLOP/s (6.9% of f32 peak) while the trunk's WMMA GEMM holds 5.5-6.25 TFLOP/s at exactly this shape. Costs f16 Q/K/P for the attention operands; the embedding error is unchanged to four digits (1.51e-4 eager vs 1.40e-4 tiled against the NumPy oracle). |
| `HIPFIRE_EMBED_PHASE` | `1` prints the per-phase timing of one `embed` request | `assemble \| encode \| mask+normalize \| serialize+write`, on stderr. **Its own knob, deliberately not `HIPFIRE_EMBED_DEBUG`**: that one also arms the encoder's per-layer finite `trace()`, which syncs the GPU every layer and inflated the same measurement from 334 ms to 678 ms — the first reading of it was wrong for exactly that reason. |
| `HIPFIRE_EMBED_DEBUG` | `1` traces the embedding encoder | Prints a per-layer finite-value trace of the residual stream (absmax + non-finite count) and the VRAM free/total after load. Downloads one tensor per call, so it is strictly a debugging path — it is what localized a weight-format bug that NaN'd the last rows of every projection. |

### GGUF-native (dense Qwen3.5 / 3.6 / 3.8, gfx1201, developer, default off)

| Variable | Default / sense | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_GGUF_NATIVE` | `1` loads a `.gguf` in the dev tools (`eval_hipfire`, `bench_qwen35_mq4`, `gguf_native_gen`) as native ggml blocks | Unset refuses with a named reason. Weights stay in their ggml block bytes (IQ4_XS, Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K, Q8_0; F32/F16/BF16 float matrices are widened to F32). Decode is the `ggml.a8q81` route (q8_1 activation producer plus mmvq); prefill dequantises one weight per GEMM into F16 and runs the F16 WMMA GEMM. The NextN/MTP block is never loaded; a file whose MTP block holds a type outside the admitted set (for example Q4_0) loads AR-only with the named reason from `GgufNativeModel::mtp_refusal()`. The load log prints `bpw=… route=ggml.a8q81 kld_class=UNMEASURED`. |
| `HIPFIRE_GGUF_CODEC_ONLY` | `1`: quality-measurement tier | Prefill and scoring only (decode always runs the shipped route). Weights are dequantised with BF16 rounding; activations stay F32-class (carried as an exact BF16 hi part plus an F16-rounded residual through the F16 WMMA GEMM, F32 accumulate); no q8_1 activation rounding. Isolates the file's quantiser from the arithmetic route. |
| `HIPFIRE_GGUF_A8_EMU` | `1`: shipped-route measurement tier | Prefill and scoring only. Rounds every prefill GEMM input through q8_1 (per 32, f16 scale) before the multiply, so a prefill-mode KLD run charges the activation quantisation the decode route applies. The integer dot and f32 scale fold of the decode kernels are replaced by the F16 GEMM on dequantised weights. |

### Vision tower sidecar

| Variable | Default / sense | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_VISION_SIDECAR` | explicit vision-tower path (overrides the registry sidecar); empty opts out | Read via `developer_var` (env beats `developer.vision_sidecar`); wired into the daemon load as `params["vision"]`. Skipped while `vision_mode=off`. |
| `HIPFIRE_VISION_MODE` | tower sidecar gate: `auto` (default; was `off` before 0.4.4) / `resident` / `on` / `off` | Env-compat for config `vision.mode`; projected into load params as `vision_mode` and enforced daemon-side. `hipfire bench` pins `off`. |
| `HIPFIRE_IMAGE_DECODE` | VL image JPEG decode path: `cpu` (default) / `vcn` / `auto` | Env-compat for config `image.decode`; read via process snapshot in `hipfire-arch-qwen35-vl`. The standard daemon build compiles the `vcn-jpeg` path in (default feature). Runtime default remains `cpu`, which never enters the VCN prepass. `vcn` and `auto` attempt shared VCN JPEG decode and fall back to CPU for unsupported inputs, platforms where VCN is unavailable, or recoverable decode failure. A failed terminal GPU completion fails closed by quarantining the shared VA session, emitting a request error, and exiting the daemon nonzero (restart required) instead of unsafe same-device CPU fallback. When VCN runs, pooled decode surfaces stay leased until GPU preprocessing completes; the learned vision tower is unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_VL_FILE` | explicit `.vl` tower file for the multi-slot engine | Checked before the `<stem>.vl` sibling probe in `hipfire-runtime` `sidecar::resolve_vl_sidecar`; a set-but-missing path is reported, not silently ignored. |
| `HIPFIRE_VIT_ATTN` | `auto\|wmma\|qtiled\|naive` (default `auto`) selects the ViT attention kernel | `auto`: WMMA flash kernel (`vit_attention_wmma_f16`, head_dim <= 80 padded to 80) on wave32-WMMA arches (gfx11/gfx12), else the scalar Q-tiled kernel for head_dim <= 128, else naive. `wmma` errors if the arch lacks WMMA or head_dim > 80; `qtiled` and `naive` force those kernels for rollback/A-B. |
| `HIPFIRE_VIT_GEMM` | `auto` (default) / `mb8` | Selects the ViT tower linear GEMM in `hipfire-arch-qwen35-vl`. `auto` runs the LDS-tiled WMMA GEMM (X cast to F16, F32 accumulate: the same rounding mb8 applies inside its kernel, bit-identical on the gfx1201 tower shapes) on gfx1201 and gfx11 wave32-WMMA arches for `out_dim >= 256`, with fc2 (K = 4304) stored zero-padded to K = 4352 at load. `mb8` keeps the register-blocked `gemm_f16_wmma_mb8` + `bias_add_f32` path (packed weights, no LDS staging) as rollback/A-B. Read once per process; set before loading the tower since it decides the fc2 weight layout. |

### Graph / MMQ / prefill

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_GRAPH` | hipGraph capture opt-in (quality caveats; AR-oriented). Raon-1-mini (arch 19) decode defaults to a captured graph (one per admitted context bucket, replayed per token; re-recorded when the latent mapping or K/V mirror grows); `HIPFIRE_GRAPH=0` is the per-launch eager control. A failed capture is an error, never a silent eager fallback. |
| `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_GRAPH` | Gemma4 per-model decode graph: `1` enables, `0` disables (kill switch). Hand carrier defaults on. Lowered carrier: this switch wins, then `HIPFIRE_GRAPH=0/1`, then the arch default, which is on for exact gfx1201 and off for every other arch (gfx1100, gfx1151, gfx1200, …). Lowered captures after one eager warm-up, stages position on-device before replay, and re-captures when KV/weight/scratch allocations or shapes change. Dense bodies and indexed MoE with HFQ4G128 expert down are admitted; CPU expert fallback and atomic Q8 expert down remain eager. |
| `HIPFIRE_GRAPH_MOE` | MoE graph opt-in |
| `HIPFIRE_VERIFY_GRAPH` / `_TIMING` / `_TREE` | verify-side graph diag |
| `HIPFIRE_MMQ` / `HIPFIRE_WO_MMQ` | MMQ activation |
| `HIPFIRE_MMQ_SCREEN` / `HIPFIRE_MMQ_SCREEN_THRESHOLD` / `HIPFIRE_MMQ_MIN_BATCH` | screening |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_GATE_UP_TILE_LOOP` | gfx1201: on unless `0`; ignored elsewhere. Exact gfx1201, MQ4G256V2 MLP gate+up one-tile verify kernel (`gemm_gate_up_mq4g256v2_wmma_gfx12`, N 1..=16 on the plain arm): launches a reduced row-tile grid (`ceil(row_tiles / t)`, `t = ceil(row_tiles / (CUs * 32))`) and loops over row tiles with stride `gridDim.x`. Byte-identical; `0` restores grid x = row tiles. |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_BATCHED` / `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` | batched prefill knobs (config mirrors). PFlash (`speculation.prefill.*`, `HIPFIRE_PFLASH_*`) is **deprecated since 0.4.0, removal in 0.5.0** |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_BATCHED` | RuntimeConfig: on unless `0` (Qwen-style batched prefill gate — **not** the LFM flag) |
| `HIPFIRE_FLASH_PREFILL` | Developer override for Q8 WMMA flash prefill: `0` forces off, `1` forces on; unset uses the architecture/workload envelope. |
| `HIPFIRE_FA_PREFILL_PM` | Q8_0 and native FP8: unset = on where qualified (`FP8_KV_QUALIFIED`, exact gfx1201 H16/KV2/D256) | Typed boolean (`developer.fa_prefill_pm`), exact gfx1201 H16/KV2/D256 PeaceMaker prefill. `1`/`true` opts in for the actual cache codec; `0`/`false` retains the incumbent. See [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_FA_PREFILL_PM_QPREP` | Q8_0 and native FP8: unset = on where qualified (`FP8_KV_QUALIFIED`, exact gfx1201 H16/KV2/D256) | Typed boolean (`developer.fa_prefill_pm_qprep`), exact F5 Q-prep/writer/direct-attention integration. Nested beneath both codec-resolved flags, so FP8 requires both enabled. Unsupported cases retain the incumbent; explicit `0` kills the fold. |
| `HIPFIRE_FA_QRESIDENT_PM` | **unset = on for exact gfx1201** (off on other archs); `0` is the kill switch, `1` forces on | A3B H16/KV2/D256 native-fp8 Q-resident FA2 prefill attention (`Gpu::attention_fp8_e4m3_fa2_gqa_qresident_v2_q8_a3b_gfx1201` direct and `..._range_a3b_gfx1201`) on exact gfx1201: on (default) launches the embedded PeaceMaker twin `fa_qresident_a3b_pm_gfx1201` (`attention_fp8_e4m3_fa2_gqa_qresident_v2_q8_a3b_pm_gfx1201` / `..._range_a3b_pm_gfx1201`) instead of the hipcc module; `0` keeps the hipcc kernel byte-for-byte. The twin's output is byte-identical to hipcc (oracle 2694 cases, 0 mismatches) and 3.7-5.6% faster per launch; served A3B pp16000 +0.7% (6/6 reps). Same 68-byte kernarg, grid `[ceil(min(batch,512)*8/384),2,ceil(batch/512)]`, block 768, dynamic LDS 49,936 and the same shape/span/alias validation, launched through the same recorded blob path. Read through `FeatureFlags::fa_qresident_pm`; no typed config key. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_PACKED_MQ4_PREFILL` | Experimental, default off, exact gfx1100: `1` runs the dense 27B FFN gate/up and down (17408x5120 / 5120x17408) of uniform MQ4G256 models through the packed MQ4 GEMM (144-byte DS4 activation, native per-32 Q8 scale) for 256-multiple chunks. Full (not lean) PBS; graph capture and retained recording stay on the native route. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_MQ4_WIDE_PREFILL` | Experimental, default off, exact gfx1100: `1` admits dense 27B models whose projections are all uniform MQ4G256 to widened ordinary prefill (`HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` 512..8192, memory admission may reduce it) on native MMQ and full PBS, keeping 512-row recurrent commits. Rejects unaudited dispatch overrides. With `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_PACKED_MQ4_PREFILL=1`, complete 512-row chunks coalesce and irregular tails keep their legacy grouping, so each row's quantization route is unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_FLASH_PREFILL_FIXED_HD` | Developer ablation: fixed-head-dimension specialization is on unless `0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_FLASH_PREFILL_PREFETCH_V` | Developer ablation: gfx12 V prefetch is on unless `0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX11_FA2_PREFILL` | GQA-fused FA2 prefill on gfx1100/gfx1151 (Qwen NH24/NKV4/HD256, N 64..512 step 16, ctx 64..32768) — default ON (`kernel.gfx11_fa2_prefill`); `=0` opts out toward the byte-identical incumbent |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX11_Q8_FA2_WIDE` | Whole-chunk Q8/Q8 FA2 prefill (B 64..8192, above 512 aligned to 512; Qwen NH24/NKV4/HD256) — **auto ON on exact gfx1100 and exact gfx1151** (`kernel.gfx11_q8_fa2_wide`, experimental); other arches off; `=0` opts out. Requires `kernel.gfx11_fa2_prefill`. gfx1151 was enabled deliberately in commit `ae9c5cf9d12a5a63ae60630637d22d00a4fc964e` through the exact-gfx1151 FA2 twin (`HIPFIRE_GFX1151_FA2_TWIN`: CU mode, heaviest q-tiles first) with the same arithmetic, byte-identical output. Evidence (Strix Halo, H2 layer; unreleased [`CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md) entry): pp8192 ABBA +2.13 %, BAAB +2.11 %, captured layer −27.5 % / −29.7 %, KLD pins unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_FA2_FILL` | Warp-specialized K/V fill in that FA2 kernel on gfx1100/gfx1151 (bit-exact; helper waves dequantize the next K/V tile while compute waves run QK/PV) — default ON; `=0` restores the all-wave per-tile fill |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_FA2_R3` | Exact-gfx1100 variant of that FA2 fill body (bit-exact; CU mode, bank-conflict-free helper plane stores, O rescale skipped when alpha is exactly 1, heaviest q tiles first; symbols `attention_q8_0_fa2_gqa_gfx1100` / `attention_fa2_q_preconvert_gfx1100`) — default ON; `=0` restores the shared gfx11 body |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_FA2_SPLIT_VERIFY` | Default **on only on exact gfx1100** (`kernel.gfx1100_fa2_split_verify`); `=0` restores the incumbent verifier, and `HIPFIRE_VERIFY_ATTN=0` remains the independent parent opt-out. Replaces the plain-Q8 K-and-V DFlash verifier's eager R4/R8 attention with FA2 split-KV S8 only for dense Qwen H24/NKV4/HD256 sequential batches 4..32 on **VMM KV** once the **live logical context** exceeds `HIPFIRE_FA_PERTOKEN_MIN_CTX` (4,096 by default; smaller non-zero overrides are clamped to the measured crossover). An in-envelope load resolving legacy KV is refused rather than silently changing backend; use `memory.kv_backend=vmm` or opt out. A runtime that cannot reserve address space resolves legacy automatically (see `memory.kv_backend`); the route stays off there instead of refusing the load. HipGraph/retained recording still require the precompiled kernel set, fixed-address Q16 scratch and enough S8 `flash_partials`; undersized scratch fails closed. Redline/PM4 remains B=16 with its existing single-GPU/state guards. The split partial/merge reduction order is not byte-identical to the incumbent route; promotion is backed by logit KLD, a hard30 task tie and canonical-XTS DFlash A/B evidence. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_WMMA_VERIFY_ATTN` | Default **on only on exact gfx1100**; `=0` restores the incumbent verify attention (the FA2 split-KV verifier above, the VerifyAttn rows twin or the R4/R8 tile), and `HIPFIRE_VERIFY_ATTN=0` opts out too. Inside the admitted eager multi-row Q8 verify attend step (plain Q8 K and V, sequential non-tree, past `HIPFIRE_FA_PERTOKEN_MIN_CTX`; MTP K=3 verify = 4 rows) for dense Qwen H24/NKV4/HD256 and 1..=8 rows, runs the WMMA split-K kernel (`attention_wmma_verify.rs`, `kernels/src/attention_verify_wmma.gfx1100.hip`): a fixed grid of 4 kv heads x 24/36 key spans (interleaved 32-key tiles), K/V dequantized into f16 LDS planes, S = Q.K^T and O = P.V on wave32 WMMA for all 6 q heads x rows of a kv head, then a span reduce. Eager only; HipGraph capture and Redline recording keep the incumbent routes. Not byte-identical (f16 WMMA operands, f32 accumulation; KLD-gated); the modules are in the gfx1100 kernel registry and pack (no first-request JIT); logs one `[wmma-verify-attn] route active` line per kernel on first use. `bench_verify_attn_wmma` (rdna-compute example) checks it against an f64 reference and times it against the incumbent routes. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA2_SPLIT_VERIFY_FP8` | Native-fp8 split-KV FA2 verifier for the fp8 verify attend (`attention_flash_fp8_e4m3_tile_batched`: DFlash chain verify, MTP/ngram verify blocks and prefill tails), **default ON at 16 splits on exact gfx1201** once the LIVE context (`start_pos + rows`, never a verify graph's `physical_cap` grid) reaches 8,192, for blocks of 2..=16 rows (byte-identical to VerifyAttn below the floor and above 16 rows); `=0` (or any value outside `2..=64`) restores VerifyAttn, `=<2..=64>` picks the split count with no context floor and admits 2..=32 rows. Exact gfx1201 dense H24/KV4/D256, non-tree, not Redline-recording, partials large enough. **NUMERIC, not byte-identical above the floor.** The DFlash verify graph is capture-safe: a capture takes the route only when the module, merge and pre-convert entries are loaded and the Q scratch is grown (the verify graph prepares them right before capturing a window the route serves), each captured graph carries its route class and is re-captured when a later window's class differs, and the lane-batched CB DFlash verify keeps the singleton launch for lanes the singleton serves with the route (batched == singleton). The module is in the gfx1201 kernel inventory (compiles before ready). Read once per process. Evidence: unreleased [`CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md) entry. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_DRAFT_VOCAB` | Default **on only on exact gfx1100**; `=0` keeps the full-vocab MTP draft, `=1` also enables it on other arches (untested there). When a Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8 MTP head ships no compressed `lm_head_draft` (the 27B `.mtp`), the loader copies the rows of a fixed 65,536-token subset (`crates/hipfire-arch-qwen35/data/qwen35-draft-vocab-65536.bin`, compiled in) device-to-device out of the trunk lm_head (ascending ids, the trunk's own row bytes; 178 MiB on the 27B MQ4G256V2 trunk) plus the draft->full vocab map (`draft_vocab.rs`), so the existing compressed draft paths (greedy device token chain, p_min, cvs-sampled) draft over the subset instead of the 248,320-row lm_head. Verify and the accept rule are unchanged: greedy output is the trunk's, cvs-sampled stays distribution-exact, and a token outside the subset can only be rejected (tau). Logs one `MTP draft vocab:` line; keeps the full-vocab draft (logged) when the tokenizer's vocabulary SHA-256 differs from the subset's, the lm_head has no plain row layout, or the head has its own compressed sidecar. The subset is built only from committed public files (`examples/mtp_draft_vocab.rs`; `tests/draft_vocab_rebuild.rs` rebuilds it byte-for-byte). No new kernels. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_FA2_TWIN` | Exact-gfx1151 twin of that FA2 fill kernel (CU mode, heaviest q-tile first, conflict-free helper V stores; bit-exact) — default ON; `=0` restores the gfx11 module |
| `HIPFIRE_PM_DECODE` | Oracle-accepted PeaceMaker twins of the five W1 decode projection modules (QKVZA, QKV, gate/up, multirow-r2, residual MQ4v2) — **auto ON on exact gfx1201** (`kernel.pm_decode`); `=0` restores the hipcc modules, which stay in the pack. Embedded images are digest-pinned and refused on mismatch; outputs are byte-identical (27B retained PM4/graph traces, MTP state, KLD pins). Other arches ignore it. |
| `HIPFIRE_Q8_GEMV_FAST` | **`1`**; `0` | Developer / kill switch (`crates/rdna-compute/src/gemv.rs`, read once). Controls only the row-exact multi-row Q8_0 route: `Gpu::gemv_q8_0_rows_exact` with K > 1536 and 2..16 rows on gfx1100 / gfx1201 runs `gemv_q8_0_fast_r2..r16` (8 weight rows per 256-thread block, weights fetched with dword loads into LDS, the activation rows staged once per block, each weight byte applied to all rows, next chunk prefetched). Every output row has the same single ascending FMA chain per lane and the same shuffle tree as the decode `gemv_q8_0`, so the results are bit-identical to it. Requires K % 64 == 0 and a 4-byte-aligned weight / 16-byte-aligned activation; other shapes and archs, and a single row, keep the one-wave-per-row kernels. The decode `gemv_q8_0` itself never takes the fast kernel (measured slower at n == 1). `0` forces the one-wave-per-row kernels for the multi-row route too (A/B handle). |
| `HIPFIRE_PM_TUNE_TABLE` | Unset (the default): no table, no file I/O, shipped images and a byte-identical plan. `<path>` to a `hipfire.tune_table.v1` file exported by `pm-census` selects a promoted code object for the exact key `(arch, symbol, M, K)`; this slice covers only the non-bf16 slab `gemm_mq4g256v2_gate_up_silu_mmq_iu4_b1s` (27B prefill on gfx1201). The route planner and the dispatch selector read the same table, so the variant loads under module `pm_tune:<sha16>` and its kernel symbol is bound to it before the first dispatch. A row is refused with a one-time `pm_tune:` stderr note (the shipped image runs) when its `incumbent_image_sha256` is not the sha256 of this build's shipped image, its code object is unreadable or differs from `code_object_sha256`, or two rows share a key; a table that is unreadable or not a table document is a hard error at first lookup, and a symbol already bound to a different image in the process fails the launch. N is not keyed (one image per symbol per process). |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA2_PREFILL` | GQA-fused FA2 prefill on exact gfx1201 (same Qwen NH24/NKV4/HD256 envelope) — default ON (`kernel.gfx12_fa2_prefill`); `=0` opts out toward the byte-identical incumbent |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA_PACKET` | Packet-minimal Q128 FA2 body on exact gfx1201 (same Qwen envelope as `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA2_PREFILL`) — default ON (`kernel.gfx12_fa_packet`); `=0` opts out to the byte-identical route-N body |
| `HIPFIRE_ATTN_QRESIDENT_V2` | Bit-exact v2 schedule of the gfx1201 register-resident-Q FA2 prefill kernel (same Qwen envelope; only where `kernel.attn_qresident` selects the Q-resident route) — default ON (`kernel.attn_qresident_v2`); `=0` restores the byte-identical v1 Q-resident kernel |
| `HIPFIRE_MOE_A8_XLDS` | GEN-MOE packet A winner M3 inside `Gpu::gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_a8_sym` (W4A8 grouped routed experts, Raon prefill gate/up and down): symbol `moe_grouped_qt44_a8_sym_gfx1201_xlds_nt4r1w8` (`kernels/src/moe_grouped_qt44_a8_sym_xlds.gfx12.hip`, own module; the `nt{1,2}r{1,2}` module is untouched) — default **ON** (developer); `0` kills and restores the incumbent entry. Taken only on exact gfx1201 with `m_total >= MOE_A8_XLDS_MIN_ROWS` = 2304 (grouped rows, the launch bound, NOT tokens; receipt `receipts/selectors/moe_a8_xlds_min_rows.json`, `--row-key launch_rows`) and `M % 128 == 0`; everything else runs `select_entry_a8`'s incumbent entry. 256 threads (8 waves x one 16-row fragment = 128 weight rows), dynamic LDS 17920 (the X epoch tile of a 4-tile / 64-slot expert run staged once per CTA in a 2-stage ring), grid `[M/128, m_total/16, 1]`, same 72-byte kernarg image; bit-identical to the incumbent at every judged row class |
| `HIPFIRE_ATTN_FA2_GENERIC` | GEN-FA2 F2a flash-prefill body (`kernels/src/latent_attention_flash_fa2.hip`, symbol `latent_attention_flash_prefill_fa2_q128w8`) inside the generic `Gpu::latent_attention_flash_prefill` (BF16 non-wide path) and `Gpu::latent_attention_flash_prefill_q8` (Q8 mirror path, symbol `latent_attention_flash_prefill_fa2_q8_q128w8`, `kernels/src/latent_attention_flash_fa2_q8.hip`); the wide path is unchanged — default **ON** (developer); `0` kills and restores the incumbent. Taken on exact gfx1201 with `n_rows >= 17` (`FA2_MIN_ROWS` / `FA2_Q8_MIN_ROWS`, receipts `receipts/selectors/fa2_min_rows.json`, `fa2_q8_min_rows.json`) and on exact gfx1100 through the gfx11 twins (`kernels/src/latent_attention_flash_fa2.gfx1100.hip`, `latent_attention_flash_fa2_q8.gfx1100.hip`, same symbols, dynamic LDS 62464: the Q rope tail is staged in LDS) with `n_rows >= 256` (`FA2_GFX1100_MIN_ROWS` / `FA2_Q8_GFX1100_MIN_ROWS`, receipts `fa2_gfx1100_min_rows.json`, `fa2_q8_gfx1100_min_rows.json`), both at the judged geometry `wa 128 / wb 64 / dv 128`; everything else runs the incumbent. Block 256, dynamic LDS 44032, grid `[ceil(n_rows/128), n_heads, 1]`, same 21 kernargs; bit-identical to the incumbent on both the BF16 and Q8 mirror paths |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA_PREP_FUSED` | Exact gfx1201 FA Q/K norm and RoPE fusion (`kernel.gfx12_fa_prep_fused`); default ON only on gfx1201, `=0` restores separate launches |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA_PREP_FP8Q` | Preconvert Q to E4M3 codes for gfx1201 Q-resident v2 attention (`kernel.gfx12_fa_prep_fp8q`); default ON only on gfx1201, `=0` retains F32 Q; requires fused prep and Q-resident v2 |
| `HIPFIRE_FA_RAGGED_PACKET` | Developer kill switch (default ON, read through `developer_bool`). Exact gfx1201 native-fp8 KV, Qwen NH24/NKV4/HD256, full fp8q eligibility (fused prep, `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FA_PREP_FP8Q`, Q-resident and Q-resident v2 attention, no tree/independent/verify scope), ordinary widened prefill (`commit_stride` exactly 512) with `512 < n <= 32768` whose tail `n % 512` is 64..=511 and whose context ends exactly at the chunk end (`start_pos + n == max_ctx_len <= physical_cap`): the chunk's fp8q prep output is attended through the whole-`n` Raw Q (code plane then scale plane) as a whole-512-row prefix launch plus a tail launch, with one whole-`n` K/V write; the A4 attention epilogue is not used for such chunks and the existing whole-`n` sigmoid output producer still runs. `=0` restores the incumbent admission exactly (F32 prep and segmented attention for ragged chunks). Aligned chunks, tails below 64 rows and every other architecture, tier or mode are unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_FP8_DECODE_ATTN_GQA` | Exact-gfx1201 native-fp8 **decode** attention (head_dim 256, GQA group 6, tile 128, no output gate: H2): GQA-shared flash tile (one 256-thread workgroup per kv head and 128-key tile serves its six q heads, so K/V are read once) + head-dim-split reduce (`attention_flash_fp8_e4m3_tile_gqa_gfx1201` / `attention_flash_reduce_dsplit_gfx1201`); byte-identical partials and output — default ON; `=0` restores `attention_flash_fp8_e4m3_tile` + `attention_flash_q8_0_reduce` |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_DECODE_ATTN_GQA` | Exact-gfx1100 Q8_0 **decode** attention with the gated AWQ MQ-rotating epilogue (head_dim 256, GQA group 6, tile 128, full causal: H2): GQA-shared flash tile (`attention_flash_q8_0_tile_gqa_gfx1100`, one 256-thread workgroup per kv head and 128-key tile, K/V read once for the six q heads, every load issued at entry) + load-ahead reduce/gate/rotate (`attention_flash_q8_0_reduce_gated_mq_rotate_awq_dec_gfx1100`, one 1024-thread workgroup per head); byte-identical partials and output — default ON; `=0` restores `attention_flash_q8_0_tile` + `attention_flash_q8_0_reduce_gated_mq_rotate_awq_gfx1100` |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_Q8_DECODE_ATTN_GQA` | Exact-gfx1151 Q8_0 **decode** attention (head_dim 256, GQA group 6, tile 128, full causal, no output gate: H2): GQA-shared flash tile (one 256-thread workgroup per kv head and 128-key tile serves its six q heads, so K/V are read once) + head-dim-split reduce (`attention_flash_q8_0_tile_gqa_gfx1151` / `attention_flash_reduce_dsplit_gfx1151`); byte-identical partials and output — default ON; `=0` restores `attention_flash_q8_0_tile` + `attention_flash_q8_0_reduce` |
| `HIPFIRE_CALIB_BF16` | Calibration-only: keep native-BF16 teachers in BF16 (`kernel.calib_force_bf16`, default off; shipped inference unaffected) |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_GATEUP` / `_RESID` / `_QKVZA` / `_QKV` | gfx1201 FP8-WMMA MQ4v2 prefill route — default ON on exact gfx1201 (widened prefill via `prefill.chunk_rows`: 8192 by default on the Qwen35 dense route); `=0` on any one moves that family (its GEMM and its `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FP8_STREAM` producer) to the F16 path (chunk 384). `=1` forces on; launchers stay exact-gfx1201-only, so other arches are unchanged |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_SLABS` | Two-slab S2BT8 FP8 symbols by default; `=1` selects the single-slab symbols |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_V2` | gfx1201 FP8-WMMA MQ4v2 staged-tile v2 route — default ON on exact gfx1201 (`kernel.gfx12_mq4v2_fp8_v2`); `=0` restores the s2bt8/BT symbols. The four family flags remain prerequisites |
| `HIPFIRE_FP8_SYMFOLD` | Developer opt-out for centered FP8-v2 GEMM twins on exact gfx1201 symmetric MQ4V2 artifacts (`mq4v2.symmetric`): default enabled when the artifact marker and v2 route are both active; `=0` restores the asymmetric v2 entries. Non-symmetric artifacts are unchanged |
| `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_PLE_BRANCH_BATCHED_PREFILL` | Exact-arithmetic batched Gemma 4 E-series PLE branch projections (`kernel.gemma4_ple_branch_batched_prefill`, experimental) — **auto ON on exact gfx1100 and gfx1201**; other arches (gfx1101, gfx1102, gfx1151, gfx1200) off; `=0` opts out. Independent of `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_PLE_BATCHED_PREFILL` (model projection, default off). Benchmark history: [`GEMMA4_ESERIES_GFX1100_PREFILL.md`](GEMMA4_ESERIES_GFX1100_PREFILL.md), [`GEMMA4_ESERIES_LOGIT_STABILITY.md`](GEMMA4_ESERIES_LOGIT_STABILITY.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_V2_GEOM` | v2 tile geometry: `128x128` (default on exact gfx1201, the measured pin), `64x256`, `128x64`, `256x64` (prior default, still selectable) |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ6_FP8` | Exact-gfx1201 native-FP8 MQ6G256V2 prefill GEMM (`kernels/src/gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_fp8.gfx1201.hip`, `Gpu::gemm_mq6g256v2_fp8_prepared`): the 6-bit codes are decoded in-register into E4M3 WMMA operands (no weight conversion pass, no resident FP8 weight copy); activations come from the MQ4v2 FP8 producer. Eager-only, `K % 256 == 0`. Opt-in research route: `1` runs every eligible MQ6G256V2 prefill projection on it (any scope); unset or any other value keeps the F16 incumbent. On Flash-Next gfx1201 pp16384 it lost (exact `split` -6.5%, lossy `round` +2.7% but over the KLD bar), so it is off by default. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ6_FP8_ARM` | Weight-plane arm of the MQ6 FP8 GEMM: `split` (default, exact: `q-32 = 8*hi + lo` as two E4M3 planes, two WMMAs per tile, weight side bit-exact) or `round` (one lossy `E4M3_RNE(q-32)` plane, one WMMA per tile) |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ6_FP8_DB` | Developer: `1` selects the double-buffered continuous-pipeline variant of the MQ6 FP8 GEMM (`_db_` symbols; A/W/S/SZ double-buffered in LDS, raw prefetch of the next K64 slab, one barrier per slab, 1 block/CU); anything else (default) keeps the single-buffer kernel until measured |
| `HIPFIRE_MQ6_I8` | Exact-INT8 MQ6G256V2 prefill GEMM (`kernels/src/gemm_mq6g256v2_mmq_i8.gfx12.hip` / `.gfx11.hip`, `Gpu::gemm_mq6g256v2_i8_prequant`): the 6-bit codes enter `v_wmma_i32_16x16x16_iu8` raw and unsigned and the activations are the existing A8 `block_i8_128` blocks (one FWHT + A8 quantization per K shared by every MQ6 member of a projection group), centered through the accumulator seed so the integer core is exact; output F32 or BF16 (RNE). Eager-only, `K % 256 == 0`, never inside few-row / verify forwards. **Default ON on exact gfx1201 inside the Qwen4 (Flash-Next) forward at `route_rows >= 512` (the F16 PM route's own threshold)**: on Flash-Next pp16384 (R9700) it measured +1.1% (4680 → 4734 tok/s, 3 fresh processes) with the KLD pair inside the bar (WT2 +0.0006, code24 −0.0025). `0` opts out (the F16 PM route runs, byte for byte); `1` runs every eligible MQ6G256V2 prefill projection on it for any scope and any arch (exact gfx1201 / gfx1151). gfx1151 is opt-in only: there the iu8 rate equals F16 and the route measured slower than the incumbent. |
| `HIPFIRE_MQ6_I8_PM` | Selects the kernel of the exact-INT8 MQ6G256V2 prefill GEMM (`Gpu::gemm_mq6g256v2_i8_prequant`) on exact gfx1201: **default ON** is the embedded certified builder (PeaceMaker) module `kernels/gemm_mq6g256v2_mmq_i8_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (entries `gemm_mq6g256v2_mmq_i8_pm_set_gfx1201` / `_set_bf16_gfx1201`: grid `[ceil(M/128), ceil(N/128)]`, 256 threads, 36864 B dynamic LDS, and the default-for-wide-launches `_t128x256` pair: grid `[ceil(M/128), ceil(N/256)]`, 512 threads, 55296 B; `mq6_i8_pm_token_tile` picks the 128x256 tile unless it would pad more tokens than the 128-token tile or spread its CTAs over the WGPs worse, e.g. M=128, N=4096), bit-identical to the hipcc `gemm_mq6g256v2_mmq_i8_{set,set_bf16}_gfx12` (same integer core and fold chain; checked by the `i8pm` arm of `mq6_fp8_gemm_bench`). `1` and unset take the twin for every launch the INT8 route admits that also meets its preconditions (`K % 256 == 0`, `M % 4 == 0`, 4-byte aligned `Y`, output and weight extents under 2^31 bytes); a launch that does not meet them runs the hipcc kernel. `0` restores the hipcc kernel everywhere. Does not change `HIPFIRE_MQ6_I8` admission. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_GDN_PRE_FUSED` | gfx1201 batched-prefill GDN preamble fusion (sigmoid+conv+qknorm 3→1, byte-exact) — default ON on exact gfx1201 (`kernel.gfx12_gdn_pre_fused`); `=0` restores the 3-launch sequence |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_GDN_SCAN` | Exact-gfx1151 twin of the GDN chunk scan (`gdn_chunk_scan_gfx1151`: pipelined chunk loop, K staged transposed, one value half per 256-thread workgroup; byte-identical `out` and state) — default ON; `=0` restores `gdn_chunk_scan` |
| `HIPFIRE_V2B_A4_EPI` | Developer A4-fusion arm of the exact-gfx1151 V2B gate/up SiLU launch (eager prefill only; admitted only where the certified V2B tile is: `gate_m == up_m`, `gate_m % 256 == 0`, `batch % 128 == 0`, `k % 256 == 0`; graph capture and Redline recording keep the unfused path). Unset, `1` and any other value (default ON on exact gfx1151): the fused kernel `gemm_mq4g256v2_gate_up_silu_a4_iu4_pm_v2b_gfx1151` also writes the w_down A4 sidecar (72-byte `block_i4_128` records) into a dedicated scratch slot, so `h` is not written and the hin producer is skipped; byte-identical. `0`: opt-out, the V2B SiLU + `fused_silu_hin_rotate_mq_i4_batched` path. `retile`: the stage-1 m512 twin replaces the V2B SiLU launch (same `h`, same downstream producer). Read on every call, so one process can switch arms. `HIPFIRE_V2B_PM=0` and `HIPFIRE_F1LITE=0` also disable it; other arches are unaffected |
| `HIPFIRE_V2B_A4_PM_BUNDLE` | Developer: `=<path>` loads the A4-fusion builder bundle (`gemm_mq4g256v2_gate_up_silu_a4_iu4_pm_v2b_gfx1151.hxaco`) from a file instead of the embedded image, for every `HIPFIRE_V2B_A4_EPI` arm except `0`. Read once; an unreadable file fails every A4-fusion launch |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FP8_STREAM` | gfx1201 RMSNorm+rotate producer → MQ4v2 FP8 pre-pass fusion (byte-identical `prepare_mq4v2_fp8_x_f32` outputs for the qkvza/gate_up/qkv/residual inputs; standalone pack launch disappears) — default ON on exact gfx1201 (`kernel.gfx12_fp8_stream`); `=0` opts out; other arches off. A producer runs only for a family whose `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_*` route is on (uniform MQ4v2) |
| `HIPFIRE_G12_NORM` | gfx1201 `_v2` RMSNorm and gated-norm int4 producers (batched sum-of-squares loads + one-reciprocal RTN codes; one wave per gated-norm group; bit-identical) — default ON on exact gfx1201 (`kernel.g12_norm`); `=0` restores the incumbent `_gfx12` symbols |
| `HIPFIRE_G12_DEC_NORM` | gfx1201 and gfx1151 decode norms as multi-workgroup grids (f32 AWQ RMSNorm+FWHT: K/256 workgroups, each redoing the row's reduction and rotating one group; out-of-place single-row `rmsnorm_f32`: n/256 workgroups; half-split partial RoPE: one workgroup per head; bit-identical) — default ON on exact gfx1201 and exact gfx1151 (`kernel.g12_dec_norm`); `=0` restores the single-workgroup launches |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_DEC_NORM` | gfx1100 decode norms as multi-workgroup grids (the `HIPFIRE_G12_DEC_NORM` twins built for gfx1100: f32 AWQ RMSNorm+FWHT K/256 workgroups, out-of-place single-row `rmsnorm_f32` n/256 workgroups; bit-identical) — default ON on exact gfx1100 (`kernel.gfx1100_dec_norm`); `=0` restores the single-workgroup launches |
| `HIPFIRE_G12_A4C2` | gfx1201 int4 producers search two activation scales ({5,7}, as gfx11's `-DIU4_A4_CANDIDATES=2`) instead of RTN d = amax/7; one-pass producer-layout search, bit-identical to that flag — default ON on exact gfx1201 (`kernel.g12_a4c2`; appends `-DIU4_A4_CANDIDATES=2` to the gfx1201 JIT flags); `=0` restores RTN |
| `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` | Widened ordinary-prefill chunk ceiling (`prefill.chunk_rows`). The registered schema value `512` is only a fallback and is not exported to the process config, so an unset knob resolves per model: the Qwen35 dense 27B widened route takes the architecture default 8192 on exact gfx1100/gfx1151/gfx1201, the resident-MoE rule below applies to Qwen35 MoE, and every other route keeps its own incumbent chunk (explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH` wins; VRAM admission may admit a smaller rung). **Qwen3.5-class resident MoE (A3B/A10B/A17B), memory-aware 4096:** the widened route used to admit only the dense 27B shape, so MoE kept the incumbent per-arch chunk (512 on exact gfx1100, 384 or 512 under the full FP8 path on exact gfx1201, 256 elsewhere). Those are the dense MQ4V2 gate-up sweet spots measured on those cards and 512 matches the dense 512-row commit seam; they are not a measured MoE optimum. With the knob unset, a MoE now defaults to one 4096-row chunk, never an intermediate 1024/2048 rung, and only when the model is fully resident on one device (no pager, EP shard, host-mapped or CPU-deferred experts or projections), every layer passes batched-prefill producer admission, the routed and shared experts are uniform MQ4G256 or MQ4G256V2 (MQ6, E8, Lloyd, PARO and mixed tiers keep the incumbent), the DeltaNet state is Q8 with its EF owners and single-end requant, the KV tier has batched flash attention, and the device has the WMMA grouped producer (capability, not card or tag) — and only when the projected full prefill scratch, including the grouped MoE scratch, the DeltaNet `dn_z_fold_batch`/`dn_normed_batch` buffers (the legacy projection omits them; the resident census adds a checked `rows * (2 * v_dim + 256) * 4` bytes for the rung, the owner and the cache credit, and the global legacy placement mirror is unchanged), the projection prelude and the existing 1 GiB transient/driver headroom (requested tensor payload only), plus the flash-attention Q16 scratch of the full selected 4096-row chunk (net of live Q16 bytes) fits the actual free VRAM (net of pending deductions and of a cached widened owner only when this call actually reuses it in place: non-lean, rows covering the call's owned PBS, no replacement; a smaller, lean or mismatched cached owner is freed and replaced, `free_gpu` only returns it to the allocator pool (bucket-local, no guaranteed physical reclaim), so it earns no credit and the full rung is charged; the Q16 direct-HIP live-growth credit is unchanged; capture, tree-verify, GDN-tape, hidden-ring, recording, max-layer, fusion and cap gates, the call's actual row count N (the batched path must run for N and for the 512-row seam) and the scalar/config-off facts are shared and evaluated before any virtual PBS promotion, KV mapping, override or allocation, and a negative reuses the on/off decision so the incumbent is preserved; the wrapper's virtual same-target owner is model-bound (held in the model's scratch across calls) and credited only when reused without replacement, the implicit wrapper owner never; the DeltaNet state geometry check covers every S and EF owner through borrowed iterators without temporary `Vec` allocation; the default reuses the existing explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH=4096` PBS cache owner and its clamps, so a caller that clips to 512 rows can still execute 512); a failed free-memory query or an overflow keeps the incumbent. **This is a default-admission change onto the existing explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH=4096` path (the j1405 reference set both `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH=4096` and `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS=4096`; `prefill.chunk_rows` alone never raised the legacy MoE ceiling):** no MoE kernel or commit code changed, the receipt's generic `commit_stride=512` does not by itself prove the MoE kernels commit per 512 rows, and the dense 512-row commit is unchanged. Precedence: explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH` leaves the incumbent unchanged; an explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` / `prefill.chunk_rows` below 4096 opts the MoE out, and 4096 or more requests the single 4096 rung (never wider). The first multi-token prefill, and any later change of the admitted ceiling, logs `prefill_chunk: requested=R admitted=A commit_stride=S` (a fallback logs the incumbent and `commit_stride=none`). Offloaded (host-mapped), TP, EP, dense, Qwen4 and narrow-link 8192 loads are unaffected. Qwen4 (Flash-Next) reads it as its prefill chunk ceiling, rounded down to 256 rows. **Placement:** the auto expert placement reserve, forward census and pool workspace (hence the resident-layer split) are sized from the same chunk: the explicit value, else 8192 on exact gfx1151, on exact gfx1201 16384 under the compact Flash-Next forward-scratch plan (`FnSymGfx1201`, the 0.4.3 default for slab and symmetric-IU4 artifacts; phase-arena scratch 4776 MiB at 16384 rows against 4328 MiB for 4096 rows under the full plan; faster long prefill, slower decode, since fewer routed experts stay resident than at a 4096-row compact chunk; `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS=4096` is the documented override for decode-heavy use) and 4096 under the full plan (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_COMPACT_SCRATCH=0`), 1536 elsewhere. Exact gfx1100 on a measured narrow discrete host link (< 16 GB/s, the load-time probe described at `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE`, run before placement is sized) defaults to 8192 rows, so the placement cedes resident expert layers for the wider chunk; gfx1100 on a fast, unmeasured or unified-memory link keeps 1536, and no other arch and no explicit value ever probes. **Explicit values are authoritative:** `=1536` opts out of the narrow-link default, `=8192` charges the 8192-row reserve on any link. One gfx1100 XTX hold (single run each, same model and prompt shapes, `g2-xtx-default/hold-20261017T102413Z`): 1536-row placement 10 resident layers, pp512 72.2 / pp8192 90.1 / tg64 5.71 tok/s; 8192-row chunk 5 resident layers, pp512 62.0 / pp8192 931.7 (10.3x) / tg64 5.09 tok/s (-14% pp512, -11% tg64). The narrow-link default's own performance is not claimed here. The PLE row staging follows the requested ceiling, and load admission steps down 8192/4096/2048/1536 until the chunk scratch fits free VRAM with 1 GiB to spare. Flash-Next pipeline parallelism (`topology.pp`) on gfx1201 defaults to 4096-row chunks (single card keeps 16384 under the compact plan); an explicit value wins and `16384` restores the previous PP chunk |

### LFM (arch 11) — lowered forward and evidence scope

| Variable | Status | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_LFM2_GRAPH` | LFM graph experiments | Source in LFM crate/daemon |
| `HIPFIRE_FORWARD_LOWERED` | default on (several other arches); `=0` opts out | Shared lowered forward escape hatch. **LFM and the Qwen3.5 family no longer read it**: their decode is one typed step program with no hand loop to fall back to (for Qwen3.5 this includes DFlash hidden capture, VL mrope and CPU-spilled models, whose GEMV steps take the CPU splice). The sealed LFM [`admissions.yml`](admissions.yml) evidence row still records `=0` as a historical knob of that screen; **not** an automatic Redline runtime-default opt-out. |
| Other `HIPFIRE_LFM*` | diag/trace | Inventory |

`HIPFIRE_LFM2_PREFILL_BATCH`, `HIPFIRE_LFM2_PREFILL_MAX_BATCH`, and `HIPFIRE_LFM2_GFX1201_DECODE_FUSION` have **no reader under `crates/`** in this tree: setting them changes nothing, LFM prefill is the eager loop, and there is no batched-prefill or decode-fusion selector. They appear only in design/planning docs, the sealed [`admissions.yml`](admissions.yml) evidence row (historical `explicit_opt_outs`, not runtime opt-outs), and branch-pinned wording (`lfm-redline@692a726dde53508cb53de1a74c720e75a7c9f33e`, absent from `origin/beta@202282de…`). [`admissions.yml`](admissions.yml) remains the sole product-admission authority (schema v2; exactly one earned retained-PM4 product row).

### CASK (deprecated, removal in 0.5.0) / serve / multi-GPU

CASK / TriAttention eviction is deprecated and will be removed in 0.5.0; it is
not supported. `HIPFIRE_CASK_SIDECAR` is the legacy env alias of
`memory.cask.sidecar` and goes with it; setting it (or any CASK key) makes the
daemon print a deprecation warning at
load. `HIPFIRE_CASK_OFF` is a retired compatibility name that the Rust control
plane does not consume; CASK is already off unless `memory.cask.sidecar` is
set or `memory.cask.auto_attach=true`. The old name remains only in developer
harness exports pending their cleanup.

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_IDLE_TIMEOUT` | Serve idle unload seconds |
| `HIPFIRE_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` | Body cap |
| `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MAX_QUEUE` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_QUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS` | Admission queue |
| `HIPFIRE_SERVE_VMM_BATCH` | Compatibility spelling of `serve.vmm_batch` (default off, experimental): the VMM continuous-batching route for concurrent requests. Qwen dense uniform MQ4G256V2 also admits exact gfx1100 with Q8 VMM KV: complete singleton target layers run per lane, with no cross-request DFlash/MTP verification installed; Q8 recurrent state requires error feedback, otherwise serve staging keeps the singleton route.
