Also gates the Flash-Next (qwen4, arch 16) exact AR fn-lanes route, which additionally needs `serve.continuous_batch_size >= 2` (greedy text chat only; see [SERVE.md](SERVE.md#flash-next-exact-lane-batching-qwen4-arch-16)). `0` stages no lane store and keeps the serial singleton route. **Default for Flash-Next EP:** `hipfire serve` on a Qwen4 (`arch_id` 16) HFQ with `topology.ep` >= 2 over exact gfx1201 cards batches by default (`serve.vmm_batch` on, `serve.continuous_batch_size` 8, `hipfire-cli` `serve_auto_batch_*`) when neither key is set by any source (CLI `--continuous-batch-size`, config, registry, `HIPFIRE_SERVE_VMM_BATCH` / `HIPFIRE_CONTINUOUS_BATCH_SIZE`); an explicit value, including `0` / `false` / `1`, is the kill switch. Every other model, topology and GPU keeps the built-in `off` / `1`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EXPERIMENTAL_BUDGET_ALERT` | Research budget nudge |
| `HIPFIRE_FA_PERTOKEN_MIN_CTX` | Context length past which an exact-gfx1100, exact-gfx1201 or (opt-in) exact-gfx1151 Q8 small-batch (n = 4..32, head_dim 128/256, sequential non-tree, HIP graph capture off, retained replay recording off) attend step leaves the batched flash kernel for the multi-row tile; default `4096` on gfx1100/gfx1201, unset on gfx1151 (setting any value > 0 opts gfx1151 in), `0` disables the route. Other arches, KV modes, shapes, and semantics retain the batched route. |
| `HIPFIRE_RCCL_LIB` | Explicit `librccl.so` path, tried before the ROCm root. For distributions whose ROCm prefix does not carry RCCL (nixpkgs: `rocmtoolkit-merged` has HIP/HSA, `librccl` is a separate store path). |
| `HIPFIRE_DEVICES` / `HIPFIRE_DEVICE` / `HIPFIRE_TP_USE_RCCL` | Multi-GPU / TP. (`HIPFIRE_TP` is not an input: no daemon, CLI or loader code reads it, only the parity example `qwen_dense_tp2_parity.rs`; the tensor/expert-parallel degree is `--tp N` / `params.tp`.) `HIPFIRE_DEVICES` (singular alias `HIPFIRE_DEVICE`; conflicting values fail) is the compatibility spelling of `hardware.devices`: comma-separated, in logical order, each entry a PCI-order index (`rocm-smi` order, not ROCr/HIP ordinals), `gfxNNNN` (first free card of that arch; repeat for more), `GPU-<uuid>`, or PCI address `[DDDD:]BB:DD.F` (required for no-UUID cards). Resolved from the KFD topology without touching a GPU, reserved, lowered to ROCr UUIDs/ordinals plus HIP `0..N-1`, and checked against HIP's arch and PCI bus ID after init. See [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#device-selection). |
| `HIPFIRE_EMULATE_GPUS` | **Developer-only logical GPU emulation.** A successfully parsed integer `>=2` enables; missing, malformed, `0`, and `1` disable. Requested logical IDs are aliased modulo the loaded physical count; this switch does not choose the EP rank count and is not a product admission. Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP honors it too: with `topology.ep = N` (2..=8), rank `r` runs on physical device `r % count`, one visible device is enough, each rank's free VRAM is its device's free VRAM divided by the ranks aliased onto it, and same-device peer checks are skipped. Under emulation Flash-Next EP also runs on gfx1151 unified memory (Strix Halo); that proves byte parity on one device, never a physical EP admission. |
| `HIPFIRE_EMU` | **Developer-only SKU application-capacity profile** (`developer.emu`; default unset = off; process start only, read from the startup snapshot). `<id>` is an exact lower-case registry id from `hipfire emu list` (`rx9070xt`, `rx9070`, `rx9060xt`, `rx9060xt-8g`, `rx7900xt`, `rx7800xt`, `rx7700xt`, `rx7600`, `strix-point`, `krackan`); unset, empty or `off` disables; an unknown id fails, it never silently disables. The profile caps this process's *managed application allocation* (device, pinned-host, VMM and ROCr kernarg backing) to the target SKU's memory capacity and routes the sizing queries through the same budget. It does **not** emulate CUs, clocks, power or the target ISA: the host runs at its own speed on all its CUs, and the profile is refused on a host whose detected gfx, integrated/discrete kind, physical CUs or total memory cannot stand in for it. CU masks are never used (refused on RDNA). `hipfire bench --emu <id\|off>` sets it for every bench daemon and overrides this variable, including `off`. Unrelated to `HIPFIRE_EMULATE_GPUS` (logical multi-GPU) and `HIPFIRE_EMU_BF16_H` (kernel switch). See [EMU.md](EMU.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_EMU_HOST_BYTES` / `HIPFIRE_EMU_CARVEOUT_BYTES` / `HIPFIRE_EMU_HOST_RESERVE_BYTES` / `HIPFIRE_EMU_GTT_BYTES` | **UMA scenario overrides for `HIPFIRE_EMU=strix-point\|krackan`** (`developer.emu_host_bytes`, `developer.emu_carveout_bytes`, `developer.emu_host_reserve_bytes`, `developer.emu_gtt_bytes`). Each is a checked unsigned decimal byte count; empty = default. `_HOST_BYTES` is the emulated laptop RAM H (default 32 GiB = `34359738368`), `_CARVEOUT_BYTES` the BIOS/display carve-out C (default 1 GiB = `1073741824`), `_HOST_RESERVE_BYTES` the OS/application headroom R (default 4 GiB = `4294967296`), `_GTT_BYTES` the aggregate virtual GTT limit G, which is the emulated device capacity (default 16 GiB = `17179869184`). Managed shared-host allowance S = H − C − R (default 27 GiB); `C + R < H` and `0 < G <= S` are required. Setting any of them without an active profile, or with a discrete profile, fails. These are explicit scenario assumptions, not AMD limits. They are unrelated to `HIPFIRE_VRAM_BUDGET_BYTES` (examples only). |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_PEER_ALLREDUCE_DECODE=1` | Explicit peer all-reduce selector for logical EP decode proofs; do not set it to `0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_EP_PEER_ALLREDUCE=1` | Explicit peer all-reduce selector for logical EP batched prefill/tick proofs; do not set it to `0`. |
| `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_DIRECT` / `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_ALTERNATING` / `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH` | Developer-only dense-TP2 residual reduction controls; every route computes the same rank-ordered `x + (p0 + p1)`. `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_DIRECT=0` replaces the default symmetric direct-peer read with the rooted fold. `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_ALTERNATING` and `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH` are tri-state (`1` forces on, `0` forces off, unset takes the default); **both default on for an exact gfx1201 pair and off everywhere else**, so `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH=0` is the gfx1201 kill switch. `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_ALTERNATING` uses the fact that the attention and FFN partials live in different buffers: each reduction waits only on the peer's producer event, without the consumer-complete reverse wait; unset follows `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH`, and `=0` also disables the whole-token graph. `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH` admits an exact homogeneous gfx1100 pair or an exact homogeneous gfx1201 pair (TP=2, peer access, AR decode; mixed or other arches are not admitted) and captures each rank's whole decode token as one hipGraph, with device-resident signal barriers and a host-mapped coherent payload exchange; enabling it turns on the alternating protocol unless that is set to `0`. **TP4:** an exact homogeneous gfx1201 quad on the generic mesh route takes the same whole-token graph by default (`HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH=0` or `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_PUSH=0` restores the eager event route), with every collective a bf16 peer-push kernel instead of the pack, three copies, fold and cross-device events (see `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_PUSH`). See [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#dense-tp2-reduction-protocol-developer). |
| `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_PUSH` | **Developer-only tri-state (`1` on, `0` off).** Selects the tagged peer-VRAM reduction for gfx1201 tensor parallelism: each rank pushes its partial straight into the other ranks' fine-grained VRAM mailboxes (`hipDeviceMallocFinegrained`, allocated before peer access is enabled) as 16-byte granules `{d0, g, d1, g}` (data and the generation tag twice, system-scope write-through b128 stores) and polls only its own mailbox: one kernel per rank per collective, no fence, no separate flag, no cross-device read or read-modify-write, bounded wait, two banks alternating with a device-resident generation. **TP4 (unset = on):** the bf16 exchange (`x + (r(p0) + r(p1) + r(p2) + r(p3))`, bit-identical to the event/copy route) is what lets the whole-token decode graph capture a TP4 token (see `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH`); `0` keeps the eager event route and also disables the TP4 graph. **TP2 (unset = off):** the f32 variant replaces the host-mapped coherent exchange inside the TP2 graph; bit-identical, collective chain 9.2 -> 4.3 us but decode only 54.5 -> 54.9 tok/s (+0.6%), so it is opt-in (`1`). The load qualifies the mailboxes with 64 eager exchanges compared bit for bit with a host replay; a failure disables the route for the process and prints why. Used only for decode reductions that fit the mailboxes (at most 8,192 floats; TP4 also needs the bf16 exchange, i.e. `HIPFIRE_TP_EXCHANGE` unset/`auto`/`bf16`). |
| `HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_PUSH_FAULT` | **Developer-only fault injection** for the TP peer-push graph route, read once at load: `rank:replay[:ms]` stalls `rank`'s stream for `ms` milliseconds (default 900, capped at 1500, well inside the ~2.1 s window in which a stuck dispatch would suspend the host) before the `replay`-th whole-token graph launch, once, so the other ranks' bounded waits (500 ms) time out. The step then fails with the device wait error and the generations and mailboxes are reset; the next request runs normally. Malformed values are ignored with a message. Never set it in production. |
| `HIPFIRE_TP_EXCHANGE` | **Developer-only A/B knob.** Format policy the dense Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8 TP row-parallel all-reduce exchanges between ranks (read once per process): unset or `auto` (default) takes `bf16` for every multi-row reduction (prefill, verify) at any TP degree and for single-row decode at TP>=3, and `f32` for single-row decode at TP2 (measured -1.4% with bf16 there); `bf16` forces bf16 everywhere and converts each rank's f32 partial to bf16 once, and every rank sums the rounded, rank-ordered partials in f32 into its f32 residual (`x + (r(p0) + r(p1) + …)`); `f16` does the same with IEEE half (a partial above 65,504 in magnitude becomes infinity: not guarded); `f32` forces the f32 exchange (`x + (p0 + p1 + …)`) everywhere. The residual stream stays f32. It covers the TP2 direct-peer read, the TP2 whole-token graph and the TP>2 rooted/all-pull folds of both the hand and the generic dense-TP routes (they stay byte-identical to each other), for peer-access transport between distinct devices with 2-4 ranks; host-staged transport, same-device emulation, EP and the no-target `AllReduce` stay f32. Numeric route, not byte-identical to `f32`: judged by KLD (see [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#dense-tp-partial-exchange-16-bit-developer)). An unrecognised value is reported and falls back to `f32`. The dense TP load log prints `[loader] dense qwen TP exchange=<mode>`. |
| `HIPFIRE_DENSE_TP_ROUTE` | **Developer-only.** Which route `serve --tp N` loads for a dense Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8 model (`developer.dense_tp_route`; read at load): `auto` (default; also unset or empty) loads the generic mesh executor (`qwen35::mesh_tp`, every KV backend, AR decode and chunked prefill, peer or host-staged reduction chosen by byte qualification) whenever it admits the load, else the hand dense-TP route with the log line `[loader] dense qwen TP route=auto -> hand: <reason>` (`speculation.mtp = on`, or an uneven TP3/TP5 split); `mesh` forces the mesh route and refuses MTP and uneven splits; `hand` is the rollback to the hand route. Any other value is a load error. The mesh route builds no drafter and refuses sessions and prefix repair. See [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#generic-mesh-executor-tp-collectives-developer). |
| `HIPFIRE_ALLOW_MIXED_ARCH=1` | Mixed arch pairs |
| `HIPFIRE_PP_LAYERS` | Pipeline-parallel band layer counts: `a,b,…` lists the trunk layer count of each band in **logical band order** (the first count is band 0, the first layers; the last count is the last band; it is not the card order), `pp` entries, none zero, summing to the trunk layer count. Read once at admission; unset or empty gives the uniform split (remainder on the first bands). **Flash-Next/Qwen4** (`topology.pp` 2..=8) also needs band 0 to hold at least 2 layers (the PLE layer) and runs the last band on the output card, physical card 0; a bad list is refused naming `topology.pp` and `HIPFIRE_PP_LAYERS`. **Flash-Next/Qwen4 auto-fit:** with the list unset the load keeps the uniform split at the default prefill chunk whenever every card holds it; when a card is short it steps the chunk down (2048, 1024, 512, 256; only rungs below the default) to the largest chunk at which some split fits, takes the most balanced fitting split (lowest maximum required/free, ties to the split closest to uniform) and logs `qwen4 PP auto-fit: layers a,b chunk C (uniform u,v at chunk R short by X MiB on rank r)`. Setting the list pins the split and turns the search off entirely (a short card refuses exactly as before); an explicit `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` is never stepped down and only the split is searched. The chunk changes numerics, the split does not. The generic Qwen3.5/3.6 route (`params.pp`) skips the uniform free-VRAM delta check when it is set. See [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#band-placement-hipfire_pp_layers) and [Per-band memory budget](multi-gpu.md#per-band-memory-budget). |
| `HIPFIRE_TOPOLOGY_PP` | Compat env for `topology.pp` (experimental; integer `1..=8`, default `1`): the pipeline degree `hipfire run`, `serve` and `bench` send as the load's `pp`. There is **no load-time `--pp` CLI flag** (`hipfire bench --pp` is the prompt-size list). An explicit `params.pp` above `1` on a daemon load wins and this value is not read; a request of `1` or none uses it. An invalid configured value is refused naming `1..=8` only when it is the value in effect. Flash-Next/Qwen4 admits `2..=8` with `tp = ep = 1` and host-staged edges; generic Qwen3.5/3.6 HFQ uses the same degree over peer memcpys. See [multi-gpu.md](multi-gpu.md#flash-next-pipeline-parallelism-host-staged). |
| `HIPFIRE_MESH_TRANSPORT` | **Developer-only.** How the admitted pipeline mesh moves each band edge's HC streams; read once at admission, only for Flash-Next (Qwen4) `topology.pp` 2..=8: `auto` (default; also unset or empty), `host` or `peer`. `auto` and `host` are host-staged: two pinned host banks per edge, peer access is never enabled. The decision is per directed edge and made before any allocation or peer enable; there is no runtime fallback. `peer` needs pre-side-effect byte-qualification evidence for every edge; none exists yet, so `peer` is refused at admission naming `topology.pp`, and `auto` resolves to host-staged edges. Any other value is refused (`is not one of auto, peer, host`). The generic Qwen3.5/3.6 PP route does not read it: its band edges are peer memcpys. |
| `HIPFIRE_PP_PFLASH` / `HIPFIRE_PP_DFLASH` | **Experimental, generic Qwen3.5/3.6 PP only** (`pp>1`; `HIPFIRE_PP_PFLASH` is deprecated with PFlash). `=1` makes the daemon accept a PFlash compose or a DFlash `draft` that it otherwise refuses with `pp>1` (DFlash: cross-card speculative generate is not fully implemented). Neither is an admission, and neither changes Flash-Next PP, which still refuses PFlash and DFlash. |
| `HIPFIRE_UNIFORM_VRAM_TOLERANCE_GB` | Uniform init tolerance |
| `HIPFIRE_SLOTS_PAGED` / `HIPFIRE_SLOTS_PAGED_PAGES` | Multi-slot engine: `1`/`on`/`true` selects the paged KV pool instead of fixed per-slot slabs (implied by `serve.prefix_cache`); `_PAGES` overrides the physical page count (clamped to ≥ 1). |
| `HIPFIRE_VL_SEQUENTIAL` | Multi-slot engine: `1`/`on`/`true` forces the legacy one-image-at-a-time VL prefill. Refused together with the paged pool. |
| `HIPFIRE_VL_PREFILL` | **Developer-only.** Single-slot daemon image-turn prefill route: `auto` (default; unset or any other value) runs the whole image prompt through the batched `forward_prefill_batch` chunks with the vision embeddings spliced over the `<\|image_pad\|>` rows and the same absolute M-RoPE phases the per-token path uses, when the model is dense and batch-admissible, the KV tier has batched attention, and eviction/multi-GPU are off; otherwise it falls back to the per-token loop. `token` forces the per-token loop (the pre-batched behaviour). Logged as `[daemon/vl] prefill: batched` or `prefill: per-token (reason)`. Batched prefill uses the batched GEMM/DeltaNet/attention kernels, so greedy output may differ from `token` in late tokens (the text-prefill-vs-decode contract); decode is unchanged. |
| `HIPFIRE_PAGE_EVICTION` | `0` keeps model pages in the host page cache after upload (default: evict only on UMA). Shared by the sequential loader and the multi-slot engine. |
| `HIPFIRE_BENCH_MULTI_SLOT` / `HIPFIRE_BENCH_MULTI_SLOT_SLOTS` / `HIPFIRE_BENCH_MULTI_SLOT_CTX` | `hipfire bench`: load the model on the multi-slot engine with the given slot count and per-slot context. |
| `HIPFIRE_MTP_TRACE=1` | Per-cycle MTP draft/verify trace on stderr. |
| `HIPFIRE_DEBUG_POOL_INVARIANTS=1` | Multi-slot engine: assert page-pool / slot-lease invariants after every scheduler tick (debug; slow). |
| `HIPFIRE_FAULT_HIP` / `HIPFIRE_FAULT_PREFIX_PUBLISH` / `HIPFIRE_FAULT_MTP_FULL_REJECT` | Test-only fault injection used by `test_serve_prefix_cache`: `HIP` fails one `upload`/`launch`/`sync` below the HIP bridge (`HIP=reserve:<N>` makes the first `N` `hipMemAddressReserve` calls return `hipErrorOutOfMemory`, the way a runtime that cannot reserve address space does; `scripts/vmm_reserve_fallback_check.sh` drives it), `PREFIX_PUBLISH=1` fails the first prefix-cache publication, `MTP_FULL_REJECT=1` forces every MTP draft to be rejected. Never set in production. |
| `HIPFIRE_MODULE_LOAD_AUDIT=1` | Developer-only module-load audit (default off): records HIP recipes, module handles/images and resolved symbols for offline route-closure audits. Qwen3.5-family loads also print `route plan axes:` with the resolved architecture, heads, speculation route, context ceiling, KV/state formats, backend and prefill chunk, including when `HIPFIRE_ROUTE_PLAN=0` is used. A sealed route has every planned module bound before `loaded` and no unplanned loads afterwards. Never set in production or timing runs. |

`HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_MAX_ACTIVE` (developer; default 8, native C8 -- was 3): the step-level
switch of multi-card native-MTP lanes (`lane_switch::max_active`) runs native windows while a
step carries at most this many lanes and AR windows from `max_active + 2` lanes up (the
previous count holds in between), and only that many store slots carry MTP state; at the
default every slot of an 8-lane serve store runs native windows. `=3` restores the previous
switch (AR from 5 lanes up).

`HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_NATIVE_ALL=1` (developer, default off) makes every lane slot
an MTP lane and stops the step-level switch from forcing AR windows by lane count
(`lane_switch::max_active` becomes unbounded, `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_MAX_ACTIVE` is
then ignored); the per-lane floor (`LaneCostModel`-priced, AR-row units) decides per
window whether native beats an AR row. With it on and `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_K` unset the
lane draft-depth cap is 5 instead of 2 (each lane's `batched_depth` picks its own depth
from its measured per-position acceptance, so code lanes run deep and story lanes stop at
2-3). It reserves MTP state for every lane (fewer resident experts) and is not a default:
the 8-lane sampled oracle flags slot 7 when slot 7 carries MTP state.

Flash-Next/Qwen4 PP native MTP serves greedy requests and sampled requests with
the default Leviathan verify (`HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED_MODE` unset or `leviathan`)
and neutral penalties. Penalized requests (repeat/presence/frequency),
sampled requests with `HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED=0` (`speculation.mtp_sampled =
false`, the kill switch) and sampled requests with
`HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED_MODE=naive` (draws from the process-wide AR sampler) decode
AR through the same PP mesh instead of failing the request. The route decision
logs once per process. The sampled-MTP chi-square oracle
(`sampled_mtp_distribution_hw`) runs its short arms on a PP2 mesh with
`HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_TOPOLOGY=pp2` (test harness variable, with
`HIPFIRE_PP_LAYERS`). This does not admit other speculative engines under PP.
`HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_EXECUTOR=lanes` with `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_LANES=1..8` (any lane
count 1 to 8) selects
the lane suite of the same test (arms `lane_ar`, `lane_batched`, `lane_adaptive`,
`lane_copy`, `lane_cross`; topology `single`, `pp2` or `ep2`; merged and judged per lane in a
separate output directory per executor). Under `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_TOPOLOGY=ep2`
(`HIPFIRE_TOPOLOGY_EP=2`, two visible cards) each arm process runs the EP2 lane
executor over a `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_SHARD=START..END` trial shard: `lane_ar` is
sampled AR lanes (the reference), `lane_batched` / `lane_adaptive` are
native-MTP mesh lanes with root Leviathan acceptance. `lane_copy` (`ep2`,
`HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_LANES=1` or `2` only: the EP copy lane is on by default in
steps of at most two lanes since `047ed76122`) stages the copy lane the serve
stages by default (on the serve's eight-lane store) and runs the same mesh step.
Its own `ep2` cohort is `lane_ar` + `lane_copy` on `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_PROMPT=copy`
(a rewrite request whose assistant turn already holds the start of the passage:
verbatim repetition, so the copy matcher proposes on the first window), and its
coverage fails unless the lane counters show copy windows (takeovers, each
verified against the point-mass draft distribution). The neutral sampler
(1.0 / 0.95) leaves that fixture's four ids nearly deterministic (the chi-square
has one degree of freedom): run the cohort at a flat sampler too
(`HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_TEMP=2.0`, `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_TOP_P=1.0`;
`scripts/job_sampled_lane_shards.sh` takes `SAMPLED_TEMP` / `SAMPLED_TOP_P`), where
rejections and native windows occur. The fixed-count
native-MTP arms pin `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_MAX_ACTIVE=8` so that `N` lanes run
native windows at every `N`; they are native-window coverage arms, not the shipped
switching policy. `lane_cross` (`ep2`, `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_LANES=8` only; the
`ep2` cohort of any other lane count has no such arm) is the switching policy
of the previous default (`max_active` 3; the default is now 8, native C8):
native batched drafting (`HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=0`, `HIPFIRE_MTP_AR_FLOOR=0`)
under `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_MAX_ACTIVE=3` (the previous
`lane_switch::DEFAULT_MAX_ACTIVE`, pinned so the identity records it) with a
staggered trial: 3 of the 8 requests are admitted at tick 0 and the other 5
once the first group has forwarded its prompt, so the early lanes' first decode
window runs native with 3 active lanes and their later windows run AR with 8
(AR from `max_active + 2` = 5 lanes; 4 lanes is the hold case, recorded in the
occupancy histogram). Its coverage fails (with a clear message, never a
verdict) when no request ran both a native and an AR window or when mesh steps
never had occupancy <= 3 and >= 5; it is judged against `lane_ar` like
`lane_batched` (pooled, per lane, merge route `cross`). The native arms
(`lane_batched`, `lane_adaptive`, `lane_cross`) draft at the lane depth chosen
with `HIPFIRE_SAMPLED_MTP_LANE_K` (test harness variable: `2`, the shipped
`HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_K` default, or `3`, the sensitivity run; anything else is
refused) and pin `HIPFIRE_EP_MTP_LANE_K` to it; `lane_ar` pins nothing. The
depth is part of every shard's merge key and identity (a K=2 set never merges
with a K=3 set) and of the evidence (`lane_k`, `max_offered`,
`first_window_offered`): a window offering more than K drafts, or first windows
that never offered K, fail the coverage.
The test never hashes artifacts: the model, its
`.ple-q4` and `.expert-hotset` need records in `<HIPFIRE_HOME>/cache/verified-artifacts.json`,
and `HIPFIRE_FABRIC_ADMISSION` is excluded from the compared identity (it names
the job). The single and pp2 lane topologies validate their configuration and
refuse. `scripts/sampled_lane_suite.sh` runs the suite (`TEST_EXE` runs a staged
test executable).

#### Logical GPU emulation (developer-only)

`HIPFIRE_EMULATE_GPUS` is an environment-only diagnostic switch captured in
the startup snapshot. It is deliberately not a user-facing CLI or persistent
TOML/config key. The value is parsed as `usize`: only a successfully parsed
integer `>=2` enables emulation. Missing, malformed (including negative
values), `0`, and `1` leave emulation disabled.

When enabled, each requested logical device ID is resolved modulo the loaded
physical-device count:

| Physical devices loaded | Requested logical IDs | Resolved physical IDs |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `[0, 1, 2, 3]` | `[0, 0, 0, 0]` |
| 2 | `[0, 1, 2, 3]` | `[0, 1, 0, 1]` |

The switch is an enable switch, not a rank-count setting. `Gpus::init_ep(2,
...)` owns a two-logical-rank EP layout, and `Gpus::init_ep(4, ...)` owns a
four-logical-rank layout; Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP takes its rank count from
`topology.ep` (2..=8) and maps rank `r` to physical device `r % count`, with
the arch still checked (exact gfx1100 or gfx1201, one arch; gfx1151 is admitted under emulation only), same-device peer
checks skipped, and each rank's free VRAM divided by the ranks aliased onto its
device. Duplicate physical IDs remain rejected in ordinary
mode; the snapshotted emulation state is the explicit diagnostic exception for
sealed, load, and batch admission.

For a one-device proof, leave `HIPFIRE_DEVICES` unset and set
`HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`; set both peer selectors above explicitly to `1`.
Leave `HIPFIRE_TP_USE_RCCL` unset—`=0` is not a host all-reduce fallback.
The complete route-oracle workflow and evidence boundary are in
[`multi-gpu.md`](multi-gpu.md#logical-gpu-emulation-developer-only).

### Redline / retained replay

Policy owner: [`REDLINE.md`](REDLINE.md) (**shipped / ref-pinned**). Timing is not route-certified without same-report timed-arm proof. Registry admission is not canonical certification.

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_BACKEND` | `hip` / `off` / `shadow` / `auto`. Unset may select `auto` only from the automatic product defaults in `retained_redline_default`: `mq4r_redline_default` — exact GPU arch `gfx1100`/`gfx1151`/`gfx1201` + case-insensitive `.mq4r` + pp=tp=1 (model-family agnostic; no `arch_id` gate; `gfx1200` and all other arches remain opt-in); Qwen3.5 dense (`qwen3_5`, any weight format) plain-AR decode on exact `gfx1201` with pp=tp=1 and no drafter (retained PM4; byte-identical to the HIP AR graph); Qwen3.5 dense `.mq3-xt` (`qwen3_5`, case-insensitive `.mq3-xt` extension) plain-AR decode on exact `gfx1100` with pp=tp=1 and no drafter (retained PM4 with the GFX10/GFX11 scratch rings, `HIPFIRE_PMSCRATCH_PM4_SCRATCH`; served `generate` greedy token ids identical to the HIP graph and to the ring kill switch on four prompts x 512 tokens including a 4,519-token prompt, with every request's route proof `replays` >= 511 — fabric j4449 and, on the default-selection binary with no backend env, j4456; qwen3.8-27b `.mq3-xt` on a 7900 XTX served decode 62.5 vs 53.4 tok/s at ctx ~30 (x1.17) and 58.7 vs 50.6 at ctx ~4.5K (x1.16), ABBAABBA fresh daemons, j4456; other `gfx1100` formats/families stay opt-in until they have their own identity + timing receipts); Qwen4 (Flash-Next, `qwen4`) plain-AR decode on exact `gfx1201` with pp=tp=1 and no drafter, i.e. an MTP-off load (retained PM4, unpaced; greedy text identical to the HIP route); and DeepSeek4 `.mq2r` AR on gfx1151. A Qwen3.5-family process with a CPU-executed partial-offload spill (`memory.offload_exec=cpu` plus a manual layer/routed-expert budget or explicit `auto`/`-1` that resolves to a spill) gets no retained default — its spilled GEMVs run on the host between GPU launches, which a retained tape does not record — and an explicit non-`hip` backend there is refused at load. Unset/null budgets never request a spill. Existing LFM `.mq4` registry evidence is **not** automatically selected because it is not `.mq4r`; any usable non-default retained route is explicit opt-in and must still prove route support. The sealed LFM [`admissions.yml`](admissions.yml) row is registry evidence/admission only and does not wire runtime defaults. Runtime default ≠ Redline certification/registry admission. Built-in `hip` config profile, another explicit backend selection, `replay.backend = "hip"` or `=hip` disables the automatic default. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1201_PM4_PACING` | NOP pacing of the retained gfx1201 Qwen3.5-dense decode PM4 tape (`replay.gfx1201_pm4_pacing`): `auto` (default) = one 64-body-dword `NOP` after every `DISPATCH_DIRECT`; `off`/`0` = unpaced tape; `nop:N` = N-body-dword NOP. Applied only when the Redline default admits exact gfx1201 + `qwen3_5` (not MQ4R, not MoE, not gfx11). NOPs write no register or memory, so decode is byte-identical. railgun's gfx1201 lowering (`HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_BACKEND=railgun`) paces the same way by its per-arch default; an explicit `off`/`nop:N` applies to it too |
| `HIPFIRE_PMSCRATCH_PM4_SCRATCH` | **On** (unset); `0`/`false`/`off` is the kill switch (developer, read at retained-PM4 prepare). Exact gfx10/gfx11 retained PM4 (`crates/rdna-compute/src/replay.rs`, `crates/redline-rocr/src/pm4_gfx10_scratch.rs`): kernels with a private segment (scratch/spill) get a device-memory scratch ring per queue lane, programmed through `COMPUTE_TMPRING_SIZE` (+ `COMPUTE_DISPATCH_SCRATCH_BASE` on gfx11, V#/flat-scratch user SGPRs on gfx10), so a retained route no longer falls back to the HIP graph for one spilling kernel (gfx1100 qwen3.8-27b `.mq3-xt`: `gemv_mq3g256v2_residual`, 16 B/lane). Proven: kernel output byte-identical between plain AQL dispatch and the PM4 IB on gfx1100, gfx1151 and gfx1030 (incl. the product residual GEMVs and a 2x-full-occupancy alias probe), a 32-minute retained decode soak and repeated runs on gfx1100 without a queue hang, and served `generate` greedy identity on gfx1100 qwen3.8-27b `.mq3-xt` (rings vs HIP graph vs this switch at `=0`: identical token ids on story / code / 4,519-token prompts x 512 tokens, retained route proven engaged per request by `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_ROUTE_PROOF replays=511..512` and the `=0` arm logging the `falling back to HIP: gemv_mq3g256v2_residual ... no scratch ring` message; fabric j4449, j4456). Which models take the retained route is decided by `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_BACKEND` / `retained_redline_default`, not by this switch: exact gfx1100 `.mq3-xt` Qwen3.5 dense plain-AR is now a product default (see that row); other gfx10/gfx11 retained routes stay opt-in. `0` restores the pre-ring refusal (`kernel needs a N-byte private segment per lane but ... has no scratch ring`), which under the `.mq3-xt` default makes the load fall back closed to the HIP graph. Dynamic-callstack kernels and gfx12 stay unsupported. Zero-scratch tapes are untouched either way. |
| `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_TRANSPORT` | `pm4` / AQL family |
| `HIPFIRE_UNSAFE_WSL_REDLINE` | **Unsafe until certified.** `replay.unsafe_wsl_redline`, default off. Under WSL2/ROCDXG (`/dev/dxg` present, `/dev/kfd` absent) the retained Redline PM4 default falls back to the HIP graph with one `[redline] retained default refused` log line, and an explicit `replay.backend = "redline"`/`"shadow"` makes the daemon exit at startup. `1` lifts both. No effect on native Linux; native Windows (no ROCr) stays refused. |
| `HIPFIRE_UNSAFE_WSL_VMM_KV` | **Unsafe until certified.** `memory.unsafe_wsl_vmm_kv`, default off. Under WSL2/ROCDXG automatic KV selects `legacy` (reason in `kv_backend_reason`) and an explicit `kv_backend = "vmm"` is refused, because WDDM VA growth may alias earlier KV segments as it does on native Windows. `1` lifts it. Native Windows stays legacy-only. |
| `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_MANUAL_CAPTURE` | Manual capture delimiters |
| `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_PM4_*` | PM4 research knobs — inventory |
| `HIPFIRE_REDLINE_GAP_TIMING` | Developer-only diagnostic: `1` = per-token host breakdown of the Qwen3.5 plain-AR decode step (embedding, position copy, retained-PM4 patch, submit+wait with its in-IB GPU span, or the HIP-graph launch, and the host time between consecutive steps), one `[gap-timing]` stderr summary per run of consecutive decode positions. Unset reads no clocks. |
| `HIPFIRE_REDLINE_LEDGER` | Developer-only, default off (unset/empty/`0`): a file path. When set, every retained PM4 program prepared by Redline appends JSONL rows to it at **retain time** (off the replay path): one `program` row (arch, dispatch count, tape sequence hash) and one `dispatch` row per retained launch (kernel symbol, code-object origin/module, image SHA-256 and byte length, grid, block, shared memory, grid binding). Replay only increments two counters per program (replay count and the whole-program GPU span the tape's bracketing timestamps already return); a cumulative `replay_summary` row per program is written at controller shutdown and at daemon exit (stdin EOF — a daemon killed by SIGKILL leaves none, and `pm-census ledger` refuses such a ledger). It exists because retained PM4 dispatches are invisible to rocprofv3; ingest with `pm-census ledger` (dispatch-count-weighted CensusV1 cell, origin pm/hipcc by image-SHA join). Default tape unchanged: no GPU timing effect. |
| `HIPFIRE_REDLINE_LEDGER_TIMING` | Developer-only, default off; exact `1` and only together with `HIPFIRE_REDLINE_LEDGER`. **Perturbing:** prepares the per-dispatch-timestamp tape (one GPU-clock write after every dispatch, single queue, gfx12 dispatch pacing off — the `HIPFIRE_REDLINE_DISPATCH_PROFILE` instrument) and sums per-dispatch GPU spans into the ledger, so `pm-census ledger` can emit a time-weighted cell. Spans include the PM4 boundary packets before each dispatch. Run it separately from any throughput measurement. gfx12 (gfx1201) is the supported target: gfx11 yields boundary/kernel span pairs that `pm-census ledger` refuses, gfx10 prepare refuses. |
| `HIPFIRE_DEBUG_PM4_NO_CWSR_PRIV` | Developer-only negative control, default off: `1` stops gfx11 retained PM4 dispatches from setting `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC1.PRIV`, which CWSR needs; with queue preemption active this reproduces the SQC page-0 fault in [`investigations/2026-10-29-gfx11-pm4-cwsr-priv.md`](investigations/2026-10-29-gfx11-pm4-cwsr-priv.md). Never set it outside that repro. |
| `HIPFIRE_DEBUG_FORCE_EVICT_MS` | Developer-only repro aid, default off: `N` (ms, >0) starts one thread on the first PM4 replay that registers a 2 MiB host buffer with `hipHostRegister` and drops/re-faults one page every N ms, so the userptr MMU notifier makes KFD evict and restore (CWSR save/restore) the process's queues. Forces queue preemption without a profiler; costs throughput. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_PM4_EXPERIMENTS` | Developer-only diagnostic: `1` lets the gfx1151 `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_PM4_INITIATOR` / `_INTERLEAVE` / `_RESOURCE_LIMITS` knobs apply to gfx1100 too. Unset, gfx1100 keeps its legacy encoding byte for byte. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1100_Q8X_VERIFY` | Default on for exact gfx1100 (other arches never take the route); `0` restores the f16 WMMA residual tiers. Runs 2..=5-row MQ4G256V2 projections (MTP speculative verify; also 2..5-row prefill tails) through the int8-activation small-batch GEMV (`gemv_q8x.rs`, `kernels/src/gemv_mq4g256v2_q8x.hip`: activations quantized once per projection group, `v_dot4_i32_iu8`, one launch per q/k/v, GDN qkv/z/beta/alpha and gate/up group) instead of the f16 WMMA residual tiers. Eager only; HipGraph capture and Redline recording keep the WMMA tiers. Not byte-identical (q8_1-class activation quantization, KLD-gated); the modules are in the gfx1100 kernel registry and pack (no first-request JIT); logs one `[q8x-verify] route active` line per kernel on first use. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_SHADOW` | Developer-only railgun M1 shadow: a `railgun-jit-corpus` directory. railgun authors its program next to every Redline recording from the corpus facts and diffs its prepared PM4 lowering against the gfx12 tape; never submitted, route unchanged. See REDLINE.md §5 "railgun shadow diff". |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_SHADOW_OUT` | Directory for the shadow's JSON reports (one per prepare). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_INVENTORY` | `railgun-cert recording-inventory json` output; the matched route's recording-dependent decisions go into the shadow report. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CACHE_TABLE` | railgun M2: a G4 probe `cache-table.<arch>.json` (`cargo run -p railgun --features g4-probe --example g4_probe`). Attaches silicon receipts to the cache-table rows whose tier-D rung the probe ran clean for ≥2²⁰ trials; a row whose own rung the probe saw stale fails the prepare. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_BACKEND` | railgun M2, developer-only: `railgun` makes the gfx12 single-IB prepared tape execute railgun's lowering (needs `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_SHADOW`); any refusal fails the prepare closed to HIP, never to the Redline planner. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK` | railgun M2 check mode, developer-only: `off` (default) / `sample(N)` / `always`. Runs the prepared Qwen3.5 or Qwen4 PM4 lowering and its HIP twin on the same state and byte-compares every surface: `always` = every live `State` allocation of the `hip_bridge::registry` allocator registry (the immutable `Weights`, which both loaders register under that role, digested before and after the first checked step of each prepared program), `sample(N)` = one step in N, the program's written allocations only. A difference poisons the route; the twin's result stands. The registry records allocations only while this (or `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_DIGEST=1`) is set. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK_OUT` | railgun M2: file receiving one JSON line per replayed step (check result, phase timings, digests). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` | railgun M2 check-mode negative controls, developer-only: `flip_byte` XORs one byte of one surface into the PM4 arm's result at every checked step, which must come back unequal on exactly that byte (`negative_control.caught` in `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK_OUT`); `drop_boundaries` executes railgun's lowering without any mid-segment wait/acquire (with `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_BACKEND=railgun`), so `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK` must report a difference. Never for serving. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_DIGEST` | railgun M2: `1` = after every replayed step, digest every live `State` allocation (paired by allocation order) into `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK_OUT`; the G2 third arm compares these between a railgun and a pre-railgun process. |
| `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_ROUTE_PROOF_LOG` | Developer-only / one-shot compat for `diagnostic.replay.route_proof_log`. When `1`/`true`/`on` (or TOML `true`), the daemon emits one post-generate retained-route proof marker per successful request: `HIPFIRE_REPLAY_ROUTE_PROOF transport=<name> position=<n> request_id=<id> replays=<count>`. Off by default; product coherence smoke enables it only via temporary serve_harness `config.toml`, not ambient env. A driver that speaks the daemon protocol directly must answer `commit_ready` with a matching `commit` (as `serve` does): an unacked turn aborts after 30 s, rolls back and invalidates the request's observation window, so the request prints no marker even when the retained route replayed every token (`tools/redline/serve_identity_ab.py` is a reference driver). |

### Chat template

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_CHAT_TEMPLATE_FILE` | External jinja path |
| `HIPFIRE_DEFAULT_CHATML` | Only load-bearing at `0` (disable ChatML fallback) |
| `HIPFIRE_JINJA_CHAT` | Serve jinja path toggle |
| `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_GRAMMAR` | Tool grammar |

### Perf / diag (common)

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_DPM_WARMUP_SECS` | DPM warmup before timing |
| `HIPFIRE_HOST_TIMING=1` | Host timing JSON |
| `HIPFIRE_HOST_SITE_LEDGER=<dir>` | Developer diagnostic (lab census, never for timing). Every host synchronization (stream / event / device), HIP copy or memset and HIP allocation / free issued through `hip-bridge`, plus the EP AR host arrival gate wait, appends one line to `<dir>/ledger-<pid>.tsv` with its start, the host time blocked inside the call, bytes and the caller's return-address stack; `<dir>/maps-<pid>.txt` holds `/proc/self/maps` for offline symbolization (build with `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=line-tables-only` for `file:line`). With `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1` each served lane step also writes a step mark, so a census divides every call site per decode step. Off (default) each hook is one relaxed atomic load. |
| `HIPFIRE_PROFILE` / `HIPFIRE_PROFILE_DECODE` / `HIPFIRE_PROFILE_CYCLES` | Profiling |
| `HIPFIRE_DETERMINISTIC` | Determinism toggles in dispatch |
| `HIPFIRE_STREAM_ORDERED_COPIES` | On; `0` off (developer). The blocking copies and fills of steady-state serving (Qwen4 lane top-1 / draft / logits readbacks, head-prologue and lane token-id uploads, lane and state reset memsets, the D2 tail log readback, and the first-request FWHT sign and HC/GDN counter initializers) run on the rank's active stream with one wait on it (`Gpu::dtoh_stream_ordered` / `upload_stream_ordered` / `memset_stream_ordered`), not on the legacy null stream. A blocking null-stream copy puts a packet on the null stream's own hardware queue, and that packet waits for the work queued on the compute stream. In about 1 in 5 fresh EP2 loads the queue mapper put that queue on the same CP pipe as the compute queue. The CP then time-sliced the two queues: 43% of dispatches on that card took 15.4 us instead of 3.1 us, and decode ran 30-35% slower for the life of the process. Same values; `0` restores the null-stream copies. |
| `HIPFIRE_STEADY_READBACK_PINNED` | On; `0` off (developer, read when the `Gpu` is built). `Gpu::dtoh_stream_ordered` (the Qwen4 lane draft and verify top-1 readbacks) lands its copy in a reusable pinned buffer (64 KiB at first use, grown only for a larger read) with one non-blocking `hipMemcpyAsync` and one stream wait, then copies out, instead of an async copy into the caller's pageable slice (which HIP makes host-blocking through its staging path) followed by a stream wait that still cost ~22 us per readback. Same bytes; kept on the old path during graph capture / replay recording. |
| `HIPFIRE_NULL_STREAM_LEDGER=1` | Developer diagnostic. Every HIP call that submits to the legacy null stream (blocking `hipMemcpy` / `hipMemset`, the `*_default` and `stream: None` async variants, a launch, event record or stream wait without a stream) prints `[null-stream] <api> at <file:line> count N` to stderr the first time and at every power of two of its count. Use it to keep the steady-state serving path free of null-stream work. |
| `HIPFIRE_WEIGHT_SLAB` | `off` \| `2m` \| `16m` (developer, also `[developer] weight_slab`). Packs device-resident weight tensors of at most 2 MiB / 16 MiB into shared slabs (zero-filled, 2 MiB-multiple `hipMalloc`s, 32 / 64 MiB) at their exact size on a 4 KiB boundary, instead of one `hipMalloc` each. ROCr rounds every allocation above 1 MiB up to a multiple of 2 MiB, so a model of thousands of 1-8 MiB tensors loses gigabytes (Raon on a 7900 XTX: 4,758 MiB of 21,624 MiB). Takes effect inside a weight-load scope: single-device carriers (`load_admitted`), Flash-Next ranks (single, EP rank thread, PP band), the Raon weight loader. Not packed: host-mapped/offloaded tensors, VMM/arena paths (KV, Raon mirror, S1 arenas, Flash-Next per-layer expert arenas), DFlash drafter weights, tensors above the threshold, EP/TP/PP loaders outside the Flash-Next rank funnel, and anything under `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK`/`HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_DIGEST` recording. A freed weight view is released, never pooled; a slab is returned when its last view is gone (`drain_pool` at unload; `unload_model` reports a slab still held). Output is byte-identical to `off` (same bytes, different addresses). Default `2m` (frees 968 MiB of Raon's 4,758 MiB rounding on the XTX, more than the 550 MiB the chunk-1024 mirror needs, speed within noise on every model measured); `16m` frees 4,412 MiB on Raon but its pp4096 on the R9700 qwen3.8-27B / A3B runs showed a -17% process and a -3.9% mean (n=3, unexplained), so it is opt-in globally; a carrier may set its own unset-env default (`Carrier::weight_slab_default`): Raon loads on gfx1100 take `16m` (the XTX expanded K/V mirror then covers the reserved context instead of falling to the absorbed route past ~12K rows; `hipfire-arch-raon::weights::weight_slab_default`), and an explicit `HIPFIRE_WEIGHT_SLAB` always wins. The mode is resolved per outermost weight-load scope; `off` restores one allocation per tensor. The planned-capacity ledger charges every `hipMalloc` at its ROCr-rounded size (4 KiB up to 1 MiB, 2 MiB above, a slab at its slab bytes) plus a 140 MiB HIP-ring constant, so `own + ring + unledgered` equals the process's fdinfo `drm-memory-vram`. |
| `HIPFIRE_DS4_DENSE_ACT_DIR` | DeepSeek4 calibration-only dump of P1 projection inputs in `collect_e8_hessian` format; direct evaluator flag `--dump-dense-acts` is preferred. |
| `HIPFIRE_HIPCC_EXTRA_FLAGS` | Compatibility alias for `diagnostic.compiler.hipcc_extra_flags` |
| `HIPFIRE_KERNEL_CACHE` | Kernel cache dir (`var_os`) |
| `HIPFIRE_PACK_JOBS` | Kernel-pack builder only: positive worker count, bounded by CPU affinity and available RAM (~1.5 GiB/job). Defaults to `available_parallelism()` minus a reserve of at least one core (one per eight cores). `build-kernel-pack.sh` treats this as the total budget shared by concurrent architectures. |
| `HIPFIRE_JIT_JOBS` | Load-time JIT and synchronous batch compiles: positive worker count. Default `available_parallelism()` minus one core per eight (at least one); always clamped to CPU affinity and to `(MemAvailable − CMA − reserve) / 1.5 GiB`, honoring a tighter cgroup limit; unknown RAM admits one job. Reserve is 1 GiB, plus 2 GiB of loader staging headroom while compiles overlap the weight load. `1` disables concurrency but keeps the pre-ready barrier (it never restores post-ready lazy JIT). `0` or a non-integer fails the load before any GPU allocation. Not read by the pack builder (`HIPFIRE_PACK_JOBS`). |
| `HIPFIRE_ROUTE_PLAN=0` | Developer kill switch for load-time kernel preparation: every load keeps lazy per-kernel loading. `scripts/route_plan_walk.py` sets it to observe actual dispatch rather than preload. Unset or `1` enables exact plans and open-route predictive preparation. Never set in production. |
| `HIPFIRE_ROUTE_PLAN_SEAL=1` | Developer-only strict replay of an exact route plan, without predictive extras. `scripts/route_plan_walk.py --sealed` sets it to prove every replayed request uses only planned kernels. A refused, open, mismatched or incomplete replay cannot certify a `#! sealed` plan. Never set in production. |
| `HIPFIRE_NO_DEVICE_COMPILER=1` | Require verified installed kernel objects instead of JIT; a missing/stale index, wrong symbol/source/flags/profile/ABI/toolchain identity or object SHA-256 fails before HIP loads it. Hot JIT keys remain toolchain-specific. |
| `HIPFIRE_*_DUMP` / `*_TRACE` / `*_PROFILE` | Diagnostic families — see inventory |


For a full indexed install, run `scripts/compile-kernels.sh gfx1201` from the
matching source revision with hipcc available, and install the resulting
`kernels/compiled/gfx1201/` directory beside the daemon executable. Native
installers and container/Nix builds perform this registry packaging directly.
Do not copy bare `.hsaco` or `.hash` files from older installations.

Pack compilation uses one compiler per worker and leaves source, flags, objects
and index contents unchanged. `hipfire-kernel-pack --print-jobs` prints the
current host budget; `HIPFIRE_PACK_JOBS=1` selects serial compilation.
For release tarballs, `scripts/build-kernel-pack.sh --tag TAG --jobs 3
gfx1201 gfx1100 gfx1151` builds up to three architectures concurrently, splitting
the total CPU/RAM budget between them rather than launching three full pools.

**Load-time kernel barrier.** The common loader prepares predictable kernels
before acknowledging `loaded`. Exact routes selected by
`kernel_registry::route_entries` use their header, architecture, policy and
walked-context gates; a sealed route rejects an unplanned kernel after ready.
Every open or unsupported route prepares the architecture's entire exact HIP
registry inventory, excluding modules already in its plan and HIP modules
replaced by accepted native twins. A family trace never limits this compile
set: newly registered predictable kernels must not fall through to a request.
For an exact plan, family traces and matching walked rows prove which
unambiguous, route-compatible symbols may bind eagerly. Unproven exports stay
compile-only until selected. Predictions are not closure proofs; genuinely
unpredicted kernels remain lazy and identify their phase in the log.

Missing compiles overlap the weight sweep using the bounded
`HIPFIRE_JIT_JOBS` budget. If no worker fits alongside weights but one fits
before them, preparation runs before weights. If neither budget fits, or a
predicted compile fails, the load fails instead of deferring that work to the
first request. Verified installed packs satisfy matching recipes without
compiler processes. Readiness logs identify the prediction basis, modules
prepared before ready, eager bindings and compile-only modules. A
`<module>: hipcc compiled (<arch>, key <effective-key>)` line records an actual
cache-miss compiler run; cache hits and embedded-image loads never emit it.
Unplanned-request counters include binds and image loads, not just compiles.
`HIPFIRE_ROUTE_PLAN=0` remains the explicit developer route-walking opt-out,
not a product cold-start policy.

See [the artifact inventory](first-request-jit-inventory.md) for exact shipped
identities and plan coverage. A pack's module inventory is not proof that a
model's route is closed. This is a cold-start/readiness change, not a
steady-state speedup.

Walked plans are generated, not written by hand:
`scripts/route_plan_walk.py` drives real dispatch through the route's request
axes under `HIPFIRE_MODULE_LOAD_AUDIT=1`, and
`scripts/route_plan_build.py` records the exact header layout, architecture,
resolved policy axes, walked context ceiling and source/image identities in
`crates/rdna-compute/src/route_plans/<family>.<arch>.tsv`. Flash-Next records
its QSA/GDN axes; dense 27B and A3B additionally pin head geometry, expert
geometry/placement, KV formats, state format, backend and prefill chunk.
Native-MTP head metadata is fingerprinted separately where needed.

An initial walk produces an open plan. Replay it with
`route_plan_walk.py --sealed`, then pass the successful replay to
`route_plan_build.py --sealed-replay ROUTE=DIR` to certify `#! sealed` for
that route. A predicted, mismatched or unplanned replay cannot certify closure.
Stale source/image identities refuse the exact plan and use open predictive
preparation instead; the route-plan CPU tests reject stale checked-in rows.
Regenerate after changing dispatch or kernel sources. Pack membership and
real route closure are separate requirements.

To build a compiler-free `gfx1201` RMSNorm package for the production
`Gpu::rmsnorm_f32` route, run
`hipfire-kernel-pack --arch gfx1201 --output <daemon-bin-dir>/kernels/compiled/gfx1201 --extra-flags '-DIU4_A4_CANDIDATES=2' --kernel rmsnorm_f32:rmsnorm_f32:kernels/src/rmsnorm.hip`
with the selected ROCm hipcc installed. The tool writes `rmsnorm_f32.hsaco`,
`rmsnorm_f32.hash` (portable cold key), and `rmsnorm_f32.index.json` (versioned
source/flags/profile/ABI/symbol/toolchain/object-SHA record). Compiler flags
must match the daemon's active `FeatureFlags` (including arch defaults);
the exact-source registry exporter emits these flags for `--registry` builds.
A compiler-free `daemon --precompile --module rmsnorm_f32` probes the real
`Gpu::rmsnorm_f32` kernel load and verifies numerical output. The installed
objects must be beside the actual daemon executable, not merely in the CWD;
the cache override controls only writable hot JIT entries.
The runtime never writes under the installed pack: a rejected or stale packaged
object is rebuilt into the hot cache only, so repair an install by re-running the
installer (or `hipfire kernel-pack install`), not by relying on a runtime refresh.
Kernel-selector and arch-specific `HIPFIRE_GFX*` / `HIPFIRE_RDNA*` /
`HIPFIRE_MOE_*` levers are **research/power-user**. Centralized
`FeatureFlags` controls now have typed TOML keys under `kernel` or
`diagnostic.kernel`; defaults and safety remain source-defined and none are
registry-authorized.

### gfx1100 decode-campaign levers (developer, default off)

Evidence and certification for everything in this table:
[`docs/perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md`](perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md)
and the 2026-08-22/23 perf-checkpoint chain. Unless noted, each lever is an
explicit `=1` opt-in, off by default, and certified bit-exact on the stated
shapes before measurement.

| Variable | Notes |
|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_FA_KVWRITE_FOLD=1` | Fold the single-token Q8_0 K/V cache write into the FA-prep epilogue (16Q/4K, head_dim 256, non-compact decode). Bit-exact (probe + greedy e2e). Decode: +0.5-1% (gfx1100 fixture record); +0.19% paired, 10/15 wins (gfx1101, 2026-10-09 verify). |
| *(removed 2026-10-09)* | The v1-only levers were discarded with the legacy quant family: the BT2 prefill family (`HIPFIRE_BT2_DISABLE` + the per-GEMM ``*_BT2_FORCE``/``KS2`` arms), the qkvza/qkv/gate_up fusednorm negative oracles, and the residual-family oracles (`RESIDUAL_PERSIST_R2`/`_SCOPE`, `RESIDUAL_LUT`, `RESIDUAL_DUALROW`). They were reachable only from the legacy HFQ4G256/MQ4G256 GEMM and residual kernels; Magnum V2 dtypes take their own GEMM/GEMV families (several with batch-tiled arms that already ship upstream). The measurements, retractions and removal rationale are recorded in [`docs/perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md`](perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_LM_HEAD_HFQ4=1` / `HIPFIRE_LM_HEAD_HFQ3=1` / `HIPFIRE_LM_HEAD_HFQ2=1` | Research-only load-time requant of a Q8_0 output projection to HFQ{4,3,2}G256 (disk file untouched). Lossy: output diverges from the Q8_0 head's tokens. HFQ3 decode: +~10% (gfx1100 record); +13.5% (gfx1101, 2026-10-09 verify). Excluded from campaign headline numbers by scope ruling. |
| ~~`HIPFIRE_XLDS_R`~~ | **Removed 2026-10-09.** The LDS-staged activation residual probe was invalid: its kernel paired nibbles 4..7 of each lane's word with x offsets 0..3 (it needed a second `float4` at +4), so it never computed the GEMV it claimed, and its launch requested 0 bytes of dynamic LDS for a kernel that stages `k` floats in `extern __shared__`. The 2026-08 amendment-2k `-16%..-37%` table and its "BITEXACT" claim are void; see [`docs/perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md`](perf-checkpoints/2026-10-09-gfx1100-campaign-reverify.md). |
| `HIPFIRE_AWQ_NORM_WAVEGRID=1` | Multi-CU AWQ norm on gfx1100 (null result; infrastructure kept). **Not bit-exact:** on Qwen3.5-4B MQ4 the decode logit bits differ from the default route (256 greedy tokens identical; xtx, 2026-10-13). Leave unset for exact runs. |
| `HIPFIRE_GFX1101_GFX1100_CAMPAIGN=1` | **Experimental**: admit the gfx1100-certified fusions (conv scalar-prep, `fa_prep`, `fa_epilogue`, FA kvwrite fold) on gfx1101 (same RDNA3 ISA). Diagnostic only, not a certified path; `probe_fa_prep` shows 1-ulp rope-branch diffs on gfx1101. |
| `HIPFIRE_SLOW_TOKEN_LOG=1` | bench_qwen35_mq4 prints any decode token slower than 50 ms (environmental-contention detector). |

---

### Raon (Raon-1-mini-preview, developer)

All developer-only, read once at load through the `[developer]` namespace (`hipfire_config::developer_var`); a malformed value is a named error, never a silent fallback.

| Variable | Default / sense | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_PREFILL` | **`batched`**; `serial` | Prefill route (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/bundle.rs`). `batched` is the product route (N rows per module call); `serial` (one `forward_token` per prompt token) is the explicit oracle/control. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS` | **unset / `auto`**; `512`, `1024`, `2048`, `4096` | Rows of one batched prefill chunk (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/carrier.rs`, read once at load). Auto admits the widest of 4096 / 2048 / 1024 / 512 (each capped at the reserved context `max_seq`) whose scratch + module-work bytes fit the free VRAM after the weights and still leave an expanded-mirror budget at least as large as the 512-row policy's for the same `max_seq` (or large enough to cover the whole reserved context); 512 is the floor and is always taken when nothing wider fits, so a 20 GB card degrades to 512/1024 and never fails to load. An explicit value forces that cap: the mirror comparison is skipped, but a rung whose bytes do not fit is a named load error (512 is never an error). A wider chunk streams the routed-expert weights once per chunk instead of once per 512 rows; the MLA decompression tile stays 512 rows and wider chunks expand their new rows tile by tile. The admitted rows and the projected mirror budget are logged once at load (`raon1_mini: prefill chunk rows requested=… admitted=…`). Any other value is a named error. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_PREFILL_PRECISION` | **unset = arch default table** (gfx1201: every site `a8`, i.e. W4A8 FFN + W8A8 attention class; gfx1100: every site `a8` too, i.e. W4A8 routed experts on the gfx11 grouped twin and W8A8 on the Q8_0 sites (attention class, and the shared/dense FFN of the Q8F16 artifact) on the i8-MMQ 64x64 kernel, which needs K % 128 == 0 (only K=576 `mla_q_b` runs F16); gfx1151: `routed_gu`/`routed_dn` `a8`, every other site `f16` (the Q8_0 i8 route is admitted there as an opt-in); A4/FP8 are gfx1201-only; table `default_table` in `crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/precision.rs`; other arches all F16); `f16` (kill switch: every site F16), `a4`, `a8`, `fp8` (every site requests it), or `[<base>,]<site>=<tier>,...` | Per-site prefill activation precision of Raon-1-mini, resolved once at load and logged per site. `<base>` is `default|f16|a4|a8|fp8` (default if omitted); `<site>` is `routed_gu|routed_dn|shared|dense|mla_q|mla_kv|mla_go|rda_qkvg|rda_o`; `<tier>` is `f16|a4|a8|fp8`; `site=f16` is that site's kill switch (e.g. `f16,mla_q=a8`, `rda_o=f16`). Unknown site or tier is a load error. A tier takes effect only where the site's weights can run it (qt44 FFN weights with symmetric headers: a4/a8/fp8; Q8_0: a8/fp8) on gfx1201 at prefill chunks of >= 64 rows outside graph capture; anything else runs F16. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN` | **`auto`**; `1`; `0` | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/railgun_decode.rs`, read once by `RaonBundle::new`). Tri-state, default `auto`. Raon plain-AR decode (`decode_step`; DFlash verify and prefill are untouched) runs as ONE certified railgun PM4 program per admitted binding instead of the HIP graph: the decode body is planned under the plan sink, every node needs a tier-D receipt for its exact loaded code object in the receipt corpus (a missing/stub receipt, declared words, a non-PM4 node after the witnesses, an untypable copy, a failed `SealedTable` seal/bind or a multi-segment plan declines), then lowered through the owned gfx12 transport. `auto` creates the owner only for exact gfx1201, whole band, graph decode route (`HIPFIRE_GRAPH` not `0`), no retained replay, no CPU-executed weights, AND a receipt corpus found (`HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_CORPUS`, else the `railgun-corpus` directory beside the installed kernel pack); otherwise there is no owner and the route is the HIP graph byte for byte. `1` forces the owner whatever the arch or corpus and logs every decline once per kind. `0` is the hard kill: no owner, no corpus discovery, no allocation. An unrecognised spelling logs a message and is treated as `0`. A step the owner declines (census tap, `HIPFIRE_MOE_ROUTE_DUMP`/`HIPFIRE_MOE_ACT_CAPTURE`, absorbed MLA route, cold decode graph, no receipt for an object, ...) runs the HIP graph step exactly once with nothing consumed; the program is rebuilt on any key change (binding, mapped/mirror rows, route, scratch epoch, model/flag digest, object digests, sealed-table generations). A failure after a submit never retries through HIP: a quiescent failure aborts the request, unknown quiescence quarantines the bundle (its tensors are leaked, not freed). Stats: `RaonBundle::railgun_stats`. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_CORPUS` | **unset → the corpus beside the installed kernel pack, else the per-user one** | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/railgun_decode.rs`, read once by `RaonBundle::new`). Directory of the railgun JIT receipt corpus (a `railgun-jit-corpus` build's output directory, holding `index.json`) the owner looks node facts up in, keyed by the exact loaded code-object SHA-256 under the loaded toolchain. Unset: the owner looks for `<root>/kernels/railgun-corpus` where `<root>/kernels/compiled/<arch>` is the installed pack the process loads objects from (`KernelCompiler::installed_pack_dir`), then for the per-user corpus `<cache root>/railgun-corpus/<arch>` (a sibling of the arch-keyed hot kernel cache; see `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_LOCAL_CORPUS`). Under `auto` an explicitly set directory that holds no `index.json` is reported once and no owner is created; a corpus built from another pack or toolchain declines on the facts lookup, never runs. Optional G4 silicon receipts: `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CACHE_TABLE`, as the DFlash window. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_LOCAL_CORPUS` | **`1`**; `0` | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/railgun_provision.rs`, read once by `RaonBundle::new`). With no receipt corpus anywhere (and `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN` not `0`, on an admissible context), the owner builds the per-user corpus once per process: right after the decode graph's warm-up step (which JIT-compiles every decode module into the hot cache) and after loading railgun's own copy/fill transport kernels (the program's transport nodes name their modules, which exist in the hot cache only once something loaded them) it runs the shipped `railgun-jit-corpus from-cache --arch <arch> --program raon-ar-decode-<arch> --cache <arch-keyed hot cache dir> [--pack <installed pack dir>] --out <cache root>/railgun-corpus/<arch>` and blocks on it (up to 180 s, then it is killed), then builds the owner. The binary is looked up beside the running executable, in `$HIPFIRE_HOME/bin`, then on `PATH` (the runtime never links the analyzer). The corpus is only ever written under the per-user cache root, never the install root. A missing binary, a refusal (exit 2), a failure, a timeout or an unwritable output directory declines to the HIP graph with the exact reason (one log line) and is recorded in `<hot cache dir>/railgun-corpus.refused.json` keyed by a fingerprint of the arch, toolchain, binary and hot cache contents, so a fresh process with the same cache and binary does not re-run it (delete the file to retry); a refusal that names modules with no hot cache entry is not recorded (a later process retries), and a refused build latches: every later step of the process declines in O(1) with no allocation; the last run's output is `<hot cache dir>/railgun-corpus-from-cache.log`. `0` never spawns anything. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_CORPUS_GFX1100` / `_GFX1201` | **unset** | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/railgun_decode.rs`). The corpus directory of the Raon railgun owner for exactly that arch; checked before `HIPFIRE_RAON_RAILGUN_CORPUS`. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CACHE_TABLE_GFX1100` / `_GFX1201` | **unset** | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/railgun_decode.rs`). The G4 probe table (`cache-table.<arch>.json`) the Raon railgun owner attaches for exactly that arch; checked before `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CACHE_TABLE`. The file must carry the device's own arch identity (another arch's table declines with `cache_table`); without any table, unreceipted rows lower with full System fences (`UnreceiptedSystem`). |
| `HIPFIRE_BF16W_SMALL_ROWS` | **`1`**; `0` | Generic, gfx1201 and gfx1100 only: the mHC stream-mix selector GEMM (BF16 activation rows, M <= 32) and the grouped-MoE router GEMM (F32 activation rows, M > 32) (`crates/hipfire-dispatch/src/pipeline/stream_mix.rs`, `moe_program.rs`) run the exact-order register-tiled `gemm_bf16_small_rows` kernels (`gemm_bf16_xf32_small_rows_{r1,r4,r8}`, `gemm_bf16_xbf16_small_rows_{s1,s4,s8}`) at every row count, the 1 / 4 / 8-rows-per-CTA profile picked by row count with per-arch thresholds (`small_rows_profile`: gfx1201 router 16 / 256, selector 80 / 512; gfx1100 router 24 / 384, selector 160 / 1024). Bit-identical to `gemm_f32_batched` on the widened weight (same lane-strided FMA order and width-32 shuffle tree). The kernels have no k tail: the route needs `K % 512 == 0` (Raon K = 2560 router / 10240 selector); other K keeps the batched/tiled routes. `0` restores the `gemm_bf16_xf32_batched` / tiled routes (`HIPFIRE_BF16W_TILED_ROWS` below). |
| `HIPFIRE_BF16W_TILED_ROWS` | **`1`**; `0` | Generic, gfx1201 only: the mHC stream-mix selector GEMM and the grouped-MoE router GEMM (`crates/hipfire-dispatch/src/pipeline/stream_mix.rs`, `moe_program.rs`) run the x-stationary `gemm_bf16_xf32_tiled_rows` for calls of 512 rows or more (prefill). Decode and speculative-verify row counts (below 512) keep `gemm_bf16_xf32_batched`, which is far faster there (Raon R9700 decode step 13.8 ms vs 25.5 ms with a tiled kernel). Both read the BF16 router / selector weight and widen it exactly in registers; both are bit-identical to `gemm_f32_batched` on the widened weight (same lane-strided FMA order and width-32 shuffle tree), the tiled one reading each activation row once per 8×MPW outputs instead of once per output. Other archs keep `gemm_bf16_xf32_batched` (gfx1100 measured 2.3–2.9% slower with the F32 tiled kernel). `0` runs `gemm_bf16_xf32_batched` on gfx1201 as well (kill switch). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_ATTN_TILED` | **`1`**; `0` | MLA tiled causal prefill + split-KV decode kernels (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/mla.rs`). `0` selects the serial reference kernel. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_MLA_ABSORB` | **`1`**; `0` | Attend over the latent cache (absorbed algebra) once a context exceeds the expanded-mirror budget. Not bit-exact to the decompressed route (KLD matched). `0` refuses contexts past the budget. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_MIRROR_Q8` | **`1`**; `0`; `force` | Expanded MLA K_nope/V mirror format (Q8 = INT8 codes + one F16 scale per (row, head, plane): 72,800 B/token instead of 143,360, so twice the rows fit). Default rule: Q8, except that a speculative (DFlash2) slot keeps BF16 while its budget covers the whole reserved context (its verify rows run the tiled kernel, faster on BF16); past that it takes Q8 too. R9700, v2 artifact: Q8 decodes +1% / +4% / +11.5% / +11.5% faster at 1K / 4K / 16K / 32K context and prefills -2.3% / -5.6% slower at 16K / 32K; KLD unchanged (receipts `raon/pfx/ev/fp8u/mir/`). Tiled kernels only (forced off with `HIPFIRE_RAON_ATTN_TILED=0`). `0` always keeps the BF16 mirror (kill switch); `force` takes Q8 for speculative slots as well. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_DFLASH_ROLLBACK` | **`recurrence`**; `replay` | DFlash2 partial-accept rollback of the Raon target (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/rollback.rs`). `recurrence` restores the pre-verify slab snapshot (RDA matrices, conv histories, `seq_len`), reruns only each RDA layer's gated-delta recurrence over the kept rows from the inputs the armed verify recorded in a per-layer ring (`q, k, v', log_alpha, beta` and the raw conv inputs; no extra launches, the forward writes those planes in place), and rebuilds the conv histories by device copies (one restore + 18 recurrence launches + 36 pitched copies instead of a second forward). `replay` is the kill switch: restore + full eager batched replay of the kept rows (previous behavior; the ring is then not armed). Read once per process; a malformed value is a named error. Oracle: `raon_dflash_oracle rollback` gates the two arms on the whole persistent slab (byte equality per `keep`). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_VERIFY_ROWEXACT` | **unset/`auto`**; `1`; `0` | Developer (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/projection.rs`, read once). Unset/`auto`: speculative-verify blocks of 2..4 rows on gfx1201 and 2..9 rows on gfx1100 (the widest blocks where the route is no slower than the batched one; other arches none) take the row-exact route; `1`: every block of 2..16 rows; `0`: never. Blocks on the row-exact route run every row exactly as the single-row decode step does, so each verify row is bit-identical to the decode row (greedy DFlash2 on Raon-1-mini is then byte-identical to AR): Q8_0 projections `Gpu::gemv_q8_0_rows_exact` (weights read once for up to 16 rows; hipcc multi-row kernels on gfx1100/gfx1201, per-row singleton launches elsewhere), MoE `GroupedMoeProtocol::RowsExact` (routed experts on the indexed decode core, the shared expert's Q8_0 projections through the multi-row GEMV), MLA attention `MlaRowsRoute::DecodeExact` (one multi-row split-KV decode launch pair; row r equals the rows == 1 decode call at its length), dense layer 0 run row-exact, F32 / qt44 projections run the decode projection once per row. The DFlash2 drafter's head over draft rows stays on the batched projection. Costs a little at 2-4 rows and saves at larger blocks (R9700 verify 2/3/4 rows 16.88/17.69/19.46 ms vs 17.23/18.18/19.20 ms off; decode unchanged). Not graph-specific; applies to the eager and the recorded verify alike. Diagnostics: `raon_dflash_oracle rowexact --census on`. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_LM_HEAD` | **unset → Q8F16**; `qt44` | Quantizer only (`crates/hipfire-quantize/src/raon.rs`): `lm_head.weight` storage. `qt44` keeps the symmetric qt44 head (smaller artifact). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_MHC_FUSED` | **`1`**; `0` | Raon mHC site chain (`crates/hipfire-dispatch/src/pipeline/stream_mix.rs`, `kernels/src/stream_mix_route_aggregate.hip`): fuses the Helmert route finalize and the stream aggregate+RMSNorm of one site into one pass over the old streams (`StreamMixStage::RouteAggregate`) for prefill batches of 64 rows or more and geometry 4 streams x 2560; the unfused chain reads the stream stack twice more before the branch (weightless RMS in `stream_mix_route_helmert`, aggregate). `pre`/`post`/`res`/branch input are bit-identical to the two-kernel chain (same per-thread accumulation order, same reduction trees; `res` keeps the runtime stream count so the compiler cannot re-fold the Helmert scales). `0` is the kill switch (also disables `HIPFIRE_RAON_MHC_BF16_STREAMS`). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_MHC_BF16_STREAMS` | **`1`**; `0` (needs `HIPFIRE_RAON_MHC_FUSED` not `0`, BF16 selectors) | Stores the mHC stream ping-pong planes (`stream_a`/`stream_b`) as BF16 bits instead of F32 planes carrying BF16 values (every stream write is bf16-rounded, so values are identical, bytes halved). Selector GEMM (`gemm_bf16_xbf16_*`), fused route+aggregate, postmix (`stream_mix_postmix_bf16`), embedding broadcast and final mean read/write the BF16 planes at every row count including decode and verify; taps and the DFlash capture widen exactly. Read once when the scratch is allocated (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/mhc.rs::bf16_streams_enabled`). `HIPFIRE_RAON_MHC_FUSED=0` kills both. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_GFX1100_DOWN_PRODUCER` | **`1`**; `0` | Raon-1-mini-preview routed-expert down site on exact gfx1100 (7900 XTX): the grouped activation, FWHT rotation and A8 `block_i8_128` quantizer run as ONE launch (`ffn_situ_grouped_rotate_i8`, `kernels/src/ffn_grouped_activation_rotate_i8.gfx1201.hip`, one wave per (grouped row, 256-column group); the F32 activation and rotated planes are never written) instead of `ffn_situ_grouped_f32` + `mq_rotate_x` + `quantize_int8_mmq_ds128`. Output bit-identical to the three-launch chain (byte oracle), so KLD is unchanged. `0` restores the three-launch chain. gfx1201 keeps `HIPFIRE_GFX12_PRODUCER_QUANT_FUSED`; the shared/dense rows entry stays gfx1201-only. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RDA_FUSED_PREP` | **`1`**; `0` | Raon RDA layer producer and output norm on exact gfx1201 (the conv + prepare producer alone also on exact gfx1100; the gated norm stays gfx1201-only) for prefill batches of 64 rows or more (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/rda.rs`, `kernels/src/raon_rda_conv_prepare.hip`, `kernels/src/gated_rmsnorm_sigmoid_rows_w128.hip`): the three depthwise convs (q/k/v, K=4, SiLU, BF16 round, in-place raw history) and `raon_rda_prepare` run as ONE launch with no materialized conv planes (one wave per (head, row tile), conv window in registers, the incumbent's fixed LDS L2 tree evaluated as a wave-shuffle tree), and the per-head gated RMSNorm runs as one wave per (row, head) with float4 accesses. Outputs, conv histories and the recurrent state are bit-identical to the incumbent launches (same expressions and reduction order, explicit `__fmul_rn`/`__fadd_rn` where a product feeds a sum). Decode, verify and rollback-replay rows (< 64 rows, or a taped verify) keep the incumbent launches. `0` is the kill switch. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_RDA_SEGMENT` | **`1`**; `0` | With the chunked RDA core (`HIPFIRE_GATED_DELTA_VECTOR_CHUNKED`), an ordinary prefill chunk of more than 256 rows runs each RDA layer as consecutive 256-row segments (producer, chunked core, gated norm per segment, state and conv history chained through the in-place tensors) instead of three whole-chunk passes, so the planes the core consumes are still cache-resident and the core workspace stays small. Segments are whole C64 chunks, so every output and state is bit-identical to the single pass. `0` restores the single pass (`crates/hipfire-arch-raon/src/rda.rs`). |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_SHARED_FFN` | **unset → `qt44`**; `q8f16`, `bf16` | Quantizer only (`crates/hipfire-quantize/src/raon.rs`): storage of the always-active FFN weights (shared-expert gate/up/down of every MoE layer and the dense layer-0 gate/up/down). `q8f16` stores them as Q8F16 (qt3, group 32), `bf16` as source-exact BF16 (qt16); neither carries an AWQ sidecar, and the shared `down_proj` keeps physical K 1280 (zero-padded columns) for both. Recorded as `hipfire_raon.shared_ffn` in the artifact metadata (`q8f16`/`bf16`, or `<storage>:shared` / `<storage>:shared-gateup` / `<storage>:dense` for a partial-scope swap). An unknown value is a conversion error. `hipfire-quantize --raon-shared-ffn <q8f16\|bf16> --input <shipped.hfq> --raon-source <checkpoint dir> --output <out.hfq>` builds the same variant from a shipped qt44 artifact without re-running calibration; `--raon-shared-ffn-scope <all\|shared\|shared-gateup\|dense>` (default `all`) restricts it to the MoE layers' shared experts (or only their gate/up weights) or to the dense layer-0 MLP, and `--raon-shared-ffn-verify` takes the same scope flag. |
| `HIPFIRE_RAON_TOKENIZER` | unset → test skips | Test-only: HF `tokenizer.json` path (or its directory) for the `#[ignore]`d Raon tokenizer HF-parity check in `crates/hipfire-runtime/src/tokenizer.rs`; never read by runtime code. |

## Manual — tokenizer differential test (dev-only, not runtime)

Read only by `crates/hipfire-runtime/tests/hf_tokenizer_differential.rs` (see [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md#tokenizer-differential-test-hf-oracle)); the pure-Rust HF `tokenizers` oracle is a `[dev-dependencies]` entry and never ships.

| Variable | Default | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_FUZZ_CASES` | `50000` | adversarial cases per tokenizer |
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_FUZZ_SEED` | built-in | u64 seed (decimal or `0x` hex), mixed with a per-family hash |
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_DIFF_REPORT_DIR` | `<temp>/hipfire_tokenizer_differential` | JSONL/summary report directory |
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_DIFF_MIN_PROBES` | `5000` | minimisation probe budget per unique signature |
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_DIFF_MIN_LIMIT` | `2000` | max signatures minimised per family |
| `HIPFIRE_TOKENIZER_DIFF_PRINT_LIMIT` | `50` | root signatures printed per family |

## Manual — top-level doc references

The canonical documentation checker requires every `HIPFIRE_*` token in `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` to appear in this file. Tokens historically routed from those surfaces (keep listed even if a root file is later thinned):

`HIPFIRE_ATTN_FLASH`, `HIPFIRE_DDTREE_` (prefix family; concrete vars in inventory), `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_DRAFT`, `HIPFIRE_GRAPH`, `HIPFIRE_HOST_TIMING`, `HIPFIRE_KV_MODE`, `HIPFIRE_LM_HEAD_F16`, `HIPFIRE_LOCAL`, `HIPFIRE_NORMALIZE_PROMPT`, `HIPFIRE_PROMPT_HEAT_JSON`, `HIPFIRE_PROMPT_HEAT_LIMIT`, `HIPFIRE_PROMPT_TOKEN_HEAT`, `HIPFIRE_VERIFY_GRAPH`.

## Manual — TOML key → legacy environment compatibility

Compatibility map from the `hipfire-config` schema. TOML is the supported
persistent surface. The native CLI sends typed policy directly to the daemon;
it does not project these values into the child environment. The names below
are accepted only as compatibility/one-shot input to the resolver. Message-
field-only keys (temperature, thinking, …) have no compatibility alias.

Copyable user, developer, and retained-PM4 TOML profiles are in
[`docs/configs/`](configs/README.md).

| Config key | Env |
|---|---|
| `kv_cache` | `HIPFIRE_KV_MODE` |
| `flash_mode` | `HIPFIRE_ATTN_FLASH` |
| `prompt_normalize` | `HIPFIRE_NORMALIZE_PROMPT` |
| `dflash_ngram_block` | `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_NGRAM_BLOCK` |
| `experimental_budget_alert` | `HIPFIRE_EXPERIMENTAL_BUDGET_ALERT` |
| `max_total_think_tokens` | `HIPFIRE_MAX_TOTAL_THINK_TOKENS` |
| `memory.gpu_layer_budget` | `HIPFIRE_GPU_LAYER_BUDGET` |
| `memory.moe_expert_budget` | `HIPFIRE_MOE_EXPERT_BUDGET` |
| `memory.state_quant` | `HIPFIRE_STATE_QUANT` |
| `memory.offload_exec` | `HIPFIRE_OFFLOAD_EXEC` |
| `mtp_mode` / `mtp_k` | `HIPFIRE_MTP_MODE` / `HIPFIRE_MTP_K` |
| `speculation.mtp_ngram` | `HIPFIRE_MTP_NGRAM` |
| `speculation.mtp_sampled` | `HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED` |
| `chat_template` | `HIPFIRE_CHAT_TEMPLATE_FILE` |
| `default_chatml=false` | `HIPFIRE_DEFAULT_CHATML=0` |
| `speculation` | `HIPFIRE_SPECULATION` |
| `default_model` / serve model | `HIPFIRE_MODEL` |
| `idle_timeout` | `HIPFIRE_IDLE_TIMEOUT` |
| `max_request_bytes` | `HIPFIRE_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` |
| `serve_max_queue` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MAX_QUEUE` |
| `serve_queue_timeout_ms` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_QUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS` |
| `serve.local` | `HIPFIRE_LOCAL` |
| `serve.multi_slot` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MULTI_SLOT` |
| `serve.multi_slot_slots` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MULTI_SLOT_SLOTS` |
| `serve.multi_slot_ctx` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MULTI_SLOT_CTX` |
| `serve.multi_slot_prefill_chunk` | `HIPFIRE_SERVE_MULTI_SLOT_PREFILL_CHUNK` |
| `prefill_*` | matching `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_*` |
| `mmq_screen*` | `HIPFIRE_MMQ_SCREEN*` |
| `hardware.devices` | `HIPFIRE_DEVICES` / `HIPFIRE_DEVICE`; resolves index / `gfxNNNN` / `GPU-<uuid>` / PCI entries to physical cards and synchronizes `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<their ROCr selectors>` with `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0..N-1` before GPU initialization |
| `hardware.allow_mixed_arch` | `HIPFIRE_ALLOW_MIXED_ARCH` |
| `hardware.tp_use_rccl` | `HIPFIRE_TP_USE_RCCL` |
| `topology.ep` | `HIPFIRE_TOPOLOGY_EP` |
| `hardware.uniform_vram_tolerance_gb` | `HIPFIRE_UNIFORM_VRAM_TOLERANCE_GB` |
| `generation.loop_guard_threshold` / `generation.loop_guard_window` | `HIPFIRE_NGRAM_LOOP_THRESHOLD` / `HIPFIRE_NGRAM_WINDOW` |
| `kernel.flash_partials_batch` | `HIPFIRE_FLASH_PARTIALS_BATCH` |
| `kernel.lm_head_f16` | `HIPFIRE_LM_HEAD_F16` |
| `diagnostic.prompt_*` | matching `HIPFIRE_PROMPT_*` |
